Scope: Builder → Pedals only

# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 003

Date: September 16, 2026
Scope: Builder → Pedals only

This block records builder/product relationships only. Current and historical status is preserved when the manufacturer's own catalog provides it. This is a research checkpoint, not a claim of exhaustive lifetime coverage for either builder.

## 1. JHS Pedals

Builder / brand: JHS Pedals

### Current Overdrive / Drive catalog
Verified from JHS's official Overdrive collection and current product catalog:
- AT Mini
- Notadümb̈le V2
- Morning Glory Clean
- 424 Gain Stage
- Kilt 10
- Notaklön
- Notaklön Splatter
- Morning Glory V4
- Bonsai
- Kilt V2
- AT+
- Angry Charlie V3
- The Violet / Violet
- 3 Series Screamer
- Charlie Brown V4
- Moonshine V2
- PG-14
- 3 Series Overdrive

Additional current products in the official all-products catalog with dirt function:
- Crayon — preamp / distortion / fuzz
- Colour Box V2 — preamp with dirt-oriented use and placement in the JHS fuzz/distortion collection
- Double Dragon — octave-up distortion section within an octave device

### Current Fuzz / Distortion catalog
Verified from JHS's official Fuzz / Distortion collection:
- Coyote
- EHX by JHS — Big Muff 2
- Kilt 10
- Muffuletta
- PackRat
- Hard Drive
- The Violet / Violet
- PackRat White
- Cheese Ball
- Crayon
- 3 Series Fuzz
- 3 Series Distortion
- Colour Box V2
- Double Dragon

Notes on classification:
- JHS's own collection places products into broad categories; where an individual product page gives a more precise function, that wording is preferred.
- Cheese Ball is explicitly identified by JHS as Distortion / Fuzz.
- Crayon is explicitly identified by JHS as Preamp / Distortion / Fuzz.
- 3 Series Distortion is explicitly identified by JHS as a distortion pedal that can also reach overdrive and fuzz tones.
- Double Dragon is retained because its official product page explicitly identifies an octave-up distortion section, but its primary function is an octave device. It should not be confused with a standalone fuzz-only model.

### Discontinued / rare dirt products verified from JHS's official archive
- 73 Rams Head — Fuzz
- Kilt V1 — Overdrive / drive lineage
- Colour Box V1 — Preamp / dirt-oriented product
- Double Barrel V4 — Overdrive family product
- Twin Twelve — Overdrive

Notes:
- JHS maintains a dedicated Discontinued & Rare area. The archive is useful for preserving recognized older JHS product identities without guessing from reseller listings.
- The JHS site currently lists products such as 73 Rams Head and Kilt V1 in the rare/discontinued catalog. They are recorded as historical relationships rather than as currently sold products.

Sources:

- https://jhspedals.info/products/muffuletta

# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 004

Date: September 16, 2026
Scope: Builder → Pedals only

This block records builder/product relationships only. Current and legacy/discontinued products are included when an official manufacturer catalog or authoritative manufacturer documentation identifies the product as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz. This is a checkpoint, not a claim of exhaustive lifetime coverage.

## 1. Catalinbread Effects

Builder / brand: Catalinbread Effects

### Current dirt products verified from official Catalinbread collections, product pages, and manuals

#### Overdrive / Distortion / Fuzz
- SFT
- Dirty Little Secret
- RAH
- Sabbra Cadabra
- Formula 55
- Formula 5F6
- Formula 51
- Formula No. 5 (Reissue)
- Galileo
- Galileo 4K
- Galileo Red Special
- Dirty Little Secret Deluxe
- Clean Little Secret
- SideArm Overdrive
- Misnomer
- Crooner Preamp — clean boost through overdrive, distortion, and fuzz
- Airstrip Console Pre — gain/preamp dirt product
- Fuzzrite
- Fuzzrite Germanium
- Fuzzrite Germanium (White)
- Fuzzrite Mini
- Katzenkönig
- Giygas 2K Fuzz
- Epoch Bias
- Naga Viper MKII
- Manx Loaghtan Fuzz (Reissue)
- Perseus DIO — octave device with dry boosted distortion
- Knight School Distortion
- Knight School Fuzz (DIY Kit)
- Knight School Overdrive (Built Pedal)
- Knight School Overdrive DIY Kit
- Knightdrive
- Little Secret

Notes:
- Catalinbread's official site separates some products into named series, including a Foundation Overdrive Series and a Boost/Fuzz/Distortion Series.
- Product names are kept as catalog identities. DIY-kit entries are recorded when the manufacturer's own catalog identifies the corresponding dirt effect, but they should not later be mistaken for a separate circuit/product identity from the built version without further catalog analysis.
- Product variants that are clearly cosmetic collections, such as Fireworks or White editions, are not expanded into separate catalog identities here unless the manufacturer's product catalog treats them as a separate named product.
- Crooner Preamp is retained because Catalinbread explicitly describes it as covering clean boost, overdrive, distortion, and fuzz.
- Perseus DIO is retained as a dirt-relevant compound effect because Catalinbread explicitly describes its dry channel as boosted distortion and its overall concept as fuzz plus octave-down.

- Analog Man's official King Of Tone page identifies the King Of Tone as a hand-made USA overdrive and also references the Prince Of Tone as a related Analog Man product.
- The official Sun Face page identifies Sun Face and modified Fuzz Face products as Analog Man fuzz offerings.
- The official SunLion manual identifies the Sun Face as the right side of the combined pedal, establishing the Sun Lion's fuzz product relationship.
- The official Astro Tone page identifies the Astro Tone as a fuzz pedal and explicitly describes its sound as closer to fuzz than distortion.
- The official Peppermint Fuzz page identifies the product as a fuzz pedal and states that it is hand made in the USA.
- No transistor/component information is being carried into the archive at this stage, even when the manufacturer's pages mention it.

Sources:
- https://www.analogman.com/kingtone.htm
- https://www.analogman.com/fuzzface.htm
- https://www.analogman.com/manuals/SunLionManual.pdf
- https://www.analogman.com/astrotone.htm
- https://www.analogman.com/pepper.htm
- https://www.analogman.com/manuals/KingofToneManualV4-2020.pdf

# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 007

Date: September 16, 2026
Scope: Builder → Pedals only

This block records BOSS dirt products supported by official BOSS/Roland product catalogs, the official manual archive, and BOSS historical catalog material. Current and historical products are included where the manufacturer identifies them as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz. This is not an exhaustive lifetime catalog.

## 1. BOSS

Builder / brand: BOSS

### Current Overdrive / Drive
- SD-1 SUPER OverDrive
- SD-1W SUPER OverDrive
- OD-3 OverDrive
- OD-1X OverDrive
- OS-2 OverDrive/Distortion
- BD-2 Blues Driver

- FZ-2 Hyper Fuzz

### Notes
- BOSS's current official catalog explicitly divides the dirt family into Distortion, Overdrive, and Fuzz categories.
- BOSS's 40th Anniversary Compact Pedals material documents historical models including DF-2, OD-2, DS-2, and HM-2 with production-era information. Only the product relationship is being retained here.
- The official BOSS manual archive confirms historical dirt models including DF-2, FZ-2, FZ-3, HM-2, and related models.
- OD-1 is retained as a historical BOSS overdrive based on BOSS/Roland's official documentation describing the original compact pedal lineup.
- JB-2 is retained because BOSS's official catalog identifies it as an Angry Driver created jointly by BOSS and JHS Pedals. The builder relationship here remains BOSS → JB-2, with the collaboration noted rather than splitting the product into separate identities.
- Waza Craft versions are retained as named catalog products where BOSS presents them separately. No deeper variant analysis is performed in this phase.

### Boost / dirt-adjacent

Notes:
- Friedman’s current pedal catalog lists BE-OD, BE-OD Deluxe, Buxom Boost, Dirty Shirley Pedal, Golden Pearl, and Smallbox Overdrive Pedal among its pedal products.
- Buxom Boost is retained only as a dirt-adjacent boost because Friedman describes it as a clean boost that can push an amplifier into harmonic overdrive. It is not being classified as an overdrive pedal.
- The Friedman IR-D, IR-J, and IR-X are amp/preamp products and are excluded from the dirt-pedal census rather than forcing them into overdrive or distortion.

- Octava

### Fuzz
- Gas Giant
- Star Eater
- Octava
- Disnortion

Notes:
- Pigtronix’s current catalog lists Gas Giant, Star Eater, Gamma Drive, Octava, and Philosopher’s Gold among its current pedal offerings.
- Pigtronix explicitly describes Disnortion as an analog Fuzz + Overdrive, and Octava as an Analog Octave Fuzz with a front-end overdrive stage.
- Philosopher’s Gold is explicitly described as a compressor/sustainer with germanium distortion/overdrive, so it is retained as a compound dirt product.
- Philosopher’s Tone 2 has a footswitchable Grit circuit and is described by Pigtronix as providing overdrive through distortion tones. It is retained because the dirt function is explicit, despite the pedal also being a compressor/sustainer.
- Non-dirt Pigtronix products are excluded.

Sources:

- FWW BUSS and FWW Plus are retained because Barber explicitly identifies them as overdrive products.
- Exacta is retained as fuzz because Barber explicitly describes it as a fuzz pedal.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 018

Date: September 16, 2026
Scope: Builder → Pedals only

This block records builder/product relationships only. Product names are taken from current manufacturer catalogs and official product pages where available, with official legacy pages used when needed. This is a research checkpoint, not an exhaustive lifetime catalog.

## 1. Electronic Audio Experiments (EAE)

Builder / brand: Electronic Audio Experiments

### Current dirt products verified from the official EAE catalog
- Model feT — preamp / drive product
- Greathammer — bass-focused overdrive
- Surveyor — distortion / saturation preamp-style dirt product
- Citadel — preamp-style overdrive
- Limelight — dual-channel overdrive
- Longsword — drive through massive distortion
- Halberd — transistor overdrive
- Glaive — octave fuzz / fuzz-distortion
- The Bard — overdrive

### Legacy / collaborative dirt identities verified from the official EAE site
- Dagger — drive / overdrive; discontinued
- Dude Incredible — combined drive and fuzz/distortion product; legacy identity
- 0xEAE Boost — boost/overdrive collaboration with Obstructures
- 0xEAE Bass Driver — bass drive collaboration with Obstructures
- 0xEAE Fuzz — fuzz collaboration with Obstructures
- Eldritch Blast — intermodulation fuzz collaboration with Mask Audio Electronics
- Kerria Lacca — collaboration with Electrofoods combining a percolator-derived channel with the Surveyor circuit

Notes:
- EAE's official pedal catalog currently lists Model feT, Greathammer, Surveyor, Citadel, Limelight, Longsword, Glaive, Halberd, and The Bard among its pedals; non-dirt effects are excluded.
- EAE's own product pages explicitly identify Halberd as overdrive, Longsword as spanning clean boost through massive distortion, Limelight as a dual-channel overdrive, Citadel as overdrive, and Glaive as an octave fuzz occupying the fuzz/distortion intersection.
- The EAE legacy page identifies Dagger and Dude Incredible as discontinued/legacy products and lists the 0xEAE and Eldritch Blast collaborations. Only dirt-relevant identities are retained here.
- Surveyor is retained because the official EAE page identifies its transformer-loaded gain stage as a distortion generator and its gain control as overdrive/saturation.
- Collaborative products remain EAE builder relationships in this census, with the collaborating builder named in notes rather than splitting one named product into separate identities.

Sources:
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/model-fet
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/greathammer
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/surveyor
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/citadel
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/limelight
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/longsword
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/halberd
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/blog
- https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/legacy

Builder / brand: Browne Amplification

- Browne's official press-release archive identifies the ATOM as a Nashville overdrive and the Gritador as an overdrive, and the T4 as a fuzz. The current catalog now uses ALKENE for the Nashville overdrive product.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 024

Date: September 16, 2026

This block records builder/product relationships only. Product names are taken from current manufacturer catalogs and official manufacturer documentation, with authoritative secondary sources used only where the manufacturer no longer exposes a legacy product page. This is a research checkpoint, not an exhaustive lifetime catalog.

## 1. Seymour Duncan

Builder / brand: Seymour Duncan

### Current dirt products verified from the official Seymour Duncan catalog
- 805 Overdrive — overdrive
- Diamondhead — multistage distortion + boost

### Historical / discontinued dirt products verified from official Seymour Duncan material
- Dirty Deed — distortion / wide-range dirt product combining overdrive, distortion, and fuzz characteristics
- Palladium Gain Stage — high-gain preamp / distortion product; discontinued
- FOOZ — analog fuzz synthesizer / fuzz effect

Notes:
- Seymour Duncan's current Overdrive & Distortion category currently shows 805 and Diamondhead as its two dirt catalog results.
- The official Seymour Duncan forum and product material identify Dirty Deed as a distortion pedal with a wide range from overdrive through distortion and fuzz.
- The official Palladium product page explicitly states that Palladium is discontinued and describes it as a high-gain preamp and distortion effects pedal.
- The official FOOZ manual identifies FOOZ as a fuzz and filter effect with a square-wave fuzz section capable of anything from light drive to massive fuzz.
- Other current Seymour Duncan categories such as pedal amps, pickups, compression, modulation, and delay are excluded from this phase.

- Flex Drive is retained as a dirt product because the J. Rockett manual explicitly describes it as producing clean/dirty sounds and fuzz-type tones at higher gain.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 027

Date: September 16, 2026

This block records builder/product relationships only. Product names are taken from current manufacturer catalogs and official product pages where available. This is a research checkpoint, not an exhaustive lifetime catalog.

## 1. KHDK Electronics

Builder / brand: KHDK Electronics

### Overdrive / Distortion
- No.1 — tube-like overdrive
- Ghoul Screamer — overdrive
- Ghoul JR — overdrive
- Dark Blood — distortion
- Totality — dual-path overdrive for guitar and bass
- Mad Monarch — high-gain dirt product
- Gojira Drive — harmonic overdrive
- Paranormal — parametric EQ overdrive
- Paranormal II — parametric EQ overdrive
- Abyss — bass overdrive
- Fallout Bass Overdrive — bass overdrive
- Night of the Living Shred — high-gain preamp / dirt product

### Fuzz

- Unicorn Blood — octave fuzz
- Unicorn Blood II — octave fuzz
- Ascendancy — dual dirt pedal with overdrive sections

Notes:
- KHDK's official products page currently lists multiple overdrive, distortion, fuzz, bass-drive, signature, and limited-edition products.
- The official No.1 page identifies No.1 as an overdrive covering clean boost through aggressive dirty rhythm and lead tones.
- The official Ghoul Screamer and Dark Blood product descriptions classify them as overdrive and distortion respectively; the Dark Blood manual describes lower gain as overdrive and higher gain as heavily overdriven/distorted.
- The official Totality product listing identifies it as a gritty, dual-path overdrive for guitar and bass.
- The official Gojira Drive listing identifies it as harmonic overdrive, and Paranormal/Paranormal II are explicitly described as parametric EQ overdrives.
- The official Scuzz Box listing describes it as germanium-voiced fuzz; Unicorn Blood and Unicorn Blood II are explicitly described as limited-edition octave fuzz products.
- Signature and limited-edition dirt products are retained as named catalog identities when KHDK presents them as distinct products. Non-dirt products such as compressors and coffee are excluded.
- Some preamp/amp-model products are retained where KHDK's own catalog presents them as gain-producing dirt products. No circuit or component analysis is included.

- Stone Deaf's official current home page explicitly labels PDF-2 as “Swiss Army Knife Distortion,” PDF-1X GHOST as “Parametric Clean Boost & Overdrive,” Warp Drive as “Classic Metal / Rock Amp in a box,” Fig Fumb as “50 Shades of Muff Style fuzz,” and Rise & Shine as “3 in 1 Fuzz, Analog Octave up & Tremolo Effect.”
- The official Stone Deaf expression-pedal page names PDF-2, PDF-1X, Fig Fumb, Warp Drive, Trashy Blonde, and Kliptonite as the manufacturer's overdrives, distortions, and fuzzes. Trashy Blonde and Kliptonite are therefore preserved as builder/product relationships even though their current standalone product pages are not surfaced in the current catalog.
- Limited editions remain separate named catalog identities where Stone Deaf explicitly lists them as such; no cosmetic analysis is performed.
- Non-dirt products such as Noise Reaper and EP-1 Expression are excluded.

Sources:
- https://www.stonedeaffx.com/
- https://www.stonedeaffx.com/collections/all
- https://www.stonedeaffx.com/collections/distortion-pedals
- https://www.stonedeaffx.com/pages/support
- https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/ep-1-expression

# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 032

## Builder
**BJFE / BJF Electronics — Björn Juhl (Sweden)**

## Scope
This block records BJFE/BJF Electronics dirt pedals only: overdrive, distortion, and fuzz. Non-dirt effects, customer one-offs, and unrelated One Control products are excluded. The builder identity is treated as **BJFE / BJF Electronics**, with Björn Juhl as the designer/builder. One Control pages are used where they explicitly identify a pedal as a Björn Juhl/BJF/BJFE design or reproduction; the BJFE/BearFoot forum registry and descriptions are used as the historical product index.

## Verification notes
- The BJFE/BearFoot forum's registry identifies BJFE current-production, out-of-production, limited-edition, prototype, and other pedal identities and explicitly lists the major dirt models. It is an archival community resource associated with the BJFE/BearFoot forum, not a current manufacturer storefront.
- One Control's official product pages independently identify several BJF/BJFE designs and explicitly credit Björn Juhl, providing primary manufacturer evidence for the designer/product relationship.
- This block does **not** treat every prototype or one-off in the registry as part of the core census. The goal is the durable builder → recognizable dirt-pedal relationship, not an exhaustive museum inventory of every experimental enclosure.

## Overdrive
- **Baby Blue Overdrive (BBOD)** — Overdrive / dynamic overdrive-fuzz design. The BJFE archival description identifies Baby Blue Overdrive as a BJFE pedal and traces the first FUZZ 1 into the BBOD. One Control's Baby Blue OD page identifies it as designed by Björn Juhl and based on his original FUZZ 1 design. Sources: BJFE/BearFoot registry and descriptions; One Control official product page.
- **Honey Bee Overdrive (HBOD)** — Overdrive. BJFE archival description identifies the Honey Bee Overdrive as a BJFE production pedal and describes its dynamic low-gain character. One Control's BJFE-series catalog also carries the Honey Bee design. Sources: BJFE/BearFoot registry and descriptions; One Control official catalog.
- **Little Green Wonder (LGW)** — Overdrive. The BJFE archival registry lists Little Green Wonder as a BJFE dirt pedal and its category summary places it under overdrive. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive.
- **BlueBerry Bass Overdrive (3BOD)** — Bass overdrive. Listed in the BJFE archival registry as a BJFE pedal and categorized as an overdrive. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry.
- **Mighty Blue Overdrive** — Overdrive. Listed in the BJFE archival category summary. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Sparkling Yellow Overdrive (SYOD)** — Overdrive. Listed in the BJFE archival registry/category summary, including a Version 2. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Sparkling Yellow Overdrive Version 2 (SYOD V.2)** — Overdrive. Listed explicitly in the BJFE archival registry/category summary. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Saffron Yellow Overdrive (SYOD)** — Overdrive / prototype-line identity. Listed in the BJFE archival registry and category summary. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Cranberry Over Drive** — Overdrive. One Control's official product page identifies it as part of the BJF Series FX and states that Björn Juhl designed it as a lower-gain extension of the Strawberry Red Overdrive family. Source: One Control official product page.
- **Lingonberry Over Drive** — Overdrive. One Control's official product page identifies it as part of the BJF Series FX and states that Björn Juhl designed it as a higher-gain extension of the Strawberry Red Overdrive family. Source: One Control official product page.

## Distortion
- **Dyna Red Distortion (DRD)** — Distortion. The BJFE archival registry lists Dyna Red Distortion as current production and the detailed description identifies it as a BJFE distortion pedal. One Control's official BJFE-in-Your-Hand page explicitly describes Dyna Red Distortion 4K as an approved BJFE model and credits Björn Juhl. Sources: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive; One Control official product page.
- **Emerald Green Distortion Machine (EGDM)** — Distortion. Listed in the BJFE archival registry and category summary as a BJFE distortion pedal. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive.
- **Mighty Red Distortion (MRD)** — Distortion. Listed in the BJFE archival registry and detailed description as a BJFE limited/prototype-era distortion; the archive also documents its relationship to the later Mad Professor Mighty Red Distortion. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Model R** — Distortion. Listed in the BJFE archival registry and description as a BJFE distortion model. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Dyna Blue Distortion** — Distortion. Listed in the BJFE archival prototype/category material as a BJFE design. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Concreto Distortion** — Distortion. Listed in the BJFE archival category summary. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Cliff Hanger** — Distortion / prototype identity. Listed in the BJFE archival category summary as a distortion pedal. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Cliff Hanger II** — Distortion / prototype identity. Listed in the BJFE archival category summary as a distortion pedal. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Rebel Red Distortion** — Distortion / limited or one-off identity. Listed in the BJFE archival category summary. Kept here as a documented BJFE dirt identity, not treated as a current production product. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.

## Fuzz
- **Pink Purple Fuzz (PPF)** — Fuzz. Listed in the BJFE archival registry and category summary as a BJFE fuzz pedal. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive.
- **Candy Apple Fuzz (CAF)** — Fuzz. Listed in the BJFE archival registry and detailed description as a BJFE production fuzz. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive.
- **Arctic White Fuzz (AWF)** — Fuzz. Listed in the BJFE archival registry/category summary as a BJFE fuzz identity. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive.
- **Arctic Light Fuzz** — Fuzz. Listed in the BJFE archival category summary as part of the BJFE fuzz lineage. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Big Purple Fuzz** — Fuzz. Listed in the BJFE archival category summary. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Folk Fuzz** — Fuzz. Listed in the BJFE archival category summary. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Folk Fuzz 3.5%** — Fuzz / DIY-project identity. Listed separately in the BJFE archival registry. Retained as a documented BJFE fuzz identity but marked by its source as a DIY project rather than ordinary production. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry.
- **Smokey Orange Fuzz** — Fuzz. Listed in the BJFE archival fuzz category and ancestry chain. Source: BJFE/BearFoot pedal-description archive.
- **Snow White Fuzz** — Fuzz / prototype identity. Listed in the BJFE archival registry and fuzz category. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive.
- **Sparkling Red Fuzz** — Fuzz / one-off or limited identity. Listed in the BJFE archival registry/category summary. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive.
- **Sun Burst Fuzz** — Fuzz / one-off identity. Listed in the BJFE archival registry/category summary. Source: BJFE/BearFoot registry/description archive.

## Deliberately excluded from the core list
- Boosters, treble boosters, EQs, compressors, tremolos, phasers, delays, wahs, and other non-dirt effects.
- Customer one-offs such as custom reboxes, combined pedals, and pedals built specifically for individual customers.
- One Control products that merely use a BJFE-inspired design unless the source explicitly establishes the Björn Juhl/BJF/BJFE relationship. Those belong to the appropriate One Control/BJF documentation rather than being silently counted as separate BJFE originals.
- Mad Professor products are not duplicated here merely because the BJFE archive documents circuit ancestry or collaboration. Mad Professor remains its own builder entry.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. **BJFE / BearFoot FX forum — Registry: BJFE Pedals**
   https://www.bjornjuhl.com/forum/viewtopic.php-f%3D6%26t%3D1555.html
2. **BJFE / BearFoot FX forum — Info: BJFE Pedal Descriptions**
   https://bjornjuhl.com/forum/viewtopic.php-f%3D6%26t%3D2261.html
3. **One Control USA — Baby Blue OD**
   https://one-control.com/products/baby-blue-od-oc-bbodn
4. **One Control USA — Dyna Red Distortion 4K**
   https://one-control.com/products/oc-biyh-drd4k
5. **One Control USA — Shades Of Green O.D. DLX**
   https://one-control.com/products/oc-biyh-sogoddlx
6. **One Control USA — Cranberry Over Drive**
   https://one-control.com/products/oc-cbodn
7. **One Control USA — Lingonberry Over Drive**
   https://one-control.com/products/oc-lbodn
8. **One Control USA — Silver Bee OD Limited BJFE Style Wave**
   https://one-control.com/products/oc-svbodn-bjw

## Block status
**Verified research block, with historical/archive qualifiers preserved where the source itself classifies a model as prototype, limited, DIY, or one-off.**

SOURCE FILE: research/builders/BLOCK-2026-09-16-033.md
============================================================
# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 033

## Builders
- **Foxrox Electronics — Dave Fox**
- **Pete Cornish / Pete Cornish Effects**

## Scope
Builder → Pedals only. This block records documented overdrive, distortion, and fuzz products. Non-dirt effects are excluded except where a multi-effect unit contains a clearly identified dirt section that establishes the builder's dirt work.

## Foxrox Electronics

### Overdrive
- **FR 100 Overdrive** — Dual-mode stacked overdrive. Foxrox's current product page describes a medium-gain drive mode plus a stacked high-gain lead mode. Official Foxrox documentation identifies it as a dual-mode overdrive.
- **Festival Overdrive** — Dual-mode overdrive with a separate boost/lead channel. Foxrox's official product documentation describes it as an overdrive and records it as a discontinued/classics product, last manufactured December 2020.
- **ZIM Dual Drive** — Modular dual-channel drive platform. Foxrox explicitly describes ZIM as configurable with Drive, Distortion, Fuzz, Boost, and Octave ZIMcards. Because the product is itself a dirt-capable platform rather than one fixed circuit, it is recorded as a builder → modular dirt pedal relationship.

### Distortion
- **ZIM Dual Drive** — Configurable with distortion ZIMcards. Foxrox's current ZIM page explicitly describes the pedal as supporting distortion and OD in one pedal.
- **TAR+ ZIMcard** — Distortion/overdrive ZIMcard for ZIM and Mini ZIM. Foxrox's ZIMcard catalog places ZIMcards into Overdrive, Fuzz, Distortion, Boost, and Other categories, and the TAR+ card is part of that catalog.
- **Brown Muff ZIMcard** — Distortion/fuzz ZIMcard. Foxrox's current ZIMcard catalog lists it among the available ZIMcards and identifies ZIM as a platform capable of distortion and fuzz configurations.

### Fuzz
- **Hot Silicon Fuzz** — Silicon Fuzz Face-derived fuzz. Foxrox's official product page identifies it as the same Hot Silicon previously available as a FuzzCard for Captain Coconut 2. It was last manufactured as the original Hot Silicon in June 2019.
- **Hot Silicon2 Fuzz** — Later Foxrox Hot Silicon version. Foxrox's official Classics page records it as last manufactured May 2024; the current Hot Silicon documentation describes the silicon Fuzz Face lineage.
- **CC Hybrid2** — Hybrid fuzz. Foxrox's current catalog lists CC Hybrid2, while official Foxrox documentation identifies the CC Hybrid circuit as the PNP silicon/germanium Fuzz Face derivative developed for the original Captain Coconut and the default fuzz card for Captain Coconut 2.
- **Captain Coconut / Captain Coconut2 FuzzFoot** — Integrated fuzz section. Foxrox's official Captain Coconut documentation identifies FuzzFoot as a Dallas Arbiter Fuzz Face reproduction/improvement, and Captain Coconut2 documentation identifies interchangeable fuzz cards including Hot Silicon, Vintage Germanium, and Si/Ge Hybrid. These are retained as historical Foxrox dirt identities rather than counted as separate modern standalone pedals.
- **Fat Fuzz ZIMcard** — Silicon Fuzz Face-style fuzz with a mild overdrive stage. Foxrox's official page describes the card as a medium-gain Silicon NPN Fuzz Face followed by an overdrive stage.
- **Original PNP Hybrid Fuzz Card** — Fuzz card for Captain Coconut/Captain Coconut2. Foxrox's current accessories catalog identifies it as a Fuzz Card and the Captain Coconut manual identifies the FuzzFoot as the integrated fuzz section.
- **Vintage Germanium Fuzz Card** — Fuzz card for Captain Coconut2. Foxrox's current accessories catalog lists it and the Captain Coconut2 documentation identifies Vintage Germanium as one of the three fuzz-card choices.

### Foxrox verification notes
- The Foxrox Classics page is useful for discontinued-product status and gives last-manufactured dates for Hot Silicon, Festival, ZIM, and Hot Silicon2.
- ZIMcards are not all treated as independent standalone pedals. They are recorded only when their dirt function is relevant to the builder → pedal relationship.
- Octron and Paradox TZF products are excluded because they are octave/flanger products rather than overdrive, distortion, or fuzz.

## Pete Cornish

### Overdrive
- **SS-2 Soft Sustain** — Overdrive/distortion. Pete Cornish describes SS-2 as progressing from subtle overdriven amp sounds through rhythmic crunch to blues lead and explicitly discusses it as an overdrive/distortion effect.
- **SS-3 Soft Sustain** — Overdrive / higher-output soft-sustain design. Pete Cornish's official Deluxe Series catalog lists SS-3 as Soft Sustain with passive top cut.
- **CC-1 Cornish Crunch** — Crunch/overdrive. Pete Cornish's official Deluxe Series catalog identifies CC-1 as Cornish Crunch with active bass/mid/treble tone controls.
- **GC-1 High Gain Crunch** — High-gain crunch/distortion. Pete Cornish's official Deluxe Series catalog identifies GC-1 as High Gain Crunch with active B/M/T controls.
- **BD-1 Bass Driver** — Bass overdrive/drive. Pete Cornish's official Deluxe Series catalog identifies BD-1 as Bass Driver with active bass/treble controls.

### Distortion
- **G-2** — Distortion / high-gain overdrive. Pete Cornish describes the G-2 as a distortion unit and states that it moves from rhythmic crunch into rounded dynamic lead overdrive.
- **P-2** — High-gain distortion/fuzz-oriented dirt pedal. Pete Cornish describes P-2 as a fat, smooth, harmonically rich distortion pedal spanning mellow crunch through searing high-gain sustain.
- **SS-2** — Overdrive/distortion range. The official SS-2 page describes subtle overdrive through rhythmic crunch and blues lead at maximum gain.
- **SS-3** — Soft Sustain / distortion-family pedal. Listed in Pete Cornish's official Deluxe Series catalog.
- **GC-1** — High Gain Crunch. Listed in the official Deluxe Series catalog.

### Fuzz
- **P-1** — Fuzz or distortion. Pete Cornish describes P-1 explicitly as a fuzz or distortion pedal and identifies it as a high-gain lead/rhythm unit.
- **P-2** — Distortion/fuzz-family high-gain pedal. The official Deluxe Series catalog identifies P-2 as Fuzz with passive top cut, while the product description emphasizes its distortion range.
- **NG-2** — Fuzz. Pete Cornish's official Deluxe Series catalog lists NG-2 as Fuzz+ with a bass/treble single tone control.
- **NG-3** — Fuzz. Pete Cornish's official Deluxe Series catalog lists NG-3 as Fuzz+ with bass/treble and bias controls.

### Pete Cornish verification notes
- The official Deluxe Series catalog is the authoritative source used for the current/ordered Deluxe Series product names and classifications.
- Individual official product pages are used to preserve the builder's own terminology where a pedal spans categories, particularly P-1, P-2, G-2, and SS-2.
- Custom touring systems and one-off pedalboards are excluded. This block records identifiable pedal products, not every custom Pete Cornish rig ever built.

## Primary / authoritative sources
### Foxrox Electronics
1. Foxrox official home/catalog: https://www.foxroxelectronics.com/
2. Foxrox Classics / discontinued products: https://www.foxroxelectronics.com/Classics.html
3. Foxrox FR 100 Overdrive: https://www.foxroxelectronics.com/FR100overdrive.html
4. Foxrox ZIM Dual Drive: https://www.foxroxelectronics.com/zim-dual-drive.html
5. Foxrox ZIMcards: https://www.foxroxelectronics.com/buyzimcards.html
6. Foxrox Original Hot Silicon Fuzz: https://www.foxroxelectronics.com/Original-Hot-Silicon-Fuzz_ep_66-1.html
7. Foxrox Captain Coconut2: https://www.foxroxelectronics.com/captaincoconut2.html
8. Foxrox Fat Fuzz ZIMcard: https://www.foxroxelectronics.com/FFZIMcard.html

### Pete Cornish
9. Pete Cornish official Deluxe Series: https://petecornish.co.uk/deluxeseries.html
10. Pete Cornish G-2: https://petecornish.co.uk/bfg2.html
11. Pete Cornish P-1: https://petecornish.co.uk/bfp1.html
12. Pete Cornish P-2: https://petecornish.co.uk/bfp2.html
13. Pete Cornish SS-2: https://petecornish.co.uk/bfss2.html

**Verified research block.** Current and discontinued products are distinguished where the manufacturer's own catalog provides that status. Category overlap is preserved rather than forcing pedals into a single category when the builder explicitly describes them across overdrive, distortion, and fuzz terminology.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder → Pedals Research Block 034

## Builder
**Himmelstrutz Elektro Art — Joakim Hedeby (Sweden)**

## Scope
Builder → Pedals only. This block records identifiable Himmelstrutz overdrive, distortion, and fuzz products. Amp products, modulation, utility effects, and non-dirt designs are excluded. Closely related named variants are retained when the manufacturer documents them as distinct pedal models, but the block does not attempt to enumerate cosmetic changes or every prototype.

## Overdrive
- **FITZO — Overdrive Avant-garde / Amp Enhancer** — Official Himmelstrutz manual identifies FITZO explicitly as an overdrive and describes its Drive control as the overdrive control. The same manual states that every pedal is handmade, tested, and adjusted by the original designer in Sweden.
- **FITZO+** — Overdrive/distortion variant of FITZO. The official owner manual identifies the pedal's drive/volume behavior and documents its additional gain configuration. Retained as a distinct documented model because Himmelstrutz provides a separate manual and model identity.
- **Under Over** — Overdrive / pre-EQ drive design. Himmelstrutz's official download index identifies Under Over and Under Overdriver as separate product identities in its pedal documentation archive. Retained as documented Himmelstrutz dirt products, with exact generation differences not expanded here.
- **FETTO Standard** — Overdrive/distortion. Himmelstrutz's official FETTO manual describes FETTO as an overdrive/distortion stomp box and the official download archive lists FETTO Standard as a model.
- **FETTO 9.0 / 11.1** — Overdrive/distortion. Official FETTO documentation describes the FETTO family as an overdrive/distortion stomp box and the Himmelstrutz download archive separately identifies the 9.0 to 11.1 documentation lineage.
- **FETTO Custom** — Great Wide Distortion / overdrive-distortion. The official manual explicitly describes it as an overdrive/distortion effect and identifies it as FETTO Custom.
- **FETTO Jr.** — FETTO-family drive. Himmelstrutz's official download index identifies FETTO Jr. as a distinct pedal model. Kept in the builder census without inferring a narrower category beyond the documented FETTO drive family.
- **FETTO Nord+** — FETTO-family overdrive/distortion. Official Himmelstrutz documentation lists FETTO Nord+ as a distinct model.
- **FETTO Nord 70+** — FETTO-family overdrive/distortion. Official Himmelstrutz documentation lists FETTO Nord 70+ as a distinct model.
- **FETTO Maximus** — FETTO-family high-gain drive. Official Himmelstrutz documentation lists FETTO Maximus as a distinct model.
- **FETTO Apache** — FETTO-family drive/distortion. Official Himmelstrutz documentation lists FETTO Apache as a distinct model.
- **FETTO C-Luxe** — FETTO-family drive/distortion. Official Himmelstrutz documentation lists FETTO C-Luxe as a distinct model.
- **FETTO SC** — FETTO-family drive/distortion. Official Himmelstrutz documentation lists FETTO SC as a distinct model.
- **TÔNKer** — Dirt/drive prototype-to-product identity. Himmelstrutz's official download archive lists TÔNKer among its pedal documentation. The builder's own historical interview also describes TÔNKer as a developing pedal. Retained as a documented Himmelstrutz dirt identity without over-specifying its final category.

## Distortion
- **FETTO Standard** — Overdrive/distortion. Official FETTO documentation explicitly uses both terms for the pedal.
- **FETTO 9.0** — Overdrive/distortion. The official manual repeatedly describes the unit as an overdrive/distortion stomp box and documents its gain/distortion behavior.
- **FETTO Custom** — Overdrive/distortion. Official manual identifies Drive as the drive/distortion control and calls the design a Great Wide Distortion effect.
- **GRAMPS** — Fuzztortion/overdrive-distortion family. Himmelstrutz's official download archive lists GRAMPS, and the builder's interview/documentation describes the GRAMPS family as capable of oversaturated/distorted behavior at reduced operating voltage. Retained as a documented dirt model.
- **GRAMPS+ — Fuzztortion Grande** — Fuzz/distortion. The official owner manual is titled “GRAMPS+ — Fuzztortion Grande,” documents Gain and Temp/Bias controls, and describes the pedal as producing increasingly fuzzy behavior across its bias range.
- **FUGAZI** — Dirt/drive model documented in Himmelstrutz's official pedal archive. Retained as a documented dirt identity, but no narrower category is asserted here because the available official index does not provide a sufficiently precise category description.
- **FUGAZI Jr.** — Dirt/drive model documented in Himmelstrutz's official pedal archive. Retained as a documented dirt identity without inferring a more specific category.

## Fuzz
- **GRAMPS+ — Fuzztortion Grande** — Fuzz/distortion. The official manual explicitly calls the design Fuzztortion Grande and provides a Temp/Bias control with the middle position described as most fuzzy.
- **GRAMPS** — Fuzz/distortion family identity. Himmelstrutz's own historical documentation discusses the GRAMPS family as becoming over-saturated and extreme at lower supply voltage, placing it in the builder's fuzz/distortion dirt family.

## Verification notes
- Himmelstrutz's official download archive is unusually important for this builder because it preserves manuals for current and historical pedal generations, including FETTO variants, FITZO, FITZO+, GRAMPS, GRAMPS+, FUGAZI, Mr. Nutcracker, Under Over, TÔNKer, and related models.
- The official FETTO 9.0 manual explicitly calls FETTO an “overdrive/distortion stomp box,” so the archive does not force the FETTO family into a single category.
- The official GRAMPS+ manual explicitly identifies it as “Fuzztortion Grande,” making fuzz/distortion classification direct rather than inferred.
- The Himmelstrutz archive lists many product names separately. They are retained where the manufacturer gives them separate model identities, but this block does not attempt to treat every revision number, artwork change, or prototype as a separate census entry.
- **Mr. Nutcracker** is intentionally not categorized here because the available official index confirms the product identity but the available evidence in this pass was not sufficient to confidently assign it to overdrive, distortion, or fuzz. It remains a future verification lead rather than a forced classification.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. **Himmelstrutz Elektro Art — official downloads archive**
   https://himmelstrutz.com/download/
2. **Himmelstrutz FETTO 9.0 official owner manual**
   https://www.himmelstrutz.com/downloads/himmelstrutz-fetto-9-owners-manual-001-web.pdf
3. **Himmelstrutz FITZO official owner manual**
   https://www.himmelstrutz.com/downloads/himmelstrutz-fitzo-owners-manual-001-web.pdf
4. **Himmelstrutz FITZO+ official owner manual**
   https://www.himmelstrutz.com/downloads/himmelstrutz-fitzo-plus-owners-manual-001-web.pdf
5. **Himmelstrutz FETTO Custom official owner manual**
   https://www.himmelstrutz.com/downloads/himmelstrutz-fetto-custom-owners-manual-1603-web.pdf
6. **Himmelstrutz GRAMPS+ official owner manual**
   https://www.himmelstrutz.com/downloads/himmelstrutz-gramps%2B-owners-manual-088-web.pdf
7. **Himmelstrutz founder interview / historical documentation**
   https://www.himmelstrutz.com/downloads/An_interview_with_Himmelstrutz_Elektro_Art_founder_Joakim_Hedeby.pdf

## Block status
**Verified research block.** Product names and category assignments are limited to what Himmelstrutz's own documentation supports. Unclear identities remain verification leads instead of being promoted into a category.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 035

## Builder
**Daredevil Pedals — Johnny Wator**

## Scope
Builder → Pedals only. This block records Daredevil's documented overdrive, distortion, and fuzz identities, including current products and clearly documented historical/discontinued identities. Non-dirt effects are excluded.

## Builder identity
Daredevil's official biography identifies Johnny Wator as the founder and describes the company as hand-building pedals in Chicago since 2012. The official site says the early work grew from vintage 1960s/70s circuits into predominantly original-design pedals.

## Current / recently listed dirt pedals

### Distortion
- **Deep Dish-tortion** — current 2026 Daredevil listing.
- **Necros Fuzz / Distortion** — current limited-edition listing; builder explicitly identifies both fuzz and distortion.
- **DRIVE-Bi — Dual Gain Distortion** — official product page identifies a dual-channel op-amp gain circuit with low and high distortion channels.
- **COCKED & FEARLESS** — combines Daredevil's Atomic Cock fixed-wah circuit with the Fearless distortion circuit; builder explicitly describes the Fearless section as original-design op-amp distortion.
- **the Chicago Rat** — selectable high-gain distortion mode.

### Fuzz
- **REAL COOL FUZZ** — official page identifies it as a vintage-style silicon fuzz.
- **NORTHERN CREEPER Fuzz** — current Daredevil listing; builder identifies it as fuzz.
- **Ten Tone Anniversary Fuzz** — official page identifies it as silicon fuzz and dates the project to Daredevil's tenth anniversary.
- **DAISHO — Earl Slick Fuzz Octave** — official page identifies an original-design fuzz section with an independent octave-up circuit.
- **Shrineburner Fuzz — Ltd Ed. Green** — current limited-edition listing.
- **Ron Asheton Real Cool Fuzz — Ltd. Edition Wedge** — current limited-edition listing tied to the Real Cool Fuzz identity.
- **Daredevil Cry Baby Fuzz Wah** — collaboration product with a fuzz circuit, retained because Daredevil lists it among its products and it contains a dirt/fuzz function.
- **the Chicago Rat** — selectable over-clipped fuzz mode.

## Historical / discontinued dirt identities

### Overdrive
- **Premium Overdrive** — official discontinued page describes a hand-wired transistor overdrive inspired by early-1970s Colorsound Power Boost designs.
- **Chicago Way — Fuzz & OD** — official discontinued page documents a two-in-one pedal with independent overdrive and fuzz circuits.
- **The Chicago Way** is retained as one builder identity, not split into separate fake pedals for its internal circuits.

### Distortion
- **Fearless Distortion** — official Daredevil material identifies the Fearless as an original-design op-amp distortion. Its circuit is also documented inside COCKED & FEARLESS.
- **Red Light District** — historical Daredevil identity; contemporary coverage describes it as built around the Fearless Distortion with additional fixed-wah-style filtering and gain switching.
- **British Black Belt** — appears in Daredevil's discontinued catalog. Because the current accessible manufacturer page exposes the name but not enough classification detail, it is retained as a verification lead rather than forced into the categorized distortion list.

### Fuzz
- **Supernova Fuzz** — historical Daredevil fuzz identity documented in distributor material.
- **OP Amp Fuzz V5** — historical Daredevil fuzz identity documented in distributor/artist references.
- **Real Cool Fuzz** — historical/current identity; official page provides the current classification.
- **Wolf Deluxe Fuzz** — historical Daredevil fuzz identity documented in distributor material.
- **Northern Creeper** — historical/current identity; official current listing provides the current classification.
- **Logan Square Destroyer** — historical Daredevil fuzz identity documented in distributor material.
- **Nova** — historical Daredevil fuzz identity documented in contemporary builder coverage.
- **L.S.D.** — historical Daredevil fuzz identity documented in contemporary builder coverage.
- **Almighty Bass** — official discontinued page explicitly identifies it as an original-design bass fuzz, capable of tones ranging from driven tube-amp gain to aggressive fuzz.
- **Chicago Way — Fuzz & OD** — official discontinued page documents its fuzz circuit.

## Verification notes
1. The official Daredevil homepage currently lists a 2026 product set including Deep Dish-tortion, Necros Fuzz / Distortion, Double Cocked, Silver Solo Wedge, the Chicago Rat, Ten Tone Anniversary Fuzz, HYPE FET Boost V.2, DRIVE-Bi, Real Cool Fuzz, COCKED & FEARLESS, Northern Creeper, Shrineburner Fuzz, and the Daredevil Cry Baby Fuzz Wah. Only the dirt-bearing identities relevant to this archive are included above.
2. Daredevil's official product page for the Chicago Rat explicitly states that it is not a RAT circuit and documents both high-gain distortion and over-clipped fuzz modes. The product is therefore represented under distortion and fuzz rather than as a conventional overdrive.
3. Daredevil's official pages explicitly classify Ten Tone as silicon fuzz, Real Cool Fuzz as vintage-style silicon fuzz, DRIVE-Bi as dual gain distortion, Premium Overdrive as overdrive, and Almighty Bass as fuzz.
4. Distributor documentation independently identifies Daredevil as founded by Johnny Wator in Chicago in 2012 and lists historical dirt identities including Supernova Fuzz, Chicago Way, OP Amp Fuzz V5, Real Cool Fuzz, Wolf Deluxe Fuzz, Northern Creeper, Logan Square Destroyer, Fearless Distortion, Red Light District, and Premium OD.
5. **British Black Belt** remains a verification lead because the accessible official discontinued index exposes the product name without enough classification detail. It is deliberately not promoted into a dirt category.
6. Boost-only identities such as Silver Solo and HYPE are not categorized as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz. They remain outside the census categories unless a source explicitly gives them a qualifying dirt classification.
7. Variant/cosmetic editions are not split into separate base products where the builder clearly presents them as editions of an established dirt identity.

## Primary / authoritative sources consulted
- Daredevil Pedals official biography: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/bio
- Daredevil Pedals official homepage/current catalog: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/
- Daredevil Pedals official Chicago Rat page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/shop-1/p/the-chicago-rat
- Daredevil Pedals official DRIVE-Bi page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/shop-1/p/drivebi
- Daredevil Pedals official Real Cool Fuzz page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/shop-1/p/rcf1
- Daredevil Pedals official Ten Tone Anniversary Fuzz page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/shop-1/p/tentone1
- Daredevil Pedals official Daisho page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/shop-1/p/daisho-earl-slick-fuzz-octave
- Daredevil Pedals official COCKED & FEARLESS page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/shop-1/p/cocked-fearless
- Daredevil Pedals official Premium Overdrive discontinued page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/discontinued/p/premium-overdrive
- Daredevil Pedals official Chicago Way discontinued page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/discontinued/p/chicagoway1
- Daredevil Pedals official Almighty Bass discontinued page: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/discontinued/p/almightybass
- Daredevil Pedals official discontinued catalog: https://www.daredevilpedals.com/discontinued
- Audio Distribution Group Daredevil profile/catalog summary: https://www.audiodistributiongroup.com/brands/daredevil-pedals/
- Bonedo historical distributor announcement/catalog summary: https://www.bonedo.de/artikel/w-music-distribution-uebernimmt-den-vertrieb-von-daredevil-pedals/
- Chicago Music Exchange Daredevil interview/product listings: https://www.chicagomusicexchange.com/blogs/news/inside-daredevil-pedals-q-a-with-johnny-wator

## Exclusions
- Atomic Cock / Atomic Cocked fixed-wah products are excluded because they are filter/wah effects, not dirt pedals by themselves.
- Bootleg Dirty Delay and other delay/reverb/filter products are excluded.
- Silver Solo and HYPE are excluded because their primary builder classification is boost rather than overdrive, distortion, or fuzz.
- Placebo is excluded because it is a non-effect novelty product.

## Block conclusion
**Daredevil Pedals — Johnny Wator** is added to the Builder → Pedals census as a builder with a substantial documented dirt catalog spanning overdrive, distortion, and fuzz, with both current and historical identities supported by primary Daredevil pages and authoritative distributor/catalog references.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 036

## Builder
**Dr. Scientist Sounds**

## Scope
Builder → Pedals only. This block records Dr. Scientist's documented overdrive, distortion, and fuzz identities. Non-dirt effects are excluded.

## Builder identity
Dr. Scientist Sounds is a Canadian pedal builder whose official site describes its pedals as designed and handmade in British Columbia, Canada. The current official catalog includes The Elements, Frazz Dazzler, and Dungeon Crawler among its dirt-focused products.

## Current dirt pedals

### Overdrive / distortion
- **The Elements** — official page classifies it as a dual-channel overdrive/distortion with a wide gain range and active three-band EQ. It has selectable low- and high-gain distortion channels and switchable clipping modes.

### Distortion
- **The Elements** — dual-channel overdrive/distortion; retained under both relevant categories because the manufacturer explicitly uses both classifications.
- **Dungeon Crawler** — official page identifies it as a MOSFET-based distortion designed to take a clean amplifier from high gain into extreme metal tones. It uses three high-gain MOSFET stages, six gyrator EQ stages, a silicon-rectifier Grind control, and a noise gate.

### Fuzz
- **Frazz Dazzler** — official page identifies it as a dual-channel aggressive fuzz with a wide gain range, three-band EQ, voltage-starve system, and glitch/sub-octave behavior at low headroom settings.
- **BitQuest** — official page identifies a built-in digital fuzz mode alongside its other digital effects. It is retained as a dirt-bearing multi-effect identity, but it is not treated as a conventional standalone fuzz pedal.

## Verification notes
1. Dr. Scientist's official homepage currently highlights The Elements as an equalizer/overdrive/high-gain distortion, Frazz Dazzler as an aggressive dual-channel fuzz, and Dungeon Crawler as an extreme-metal distortion product.
2. The Elements official product page explicitly uses the term "dual-channel overdrive/distortion" and documents low/high gain distortion channels and selectable clipping.
3. Dungeon Crawler's official page explicitly identifies the circuit as MOSFET-based distortion and describes its extreme-metal purpose.
4. Frazz Dazzler's official page explicitly identifies it as a dual-channel fuzz and documents its voltage-starve system and high-gain channels.
5. BitQuest is included only as a dirt-bearing multi-effect because its official documentation explicitly provides a digital fuzz mode. It is not promoted into the standalone fuzz category as though it were a dedicated fuzz circuit.
6. BoostBot is excluded because the builder classifies it as a clean boost. Other Dr. Scientist products such as BitQuest Eurorack Module, Phreak, Sunny Day Delay, Dusk, and Spectrum Analyzer are excluded because they are not overdrive, distortion, or fuzz products.
7. No unsupported historical pedal names were promoted into the census in this block. The accessible official catalog was sufficient to document the qualifying dirt products without relying on uncertain third-party legacy lists.

## Primary / authoritative sources consulted
- Dr. Scientist official homepage: https://www.drscientist.ca/
- Dr. Scientist official The Elements page: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/the-elements/
- Dr. Scientist official Frazz Dazzler page: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/frazz-dazzler/
- Dr. Scientist official Dungeon Crawler page: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/dungeon-crawler/
- Dr. Scientist official BitQuest page: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/bitquest/

## Exclusions
- **BoostBot** — clean boost, not categorized as overdrive/distortion/fuzz.
- **BitQuest Eurorack Module** — modular version of the multi-effect, not a conventional pedal entry.
- **Phreak, Sunny Day Delay, Dusk, Spectrum Analyzer** — non-dirt effects.

## Block conclusion
**Dr. Scientist Sounds** is added to the Builder → Pedals census with documented qualifying dirt products spanning overdrive, distortion, and fuzz. The current official catalog provides sufficient primary-source evidence for The Elements, Dungeon Crawler, Frazz Dazzler, and the fuzz-bearing BitQuest without requiring speculative legacy entries.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 037

## Builder
**OKKO FX — Heiko Lauenroth**

## Scope
Builder → Pedals only. This block records OKKO FX's documented overdrive, distortion, and fuzz identities from the current official catalog. Non-dirt effects and boost-only products are excluded unless they are part of a qualifying dirt unit.

## Builder identity
OKKO FX is based in Leipzig, Germany. The official site identifies the seller as Heiko Lauenroth / OKKO FX and states that the company officially launched in 2003 and handbuilds its pedals in Leipzig.

## Current / currently listed dirt pedals

### Overdrive
- **DIABLO DUAL** — official product page classifies it as a 2-channel overdrive / distortion.
- **DIABLO GH Signature** — official catalog classifies it as a super-dynamic overdrive and clean boost.
- **TWINSONIC MKII** — official catalog classifies it as a dual-stacking overdrive + preamp.
- **HOLY GRIT** — official product page classifies it as overdrive / fuzz / preamp; it is retained under both overdrive and fuzz because the builder explicitly uses both terms.

### Distortion
- **DIABLO DUAL** — 2-channel overdrive / distortion.
- **MOTÖRBASS** — official catalog classifies it as aggressive punchy bass distortion.
- **DIRTY DIABLO** — official catalog describes the current Dirty Diablo versions as variations of the classic Diablo; retained as a dirt identity but not split into separate cosmetic variants.

### Fuzz
- **HOLY GRIT** — official product page classifies it as overdrive / fuzz / preamp and describes its circuit as a heavily modified Fuzz Face / Tone Bender-derived design.
- **BFGRMWB — Big Fucking Green Russian Muff With Benefits** — official catalog currently lists the fuzz product in big-box and white editions. Cosmetic editions are treated as one pedal identity.

## Verification notes
1. OKKO's official homepage currently lists DIABLO DUAL, DIABLO GH Signature, TWINSONIC MKII, MOTÖRBASS, HOLY GRIT, DIRTY DIABLO, and BFGRMWB among its products.
2. The official DIABLO DUAL page explicitly calls the pedal a "2-channel overdrive / distortion."
3. The official HOLY GRIT page explicitly calls it "Overdrive Fuzz Preamp" and describes the circuit as based on the classic Fuzz Face / Tone Bender family but heavily modified.
4. The official homepage explicitly classifies MOTÖRBASS as bass distortion and TWINSONIC MKII as dual-stacking overdrive + preamp.
5. The official homepage lists BFGRMWB as "Big Fucking Green Russian Muff With Benefits," making its fuzz identity explicit through the Muff designation and product context. It is recorded as one identity despite separate big-box/white listings.
6. DIRTY DIABLO is retained as a current dirt identity because OKKO explicitly describes it as a variation of the classic Diablo. Cosmetic black/white editions are not split into separate archive products.
7. COCAINE and COCA COMP MKII are excluded because the current official catalog classifies them as compressor / EQ / preamp / boost and optical compressor respectively, not overdrive, distortion, or fuzz.
8. No unsupported historical names were promoted into this block. The current official catalog was sufficient to establish a multi-pedal dirt footprint.

## Primary / authoritative sources consulted
- OKKO FX official homepage/catalog: https://okko-fx.com/
- OKKO FX official DIABLO DUAL page: https://okko-fx.com/DIABLO-DUAL-p261206223
- OKKO FX official HOLY GRIT page: https://okko-fx.com/HOLY-GRIT-p469583005
- OKKO FX official terms page identifying Heiko Lauenroth / OKKO FX: https://okko-fx.com/pages/terms
- OKKO FX official privacy/contact page: https://okko-fx.com/pages/privacy-policy

## Exclusions
- **COCAINE** — compressor / EQ / preamp / boost.
- **COCA COMP MKII** — optical compressor.
- Other modulation, delay, and utility effects are excluded.
- Cosmetic editions of BFGRMWB and DIRTY DIABLO are not treated as separate base pedal identities.

## Block conclusion
**OKKO FX — Heiko Lauenroth** is added to the Builder → Pedals census as a German builder with a documented current catalog spanning overdrive, distortion, and fuzz, supported directly by OKKO's official catalog and product pages.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 038

## Builder
**SolidGoldFX — Greg Djerrahian**

## Scope
Builder → Pedals only. This block records SolidGoldFX's documented overdrive, distortion, and fuzz identities from the current official catalog and product documentation. Non-dirt effects are excluded. Dirt-bearing multi-effects are retained only when the builder explicitly identifies the dirt circuit/function.

## Builder identity
SolidGoldFX is a Canadian pedal company founded by Greg Djerrahian. Its official About page states that the company has made Canadian pedals since 2006, identifies Djerrahian as CEO & Founder, and says the pedals are made by hand in the company's Montreal shop. The official catalog currently carries SolidGoldFX and Diamond Pedals products.

## Current / currently listed dirt pedals

### Overdrive
- **Commodore Overdrive** — official product page identifies it as an overdrive pedal and describes a low- to mid-gain asymmetric-clipping drive circuit derived from a tuned BluesBreaker-style section and the Imperial output section.
- **BETA-V Bass Preamp** — official product page identifies it as a bass preamp and overdrive effects pedal, with a Drive control ranging from mild grit to warm overdrive.

### Distortion
- **No standalone current SolidGoldFX product is forced into the distortion category in this block.** The current official dirt catalog is explicit about its principal overdrive and fuzz classifications, while fuzz products are not relabeled as distortion merely because their descriptions discuss saturation or distortion.

### Fuzz
- **Fuzzbox — Bender Fuzz** — official product page identifies it as a refined low-gain silicon fuzz inspired by the Tone Bender Mk II.
- **If 6 Was 9 BC183 MKII — Silicon Fuzz Face** — official product page explicitly identifies it as a silicon fuzz pedal based on the Dallas-Arbiter Fuzz Face circuit.
- **Imperial MKII — Fuzz** — official product page identifies it as a fuzz pedal inspired by the 1970s JEN Jumbo Fuzz.
- **76 MKII — Octave-Up Fuzz** — official product page identifies it as a multi-voiced fuzz based on the Standard Fuzz family, with octave-up switching and dedicated tone-shaping controls.
- **Lysis MKII — Polyphonic Octave Fuzz Modulator** — official product page explicitly describes an analog square-wave fuzz circuit feeding the pedal's digital polyphonic modulation/pitch section. It is retained as a fuzz-bearing multi-effect identity rather than treated as a conventional standalone fuzz-only pedal.

## Verification notes
1. SolidGoldFX's official About page identifies Greg Djerrahian as CEO & Founder, dates the company to 2006, and states that its pedals are made by hand in Montreal.
2. The current official collection and dirt pages list Commodore Overdrive, BETA-V, Fuzzbox, If 6 Was 9 BC183 MKII, Imperial MKII, 76 MKII, and Lysis MKII among the company's current/active product identities.
3. Commodore's official page explicitly calls it an overdrive pedal and documents its low- to mid-gain asymmetric-clipping drive circuit.
4. BETA-V's official page explicitly calls it a bass preamp and overdrive effects pedal and documents its Drive control and warm-overdrive range.
5. Fuzzbox's official page explicitly identifies it as a fuzz and describes its Tone Bender Mk II lineage without requiring a separate historical identity to be created.
6. If 6 Was 9 BC183 MKII's official page explicitly identifies it as a silicon fuzz pedal based on the Dallas-Arbiter Fuzz Face circuit.
7. Imperial MKII's official page explicitly identifies it as a fuzz pedal inspired by the JEN Jumbo Fuzz.
8. 76 MKII's official page explicitly identifies it as an octave-up fuzz and describes its multi-voiced fuzz architecture.
9. Lysis MKII's official page explicitly identifies an analog square-wave fuzz circuit inside a broader polyphonic octave/modulation effect. It is retained as a dirt-bearing multi-effect, not promoted to a standalone fuzz-only identity.
10. The current official catalog does not provide a sufficiently explicit standalone SolidGoldFX distortion classification beyond the documented overdrive/fuzz products, so no distortion entry is invented for this block.
11. Cosmetic editions and revisions are not split into separate base identities unless the builder presents them as distinct named products. The archive therefore records the named pedal identities rather than every finish or stock-state difference.

## Primary / authoritative sources consulted
- SolidGoldFX official About page: https://solidgoldfx.com/pages/about-us
- SolidGoldFX official collection: https://solidgoldfx.com/collections/the-collection
- SolidGoldFX official dirt collection: https://solidgoldfx.com/collections/dirt
- SolidGoldFX official Commodore Overdrive page: https://solidgoldfx.com/products/commodore-overdrive
- SolidGoldFX official BETA-V page: https://solidgoldfx.com/products/beta-v
- SolidGoldFX official Fuzzbox page: https://solidgoldfx.com/products/fuzzbox-bender-fuzz
- SolidGoldFX official If 6 Was 9 BC183 MKII page: https://solidgoldfx.com/products/if-6-was-9-bc183-mkii-fuzz
- SolidGoldFX official Imperial MKII page: https://solidgoldfx.com/products/imperial-mkii
- SolidGoldFX official 76 MKII page: https://solidgoldfx.com/products/76-mkii-fuzz
- SolidGoldFX official Lysis MKII page: https://solidgoldfx.com/collections/modulation/products/lysis-mkii

## Exclusions
- Modulation, delay, reverb, compressor, and utility products are excluded.
- **Aurras** is excluded as a vibraphase/modulation product despite its product text mentioning distortion in the context of the phase-shifter signal path.
- **Beta-V** is retained because the builder explicitly classifies it as an overdrive effects pedal, even though its primary identity is a bass preamp.
- Lysis MKII is retained only as a fuzz-bearing multi-effect because the official page explicitly identifies its analog fuzz circuit.

## Block conclusion
**SolidGoldFX — Greg Djerrahian** is added to the Builder → Pedals census with a primary-source-verified dirt footprint spanning overdrive and fuzz, plus a documented fuzz-bearing multi-effect. The current official catalog does not justify forcing a separate standalone distortion entry, so the block remains conservative rather than inferring categories from generic saturation language.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 039

## Builder
**Tech 21 NYC — B. Andrew Barta**

## Verification basis
Tech 21's official About page states that B. Andrew Barta launched Tech 21 in 1989 and introduced the SansAmp, and identifies him as the designer behind the company's products. Tech 21's official support/archive pages and individual product pages were used to establish dirt-pedal identities. This block is intentionally conservative: it records pedals where Tech 21's own material explicitly identifies overdrive, distortion, or fuzz behavior. Amp-emulator/SansAmp products that merely have a Drive control are not automatically counted as standalone dirt pedals.

## Overdrive
- Boost Overdrive
- Hot-Rod Plexi
- Richie Kotzen Signature OMG
- Double Drive
- Double Drive 3X

## Distortion
- Boost Distortion
- Hot-Rod Plexi
- Double Drive
- Double Drive 3X
- XXL Original
- XXL Guitar
- XXL Bass
- Red Ripper

## Fuzz
- Boost Fuzz
- Bass Boost Fuzz
- Red Ripper
- XXL Original
- XXL Guitar
- XXL Bass
- Richie Kotzen Signature OMG

## Product notes
- **Boost Overdrive:** Tech 21's official archive describes it as an overdrive pedal with a dedicated Sparkle control and independent post-boost.
- **Boost Distortion:** official product page identifies it as a distortion pedal and describes its driven range as distortion/dirt/grit/fuzz/overdrive.
- **Boost Fuzz:** official product page and manual explicitly identify the unit as a fuzz pedal based around classic germanium fuzz behavior.
- **Hot-Rod Plexi:** official page explicitly describes Drive as overdrive and the circuit as generating tube-amp distortion characteristics. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Double Drive:** official archive describes separate Class A and Class A/B power-amp distortion stages and cascading distortion. The Class A stage is also described in terms of overdrive-like behavior, so it is retained in both categories.
- **Double Drive 3X:** official archive describes Class A and Class A/B power-amp distortions, cascading operation, and a clean-boost range at minimum drive. It is retained under Distortion and Overdrive.
- **XXL Original / Guitar / Bass:** official Tech 21 material explicitly calls the XXL distortion, while also describing minimum-Warp operation as overdrive-like and extreme settings as capable of vintage fuzz tones. Those category overlaps are preserved rather than forced into one label.
- **Red Ripper:** official Tech 21 page calls it an all-analog distortion effect and explicitly describes its range from vintage fuzz through distortion and octaver-fuzz territory. It is retained in Distortion and Fuzz.
- **Richie Kotzen Signature OMG:** official Tech 21 archive calls it a signature overdrive pedal and describes its asymmetric distortion behavior. It is retained in Overdrive and Fuzz because Tech 21's later RK5 documentation explicitly identifies the OMG section's Fuzz switch as changing the overdrive into a fuzz-style tone.
- **Bass Boost Fuzz:** official Tech 21 archive identifies it as a Boost Series fuzz product. It is retained under Fuzz; no separate distortion category is inferred solely from generic dirt language.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **SansAmp Classic and other SansAmp amp/preamp products:** excluded from the categorized dirt census when the official material primarily defines them as tube-amplifier emulators/preamp systems rather than standalone OD/distortion/fuzz pedals. A Drive control alone is not treated as sufficient classification.
- **Fly Rigs:** not counted as separate pedal identities when the dirt section is a derivative of an already documented Tech 21 pedal or when the unit is primarily a multi-effect/amp-emulation platform. Individual Fly Rig dirt sections may be used as corroborating evidence for underlying pedal classifications.
- **Character Plus products:** not promoted into the core census here despite containing explicit overdrive/fuzz sections, because they are multi-effect amp-character pedals and would substantially broaden the classification boundary. They remain useful corroborating material for Tech 21's dirt implementation.
- **Cosmetic/edition changes:** not split into separate base identities unless Tech 21 documents a materially distinct product identity.
- **Boost-only products:** excluded.

## Primary sources
- Tech 21 About Us: https://www.tech21nyc.com/about-us/
- Tech 21 Archive: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/
- Boost Overdrive: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/boost-overdrive/
- Boost Distortion: https://www.tech21nyc.com/products/effects/boost-distortion/
- Boost Fuzz: https://www.tech21nyc.com/products/effects/boost-fuzz/
- Bass Boost Fuzz: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/bassboost-fuzz/
- Hot-Rod Plexi: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/hot-rod-plexi/
- Double Drive: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/double-drive/
- Double Drive 3X: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/double-drive-3x/
- XXL Original: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/xxl/
- XXL Guitar: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/xxl-guitar/
- XXL Bass: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/xxl-bass/
- Red Ripper: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/red-ripper/
- Richie Kotzen Signature OMG: https://www.tech21nyc.com/archive/omg/
- RK5 v3 documentation: https://www.tech21nyc.com/products/flyrig/rk5v3/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Product names and category assignments above are based on Tech 21's own current/archive pages and manuals. Ambiguous multi-effect/amp-emulation products were deliberately left outside the core categorized census rather than inferred.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 040

## Builder
**Way Huge Electronics — George Tripps**

## Verification basis
Way Huge's official/current product material identifies the brand as **Way Huge by George Tripps**. Current manufacturer/distributor catalog material and official Jim Dunlop manuals were used to establish the dirt products and their categories. The research is scoped to explicit overdrive, distortion, and fuzz identities. Multi-effect products are only retained where the official documentation explicitly identifies a dirt circuit as part of the product and the product itself is meaningfully a dirt pedal.

## Overdrive
- Red Llama 3.0
- Green Rhino MkV
- Green Rhino MkIV
- Supa-Lead
- Pork Loin
- Pork & Pickle
- Camel Toe MkII
- STO
- Geisha Drive
- Beer Overdrive
- Fat Sandwich
- Overrated Special

## Distortion
- Fat Sandwich

## Fuzz
- Fuzz Box
- Foot Pig
- Swollen Pickle Jumbo Fuzz MkIII
- Swollen Pickle Jumbo Fuzz MkIIS
- Russian-Pickle
- Doom Hammer
- Stone Burner
- Pork & Pickle
- Atreides Weirding Module

## Product notes
- **Red Llama 3.0:** current Way Huge catalog identifies it as an overdrive. George Tripps is explicitly credited in the Way Huge product documentation.
- **Green Rhino MkIV / MkV:** official Way Huge material identifies both as overdrive products. They remain separate documented product identities because the manufacturer treats the generations as distinct models.
- **Supa-Lead:** official catalog identifies it as an overdrive.
- **Pork Loin:** official documentation identifies it as an overdrive. It is retained as a distinct Way Huge dirt identity.
- **Pork & Pickle:** official Jim Dunlop manual identifies it as a bass overdrive and fuzz combining the Pork Loin Overdrive and Russian-Pickle Fuzz circuits. It is retained in both Overdrive and Fuzz; the manufacturer's own title supports that classification. It is one product identity, not a separate "Pork & Pickle Bass" pedal.
- **Camel Toe MkII:** official manual identifies it as a triple overdrive combining Green Rhino and Red Llama circuits. It is retained under Overdrive.
- **STO:** current Way Huge catalog identifies it as an overdrive.
- **Geisha Drive:** current Way Huge catalog identifies the Geisha Drive as an overdrive.
- **Beer Overdrive:** current catalog explicitly names and classifies it as overdrive.
- **Fat Sandwich:** current Way Huge catalog identifies it as a high-gain overdrive/distortion design. It is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Overrated Special:** current catalog places it in the overdrive family; retained under Overdrive.
- **Swollen Pickle:** official Jim Dunlop manuals explicitly identify the product as a Jumbo Fuzz. The MkIIS documentation describes a range from mild crunch to high-gain fuzz, but the core classification remains Fuzz rather than forcing it into Distortion.
- **Russian-Pickle:** official Jim Dunlop manual identifies it as a fuzz.
- **Doom Hammer:** current Way Huge catalog identifies it as a fuzz.
- **Stone Burner:** current catalog identifies it as a sub-atomic fuzz combining fuzz with octave-down tones.
- **Atreides Weirding Module:** official Jim Dunlop documentation identifies a dedicated FUZZ control inside the analog weirding module. It is retained under Fuzz. It is not promoted into Distortion merely because the module can produce aggressive tones.
- **Fuzz Box / Foot Pig:** these are historical Way Huge fuzz identities documented in Way Huge historical material. They are retained as historical dirt products rather than treated as current catalog items.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Aqua-Puss, Supa-Puss, Blue Hippo, Attack Vector, and other non-dirt Way Huge products:** excluded from the categorized dirt census.
- **Octave/modulation products without an explicit standalone dirt identity:** excluded unless the manufacturer documentation clearly identifies a fuzz/distortion circuit as part of the dirt function.
- **Cosmetic/limited-edition changes:** not split unless the manufacturer presents a materially distinct pedal identity.
- **Boost-only products:** excluded.
- **Pork & Pickle Bass:** not created as a separate identity. The official manual's "Bass Overdrive & Fuzz" wording describes the single Pork & Pickle product.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Jim Dunlop Way Huge product catalog: https://www.jimdunlop.com/products/electronics/way-huge/
- Way Huge by George Tripps WM41 Swollen Pickle MkIII manual: https://www.jimdunlop.com/content/manuals/WM41.pdf
- Way Huge by George Tripps WHE401S Swollen Pickle MkIIS manual: https://www.jimdunlop.com/content/manuals/WHE401S.pdf
- Way Huge by George Tripps WM42 Russian-Pickle manual: https://www.jimdunlop.com/content/manuals/WM42.pdf
- Way Huge by George Tripps WM91 Pork & Pickle manual: https://www.jimdunlop.com/content/manuals/WM91.pdf
- Way Huge WHE900 Atreides manual: https://www.jimdunlop.com/content/manuals/WHE900.pdf
- Way Huge product catalog material: https://moridaira.jp/wayhuge/products/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Product identities and classifications above are based primarily on manufacturer/distributor catalog material and official Jim Dunlop/Way Huge documentation. Category overlaps are retained only where the manufacturer's own descriptions support them; uncertain category assignments were deliberately left out rather than inferred.
