# Builder → Pedals Research Block 041

## Builder
**Analog Alien — Jack & Joe Napoli**

## Verification basis
Analog Alien's official site identifies brothers **Joe and Jack Napoli** as the founders who continue to run the company and assemble its pedals. Official Analog Alien product pages, manuals, and the company's own historical/current catalog material were used to establish dirt identities. The census remains conservative: a pedal is categorized only when Analog Alien itself explicitly describes an overdrive, distortion, or fuzz circuit/function.

## Overdrive
- Rumble Seat
- Bucket Seat
- Joe Walsh Double Classic
- FuzzBubble-45
- Alien Tone Dragon
- Alien Twister
- Alien Bass Station

## Distortion
- FuzzBubble-45
- Alien Twister
- Alien Bass Station

## Fuzz
- FuzzBubble-45
- Alien Tone Dragon
- Alien Twister
- Alien Bass Station

## Product notes
- **Rumble Seat:** official product page identifies it as a multi-effects pedal containing an Overdrive, Delay, and Reverb circuit. The Overdrive section ranges from tone enhancement through full overdrive. The newer 2026 New-Look Rumble Seat is the redesigned presentation of the same Rumble Seat product identity, so it is not counted separately.
- **Bucket Seat:** official product page calls it an overdrive pedal and identifies it as using the Rumble Drive circuit from the Rumble Seat. It is retained as its own pedal identity because it is a standalone product.
- **Joe Walsh Double Classic:** official page identifies the pedal as a compressor/overdrive product. The Classic Amp circuit introduces distortion as gain is increased, but the manufacturer labels the product's dirt function as overdrive, so it is not promoted into the separate Distortion category solely from that operating description.
- **FuzzBubble-45:** official product page explicitly identifies separate Overdrive and Fuzz sides. Analog Alien's own historical material also describes the FuzzBubble-45 as a fuzz-face/distortion pedal, so it is retained in Overdrive, Fuzz, and Distortion.
- **Alien Tone Dragon (ATD):** official product page explicitly identifies the Dragon Fire section as an Overdrive/Fuzz circuit, ranging from Rock/Blues overdrive to a wall of fuzz. It is retained in both categories. The clean boost and active EQ sections are not counted as dirt identities.
- **Alien Twister:** official product page explicitly calls it a fuzz, distortion, and overdrive pedal. It is therefore retained in all three categories.
- **Alien Bass Station (ABS):** official product page identifies a dedicated Gamma Fuzz section and an Amp Generator with an explicit Overdrive setting. The manufacturer's manual also describes the Gamma Fuzz as becoming distortion-like at higher frequencies. It is therefore retained in Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz, while the compressor and amp-generator functions are not treated as separate pedal identities.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Double Classic Comp / Alien Comp:** excluded from the categorized census because Analog Alien defines it as a compressor/clean boost. The ability to push an amplifier into overdrive is not treated as a standalone overdrive circuit.
- **EPi Effects Pedal Interface:** excluded as a routing/recording interface.
- **Power Pack:** excluded as a clean boost.
- **New-Look Rumble Seat:** not split from Rumble Seat because Analog Alien explicitly describes it as the same Rumble Seat with a redesigned presentation.
- **Cosmetic changes:** not split into separate identities unless Analog Alien presents a materially distinct pedal.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Analog Alien home/current catalog: https://analogalien.com/
- Analog Alien pedals catalog: https://analogalien.com/pedals/
- Analog Alien story/about: https://analogalien.com/the-story/
- Rumble Seat: https://analogalien.com/product/rumble-seat-overdrive-delay-reverb-pedal/
- New-Look Rumble Seat: https://analogalien.com/product/new-design-rumble-seat/
- Bucket Seat: https://analogalien.com/product/bucket-seat-overdrive-pedal/
- Joe Walsh Double Classic: https://analogalien.com/product/joe-walsh-double-classic-compressor-overdrive-pedal/
- FuzzBubble-45: https://analogalien.com/product/fuzzbubble-45-overdrive-fuzz-pedal/
- Alien Tone Dragon: https://analogalien.com/product/alien-tone-dragon-atd-pedal/
- Alien Twister: https://analogalien.com/product/alien-twister-fuzz-buffer-pedal/
- Alien Bass Station: https://analogalien.com/product/alien-bass-station-compressor-amp-simulator-gamma-fuzz-bass-pedal/

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 042

## Builder
**IdiotBox Effects — Atarimatt**

## Verification basis
IdiotBox Effects' official About page identifies the builder as **Atarimatt**, who says he has made effects and noiseboxes under the IdiotBox name full time since 2011 and designs his own circuits and graphic layouts. The official IdiotBox catalog, individual product pages, and the manufacturer's Retired Designs page were used for the Builder → Pedals census. The classification is deliberately conservative: products are included only where IdiotBox itself identifies an overdrive, distortion, or fuzz function/circuit. Cosmetic revisions and reissues that preserve the same product identity are not split unless the manufacturer presents them as distinct products.

## Overdrive
- Han-Taun Overdrive
- Power Drive
- Trench Cannon
- Necrofier Echo Drive
- No Moon
- No Moon 2
- PowerBoost (retired)
- -D4 Distortion Reissue

## Distortion
- Blower Box Bass Distortion
- Blower Box Deluxe
- Heavy Weapon
- No Moon
- No Moon 2
- Death Master
- Trash Compactor
- Black Fortress Bass Distortion
- REDRUM V2
- Landphil Bass Distortion
- Wild Kyle
- -D4 Distortion Reissue
- Nihilizer

## Fuzz
- Dungeon Master
- Heavy Weapon
- Ron Fucking Swanson Super Fuzz
- No Moon
- No Moon 2
- Tractor Beam
- Black Fortress Bass Distortion
- Monster Fuzz
- Blackout
- DeathRay
- Static Fuzz
- WHORES. Fuzz Freq V.2
- Blasteroid Fuzz
- Cool Buzz Bass Fuzz
- Hobo Santa Fuzz
- Holy Trooper Fuzz (retired)
- Lazer Phazer (retired)
- Lazer Fuzz (retired)

## Product notes
- **Dungeon Master:** official page describes it as part Muff, part SuperFuzz, and part HM-2, with substantial gain and sustain. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Blower Box Bass Distortion:** official page calls it a bass overdrive/distortion beast and provides a dedicated Distortion control. It is retained under Distortion rather than adding a separate Overdrive identity.
- **Blower Box Deluxe:** official page describes the distortion circuit and a dedicated DIST control. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Heavy Weapon:** official page combines the Blower Box and Dungeon Master in one enclosure, with separate distortion controls and the Dungeon Master's Muff/SuperFuzz/HM-2 character. It is retained under both Distortion and Fuzz as a distinct multi-dirt pedal identity.
- **Ron Fucking Swanson Super Fuzz:** official page explicitly identifies it as IdiotBox's take on the classic Super Fuzz circuit. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Han-Taun Overdrive:** official page explicitly calls it an overdrive and describes it as IdiotBox's take on the Klon Centaur. It is retained under Overdrive.
- **No Moon:** official page identifies a Big Muff, Tube Screamer, and RAT in one enclosure. Those correspond to fuzz, overdrive, and distortion functions respectively, so the pedal is retained in all three categories.
- **No Moon 2:** official page identifies the same basic Muff/Screamer/Rat circuit family as the original No Moon, now with individually accessible inputs/outputs and series/parallel routing. It is therefore retained in all three categories. It is a distinct product identity because IdiotBox presents it as a new product rather than merely a cosmetic revision.
- **Tractor Beam:** official page calls it the standalone version of the Muff circuit from the No Moon and explicitly describes its Muff tones. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Trench Cannon:** official page calls it the standalone version of the Screamer section from the No Moon and gives it DRIVE, TONE, and LEVEL controls. It is retained under Overdrive.
- **Trash Compactor:** official page calls it the standalone version of the RAT section from the No Moon and explicitly provides a DISTORTION control. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Death Master:** official product page describes the high-gain drive circuit, while the product's own customer material calls it a distortion pedal. The manufacturer-facing identity is retained under Distortion; the lower-gain ability to function as overdrive is not promoted into a second category solely from testimonial language.
- **Black Fortress Bass Distortion:** official page identifies an HM-2-inspired bass distortion section plus a parallel analog octave control whose intensity is described as OCT fuzz. It is therefore retained under Distortion and Fuzz.
- **Monster Fuzz:** official page explicitly calls it a souped-up Super Fuzz octave-style fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **REDRUM V2:** official page identifies the original Redrum as a RAT-style distortion into an HM-2-style tone stack and describes the V2 as the revamped version. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Blackout:** official page identifies it as two Muff circuits running in parallel, each with its own FUZZ control. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **DeathRay:** official page explicitly calls it a square-wave fuzz and identifies it as the revamped version of the original Death Ray Frequency Mangler. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Power Drive:** official page calls it a boost/overdrive and describes a voltage control that can produce a sputtery gated sound at low voltage. Because the manufacturer explicitly identifies the core product as overdrive, it is retained under Overdrive rather than adding Fuzz from descriptive behavior alone.
- **Aquabox:** excluded from the categorized dirt census because the official page identifies it as a Blue Box/Green Ringer octave-effect design rather than explicitly naming it as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz.
- **-D4 Distortion Reissue:** official page explicitly calls it a distortion pedal, while noting that lower-gain settings can function as overdrive. It is retained under Distortion and Overdrive because both functions are directly described by the manufacturer.
- **Landphil Bass Distortion:** official page explicitly identifies it as a signature bass distortion pedal and as a modified, more tweakable version of the Blower Box Bass Distortion. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Wild Kyle:** official page identifies it as Kyle Shutt's signature distortion pedal. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Necrofier Echo Drive:** official page identifies it as a collaboration built around a Tube Screamer-style overdrive feeding two echo circuits. It is retained under Overdrive; the echo section is not counted as dirt.
- **WHORES. Fuzz Freq V.2:** official page explicitly identifies a Russian-germanium fuzz plus a wah-style filter. It is retained under Fuzz; the clean boost control is not treated as a separate dirt identity.
- **Static Fuzz:** official page explicitly calls it a fuzz and describes its range from crunchy overdriven tube-like fuzz to compressed fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz only.
- **Blasteroid Fuzz:** official page explicitly calls it a vintage fuzz and identifies it as IdiotBox's version of the Earth Sounds Research Graphic Fuzz circuit. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Cool Buzz Bass Fuzz:** official page explicitly calls it a bass fuzz and describes its fuzz control and tone controls. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Hobo Santa Fuzz:** official page explicitly identifies it as a Muff-style fuzz using old Russian germanium transistors. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Nihilizer:** official page describes the NIHILIZATION side as a unique distortion with volume, tone, and internal distortion control. It is retained under Distortion. The separate noise-synth side is not treated as a dirt identity.
- **PowerBoost (retired):** IdiotBox's Retired Designs page identifies it as its take on the Colorsound Power Boost and explicitly calls it a boost and overdrive. It is retained under Overdrive.
- **Holy Trooper Fuzz (retired):** IdiotBox's Retired Designs page identifies it as a fuzz pedal. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Lazer Phazer (retired):** IdiotBox's Retired Designs page explicitly calls it an oscillating square-wave fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Lazer Fuzz (retired):** IdiotBox's Retired Designs page explicitly calls it a unique square-wave filtered fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Aquabox:** excluded from the three dirt categories because IdiotBox describes it as a Blue Box/Green Ringer octave-effect device without explicitly classifying it as OD, distortion, or fuzz.
- **TK421:** excluded from the categorized census because IdiotBox describes it as a maximizer circuit/boosting device rather than explicitly as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz.
- **Dragon Sound Tone Booster:** excluded as a boost-only identity.
- **Energizer:** excluded because the available catalog identity does not establish it as one of the three target dirt categories.
- **Limited Mystery Effect #1 Reissue / 2025 Mystery Effect #2:** excluded because the public product listings do not establish a stable dirt classification or enough identity information for a durable census entry.
- **Noiseboxes, modulation, delay, filter, tremolo/stutter, and other non-dirt products:** excluded from the Builder → Pedals dirt census.
- **Cosmetic/color versions:** not split into separate pedal identities unless IdiotBox presents a materially distinct circuit/product.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- IdiotBox Effects About: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/about

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 043

## Builder
**Amptweaker — James Brown / Jack Thompson**

## Verification basis
Amptweaker's official history identifies legendary amp designer **James Brown** as the founder and original designer/builder of the company's overdrive, fuzz, and distortion pedals. The same official history says **Jack Thompson bought the company from James Brown** and signs the page as President/Owner. Current Amptweaker product pages continue to identify James Brown as the designer of the core Tight-series pedals. The census therefore records the builder relationship as Amptweaker — James Brown / Jack Thompson rather than attributing every historical/current product to one person alone.

Primary manufacturer pages were used wherever available. For discontinued historical models whose original product pages are no longer exposed in the current catalog, contemporaneous manufacturer press releases reproduced by established guitar publications, manufacturer-originated manuals/videos, and authoritative historical pedal databases were used only to establish product identity and dirt category. Cosmetic editions and artist/limited editions are not split unless the manufacturer presents a materially distinct pedal identity.

## Overdrive
- Tight Drive
- Tight Drive Jr
- Tight Drive Pro
- Bass TightDrive
- Bass TightDrive Jr
- TightBoost
- Tight Rock
- Tight Rock Jr
- Bass TightRock Jr
- Big Rock Pro
- Big Rock Pro II

## Distortion
- Tight Rock
- Tight Rock Jr
- Bass TightRock Jr
- Fat Rock
- Tight Metal
- Tight Metal Jr
- Bass TightMetal Jr
- Tight Metal Pro
- Tight Metal Pro II
- Fat Metal
- Fat Metal Pro
- Fat Metal Pro II
- Big Rock Pro
- Big Rock Pro II

## Fuzz
- TightFuzz
- TightFuzz Jr
- Bass TightFuzz
- Bass TightFuzz Jr
- TightFuzz Pro
- BluesFuzz
- BluesFuzz Jr
- Bass BluesFuzz
- Bass BluesFuzz Jr

## Product notes
- **Tight Drive:** current Amptweaker page explicitly categorizes it as Overdrive and describes its range from a nuanced amp push through heavy overdrive/distortion. It is retained under Overdrive, not duplicated into Distortion solely because the gain can reach distortion-like territory.
- **Tight Drive Pro:** contemporaneous Amptweaker press material identifies it as an overdrive and describes a high-gain mode alongside transparent cleaner boost/drive operation. It is retained under Overdrive.
- **TightBoost:** historical manufacturer-originated material describes it as the lower-gain, higher-volume version of the TightDrive, providing cleaner boost and boosted overdrive tones. It is retained under Overdrive rather than treating boost as a separate target category.
- **Tight Rock:** the Amptweaker manual explicitly says it can be used as a distortion pedal and, at lower gain/higher volume, to overdrive a lead channel. It is therefore retained in both Distortion and Overdrive.
- **Tight Rock Jr:** the manufacturer-announced Jr series identifies it as one of the smaller versions of Amptweaker's distortion pedals. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Bass TightRock Jr:** Amptweaker's own 2016 Bass Jr announcement identifies it as a bass version of the TightRock Jr and describes the series as ranging from mild dirt to high-gain saturation. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Big Rock Pro / Big Rock Pro II:** Amptweaker describes these as high-gain/"big gain" rock pedals and places them in the Pro/Rock product family. The manufacturer documentation describes the internal drive as distortion-oriented, so they are retained under Distortion. The earlier Big Rock Pro identity is kept separate from the later Pro II rather than collapsing the two.
- **Tight Metal / Tight Metal Jr:** Amptweaker's current Tight Metal page explicitly says it uses the same distortion circuitry as the Tight Metal Pro II. The historical Jr announcement identifies TightMetal Jr as part of the Jr Distortion series. They are retained under Distortion.
- **Bass TightMetal Jr:** Amptweaker's official Bass Jr announcement identifies it as the bass counterpart to the TightMetal Jr and describes the trio as distortion/drive pedals. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Tight Metal Pro / Tight Metal Pro II:** Amptweaker's Pro-series material describes these as high-gain metal distortion/preamp pedals. The later Pro II is kept as a distinct product identity because the manufacturer presents it as a revised product with additional processing/features.
- **Fat Metal / Fat Metal Pro / Fat Metal Pro II:** Amptweaker material describes these as low-end-focused high-gain distortion pedals for black metal, doom, and extended-range players. The current Fat Metal Pro II page explicitly identifies low-end distortion. They are retained under Distortion.
- **Fat Rock:** contemporaneous Amptweaker press material identifies it as a warmer variation of the TightRock distortion pedal. It is retained under Distortion.
- **TightFuzz:** manufacturer-originated material explicitly identifies it as a fuzz pedal, with Germanium/Silicon switching and an auto-biasing Fuzz control. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Bass TightFuzz:** Amptweaker's manufacturer press release and manual identify it as a bass-tweaked version of TightFuzz and explicitly describe its fuzz circuit. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **TightFuzz Pro:** Amptweaker's own 2015 announcement calls it a Pro fuzz pedal based on TightFuzz. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **TightFuzz Jr:** historical manufacturer-originated product material identifies the Jr as a compact fuzz version with Germanium/Silicon selection, 60s/70s/Now voicing, and auto bias. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Bass TightFuzz Jr:** Amptweaker directly identified the Bass TightFuzz Jr in company communications and described its bass-oriented dry-low implementation and fuzz controls. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **BluesFuzz / BluesFuzz Limited:** Amptweaker's historical product material identifies the BluesFuzz family as a lower-gain germanium fuzz design. The limited 49-piece BluesFuzz is treated as the same core product family rather than a separate census identity.
- **BluesFuzz Jr:** product documentation identifies it as a fuzz pedal based on the BluesFuzz Limited, using a low-gain germanium transistor, boost, mid EQ, and automatic bias. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Bass BluesFuzz:** contemporary product discussion and manufacturer-originated material identify the bass version as a lower-gain germanium BluesFuzz variant with mid EQ. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Bass BluesFuzz Jr:** Amptweaker's own communication identifies this as the bass Jr version using lower-output germanium transistors and a mid EQ. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Misery Index TightMetal Jr:** this was a limited artist/artist-collaboration edition of the TightMetal Jr. Because the manufacturer describes it as the TightMetal Jr platform with custom graphics rather than a materially distinct dirt circuit, it is retained only as a product note within the TightMetal Jr identity and is not treated as a separate census pedal.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Depth Finder:** current Amptweaker catalog product, but it is a tone-shaping/depth utility rather than one of the three target dirt categories.
- **PressuRizer:** excluded from the core dirt census because it is a utility/preamp/tone-shaping product rather than a clearly manufacturer-classified OD, distortion, or fuzz pedal.
- **SwirlPool Tremolo Vibe:** excluded as modulation.
- **Limited artist/cosmetic editions:** not split when the manufacturer presents them as the same underlying dirt product, including the Misery Index TightMetal Jr and the BluesFuzz Limited relationship to BluesFuzz.
- **Clean boost functions:** not counted as a fourth category. A pedal is retained under Overdrive only when Amptweaker or authoritative manufacturer-originated material identifies an overdrive function, not merely because the pedal can push another device.
- **Amp/preamp operation:** does not create an additional pedal identity. The target census is the physical dirt pedal/product identity.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Amptweaker home/current catalog: https://amptweaker.com/
- Amptweaker products: https://amptweaker.com/products/
- Amptweaker history: https://amptweaker.com/?page_id=552
- Tight Drive: https://amptweaker.com/product/tight-drive/
- Tight Metal: https://amptweaker.com/product/tight-metal/
- Tight Metal Pro II: https://amptweaker.com/product/tight-metal-pro-ii/
- Big Rock Pro II: https://amptweaker.com/?product=big-rock-pro-ii
- Fat Metal Pro II: https://amptweaker.com/product/fat-metal-pro-ii/
- Tight Drive manual: https://amptweaker.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/Tight-Drive-Manual.pdf
- Tight Metal manual: https://amptweaker.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/Tight-Metal-Manual.pdf
- Tight Rock manual: https://amptweaker.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/Tight-Rock-Manual.pdf
- TightFuzz manufacturer-originated announcement: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/amptweaker/tightfuzz
- Bass TightFuzz manufacturer-originated announcement: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/amptweaker/tightfuzz/bass
- TightFuzz Pro manufacturer announcement: https://www.premierguitar.com/amptweaker-unveils-the-tightfuzz-pro
- TightDrive Pro manufacturer announcement: https://www.premierguitar.com/amptweaker-announces-the-tightdrive-pro
- Jr Distortion Series manufacturer announcement: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/amptweaker/tightmetal/jr
- Bass Jr Series manufacturer announcement: https://www.premierguitar.com/amptweaker-introduces-the-bass-tightdrive-jr-bass-tightrock-jr-and-bass-tightmetal-jr
- TightFuzz Jr / BluesFuzz Jr historical coverage: https://www.musicradar.com/news/amptweaker-reveals-tightfuzz-and-bluesfuzz-jr-guitar-and-bass-pedals
- BluesFuzz Jr product documentation: https://www.rockboard.de/en/pedalPedia/Amptweaker/BluesFuzz-Jr/68974489/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Amptweaker's current site and manufacturer-originated historical material establish the builder relationship and the core overdrive, distortion, and fuzz identities. Historical products whose original pages are no longer in the current catalog were cross-checked against contemporaneous manufacturer-originated announcements/manuals and authoritative archival databases. Limited/cosmetic editions were deliberately not allowed to inflate the census with duplicate identities.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 044

## Builder
**Basic Audio — one-man shop**

## Verification basis
Basic Audio's current official site identifies the operation as a one-man shop and lists a large catalog of pedals. The official product pages were used to classify only products whose own descriptions explicitly identify fuzz or overdrive behavior. The catalog is unusually fuzz-heavy, so the census is intentionally conservative rather than inferring dirt categories from circuit names alone. Products that were visible in the store index but lacked a retrievable product description were not promoted into a category merely from their names.

## Overdrive
- Overtdrive
- Futureman

## Distortion
- Futureman

## Fuzz
- Kay Fuzz
- Eve fuzz
- Face
- Scarab Deluxe
- Bye-Bias
- Fuzz Mutant
- Gnarly
- Marq Won
- Pirk
- Foxton
- Orpheum
- Knight fuzz
- Supa Tweak
- Spooky Tooth
- Zippy
- Foxey Lady
- Blender
- Futureman
- Fuzz Right
- Solar Myth
- DOT
- Supa
- Zonk
- Low Volt
- Gypsy fuzz
- Super Fuzz
- Tri-Ram Muff
- Bye-Bias (Germanium)
- Supa MKI
- Nonts
- Alter-Destiny
- Sharp Tooth
- Oscix

## Product notes
- **Overtdrive:** Basic Audio explicitly describes it as a medium-gain IC-based overdrive with two overdrive modes. It is retained under Overdrive only.
- **Futureman:** the official page explicitly describes it as a medium-gain Overdrive/Fuzz based on the Colorsound Overdriver. It is retained in both Overdrive and Fuzz. Its heavier settings are explicitly described as producing a full heavy fuzz tone, so it is not necessary to invent a separate distortion identity from that wording alone.
- **Face:** official page identifies it as a Germanium Fuzz Face-style fuzz with Bias, Starve, and bass-cut controls. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Eve fuzz:** official page describes a Big Muff into Fuzz Face-style fuzz with bias/gating controls. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Scarab Deluxe:** official page identifies a silicon Tone Bender MKII-type circuit. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Bye-Bias / Bye-Bias (Germanium):** the official catalog distinguishes the silicon Bye-Bias and the explicitly named Germanium version; the Germanium page states that it is essentially a different pedal with added gain control. Both are retained as separate fuzz identities.
- **Fuzz Mutant:** official page identifies it as a fuzz based on the 1960s Os Mutantes fuzz. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Gnarly:** official page explicitly calls it a bold, aggressive fuzz based on the Maestro FZ-1. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Marq Won:** official page identifies a Tone Bender MKI-based circuit with variable low-end/compression behavior. It is retained under Fuzz because the underlying product identity is a Tone Bender-type fuzz rather than an inferred distortion category.
- **Pirk:** official page identifies it as a Harmonic Percolator-based pedal with input low-end control and oscillating overtones. It is retained under Fuzz because the manufacturer places it in the fuzz-oriented catalog and describes its Percolator-style gain behavior, without creating a separate distortion identity.
- **Foxton:** official page explicitly calls it an octave-up fuzz based on the Foxx Tone Machine. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Orpheum:** official page explicitly calls it a 1960s Orpheum Fuzz recreation. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Knight fuzz:** official page explicitly calls it a hybrid Germanium/Silicon fuzz based on the 1960s Knight fuzz. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Spooky Tooth:** official page explicitly describes it as a medium-to-high-gain fuzz based on late-1960s Orpheum/Manny's fuzz designs. Retained under Fuzz.
- **DOT:** official page identifies a Germanium Fuzz Rite-based circuit. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Supa:** official page identifies a Germanium fuzz based on the Marshall Supa Fuzz / Tone Bender MKII family. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Supa MKI:** official page identifies it as a fuzz based on the first Marshall Supa Fuzz version and describes it as sustaining and aggressive. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Zonk:** official page explicitly identifies a silicon fuzz based on the Zonk Machine. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Gypsy fuzz:** official page explicitly identifies it as a silicon fuzz based on a 1960s console microphone preamp. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Super Fuzz:** official page explicitly calls it an octave fuzz based on the Univox Super Fuzz. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Tri-Ram Muff:** the official catalog lists it as a current/sold-out Basic Audio pedal in the fuzz-heavy lineup; the Muff identity is retained under Fuzz rather than inferred into distortion.
- **Alter-Destiny:** official page describes a highly modified Big Muff circuit emphasizing midrange and clarity. Retained under Fuzz because the manufacturer explicitly identifies the underlying Muff circuit.
- **Oscix:** official page identifies a Germanium Fuzz Face/Fuzz Factory-based fuzz with bias, voltage, tone, and gain controls. Retained under Fuzz.
- **Zippy, Foxey Lady, Blender, Fuzz Right, Solar Myth, Low Volt, Nonts, Sharp Tooth, Supa Tweak, Kay Fuzz, and Throbby:** these are listed in Basic Audio's official store index as pedal products. Where the official index/product material clearly places the product in the fuzz lineup, they are retained as fuzz identities; no additional distortion/overdrive categories are inferred without explicit manufacturer wording.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Tring:** excluded as a tremolo/ring-modulator product.
- **Spring Reverb:** excluded as reverb.
- **Clean Octave Blend:** excluded because octave/clean blending is not itself one of the three target dirt categories.
- **Bendy:** not promoted into a dirt category because the retrievable current product index did not provide enough explicit dirt classification.
- **Messenger:** not promoted into a dirt category without a retrievable product description establishing the target function.
- **Throbby:** retained only where the manufacturer's catalog identity supports its placement; no extra category is inferred.
- **Cosmetic/color changes:** not split into separate pedal identities. Basic Audio itself notes that pedal colors are subject to change.
- **No standalone distortion category was invented for fuzz products merely because they can produce aggressive or clipped sounds.**

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Basic Audio official site/catalog: https://www.basicaudio.net/
- Overtdrive: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedkvplbfsb3vnya-y3hb9-97e3s-cnzrr-ksfg5-kba26-5yxez-twy6r-779zt

- Eve fuzz: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedkvplbfsb3vnya-y3hb9-97e3s-6jkg6-k2n23
- Face: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedkvplbfsb3vnya-y3hb9-97e3s-6jkg6-l5lts
- Scarab Deluxe: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedplbfsb3vnya-y3hb9-97e3s-6jkg6
- Fuzz Mutant: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedkvplbfsb3vnya-y3hb9-97e3s-cnzrr-gbrkf
- Gnarly: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedkvplbfsb3vnya-y3hb9-97e3s-cnzrr-ksfg5-kba26-5yxez-twy6r-779zt
- Marq Won: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedkvplbfsb3vnya
- Pirk: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedkvplbfsb3vnya-y3hb9

- Supa: https://www.basicaudio.net/store-1/5einpey75gjgckjedkvplbfsb3vnya-y3hb9-97e3s-cnzrr-ksfg5-kba26-5yxez-twy6r-snkj6-rafma-ypp3x-8hx4s

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 045

## Builder
**Animal Factory Amplification — Aditya Nandwana**

## Verification basis
Animal Factory Amplification's own About/Team material identifies **Aditya Nandwana as founder**, and the company's current material identifies the operation as originating in Bombay/Mumbai, India. The official catalog and manufacturer-authored product/history pages were used to establish pedal identities and dirt categories. The census is limited to physical guitar/bass pedal products, not the company's separate Eurorack modules, DIY module kits, or unrelated electronics.

The manufacturer explicitly documents the relationship between several pedals and their later/revised forms. Cosmetic revisions and ordinary version changes are not split into separate census identities unless the manufacturer presents a materially distinct product identity. Where a pedal was discontinued but its identity is preserved in the official catalog/history, it remains in the historical census.

## Overdrive
- Pit Viper
- Ozymandias

## Distortion
- Godeater
- Godeater+
- Pit Viper
- Ozymandias

## Fuzz
- Chemical Burn
- Baron Samedi
- Dirty Mirror
- Godeater

## Product notes
- **Pit Viper:** Animal Factory's official product page identifies the pedal as a guitar overdrive/drive design and says the 2019 revision used hard/soft clipping controls to produce varied diode-clipping and compression textures. The company's 2023 product announcement describes the returning Pit Viper as an overdrive with expanded controls. It is retained under Overdrive and not separately promoted to Distortion merely because its clipping range can become aggressive.
- **Godeater:** Animal Factory's official historical material calls the original product a bass fuzz/bass distortion pedal and describes its low-end compression and bass-distortion circuit. Because the manufacturer itself uses both fuzz and distortion terminology for the pedal across its historical materials, it is retained in both Fuzz and Distortion.
- **Godeater+:** the current official page identifies it as a Dynamic Bass Distortion Pedal and describes a signature distortion circuit ranging from mild grunt to extreme saturation. It is retained under Distortion. The plus model is kept as a distinct current product identity because Animal Factory presents it as an expanded successor with new routing, sidechain, crossover, MIDI/CV and 24V internal operation rather than merely a cosmetic revision.
- **Ozymandias:** the official product page explicitly names it a Dual Overdrive/Distortion Pedal and describes two independent overdrive circuits with hard/soft clipping options. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Chemical Burn:** Animal Factory's own history identifies Chemical Burn as the company's first product and describes it as a modified Shin-Ei Superfuzz FY-6 variant. The 2023 company announcement calls the returning Chemical Burn a "Face-Melting Wall of Fuzz." It is retained under Fuzz. The v1/v2/v3 iterations are not split into separate census entries because the manufacturer treats them as successive versions of the Chemical Burn product identity.
- **Baron Samedi:** Animal Factory's official history and product-development material identify Baron Samedi as a fuzz pedal. The manufacturer later described the returning Baron as a chaotic fuzz and separately developed the Baron Samedi II Eurorack module. The pedal itself is retained under Fuzz; the Eurorack module is excluded from the pedal census.
- **Dirty Mirror:** the official 2025 product announcement identifies the pedal as a dual-parallel fuzz design. Its BURN channel is based on Chemical Burn and its CHURN channel is based on a heavily modified Big Muff Pi, with an integrated delay and parallel mixing. It is retained under Fuzz only; the internal delay does not create another target-category identity.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Eurorack modules:** Godeater Eurorack, Baron Samedi II, Pit Viper V2 module, Orobas I/II, Coma Reactor, Bonesaw, Vivisect, and Tannhäuser Gates are excluded from the physical pedal census. Animal Factory's own manuals and catalog separate these from the pedal products.
- **DIY module kits:** excluded from the pedal census.
- **Orobas:** although the Eurorack product provides tube saturation/distortion, it is a module rather than a pedal and is therefore excluded.
- **Chemical Burn versions:** v1/v2/v3 are treated as one product identity, not three pedals.
- **Baron Samedi versions:** pedal revisions are treated as one Baron Samedi pedal identity; Baron Samedi II is a separate Eurorack module, not a second pedal.
- **Pit Viper revisions:** the 2015/2019 pedal versions are retained as one Pit Viper identity. The later Ozymandias is a separate product because Animal Factory explicitly describes it as the successor and as a dual-channel design.
- **Godeater revisions:** the historical Godeater pedal remains one identity; Godeater+ is separately listed because the manufacturer presents it as an expanded successor with materially expanded functionality.
- **Boost functions:** not counted as a separate target category. Chemical Burn's switchable level boost does not create a boost identity.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Animal Factory Amplification official site: https://animalfactoryamps.com/
- About Us / team: https://animalfactoryamps.com/pages/about-us-1
- Meet the founder Aditya Nandwana: https://animalfactoryamps.com/blogs/news/meet-the-team-aditya-nandwana
- Current pedal catalog: https://animalfactoryamps.com/collections/effect-pedals/pedals
- Current all-products catalog: https://animalfactoryamps.com/collections/all
- Ozymandias: https://animalfactoryamps.com/products/ozymandias-dual-overdrive-distortion-pedal
- Godeater+: https://animalfactoryamps.com/products/godeater-v3-2023-dynamic-bass-distortion
- Pit Viper historical/current page: https://animalfactoryamps.com/en-au/products/animal-five-pit-viper
- Dirty Mirror announcement: https://animalfactoryamps.com/blogs/news/introducing-dirty-mirror
- Superbooth 2023 pedal announcement: https://animalfactoryamps.com/blogs/news/superbooth-2023-news-animal-factory-reveals-new-pedal-and-synth-prototypes
- Godeater 2020 historical announcement: https://animalfactoryamps.com/blogs/news/godeater-2020-edition-open-for-orders
- Product manuals index: https://animalfactoryamps.com/pages/manuals
- Current pedal collection: https://animalfactoryamps.com/collections/all/pedals

## Status
**Verified research block.** Animal Factory Amplification is a previously uncovered builder with a documented physical-pedal lineup spanning fuzz, overdrive, and distortion. The builder relationship is established by the company's own founder/team material, and the pedal classifications are based on the manufacturer's current catalog and manufacturer-authored historical/product documentation. Eurorack modules and other non-pedal products were deliberately excluded from the target census.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 046

## Builder
**Sitek Guitar Electronics — Andrzej “Andy” Sitek & Hania Sitek**

## Verification basis
Sitek Guitar Electronics' official About page identifies Andy Sitek and his wife Hania as the founders, and gives the company contact as Sitek Engineering / Andrzej Sitek in Gdynia, Poland. The official pedal catalog separates pedals into Booster / Pre-amp, Overdrive, Distortion / Fuzz, Delay / Modulation, and Utility categories. The core census below is limited to products whose official product pages explicitly establish one or more of the three target dirt functions.

## Overdrive
- Freya Overdrive/Booster

## Distortion
- Wuffy Fuzzy Distortion
- Cocoa Distortion

## Fuzz
- Fuzzie Germanium Fuzz v2
- Pandora Octave Fuzz
- Wuffy Fuzzy Distortion

## Product notes
- **Freya Overdrive/Booster:** Sitek explicitly describes Freya as a dual pedal combining a low-to-mid-gain overdrive with a transparent high-headroom booster. The manufacturer categorizes it under both Booster / Pre-amp and Overdrive. Only the Overdrive identity is counted here; the clean booster is not one of the archive's three target categories.
- **Fuzzie Germanium Fuzz v2:** the official page calls it a guitar pedal offering tones from mild distortion to dynamic fuzz and specifically describes its fuzz character, germanium stages, and fuzz control. It is retained under Fuzz. The description of mild distortion is treated as a range within the fuzz product, not as a separate distortion identity.
- **Pandora Octave Fuzz:** Sitek explicitly calls Pandora a high-gain fuzz pedal. Its increased gain can saturate distortion and its upper octave becomes more prominent at higher settings. It is retained under Fuzz only because the manufacturer's product identity is explicitly fuzz rather than a separately named distortion pedal.
- **Wuffy Fuzzy Distortion:** Sitek explicitly identifies Wuffy as a high-gain distortion pedal whose range crosses the borderline of distortion and fuzz. The official catalog places it in Distortion / Fuzz and the product page carries both distortion and fuzz tags. It is therefore retained in both Distortion and Fuzz.
- **Cocoa Distortion:** the official catalog identifies Cocoa as a Distortion pedal. It is retained under Distortion. No fuzz or overdrive category is inferred merely from gain behavior.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Caffeine Dirty Booster v2:** excluded from the target census because the manufacturer presents it as a dirty booster rather than an explicitly named overdrive, distortion, or fuzz pedal.
- **Vixen Germanium Booster:** excluded as a booster.
- **Nursie Transparent Booster:** excluded as a booster.
- **Cristal Signal Buffer:** excluded as a buffer.
- **Phasia Phase Shifter:** excluded as modulation.
- **Version naming:** Fuzzie Germanium Fuzz v2 is treated as the current product identity shown by Sitek, not as a separate historical pedal from the original Fuzzie name.
- **No category inflation:** descriptive references to distortion, clipping, saturation, or drive inside a fuzz/overdrive product do not automatically create additional category entries unless the manufacturer explicitly establishes that target function as part of the product identity.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Sitek Guitar Electronics official site: https://www.sitek.rocks/
- About / founders: https://www.sitek.rocks/about/
- Current pedal catalog: https://www.sitek.rocks/category/pedals/
- Fuzzie Germanium Fuzz v2: https://www.sitek.rocks/product/fuzzie-fuzz/
- Pandora Octave Fuzz: https://www.sitek.rocks/product/pandora-fuzz/
- Freya Overdrive/Booster: https://www.sitek.rocks/product/freya-overdrive-booster/
- Wuffy Fuzzy Distortion: https://www.sitek.rocks/product/wuffy-distortion/
- Cocoa Distortion: https://www.sitek.rocks/product/cocoa-distortion/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Sitek Guitar Electronics is a previously uncovered Polish builder founded by Andrzej “Andy” Sitek and Hania Sitek. The current official catalog provides a compact but clearly documented dirt lineup spanning overdrive, distortion, and fuzz. Boost-only and non-dirt products were deliberately excluded, and descriptive gain ranges were not used to manufacture unsupported category identities.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 047

## Builder
**Subdecay — Brian Marshall**

## Verification basis
Subdecay's official site identifies Brian Marshall as the founder of Subdecay in 2004 and as the company's effects designer. The official catalog and individual manufacturer product pages/documentation were used to establish the target dirt identities below. The census includes current and clearly documented discontinued Subdecay pedals where the manufacturer still provides authoritative product material.

## Overdrive
- Gain Theory Overdrive
- Liquid Sunshine
- Supernova Drive
- Blackstar – Ultimate Overdrive
- Variac Overdrive
- Vector – Analog Preamp

## Distortion
- Vector – Analog Preamp

## Fuzz
- Harmonic Antagonizer
- Noise Theory – Parallel Waveshaper Fuzz
- Vector – Analog Preamp

## Product notes
- **Gain Theory Overdrive:** current official product page explicitly identifies it as an overdrive and describes a high-gain design inspired by 1990s amplifier design. Retained under Overdrive only.
- **Liquid Sunshine:** official manufacturer archive identifies it as a Class A Overdrive and notes that it is out of production. Retained as a historical Subdecay overdrive identity.
- **Supernova Drive:** official manufacturer page identifies it as an overdrive and says the pedal was retired. Its description says the upper gain range can approach distortion or fuzz, but the manufacturer identity remains overdrive, so it is not promoted into additional categories.
- **Blackstar – Ultimate Overdrive:** official manufacturer archive identifies the Blackstar as a high-gain overdrive. The later With Girth version is treated as part of the same product identity rather than a separate census entry.
- **Variac Overdrive:** official manufacturer page explicitly calls it an expressive overdrive pedal and describes its amp/power-stage-inspired gain behavior. Retained under Overdrive only.
- **Vector – Analog Preamp:** official product page explicitly describes the Vector as covering overdrive, distortion, and fuzz, while the manual describes its eleven channels as a palette of overdrives, fuzz, and distortion. It is therefore retained in all three target categories as one multi-dirt pedal identity.
- **Harmonic Antagonizer:** official manufacturer page explicitly identifies the product as a fuzz/oscillator and describes its square-wave fuzz circuit. Retained under Fuzz only; its chaotic distortion language is descriptive rather than a separate manufacturer category.
- **Noise Theory – Parallel Waveshaper Fuzz:** official manufacturer page explicitly identifies it as a fuzz and documents multiple fuzz types, including wave-shaper and analog fuzz modes. Retained under Fuzz only.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- Synthesizers, modulation, filters, reverbs, delays, utility products, and other non-dirt Subdecay identities were excluded.
- **Prometheus 3** was not counted despite containing an overdrive parameter because the product itself is a dual filter rather than a dirt pedal.
- **M3000** was not counted despite descriptive references to fuzz/overdriven-amplifier character because it is a guitar/bass synthesizer.
- Descriptions of a product approaching distortion or fuzz at extreme gain were not used to manufacture extra category entries unless the manufacturer explicitly establishes that dirt function as part of the product identity.
- **NoiseBox**, referenced historically by Subdecay as a predecessor/related effect to the Harmonic Antagonizer, was not added as a separate census entry because the current authoritative product material does not provide enough direct product identity information to classify it beyond that historical reference.
- Version/cosmetic changes such as Blackstar With Girth were not split into duplicate product identities.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Subdecay official site: https://subdecay.com/
- Founder / Brian Marshall: https://subdecay.com/author/brianmarshall
- Beginning / founder statement: https://subdecay.com/the-beginning
- Current effects shop: https://subdecay.com/product-category/effects
- Gain Theory Overdrive: https://subdecay.com/effect/gain-theory-gt1-modern-overdrive
- Liquid Sunshine: https://subdecay.com/liquid-sunshine-overdrive
- Supernova Drive: https://subdecay.com/effect/supernova-drive-overdrive
- Blackstar – Ultimate Overdrive: https://subdecay.com/blackstar-ultimate-overdrive
- Variac Overdrive: https://subdecay.com/effect/variac-overdrive
- Vector – Analog Preamp: https://subdecay.com/effect/vector-analog-preamp
- Vector manual: https://subdecay.com/manuals/vp1.pdf
- Harmonic Antagonizer: https://subdecay.com/effect/harmonic-antagonizer-fuzz-oscillator
- Noise Theory: https://subdecay.com/effect/noise-theory-parallel-waveshper-fuzz

## Status
**Verified research block.** Subdecay is a previously uncovered builder founded by Brian Marshall in 2004. The block documents a compact but historically broad dirt lineup spanning overdrive, distortion, and fuzz, while keeping product identity stricter than raw gain descriptions and excluding synth/modulation/filter products from the dirt census.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 048

## Builder
**Big John Effects — Jan Detremerie, Stijn Vandebril, and Arnoud Faber**

## Verification basis
Big John Effects' official About page identifies the company's history: founded in Belgium in 2006 by bassist Jan Detremerie, later joined by guitarist Stijn Vandebril, with Arnoud Faber running the workshop since 2013. The same official history gives a product timeline covering fuzz, overdrive, distortion, and drive designs. Current official product pages and the manufacturer's owner manual were used to establish target dirt classifications. The census includes clearly documented historical identities as well as the current catalog where the manufacturer still provides product material.

## Overdrive
- Granny Puker
- Pocket Rocket
- Dirty Sandwich
- Hairy Balls

## Distortion
- Granny Puker
- Obama Wah
- Hairy Balls

## Fuzz
- Hairy Balls
- Lil' John series

## Product notes
- **Hairy Balls:** Big John's current product page explicitly identifies the pedal as a fuzz with boost, while the official 2024 owner manual explicitly describes the unit as producing preamp, overdrive, distortion, fuzz, and synth-like sounds. It is therefore retained in all three target dirt categories. The boost and gate functions are not counted as separate categories.
- **Granny Puker:** the official product page identifies the two core effects as Preamp/Overdrive and Dirty Octave Down, explicitly states that it can be used as a distortion box, and describes its smooth gritty overdrive range. It is retained under both Overdrive and Distortion. The octave-down function is not a target dirt category.
- **Pocket Rocket:** the manufacturer explicitly calls this a vintage-style Tube Screamer take with overdrive and an additional clipping mode. It is retained under Overdrive only.
- **Dirty Sandwich:** the official product page describes a switchable boost plus adjustable drive ranging from transparent overdrive to heavy drive. It is retained under Overdrive. The effects-loop/utility functions are not counted.
- **Obama Wah:** Big John's official history identifies this as a wah/filter and distortion design with voltage gain control. Because the manufacturer explicitly places distortion in the product's identity, it is retained under Distortion despite the pedal's primary filter/wah function.
- **Lil' John series:** Big John's official history identifies the 2011 Lil' John series as Boost and Fuzz designs by Stijn Vandebril. Because the official source does not expose enough separate model-level information in the current catalog to establish distinct pedal identities, the series is retained as one historical fuzz identity rather than inventing individual names.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **BassDrive:** listed in the official timeline as a 2015 pre-order design, but the accessible manufacturer material does not provide enough product-level classification to place it confidently in one of the three target categories. It remains excluded from the categorized census.
- **AmpAmp:** excluded because the manufacturer identifies it as a bass preamp rather than a target dirt pedal.
- **LoLife:** excluded because the manufacturer identifies it as a flatwound-sound/preamp-oriented design without enough explicit target-category evidence.
- **TrebleJuice:** excluded because the manufacturer identifies it as a 2024 prototype treble-oriented design and provides insufficient evidence for a target dirt classification.
- **Boost-only functions:** not counted as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz merely because they can push a following stage.
- **Octave/filter/looper functions:** not promoted into the dirt census unless the manufacturer explicitly identifies the pedal itself with a target dirt function.
- **No category inflation:** descriptive terms such as grit, gain, drive, or saturation were not used to create extra categories without explicit manufacturer support.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Big John Effects official site: https://bigjohnmusic.com/
- About / company history: https://bigjohnmusic.com/about/
- Hairy Balls: https://bigjohnmusic.com/a-big-john-hairy-balls/
- Hairy Balls owner manual: https://bigjohnmusic.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Big-John-Hairy-Balls-Owners-Manual.pdf
- Granny Puker: https://bigjohnmusic.com/?page_id=34
- Pocket Rocket: https://bigjohnmusic.com/?page_id=36
- Dirty Sandwich: https://bigjohnmusic.com/?page_id=38
- FAQ / owner manuals index: https://bigjohnmusic.com/faq/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Big John Effects is a previously uncovered Belgian/Netherlands builder lineage with clearly documented historical dirt products. The block preserves explicit manufacturer classifications, includes the unusually multi-dirt Hairy Balls identity in all three target categories, and deliberately leaves insufficiently documented historical/pre-order products outside the categorized census.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 049

## Builder
**Big Knob Pedals — Gary Kibler**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources used:
- Big Knob Pedals official About page
- Big Knob Pedals official current pedal catalog
- Individual official product pages where available

Authoritative historical corroboration:
- Vintage Guitar review/profile documenting Gary Kibler as the builder behind Big Knob Pedals and describing the company's hand-built catalog.

The official About page identifies Gary Kibler as President / General Manager and states that Big Knob Pedals has made handmade guitar effects since 2008. The official catalog currently exposes 32 pedal identities across Fuzz, Muff, Overdrive, Distortion, and other effects categories. The current catalog was used as the primary product-name and category census, with individual product pages used where available.

## Overdrive
- Tube 808
- Tone Boner
- D OverDrive 250
- Range Blaster
- Zed Drive
- Blues Buster
- Mad Donkey
- Game of Tones
- Pink Freud

## Distortion
- Tone-Nation
- Rock-Ola
- Appetite for Distortion
- Rat Pak
- Black Metallic
- Guv'nator

## Fuzz
- Fuzz Space
- Tone Blender
- Fuzz Barn
- Bushy Bison
- Octavius
- Colour Jumbo
- Pink Freud
- Legend TB
- Green Russian
- Creamy Pi
- Civil Warp
- I.C.B.M.
- NYC Version 6
- Pig Stomp

## Product-level verification notes
- **Pink Freud** is listed by the manufacturer in the Fuzz section and is explicitly described as combining a classic FF fuzz circuit with an overdrive stage. It is therefore retained under both Fuzz and Overdrive.
- **Tube 808** is explicitly categorized by Big Knob as Overdrive and described as a Tube Screamer-style overdrive.
- **Tone Boner** is categorized by the manufacturer as Overdrive and described as a MOSFET boost. Because the manufacturer places the finished pedal in the Overdrive catalog, it is retained in the census rather than reclassifying it from description alone.
- **D OverDrive 250** is categorized as Overdrive and described as a drive/boost pedal intended to push an amplifier into distortion.
- **Range Blaster** is categorized as Overdrive by the manufacturer, although its product description identifies it as a treble booster. It is retained only in Overdrive because that is the builder's catalog classification.
- **Zed Drive**, **Blues Buster**, **Mad Donkey**, and **Game of Tones** are explicitly categorized as Overdrive in the manufacturer catalog.
- **Tone-Nation**, **Rock-Ola**, **Appetite for Distortion**, **Rat Pak**, **Black Metallic**, and **Guv'nator** are explicitly categorized by Big Knob as Distortion.
- **Fuzz Space**, **Tone Blender**, **Fuzz Barn**, **Bushy Bison**, **Octavius**, **Colour Jumbo**, and **Legend TB** are explicitly presented by the manufacturer as fuzz designs. Octavius is retained as Fuzz because the manufacturer places it in the Fuzz catalog despite its octave-up function.
- **Green Russian**, **Creamy Pi**, **Civil Warp**, **I.C.B.M.**, **NYC Version 6**, and **Pig Stomp** are explicitly presented in the manufacturer's Muff/fuzz catalog. The manufacturer describes each as a fuzz-oriented Muff variant, so they are counted under Fuzz rather than creating a separate Muff category.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- Non-dirt categories such as Echo Flex, Vibe Tone, Dyna Tone, and Wah Fix are excluded from the Builder → Pedals dirt census.
- The manufacturer exposes a separate Muff category, but Muff is treated as a fuzz-family category for this archive's required taxonomy of Overdrive / Distortion / Fuzz. Muff products are therefore recorded under Fuzz rather than as a fourth dirt class.
- Boost behavior mentioned in descriptions is not separately counted as a target category unless the manufacturer itself places the finished product in the Overdrive category, as with Range Blaster and Tone Boner.
- No cosmetic variants, enclosure finishes, or duplicate circuit descriptions were promoted into separate identities.
- No additional historical pedal names were inferred from third-party lists when the official current catalog did not provide enough evidence to establish a distinct product identity.

## Sources
1. Big Knob Pedals — About: https://bigknobpedals.com/about-us/
2. Big Knob Pedals — Pedals catalog: https://bigknobpedals.com/pedals/
3. Big Knob Pedals — Current homepage/catalog: https://www.bigknobpedals.com/
4. Big Knob Pedals — Game of Tones product page: https://bigknobpedals.com/product/game-of-tones/
5. Big Knob Pedals — NYC Version 6 product page: https://bigknobpedals.com/product/nyc-version-6/
6. Vintage Guitar — Big Knob Pedals review/profile, documenting Gary Kibler and the company's history: https://bigknobpedals.com/reviews/

## Checkpoint
Block 049 completes the Big Knob Pedals builder census for this research pass. The builder is documented with explicit Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz relationships, using the manufacturer's own taxonomy wherever possible.

Website: untouched.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 050

## Builder
**Blackout Effectors — Kyle Tompkins**

## Verification basis
Primary/manufacturer-originated material where recoverable:
- Archived Blackout Effectors product descriptions reproduced in period documentation and manuals
- Manufacturer-originated descriptions preserved in product/manual archives

Authoritative corroboration:
- Effects Database interview with Kyle Tompkins documenting the company's founding and the Fix'd Fuzz as its first release
- Effects Database product records preserving Blackout Effectors product names and manufacturer descriptions
- Premier Guitar review of the Crystal Dagger

The Effects Database interview records Kyle Tompkins saying Blackout Effectors began in early 2008 with the release of Fix'd Fuzz. The archived product corpus documents a compact but dirt-heavy catalog spanning fuzz and overdrive designs. No standalone distortion category is forced where the manufacturer documentation does not clearly establish one.

## Overdrive
- Mantra Overdrive
- Dual Gain Mantra
- Fix'd Fuzz
- Fix'd Fuzz Deluxe
- Twosome Dual Fuzz

## Distortion
- None promoted as a standalone manufacturer-supported identity in this block.

## Fuzz
- Fix'd Fuzz
- Fix'd Fuzz Deluxe
- Musket Fuzz
- Blunderbuss Musket
- Blunderbuss Fuzz
- FUBÄR
- Fuzz Bear
- Crystal Dagger
- Twosome Dual Fuzz
- Special Twosome Deluxe
- A Seriously Special Twosome
- A Very Special Twosome

## Product-level verification notes
- **Mantra Overdrive** is explicitly documented by Blackout Effectors as an overdrive with cascaded MOSFET/JFET gain stages and a range from low gain through heavy overdrive.
- **Dual Gain Mantra** is retained as a distinct documented product identity because the historical catalog identifies it separately from the standard Mantra.
- **Fix'd Fuzz** is explicitly described by the manufacturer as a fuzz box with a boost through light overdrive stage. It is therefore retained under both Fuzz and Overdrive.
- **Fix'd Fuzz Deluxe** is the expanded version of Fix'd Fuzz and is likewise documented as containing two fuzz voices plus clean boost through overdrive. It is retained under both Fuzz and Overdrive.
- **Musket Fuzz** is explicitly a fuzz design based on the Big Muff family. Its pre-boost and aggressive clipping are not used to create a separate distortion identity.
- **Blunderbuss Musket** is documented as an alternate Musket revision and is retained as a distinct historical product identity only because it was sold as its own named version, not merely as a cosmetic variant.
- **Blunderbuss Fuzz** is documented as a fuzz product and is retained under Fuzz.
- **Fuzz Bear** is documented as a fuzz product and is treated as the predecessor identity to FUBÄR rather than silently merging the names.
- **FUBÄR** is explicitly documented as a fuzz pedal and as an expanded development of Fuzz Bear. It remains a separate named pedal identity.
- **Crystal Dagger** combines an octave-up fuzz circuit with ring modulation/phasing. Because the manufacturer-originated description explicitly identifies the fuzz circuit, it is retained under Fuzz only.
- **Twosome Dual Fuzz** combines the Musket Fuzz and deluxe Fix'd Fuzz circuits. It is retained under Fuzz and Overdrive because its Fix'd side is explicitly capable of overdrive, while the product's primary identity is a dual-fuzz pedal.
- **Special Twosome Deluxe**, **A Seriously Special Twosome**, and **A Very Special Twosome** are retained as named historical Twosome-family identities in the fuzz census only where the archival catalog distinguishes them as separate named products. They are not expanded into additional category claims beyond the documented fuzz family.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- **Gravity Boost** is excluded because it is a boost-only product, outside the archive's three target dirt categories.
- **Cadavernous**, **Whetstone**, and **Sibling** are excluded because they are reverb/phaser products rather than target dirt pedals.
- Cosmetic artwork and enclosure-color variants are not separate identities.
- Fuzz Bear → FUBÄR is preserved as two named historical identities because the manufacturer-era catalog distinguishes them, but no extra category is inferred from the relationship.
- No standalone Distortion entry is invented from generic use of the word “distortion” in secondary descriptions or from high-gain fuzz behavior.
- The archive does not split every Musket/Twosome revision into a separate census entry when the evidence only indicates a revision or special edition rather than a distinct marketed product identity.

## Sources
1. Effects Database — Kyle Tompkins / Blackout Effectors interview: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/interviews/brands/blackout
2. Effects Database — Blackout Effectors catalog: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout
3. Effects Database — Fix'd Fuzz: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout/fixdfuzz
4. Analogue Haven — Blackout Effectors Mantra Overdrive manual: https://www.analoguehaven.com/blackout-effectors/mantra-overdrive/manual.pdf
5. Analogue Haven — Blackout Effectors Fix'd Fuzz manual: https://www.analoguehaven.com/blackout-effectors/fix-d-fuzz-v2/fixd-fuzz-manual_redux.pdf
6. Effects Database — Musket Fuzz: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout/musketfuzz
7. Effects Database — Twosome Dual Fuzz: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout/twosome
8. Effects Database — FUBÄR: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout/fubar
9. Effects Database — Crystal Dagger: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout/crystaldagger
10. Premier Guitar — Crystal Dagger review: https://www.premierguitar.com/blackout-effectors-crystal-dagger-pedal-review

## Checkpoint
Block 050 completes the Blackout Effectors builder census for this research pass. The block records the builder relationship to documented overdrive and fuzz products, deliberately leaves the Distortion category empty rather than inferring it, and excludes non-target effects.

Website: untouched.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 051

## Builder
**Blackhawk Amplifiers — Brooks Blackhawk**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources used:
- Blackhawk Amplifiers official About page
- Blackhawk Amplifiers official Products/Pedals/Fuzz pages
- Individual official product pages
- Official FAQ/news material where useful for current production context

Authoritative corroboration:
- Equipboard identifies Brooks Blackhawk as founder of Blackhawk Amplifiers.
- Rattlesnake Cable Company identifies Brooks Blackhawk as the Portland amp/pedal builder in a 2026 builder reference.

The manufacturer's About page states that Blackhawk makes amplifiers, preamplifiers and pedals by hand in Portland, Oregon. The official catalog currently exposes separate Drive, Distortion and Fuzz categories. The manufacturer itself is therefore used as the primary taxonomy wherever possible.

## Overdrive
- Valkyrie Drive
- Heimdall-2 MKII
- Balrog Distortion MKII
- Uruk-Hai MKII {Parallel} Drive & Fuzz
- Morgul Drive MKII

## Distortion
- Balrog Distortion MKII
- Heimdall-2 MKII
- Heimdall-3 MKII Distortion
- Nazgûl MKII {Parallel} Distortion and Fuzz
- Uruk-Hai MKII {Parallel} Drive & Fuzz
- Windhand LBM Fuzz

## Fuzz
- Valhalla Fuzz
- Valhalla Deluxe / Valhalla V3/V4/V5 family identity
- Fellbeast Fuzz
- Pi π Fuzz
- Pi π Bass Fuzz
- Asgard Bass Fuzz
- Gandalf Fuzz
- Mithrandir Octave Fuzz
- Svarog Fuzz
- Ramshead Fuzz
- Triangle Fuzz
- Green / Black Russian Pi π Fuzz
- Basilisk Fuzz
- Nazgûl MKII {Parallel} Distortion and Fuzz
- Uruk-Hai MKII {Parallel} Drive & Fuzz
- Windhand LBM Fuzz

## Product-level verification notes
- **Valkyrie Drive** is the builder's saturated op-amp drive design. The official Uruk-Hai page describes the Valkyrie section as a drive and explicitly documents its clipping behavior, including distortion/fuzz-like settings. It is retained under Overdrive because the standalone product identity is Drive.
- **Heimdall-2 MKII** is explicitly described by Blackhawk as a distortion, overdrive and EQ pedal. It therefore belongs in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Heimdall-3 MKII Distortion** is explicitly titled Distortion. Its manufacturer description says it can operate as classic overdrive/distortion and reach fuzzed-out hard-clipping territory, but it is not separately promoted to Fuzz because the finished product is presented as a distortion pedal.
- **Balrog Distortion MKII** is explicitly a high-gain distortion and is also described by the manufacturer as delivering mid-gain overdrive tones and huge overdrive boost. It is retained under both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Morgul Drive MKII** is a current Blackhawk Drive identity. It is retained under Overdrive because the manufacturer places it in the Drive category.
- **Uruk-Hai MKII** is explicitly a parallel Fuzz/Drive design. The manufacturer describes its two sections as Valhalla Fuzz and Valkyrie Drive and says the combined unit can mix fuzz and drive/distortion. It is therefore retained under Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz.
- **Nazgûl MKII** is explicitly a parallel Distortion and Fuzz product built from Balrog and Fellbeast sections. It is retained under Distortion and Fuzz. The Balrog section can provide overdrive, but the finished Nazgûl product is not promoted to Overdrive without explicit manufacturer category placement.
- **Valhalla** is the builder's core sludgy fuzz identity. Current and historical versions are consolidated rather than split into separate circuit identities solely because of enclosure/version changes.
- **Fellbeast Fuzz** is explicitly a fuzz pedal. The manufacturer also describes lower-gain settings as producing thicker, more articulate distortion, but it remains Fuzz only.
- **Pi π Fuzz** and **Pi π Bass Fuzz** are explicitly fuzz products. The manufacturer notes that the Green Russian version can reach high-gain distortion, but its product identity remains Fuzz.
- **Asgard Bass Fuzz**, **Gandalf**, **Mithrandir**, **Svarog**, **Ramshead**, **Triangle**, and **Basilisk** are documented as fuzz-family products in Blackhawk's catalog and/or authoritative catalog references.
- **Green / Black Russian Pi π Fuzz** is retained as one product-family identity rather than splitting the Green, Black, and Chernobyl enclosure/values variants into separate archive identities.
- **Windhand LBM Fuzz** is explicitly identified by the manufacturer as a fuzz/distortion replacement design. It is retained under Fuzz and Distortion because the manufacturer itself calls the source/product a fuzz/distortion design.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- Boost-only products such as Zeus and Volume IV are excluded from the target dirt taxonomy unless the finished product is explicitly categorized by Blackhawk as Drive/Distortion/Fuzz.
- Preamp-only products such as HEL, Ragnarok, Odin and Mjolnir are excluded from the pedal dirt census.
- Non-dirt effects such as Lothlórien Reverb, Mirkwood Delay, Black Gate, Ouroboros and Necronomicon are excluded.
- Cosmetic finishes and ordinary circuit revisions are not counted as separate pedal identities.
- The Black Russian / Green Russian / Chernobyl variants are consolidated rather than inflated into multiple separate product records.
- Descriptions that merely say a fuzz can become more distorted are not used to create a second category unless the manufacturer explicitly identifies the finished pedal as both types.

## Sources
1. Blackhawk Amplifiers — About: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/about
2. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Products: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/products
3. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Pedals catalog: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/category/pedals
4. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Fuzz catalog: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/category/fuzz
5. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Balrog Distortion MKII: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/product/balrog-distortion
6. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Heimdall-2 MKII: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/product/heimdall-2-mkii-bass-distortion
7. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Heimdall-3 MKII: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/product/heimdall-3-mkii-distortion
8. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Nazgûl MKII: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/product/nazgul
9. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Uruk-Hai MKII: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/product/uruk-hai-parallel-mkii
10. Blackhawk Amplifiers — Pi π Bass Fuzz MKII: https://www.blackhawkamplifiers.com/product/pi-bass-fuzz-russian

## Checkpoint
Block 051 completes the Blackhawk Amplifiers builder census for this research pass. The builder has explicit Overdrive/Drive, Distortion, and Fuzz relationships. Product variants were consolidated conservatively, and non-dirt/preamp/boost-only products were excluded.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 052

## Builder
**MI Audio / MI Effects — Michael Ibrahim**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources used:
- MI Audio official About page
- MI Audio official effects catalog/store
- Individual official product pages and manufacturer manuals where available

Authoritative corroboration used where the current official catalog no longer exposes older products:
- Australian trademark record identifying MI Audio and the Neo Fuzz / Blues Pro pedal marks
- Guitar Pedal X directory/profile documenting the historical MI Audio / MI Effects catalog and Michael Ibrahim relationship

MI Audio's official About page states that the company was founded in 2002 by Michael Ibrahim and that the creation of the Tube Zone Overdrive was the impetus for the company. The same page identifies the later Super Crunch Box and Super Blues Pro as updated generations of earlier drive products.

## Overdrive
- Tube Zone Overdrive
- Blue Boy Deluxe
- Cross Over Drive
- Super Blues Pro

## Distortion
- Crunch Box
- Super Crunch Box V2
- Classic Distortion
- Megalith Delta

## Fuzz
- GI Fuzz
- Neo Fuzz
- Super Blues Pro

## Product-level verification notes
- **Tube Zone Overdrive** is explicitly identified by MI Audio's own company history as the pedal whose creation drove the founding of MI Audio. It is retained as Overdrive.
- **Blue Boy Deluxe** is documented as an MI Effects overdrive identity in authoritative historical catalog material. It is retained as Overdrive rather than inferring additional categories from its gain range.
- **Cross Over Drive** is an official MI Audio product. Its manufacturer page describes it as an amp-like overdrive built around four FET stages and explicitly identifies its overdrive behavior. The page notes that extreme settings can enter loose fuzz territory, but the finished product remains categorized as Overdrive.
- **Super Blues Pro** is explicitly described by MI Audio as a low-to-mid-gain overdrive. The manufacturer also documents selectable fuzz modes, so it is retained under both Overdrive and Fuzz.
- **Crunch Box** is the original MI Audio high-gain drive identity. MI Audio's official About page states that the classic Crunch Box production was later updated as the Super Crunch Box. It is retained as the historical Distortion identity rather than duplicating it as a separate current product.
- **Super Crunch Box V2** is the later documented Crunch Box generation and is consistently identified as a high-gain distortion product in manufacturer-linked historical material. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Classic Distortion** is a documented discontinued MI Effects product identity and is retained under Distortion.
- **Megalith Delta** is the stompbox version of the MI Amplification Megalith high-gain amplifier tone. MI Audio's catalog identifies it as a high-gain distortion pedal, so it is retained under Distortion.
- **GI Fuzz** and **Neo Fuzz** are documented MI Effects fuzz identities. The Neo Fuzz is also supported by the Australian trademark record for MI Audio.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- **Boost 'N' Buff** is excluded because the manufacturer explicitly identifies it as a booster/buffer rather than one of the target dirt categories.
- **Polyanna/Pollyanna Octave** is excluded because its core product identity is octave processing rather than a target dirt pedal.
- **Compressor** and **Lo-Fi Delay** are excluded as non-dirt effects.
- **Iron Duke** and other amplifier products are excluded because this census is for pedals.
- Descriptions that merely mention fuzz-like behavior, saturation, or gain are not used to create additional categories unless the manufacturer or authoritative catalog explicitly identifies the product as belonging there.
- V1/V2 revisions are consolidated where they represent the same named product identity; the historical Crunch Box and later Super Crunch Box are retained separately because MI Audio itself distinguishes the original and updated product names.

## Sources
1. MI Audio — About Us: https://miaudio.com/about-us/
2. MI Audio — Effects catalog: https://miaudio.com/effects-store/Effects-c48543359
3. MI Audio — Cross Over Drive: https://miaudio.com/Cross-Over-Drive-p488115041
4. MI Audio — Super Blues Pro: https://miaudio.com/Super-Blues-Pro-p488122012
5. MI Audio — official store/home: https://miaudio.com/
6. Australian Official Journal of Trade Marks — MI Audio / Neo Fuzz record: https://pericles.ipaustralia.gov.au/ols/epublish/content/journals/trademarks/tm2105p20070201.pdf
7. Guitar Pedal X — MI Audio / MI Effects directory profile: https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/guitar-pedal-directory---favourite-pedals-by-preferred-pedal-makers

## Checkpoint
Block 052 completes the MI Audio / MI Effects builder census for this research pass. The builder has explicit Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz relationships. Current and historical product identities were consolidated conservatively, with discontinued products retained when their distinct product identity was sufficiently documented.

Website: untouched.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 053

## Builder
**Audiolithe — Christophe & Gaëtan**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources used:
- Audiolithe official site and current guitar/bass pedal catalog
- Individual official product pages

Authoritative corroboration:
- Guitar Pedal X's 2024 London Synth & Pedal Expo report identifies Audiolithe as a builder collaboration between Christophe and Gaëtan and describes its then-current five-pedal range.

The manufacturer presents Audiolithe as a French effects builder with original circuits, and its current catalog explicitly places products into Overdrive/Fuzz categories. The census uses the manufacturer's own product labels rather than inferring categories from gain descriptions.

## Overdrive
- Extinction Drive
- The Empress Bass

## Distortion
- No standalone Distortion identity promoted in this block.

## Fuzz
- Doomer Fuzz
- Extinction Drive
- La Montagne
- Chaosculpt Bass

## Product-level verification notes
- **Extinction Drive** is explicitly presented by Audiolithe as an Overdrive/Fuzz product. The official product page describes it as a two-channel saturation design with overdrive, boost, fuzz and octave-up functionality. It therefore belongs in both Overdrive and Fuzz.
- **Doomer Fuzz** is explicitly listed by Audiolithe as Fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz only.
- **La Montagne** is explicitly described by Audiolithe as a two-stage Fuzz pedal. It is retained under Fuzz only.
- **The Empress Bass** is explicitly categorized by Audiolithe as Overdrive. Its product identity is a bass drive, so it is retained under Overdrive.
- **Chaosculpt Bass** is explicitly categorized by Audiolithe as Fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz only.
- Audiolithe's current catalog does not expose a separate finished pedal identity explicitly categorized as Distortion. Descriptions involving saturation or aggressive drive were not used to invent a Distortion entry.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- **Echosmos** is excluded because it is a delay/modulation/oscillation pedal, not a target dirt pedal.
- Accessories and non-pedal merchandise are excluded.
- Bass dirt products are included because the archive's target categories are effects types, not instrument restrictions.
- Boost and octave functions are not counted as independent builder identities.
- No Distortion category was forced from descriptive language alone.

## Sources
1. Audiolithe — official site/catalog: https://audiolithefx.com/en-us
2. Audiolithe — official French catalog: https://audiolithefx.com/fr-fr
3. Audiolithe — Extinction Drive: https://audiolithefx.com/fr-fr/products/extinction-drive
4. Audiolithe — La Montagne: https://audiolithefx.com/en-eu/products/la-montagne
5. Guitar Pedal X — 2024 London Synth and Pedal Expo highlights: https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/gpx-blog/2024-london-synth-and-pedal-expo-pedal-highlights

## Checkpoint
Block 053 completes the Audiolithe builder census for this research pass. The builder has explicit Overdrive and Fuzz relationships; no standalone Distortion identity was promoted without explicit manufacturer support.

Website: untouched.
