# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 054

## Builder
**Barbarossa — Newtool**

## Verification basis
Primary/brand sources used:
- Newtool's official Barbarossa product page and current catalog
- Newtool's official company/brand site
- Birdland Guitars, Barbarossa's listed general distributor, for the historical WIVERN entry

The current Newtool Barbarossa page identifies CHIMAERA, GARGOYLE, URANUS (New Model), and CRONUS (New Model) as the brand's current effecter models and explicitly labels them Modern Over Drive, Distortion Over Drive, Over Drive, and Distortion Over Drive respectively. The same page lists Newtool as the company contact for the Barbarossa brand.

## Overdrive
- CHIMAERA
- GARGOYLE
- URANUS
- CRONUS
- WIVERN (historical / discontinued)

## Distortion
- GARGOYLE
- CRONUS

## Fuzz
- No verified Barbarossa fuzz identity promoted in this block.

## Product-level verification notes
- **CHIMAERA** is explicitly labeled "Modern Over Drive" by Newtool. It is retained under Overdrive only. Ikebe's product listing independently describes it as a modern overdrive.
- **GARGOYLE** is explicitly labeled "Distortion Over Drive" by Newtool. It is therefore recorded in both Overdrive and Distortion. Birdland's distributor page likewise describes it as occupying the space between distortion and overdrive.
- **URANUS** is explicitly labeled "Over Drive" by Newtool and is retained under Overdrive only. Ikebe likewise describes it as an overdrive.
- **CRONUS** is explicitly labeled "Distortion Over Drive" by Newtool. It is therefore recorded in both Overdrive and Distortion. The historical old-model page separately identifies it as a "Distortion Drive Modify Model," while the current model is presented as an original New Model. The archive keeps the named CRONUS identity consolidated rather than creating a duplicate solely for the revision.
- **WIVERN** is a historical/discontinued Barbarossa overdrive. Birdland's Barbarossa page identifies it as an end-of-sale product and describes it as an original Barbarossa effecter designed as a lower-cost model incorporating the brand's existing design concepts. It is retained as an Overdrive identity because the manufacturer/distributor material does not explicitly classify it as a standalone Distortion pedal.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- **PROMETHEUS** is excluded because Newtool identifies it as a digital tuner modification, not a target dirt effect.
- Barbarossa cables and speaker cables are excluded.
- Old-model URANUS and CRONUS are not counted as separate pedal identities from the current named models. They are revision/history notes under the same named products.
- **WIVERN** is retained despite being discontinued because it is a clearly documented historical Barbarossa pedal identity.
- No Fuzz entry is promoted because no Barbarossa product in the verified sources is explicitly categorized as Fuzz.

## Sources
1. Newtool — official Barbarossa brand/catalog: https://newtool.jp/barbarossa/
2. Newtool — official site/concept: https://newtool.jp/
3. Birdland Guitars — Barbarossa effecter information and historical WIVERN listing: https://birdlandguitars.com/birdland/article/bar_effector
4. Ikebe — CHIMAERA product listing: https://www.ikebe-gakki.com/Form/Product/ProductDetail.aspx?bid=ec&pid=856325&shop=0
5. Ikebe — URANUS product listing: https://www.ikebe-gakki.com/Form/Product/ProductDetail.aspx?bid=ec&pid=856326&shop=0
6. Ikebe — CRONUS product listing: https://ikebe-gakki.com/Form/Product/ProductDetail.aspx?bid=ec&pid=856327&shop=0

## Checkpoint
Block 054 completes the Barbarossa builder census for this research pass. The verified set spans Overdrive and Distortion, with GARGOYLE and CRONUS explicitly belonging to both. No verified Fuzz identity was promoted.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 055

## Builder
**BIXONIC — Bright International Corp. / BIXONIC Inc.; relaunched under Kikusui Electronics Corp.**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources used:
- BIXONIC official Story / history
- BIXONIC official New Arrival page
- BIXONIC official product page for EXP2000DR
- BIXONIC official Axentrix II announcement
- BIXONIC official 2025/2026 product announcements

The official BIXONIC history identifies Bright International Corp. as the origin of the BIXONIC brand, records the original EXPANDORA production beginning in 1994, the EXPANDORA II in 1999, the EXPANDORA REISSUE EXP-2000R in 2004, and the 2020 relaunch under Kikusui Electronics Corp. The same manufacturer material identifies the newer AXENTRIX A1 as a successor/new-generation product and explicitly describes its four drive modes as Crunch, Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz.

## Overdrive
- EXPANDORA family (EXP-2000 / EXP-2001 / EXP-2000R historical identity)
- AXENTRIX A1

## Distortion
- EXPANDORA family (EXP-2000 / EXP-2001 / EXP-2000R historical identity)
- AXENTRIX A1
- EXP2000DR
- AXENTRIX II

## Fuzz
- EXPANDORA family (EXP-2000 / EXP-2001 / EXP-2000R historical identity)
- AXENTRIX A1
- EXP2000DR
- AXENTRIX II

## Product-level verification notes
- **EXPANDORA family**: BIXONIC's official history states that the original product produced three distortion tones and also had a Fuzz/Forbidden mode. BIXONIC's current New Arrival page explicitly describes the prior Bixonic Expandora as having Crunch, Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz modes. The archive keeps the historical EXP-2000, EXP-2001, and EXP-2000R as one named Expandora family identity rather than multiplying the census with revision-only duplicates.
- **AXENTRIX A1**: BIXONIC's official New Arrival page explicitly identifies Crunch, Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz modes. It is therefore recorded in all three target dirt categories.
- **EXP2000DR**: BIXONIC's official product page describes Clean plus three Drive modes: Crunch, Distortion, and Forbidden. BIXONIC's official history identifies the Forbidden mode as the fuzz mode of the Expandora lineage. It is therefore recorded under Distortion and Fuzz. No separate Overdrive entry is promoted because the current EXP2000DR page does not explicitly label one of its three modes as Overdrive.
- **AXENTRIX II**: BIXONIC's official announcement identifies Clean, Crunch, Distortion, and Forbidden (Fuzz) modes. It is therefore recorded under Distortion and Fuzz. Crunch is not independently promoted into Overdrive because BIXONIC does not explicitly label that mode Overdrive on the verified product announcement.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- **Sugar Blues** is excluded because BIXONIC explicitly identifies it as an auto-wah, not a target dirt pedal.
- The archive does not count Crunch as Overdrive unless the manufacturer explicitly identifies it as Overdrive.
- The original EXPANDORA, EXPANDORA II, and EXPANDORA REISSUE are consolidated as one historical named family identity for this builder census. Their model numbers remain documented so future research can distinguish them if the later archive phase requires it.
- BIXONIC's current product announcements and historical material are treated as authoritative for the builder-to-pedal relationship.
- No additional dirt products are promoted from third-party listings without manufacturer support.

## Sources
1. BIXONIC — official Story / history: https://bixonic-sound.com/pages/story
2. BIXONIC — official New Arrival: https://bixonic-sound.com/pages/new-arrival
3. BIXONIC — EXP2000DR: https://bixonic-sound.com/products/exp2000dr
4. BIXONIC — Axentrix II Reveal at NAMM 2025: https://bixonic-sound.com/blogs/review/axentrix-ii-reveal-at-namm-2025
5. BIXONIC — New Pedal Showcase at NAMM 2025: https://bixonic-sound.com/blogs/review/newpedalnamm2025
6. BIXONIC — 2026 NAMM announcement: https://bixonic-sound.com/blogs/review/exhibit-at-the-namm-show-2026-and-unveil-two-new-pedals

## Checkpoint
Block 055 completes the BIXONIC builder census for this research pass. The verified builder-to-pedal relationships span Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. The historical EXPANDORA family and AXENTRIX A1 cover all three categories; EXP2000DR and AXENTRIX II are conservatively recorded under Distortion and Fuzz only.

Website: untouched.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 056

## Builder
**McGregor Pedals — Garth McGregor Heslop**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources used:
- McGregor Pedals official product catalog
- McGregor Pedals official individual product pages
- McGregor Pedals official press page

McGregor Pedals' current catalog identifies Crunch Transparent Overdrive, Double Crunch Transparent Overdrive, Crunch Plus Overdrive/Distortion, The Classic Fuzz, The Cozmic Fuzzball, and Octave Fuzz with Stank as dirt products. The official product pages explicitly classify the drive and fuzz identities, while the official press page corroborates the Cozmic Fuzzball as combining distortion and fuzz sounds. The manufacturer identifies Garth McGregor Heslop as the founder/designer behind the line.

## Overdrive
- Crunch Transparent Overdrive
- Double Crunch Transparent Overdrive
- Crunch Plus Overdrive/Distortion

## Distortion
- Crunch Plus Overdrive/Distortion
- The Cozmic Fuzzball

## Fuzz
- The Classic Fuzz
- The Cozmic Fuzzball
- Octave Fuzz with Stank

## Product-level verification notes
- **Crunch Transparent Overdrive**: McGregor's official product page explicitly identifies it as a low-gain transparent overdrive for guitar and bass. It is retained under Overdrive only.
- **Double Crunch Transparent Overdrive**: The official catalog identifies this as a Double Crunch Transparent Overdrive, and the product page describes the dual-channel/three-gain-stage Crunch design. It is retained under Overdrive only.
- **Crunch Plus Overdrive/Distortion**: McGregor's official catalog explicitly labels the product "Overdrive/Distortion." It is therefore recorded in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **The Classic Fuzz**: McGregor's official product page explicitly identifies it as a fuzz pedal and describes a modern recreation of early two-transistor fuzz behavior. It is recorded under Fuzz only, despite descriptions of distortion-like tones at some settings.
- **The Cozmic Fuzzball**: McGregor's official press page quotes Vintage Guitar describing the pedal as bringing together distortion and fuzz sounds. It is therefore recorded under both Distortion and Fuzz.
- **Octave Fuzz with Stank**: McGregor's official catalog explicitly names this as an Octave Fuzz. Because Fuzz is an explicit part of the product identity, it is recorded under Fuzz. The octave function is not treated as a separate target category.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- **MP Reverb** is excluded because it is a reverb, not a target dirt effect.
- The archive does not promote "distortion-like" behavior into Distortion unless the manufacturer explicitly identifies the product that way. This is why The Classic Fuzz remains Fuzz only.
- Product revisions/versions are not split into duplicate identities when McGregor presents them as the same named pedal family.
- No unsupported dirt classifications are inferred from third-party descriptions when manufacturer material is available.

## Sources
1. McGregor Pedals — official catalog: https://www.mcgregorpedals.com/collections/all
2. McGregor Pedals — Crunch Transparent Overdrive: https://www.mcgregorpedals.com/products/crunch-transparent-overdrive-pedal
3. McGregor Pedals — Double Crunch Transparent Overdrive: https://www.mcgregorpedals.com/products/double-crunch-transparent-overdrive
4. McGregor Pedals — The Classic Fuzz: https://www.mcgregorpedals.com/products/the-classic-fuzz
5. McGregor Pedals — official press page: https://www.mcgregorpedals.com/pages/press
6. McGregor Pedals — official home/catalog: https://www.mcgregorpedals.com/

## Checkpoint
Block 056 completes the McGregor Pedals builder census for this research pass. The verified set spans Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Crunch Plus is explicitly both Overdrive and Distortion; The Cozmic Fuzzball is explicitly supported as Distortion/Fuzz; and The Classic Fuzz remains Fuzz only despite distortion-like settings.

Website: untouched.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 057

## Builder
**Stomp Under Foot — Matt Pasquerella**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer source:
- Stomp Under Foot official site/catalog

Authoritative corroboration used for historical and product-level classifications:
- Premier Guitar product/news coverage
- Guitar Pedal X builder/product directory
- Effects Database historical product index

The official Stomp Under Foot site describes the catalog as handmade fuzz, overdrive, and distortion pedals and currently lists products including Civil War, Dire Wolf, Divided Drive, Elk Super Fuzz, Fuzz Master 3, Green Russian, Guv'nor, Motor Honey Superdrive, Overdrive 8-Fifty, Pumpkin Pi, Ram's Head, Red Army, Red Menace, Silverfish, Skinner Box, and Violet Menace. Historical sources document additional Stomp Under Foot dirt identities and identify Matt Pasquerella as the builder behind the company.

## Overdrive
- Serenity Overdrive
- Overdrive 8-Fifty
- Full Powered Halo
- Motor Honey Superdrive

## Distortion
- Halo Bender
- Full Powered Halo
- Skinner Box
- Guv'nor
- Brit Box
- Divided Drive

## Fuzz
- Alabaster
- Amherst
- Astoria
- Black Russian
- Civil Unrest
- Civil War
- Demogorgon
- Dirt Preacher
- Dirty Rooster
- Elk Super Fuzz
- Fuzz Master 3
- Green Russian
- Hellephant
- Iron Tusk
- La Scatola Nero
- Pumpkin Pi
- Ram's Head
- Red Army
- Red Menace
- Rhinosaur
- Silver Foxx
- Sonic Generator
- Sonic Warfare
- Son Of A Bee
- Supa Dirty Rooster
- The Shag MkII
- The Shag MkIII
- Tri-Muff
- Violet Menace
- Windhand Fuzz

## Product-level verification notes
- **Serenity Overdrive**: documented by the historical Stomp Under Foot catalog/product record as an overdrive identity.
- **Overdrive 8-Fifty**: current Stomp Under Foot catalog presents this as an Overdrive product. It is retained under Overdrive only.
- **Full Powered Halo**: Premier Guitar's manufacturer announcement explicitly describes one side as Overdrive and the other as Distortion. It is therefore recorded in both categories.
- **Motor Honey Superdrive**: current Stomp Under Foot catalog explicitly names the product Superdrive and places it in the drive lineup. It is retained under Overdrive rather than inferred into Distortion.
- **Halo Bender**: historical product documentation identifies it as a distortion pedal. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Skinner Box**: historical product documentation identifies it as a distortion pedal based on the original RAT. It is retained under Distortion; its ability to produce lower-gain overdrive is not used to inflate the Overdrive category.
- **Guv'nor** and **Brit Box**: current Stomp Under Foot catalog identities are retained as distortion products based on their Marshall-style drive/distortion lineage and historical product documentation; they are not duplicated into Overdrive solely because their gain controls cover lower-gain sounds.
- **Divided Drive**: current catalog identity is retained under Distortion as a dedicated drive product; no additional category is inferred without explicit manufacturer wording.
- **Hellephant**: Premier Guitar explicitly identifies the Hellephant as a silicon Fuzz Face-style fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz only.
- **Dirt Preacher**: historical product coverage identifies it as a fuzz pedal, with lower-gain overdrive available at low gain. The archive keeps it under Fuzz only to avoid category inflation.
- **Pink Hellephant** is not promoted into the core census because it was a retailer-exclusive custom collaboration for Mass Street Music rather than a normal Stomp Under Foot catalog identity.
- **Tri-Dirty Booster** is excluded because the target effect is a booster rather than a standalone Overdrive/Distortion/Fuzz identity.
- Ordinary finish variants, graphic variants, vintage-mini presentations, and retailer stock listings are not split into duplicate census identities.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- The archive follows the builder/product relationship rather than treating every retailer listing as a separate product.
- Fuzz-family products are retained under Fuzz even when their gain controls can produce overdrive or distortion-like tones.
- The archive does not infer Overdrive from the word "drive" alone when the manufacturer does not explicitly support that category.
- No unsupported historical product names are promoted merely because they appear in third-party collections.
- Non-target effects are excluded.

## Sources
1. Stomp Under Foot — official catalog/home: https://stompunderfoot.com/
2. Premier Guitar — Full Powered Halo announcement: https://www.premierguitar.com/stomp-under-foot-announces-full-powered-halo
3. Premier Guitar — Hellephant review: https://www.premierguitar.com/gear/stomp-under-foot-hellephant-review
4. Guitar Pedal X — Stomp Under Foot builder/product directory: https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/guitar-pedal-directory---favourite-pedals-by-preferred-pedal-makers
5. Effects Database — Stomp Under Foot historical product index: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model//stompunderfoot
6. Stomp Under Foot — current official catalog: https://stompunderfoot.com/

## Checkpoint
Block 057 completes the Stomp Under Foot builder census for this research pass. The verified set spans Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Full Powered Halo is explicitly both Overdrive and Distortion; Skinner Box remains Distortion despite lower-gain overdrive capability; and the large Muff/Fuzz Face/Tone Bender family remains under Fuzz without category inflation.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Research Block 058

## Builder
**Dirty Boy Pedals / Dirty Boy Co. — Alex Saraceno; revived under Danny Gomez**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources:
- Dirty Boy Co. official effects catalog
- Dirty Boy Co. official product pages
- Warm Audio official Dirty Boy Jr. product page / brand materials

Authoritative corroboration:
- Premier Guitar
- Sound On Sound

Dirty Boy's official catalog currently lists the PREAMP, AFRO FUZZ, BALL BUSTER, GERMANIUM BOY, several custom pedals, and the Dirty Boy Jr. line. The official product material explicitly identifies the target dirt functions for the PREAMP, AFRO FUZZ, and Magical Mystery Fuzz; the Warm Audio collaboration material explicitly identifies the Jr. Overdrive and Jr. Fuzz as adaptations of Dirty Boy circuits. The official Dirty Boy material identifies the original work as associated with Alex Saraceno and the revived line as developed under Danny Gomez.

## Overdrive
- PREAMP
- JUNIOR DB Overdrive / Dirty Boy Jr. Overdrive

## Distortion
- None promoted in this block

## Fuzz
- AFRO FUZZ
- The Magical MYSTERY FUZZ
- JUNIOR DB Fuzz / Dirty Boy Jr. Fuzz

## Product-level verification notes
- **PREAMP**: Dirty Boy's official product page explicitly describes it as an amplifier simulator, preamp, and "tube-sounding booster/overdrive" for an amp input. It is therefore recorded under Overdrive.
- **Dirty Boy Jr. Overdrive**: Warm Audio's official product page explicitly calls it an amp-like overdrive pedal and identifies it as a recreation of the original Dirty Boy Preamp circuit. It is recorded under Overdrive. The archive keeps the Dirty Boy/Junior relationship explicit rather than treating the collaboration as an unrelated builder.
- **AFRO FUZZ**: Dirty Boy's official catalog identifies AFRO FUZZ as a core product, and the Warm Audio collaboration documentation explicitly identifies the original Afro Fuzz circuit as the basis of the Dirty Boy Jr. Fuzz. It is recorded under Fuzz.
- **The Magical MYSTERY FUZZ**: Dirty Boy's official product page explicitly describes it as a germanium fuzz. It is recorded under Fuzz only.
- **Dirty Boy Jr. Fuzz**: Warm Audio's official collaboration materials explicitly identify it as a vintage-style fuzz built around the original Dirty Boy Afro Fuzz circuit. It is recorded under Fuzz.

## Scope decisions / exclusions
- **BALL BUSTER** is excluded because Dirty Boy describes it as a booster circuit; boost-only products are outside the target Overdrive/Distortion/Fuzz census.
- **GERMANIUM BOY** is excluded because its official product page describes it as a multi-frequency booster with a gain function rather than explicitly identifying it as Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz. Germanium clipping alone is not used to inflate the taxonomy.
- **GERMANIUM SL#T** is excluded because Dirty Boy's official page presents it as the original raw germanium booster design and does not explicitly classify it as one of the target categories.
- **SilverBOY** is excluded because the official page presents it primarily as an analog amplifier simulator/preamp and drive/boost device rather than explicitly as an Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz pedal.
- **DIRTY DOUBLE** is excluded because the official page identifies it as a dual preamp platform. Its crunch capability is not enough to infer an Overdrive category.
- Other custom product names visible in the official catalog are not promoted into the categorized census without explicit manufacturer dirt classification.
- No standalone Distortion identity is promoted in this block because the reviewed manufacturer material does not explicitly support one.

## Sources
1. Dirty Boy Co. — official effects catalog: https://www.dirtyboy.co/pages/our-effects
2. Dirty Boy Co. — official home/catalog: https://www.dirtyboy.co/
3. Dirty Boy Co. — PREAMP: https://www.dirtyboy.co/products/dirtyboy-preamp
4. Dirty Boy Co. — GERMANIUM BOY: https://www.dirtyboy.co/products/dirtyboy-germanium-boy
5. Dirty Boy Co. — The Magical MYSTERY FUZZ: https://www.dirtyboy.co/products/the-magical-mystery-fuzz
6. Dirty Boy Co. — GERMANIUM SL#T: https://www.dirtyboy.co/products/germanium-sl-t
7. Dirty Boy Co. — SilverBOY: https://www.dirtyboy.co/products/silverboy
8. Dirty Boy Co. — DIRTY DOUBLE: https://www.dirtyboy.co/products/dirty-double
9. Warm Audio — Dirty Boy Jr. Overdrive: https://warmaudio.com/dirty-boy-jr-overdrive
10. Warm Audio — official brand assets/catalog: https://warmaudio.com/brand-assets
11. Premier Guitar — Warm Audio / Dirty Boy Jr. announcement: https://www.premierguitar.com/news/warm-audio-dirty-boy-jr
12. Sound On Sound — Dirty Boy Jr. Series: https://www.soundonsound.com/news/dirty-boy-jr-series-warm-audio

## Checkpoint
Block 058 completes the Dirty Boy builder census for this research pass. The verified set spans Overdrive and Fuzz. The archive deliberately leaves Distortion empty rather than inferring it from high-gain or fuzz-soaked descriptions. Boost-only, preamp-platform, and amp-simulation identities without explicit target-category support remain outside the categorized census.

Website: untouched.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 059

## Builder
**Mojo Hand FX — founded by Brad Fee; acquired by Cusack Music in 2015 and continued under Cusack Music**

## Verification posture
This block was researched as a builder-to-pedal census, not as a history or biography entry. Product names were retained only where the manufacturer currently categorizes them as dirt or where manufacturer-originated historical material / authoritative historical documentation supports the dirt identity.

Primary manufacturer sources were prioritized. Mojo Hand FX's official About page states that the brand began in 2004 as the pedal custom shop of Tone Factor, that Brad Fee evolved it into Mojo Hand FX around 2008, and that Brad sold Mojo Hand to Cusack Music in 2015. The same page says Mojo Hand pedals are designed and built in Holland, Michigan under the current operation.

Official About:
- https://mojohandfx.com/about-us/

Official current catalog:
- https://mojohandfx.com/pedals/
- https://mojohandfx.com/all-pedals/
- https://mojohandfx.com/fuzz/
- https://mojohandfx.com/discontinued/

## Overdrive

- **Rook**
- **Rook Royale**
- **DMBL**
- **Extra Special**
- **Magpie**
- **Sacred Cow**
- **Odessa**
- **Rock City**
- **Sericon**
- **Kensrue Rook**
- **BlueBonnet**
- **Superlative** (historical/discontinued)

### Overdrive verification
- Rook: current manufacturer page explicitly calls it “Classic Overdrive” and describes classic overdrive, LED/raw drive, and boost-style operation. Historical manufacturer-originated text is also preserved in Effects Database.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/pedals/
  - https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/mojohand/rook
- Rook Royale: current manufacturer catalog explicitly labels it “Overdrive and Boost.”
  - https://mojohandfx.com/pedals/
- DMBL: current manufacturer catalog explicitly labels it “Holy Grail Amp Overdrive.”
  - https://mojohandfx.com/pedals/
- Extra Special: official product page describes it as the higher-gain development of the DMBL and places it in the medium-to-higher-gain drive family. It is retained as an Overdrive identity, not promoted to Distortion solely because of its gain range.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/extra-special/
- Magpie: official product page explicitly calls it a classic British-style overdrive and labels its low-gain setting “Overdrive.”
  - https://mojohandfx.com/magpie/
- Sacred Cow: official catalog labels it “Professional Gold Overdrive”; manufacturer-originated product copy explicitly describes overdrive behavior.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/pedals/
  - https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/mojohand/sacredcow
- Odessa: official product page explicitly labels it “Dual Overdrive” and states that it contains Rook and Magpie overdrive circuits.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/odessa/
- Rock City: official product page states it began as an improved Odessa dual overdrive and describes both internal circuits as overdrive circuits.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/rock-city/
- Sericon: manufacturer-originated release material calls it an overdrive and describes multiple gain stages from clean boost through edge-of-breakup to high-gain drive.
  - https://www.premierguitar.com/news/mojo-hand-fx-sericonoverdrive
  - https://fulleffectdistro.com/sericon/
- Kensrue Rook: official product page explicitly calls it Dustin Kensrue's Signature Overdrive and describes the Rook as a classic overdrive.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/kensrue-rook/
- BlueBonnet: authoritative historical Effects Database entry identifies the BlueBonnet as an overdrive pedal; the product existed as a Mojo Hand FX dirt identity before the current catalog was consolidated.
  - https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/mojohand/bluebonnet
- Superlative: Mojo Hand FX's official discontinued-products index identifies Superlative as “Vintage Overdrive.”
  - https://mojohandfx.com/discontinued/

## Distortion

- **Socrates**
- **Magistrate**

### Distortion verification
- Socrates: manufacturer-originated historical product copy explicitly calls it “Socrates Classic Distortion” and describes it as an LM308-style distortion with symmetrical/asymmetrical clipping. Effects Database preserves the original product description and categorizes it as distortion.
  - https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/mojohand/socrates
  - https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r0fsSYqvbDE
- Magistrate: authoritative historical product documentation identifies the Magistrate as a high-gain distortion with a three-band EQ. Barefoot Buttons also records the product specifically as “Mojo Hand FX Magistrate Distortion.”
  - https://reverb.com/p/mojo-hand-fx-magistrate
  - https://barefootbuttons.com/pedal-compatibility-list/

### Distortion taxonomy note
Do **not** promote every Mojo Hand pedal that can produce aggressive saturation or “almost distortion” into the Distortion column. In particular, Rook remains Overdrive despite its manufacturer describing one setting as “almost distortion type drive,” and El Guapo remains Fuzz despite its ability to be dialed down toward almost overdrive. The archive records the manufacturer's named/category identity rather than inferring a new category from extreme settings.

## Fuzz

- **Iron Bell**
- **Crosstown**
- **Crosstown Rasta**
- **Colossus**
- **1979**
- **1979 Baja**
- **One Ton Bee**
- **El Guapo**
- **Zephyr** (historical/discontinued)
- **Rounder** (historical/discontinued)
- **Pompeii** (historical/limited)

### Fuzz verification
- Iron Bell: current manufacturer catalog explicitly labels it “Gilmour Fuzz.”
  - https://mojohandfx.com/pedals/
- Crosstown: current manufacturer catalog explicitly labels it “Classic Germanium/Silicon Fuzz.”
  - https://mojohandfx.com/fuzz/
- Crosstown Rasta: current manufacturer catalog lists it in the Mojo Hand fuzz catalog; the product is a Crosstown fuzz identity variant and is retained as Fuzz rather than treated as a separate non-dirt effect.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/fuzz/
- Colossus: official product page explicitly calls it a Russian/Civil War Muff-style fuzz.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/colossus/
- 1979: official product page explicitly calls it “OpAmp Fuzz” and describes the fuzz circuit and tone-bypass controls.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/1979/
- 1979 Baja: current manufacturer catalog explicitly labels it “OpAmp Fuzz.”
  - https://mojohandfx.com/fuzz/
- One Ton Bee: current manufacturer catalog explicitly labels it “60s Style Fuzz.”
  - https://mojohandfx.com/fuzz/
- El Guapo: official product page explicitly calls it a fuzz machine and identifies it as voltage-starved fuzz. The ability to reach almost-overdrive settings does not change its archive category.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/el-guapo/
- Zephyr: manufacturer-originated historical product copy calls it “Zephyr Fuzz” and describes it as a fuzz circuit based on the Sam Ash Fuzz/AstroTone family.
  - https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/mojohand/zephyr
  - https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wAXyTYR5xhA
- Rounder: official Mojo Hand discontinued page identifies the pedal as a discontinued product, while the manufacturer-originated product description explicitly calls it a fuzz inspired by the Burns Buzzaround.
  - https://mojohandfx.com/discontinued/
  - https://mojohandfx.com/rounder-a-nod-to-a-rare-fuzz/
- Pompeii: authoritative product documentation identifies it as a Mojo Hand FX fuzz and places it in the limited Future Vintage Series.
  - https://www.mojopeppasauce.ca/products/mojo-hand-fx-pompeii-fuzz-pedal

## Explicit exclusions / non-promotions

- **Speakeasy** — preamp/boost; excluded from the dirt census because the manufacturer does not explicitly categorize it as Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.
- **Balloon / booster-type products and utility effects** — excluded where they are not explicitly target dirt categories.
- **Little Wonder, Mr O, Bayou, RVT, Swim Team, Dream Mender, Octaverse, SMPLoFi, Clarity, etc.** — non-target effects; excluded.
- **Rook** is not duplicated into Distortion just because the manufacturer describes one clipping mode as almost distortion.
- **El Guapo** is not duplicated into Overdrive just because the manufacturer says it can be dialed to “almost” overdrive.
- **1979** is retained as Fuzz because the current manufacturer page explicitly identifies the product as an OpAmp Fuzz, even though the description discusses grungy distortion.
- **Variant/finish-only versions** such as B-stock, Silver Modded, Goodbye Blue, and Limited Edition Colors are not split into separate builder/product identities unless the product is a distinct named circuit identity.

## Builder relationship conclusion
Mojo Hand FX is a confirmed builder/brand relationship for the archive. The official company history establishes Brad Fee as the founder/operator who evolved the Tone Factor custom shop into Mojo Hand FX, and establishes Cusack Music's acquisition of the brand in 2015. Current Mojo Hand FX material states that the brand continues under Cusack Music in Holland, Michigan.

The archive therefore records the builder as:

> **Mojo Hand FX — founded by Brad Fee; acquired by Cusack Music in 2015 and continued under Cusack Music**

## Sources used

### Primary / manufacturer

- https://mojohandfx.com/pedals/

- https://mojohandfx.com/fuzz/

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 060

## Builder
**Prescription Electronics — Jack Brossart / Prescription Electronics Inc.**

Prescription Electronics was founded in Portland, Oregon in 1993 by Jack Brossart. The company later closed, returned with reissues in 2019, and then disappeared again after Brossart's death that same year. This block treats Prescription Electronics as one builder identity and preserves documented historical/reissue product identities without splitting ordinary revisions into separate builders.

## Sources reviewed
- Prescription Electronics brand/product history: https://www.prescriptionpedals.com/ (official site referenced by manufacturer-era and reissue coverage; current site was not retrievable through the available web index during this block)
- Guitar.com, "Prescription Electronics returns, re-issues original pedals from 90s" (2019): https://guitar.com/news/gear-news/prescription-electronics-returns-re-issues-original-pedals-from-90s/
- Effects Database Prescription Electronics catalog: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/prescription
- Effects Database RX Overdriver: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/prescription/rxoverdriver
- Effects Database Experience: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/prescription/experience
- Effects Database Yardbox: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/prescription/yardbox
- Effects Database Face Lift: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/prescription/facelift
- Effects Database Depth Charge: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/prescription/depthcharge
- Jedistar Prescription Electronics historical catalog: https://jedistar.com/prescription-electronics/
- Aion FX Obscura Octave Blend documentation, identifying the Prescription Electronics C.O.B. and builder history: https://aionfx.com/app/files/docs/obscura_documentation.pdf
- Premier Guitar C.O.B. review: https://www.premierguitar.com/gear/quick-hit-prescription-electronics-c-o-b-review
- Reverb historical/product corroboration for Prescription Electronics Dual Tone, C.O.B., Face Lift, Experience, and Supernatural.

## Overdrive
- RX Overdriver
- Germ
- Yardbox
- Dual-Tone

### Overdrive verification
- **RX Overdriver:** Effects Database explicitly categorizes it as overdrive and describes it as a sonic reproduction of the Colorsound Overdriver, ranging from clean boost to full-bore distortion. The archive keeps the builder's primary identity as Overdrive rather than converting its maximum-gain behavior into a second category.
- **Germ:** historical catalog material describes a clean-boost side and a crunch side with "fat distortion." Contemporary user documentation also describes the overdrive side. The archive retains Germ under Overdrive and Distortion because the product explicitly spans both drive modes.
- **Yardbox:** the 2019 reissue coverage describes a Tone Bender-derived fuzz that can produce a thin fuzz/OD sound at reduced volume and saturated germanium fuzz at higher settings. Because the builder-era description explicitly uses both OD and fuzz language, it is recorded in both categories.
- **Dual-Tone:** historical Prescription Electronics material describes the pedal as an overdrive/distortion pedal with two preset gain levels. It is therefore recorded in both categories.

## Distortion
- Germ
- Dual-Tone

### Distortion verification
- **Germ:** historical catalog description explicitly calls the crunch side a fat distortion.
- **Dual-Tone:** historical manufacturer wording identifies it directly as an overdrive/distortion pedal, with a lower-gain rhythm mode and higher-gain lead mode.

The archive does **not** promote Face Lift into Distortion solely because a third-party listing uses a broader "distortion" category. Its manufacturer-linked documentation consistently identifies the core effect as fuzz plus octave.

## Fuzz
- Experience
- C.O.B. (Clean Octave Blend)
- Yardbox
- Face Lift
- Frantic Fuzz
- Supernatural
- Depth Charge

### Fuzz verification
- **Experience:** manufacturer-era/reissue coverage explicitly identifies it as an octave fuzz with fuzz, octave, and swell functions. Effects Database also categorizes it as octave fuzz.
- **C.O.B. (Clean Octave Blend):** Prescription Electronics product documentation and later manufacturer/reissue coverage identify it as an octave-fuzz pedal with clean blend. Premier Guitar explicitly calls it an octave-fuzz pedal. Aion FX documentation independently identifies the original C.O.B. as an octave fuzz.
- **Yardbox:** the 2019 reissue coverage describes saturated germanium fuzz and identifies its Tone Bender basis. Effects Database categorizes it as fuzz. It is also retained under Overdrive because the reissue description explicitly documents a fuzz/OD operating range.
- **Face Lift:** Effects Database identifies the product as an octave fuzz and documents silicon/germanium Fuzz Face-style versions. Third-party product documentation consistently describes it as fuzz plus octave. Variants are consolidated into one Face Lift identity.
- **Frantic Fuzz:** retained as a historical Prescription Electronics fuzz identity in the Effects Database catalog. No separate variant entries are created.
- **Supernatural:** historical Prescription Electronics product material identifies it as a germanium fuzz. The archive records the product as Fuzz only.
- **Depth Charge:** historical Prescription Electronics material identifies it as a bass fuzz with a clean blend and gain control; later documentation and user-facing product references consistently identify it as a bass fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz only.

## Exclusions / conservative decisions
- **Vibe-Unit:** excluded because it is a Uni-Vibe/modulation product, not a target dirt category.
- **Throb:** excluded because it is tremolo.
- **C.O.B. octave-only behavior:** not split into a separate octave category; the target taxonomy is Builder → Overdrive / Distortion / Fuzz.
- **Germ boost-only behavior:** not split into Boost; only its documented dirt relationship is retained.
- **Outbox:** excluded because the reviewed historical material describes it as a multi-effect/filtering/switching module rather than a clearly documented standalone OD, distortion, or fuzz identity.
- **Static:** excluded because the available catalog evidence did not provide sufficiently clear target-taxonomy support during this block.
- Ordinary electrical/finish/reissue revisions are consolidated under the same pedal identity.
- No pedal is promoted into a target category merely because a third party describes it as "gainy," "saturated," or "distortion-like" without adequate product-level support.

## Builder → Pedal relationship summary
Prescription Electronics →
- Overdrive: RX Overdriver; Germ; Yardbox; Dual-Tone
- Distortion: Germ; Dual-Tone
- Fuzz: Experience; C.O.B. (Clean Octave Blend); Yardbox; Face Lift; Frantic Fuzz; Supernatural; Depth Charge

## Verification posture
This block deliberately favors manufacturer-era descriptions and authoritative product archives over marketplace terminology. The current official Prescription Electronics site could not be retrieved through the available web index in this research pass, so current/reissue claims were cross-checked against the 2019 reissue report, archived product catalogs, Effects Database, Aion FX documentation, Premier Guitar, and historical product listings. No unsupported standalone distortion identity was added for the fuzz-only products.

# Block 061 — Skreddy Pedals — Marc Ahlfs

## Builder
**Skreddy Pedals — Marc Ahlfs**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources reviewed:
- Skreddy Pedals About page
- Skreddy Pedals current Overdrive category
- Skreddy Pedals current Fuzz category
- Skreddy Pedals current all-products index
- Skreddy Pedals Big Muff variants index
- Skreddy Pedals ToneBender variants index
- individual manufacturer product pages/manuals for selected overlapping or ambiguous dirt identities

The manufacturer identifies Skreddy Pedals as Marc Ahlfs, with Cynthia Ahlfs and occasional helpers, and says they have been designing and building guitar effects since 2004. The current manufacturer taxonomy lists 13 Overdrive products and 33 Fuzz products. The site does not maintain a separate current Distortion category, so Distortion entries below are included only where the manufacturer's own product/index/manual language explicitly identifies the pedal as distortion rather than merely describing high gain, clipping, or fuzz-like behavior.

## Overdrive
- Hybrid Overdrive
- Major Overdrive
- Skunk Drive
- Cosmos
- Serenity Fuzzdrive
- Klipper
- High Gear
- Rubber Soul
- Super 100
- Twangophile
- Lunar Module Mini Deluxe
- Screw Driver Mini Dlx
- Hybrid Fuzz Driver

The current manufacturer Overdrive category explicitly lists these 13 identities. The Skunk Drive manual further describes it as an overdrive and the product page calls it vintage cutting fuzz/overdrive. Serenity's manufacturer page explicitly describes its range from cleanish boost through overdrive to fuzz and identifies it as overdrive/distortion/fuzz. Hybrid Fuzz Driver is explicitly described by the manufacturer as boost, overdrive, and light fuzz. Lunar Module Mini Deluxe is included because the current manufacturer Overdrive category lists it there even though its product description emphasizes silicon fuzz.

## Distortion
- Serenity Fuzzdrive
- ROVER Fuzz
- Top Fuel
- Supa Tone
- Cognitive Dissonance MkIV
- Perestroika
- 1971
- Pink Flesh

Verification notes:
- Serenity Fuzzdrive: manufacturer explicitly calls it Class A overdrive / distortion / fuzz.
- ROVER Fuzz: manufacturer describes the pedal as capable of meaty distortion and hot overdrive as well as fuzz.
- Top Fuel: manufacturer index calls it distortion with hot mids; the official manual describes its sustain control as controlling the intensity of the distortion.
- Supa Tone: manufacturer index explicitly describes it as grinding distortion.
- Cognitive Dissonance MkIV: manufacturer index explicitly describes sustain/distortion.
- Perestroika: manufacturer index explicitly describes early-90s Russian muffy distortion.
- 1971: manufacturer index explicitly describes vintage amp-like lower-gain muffy distortion.
- Pink Flesh: manufacturer index explicitly describes smooth, intense, sustaining distortion.

Conservative exclusions from Distortion:
- ZERO is not promoted as Distortion solely from its manual's references to distortion character. Its current manufacturer product identity is Fuzz, and the manual's primary description is raw/aggressive/sustaining fuzz.
- Rust Rod, Mayo MkIII, Mayonaise MkIII, BC109 Fuzz, BC239 Fuzz, etc. remain Fuzz unless the manufacturer explicitly presents the identity as a distortion product rather than describing distortion behavior within a fuzz circuit.

## Fuzz
Current manufacturer material explicitly identifies the following fuzz identities:
- ROVER Fuzz
- Skunk Drive
- Cosmos
- Juicy Pig
- P19
- 1966 Modernized
- Martian Tarantula
- General Pig
- Serenity Fuzzdrive
- Angel Face
- Klipper
- Cephalopod II
- BC109 Fuzz
- BC239 Fuzz
- Supa Tone
- Pig Mine
- Cognitive Dissonance MkIV
- Lunar Module Mini Deluxe
- Hybrid Fuzz Driver
- Rust Rod
- Top Fuel
- Perestroika
- Giant Meat Pie BC239
- 1971
- ? Lady
- Ernie
- Pink Flesh
- Mayo MkIII
- ZERO
- Mayonaise MkIII
- Dragon Fuzz
- Kusanagi

The manufacturer currently states that its Fuzz category contains 33 products and groups them into Big Muff variants, Fuzz Face variants/derivatives, and ToneBender variants. The current category and all-products index directly expose the names above. Product pages/manuals were used to avoid converting non-dirt products into fuzz entries. Cephalopod II is explicitly an octave-up fuzz, and Kusanagi is explicitly presented in the manufacturer index as a MkII Tone Bender build. The manufacturer also explicitly identifies Dragon Fuzz as a silicon Fuzz Face and the current store identifies Mayo MkIII, Mayonaise MkIII, Giant Meat Pie BC239, etc. as fuzz products.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Serenity Fuzzdrive**: Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Manufacturer explicitly supports all three.
- **ROVER Fuzz**: Fuzz + Distortion. The manufacturer also describes hot overdrive capability in the product text, but the current product identity is Fuzz and the current Overdrive category does not list ROVER, so it is not promoted into the archive's Overdrive category.
- **Skunk Drive**: Overdrive + Fuzz. The current manufacturer category places it in Overdrive and Fuzz, while the manual says it is definitely in the overdrive camp.
- **Cosmos**: Overdrive + Fuzz. The manufacturer places it in both categories and calls it a Fuzz-Tone sound in an overdrive.
- **Klipper**: Overdrive + Fuzz. The current manufacturer categories place it in both.
- **Lunar Module Mini Deluxe**: Overdrive + Fuzz. Both current manufacturer categories include it.
- **Hybrid Fuzz Driver**: Overdrive + Fuzz. The manufacturer explicitly calls it boost, overdrive, and light fuzz.
- **Supa Tone** and **Cognitive Dissonance MkIV**: Fuzz + Distortion because the manufacturer explicitly labels the products as fuzz-family products while also describing them as distortion.
- **Top Fuel**, **Perestroika**, **1971**, **Pink Flesh**: Fuzz + Distortion because the manufacturer lists them in the Big Muff/Fuzz family and explicitly describes their sounds as distortion.

## Exclusions
- LZ-129: boost-only/dirty boost, not promoted into the target dirt taxonomy.
- Dynamic Mids Enhancer: preamp/clean-gain utility, not promoted as Overdrive/Distortion/Fuzz.
- Direct In: analog amp emulator, not promoted as a dirt pedal.
- Dual Loop: switching utility.
- Echo, Echo Mini, Echo Infinity, New Echo Vintage: delay products.
- Little Miss Sunshine, Swirl: modulation products.
- Galaxy Cluster: multi-effect/custom platform, not split into duplicate dirt identities here.
- Non-dirt utility products are excluded from the builder→dirt census.

## Verification notes
- Builder identity is directly supported by Skreddy Pedals' About page.
- Current product taxonomy is directly supported by the manufacturer's Overdrive, Fuzz, and all-products pages.
- Distortion relationships were treated more conservatively because the current site does not expose a separate Distortion category.
- The archive uses product identity/category relationships rather than inferring a category from generic words such as "gain," "saturation," "crunch," or "clipping."
- Revisions and cosmetic versions are not split into separate builder identities unless the manufacturer presents them as distinct named products.

## Primary source URLs
- https://www.skreddypedals.com/about

# Block 062 — Keeley Electronics — Robert Keeley

## Builder
**Keeley Electronics — Robert Keeley**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources reviewed:
- Keeley Electronics About page
- current Keeley Electronics shop/product index
- current manufacturer Distortion, Drive, Boost & Fuzz category
- individual manufacturer product pages for selected current and legacy/archived dirt identities
- manufacturer archive pages where needed to distinguish a named historical product from a cosmetic/technical variation

Keeley Electronics' own About page identifies Robert Keeley and says the company began with him and grew into its current Oklahoma City operation. The current manufacturer shop explicitly separates the target dirt bucket as “Distortion, Drive, Boost & Fuzz” and exposes multiple named drive/fuzz products.

This block records builder/product relationships only. Current products are taken from the manufacturer's current shop and product pages; historical identities are included only when a named Keeley product is explicitly documented by the manufacturer. Cosmetic editions, numbered finishes, B-stock pages, and substantially identical circuit variations are consolidated rather than split into duplicate pedal identities.

## Overdrive
Current or clearly documented named identities:
- Tube Drive Twin Triode Dual Overdrive
- Red Nectar Compressor and Overdrive
- Manis Overdrive
- Mk3 Driver — Andy Timmons Full Range Overdrive
- Noble Screamer Overdrive and Boost
- Blues Disorder Overdrive and Distortion
- Super Rodent Overdrive and Distortion
- SUPER BASS Overdrive and Distortion
- D&M Drive
- DDR — Drive Delay Reverb
- DCR Drive Chorus Rotary
- Aria Compressor Drive
- El Rey Dorado
- Super Phat Mod
- Keeley Oxblood Overdrive
- Red Dirt Overdrive
- Red Dirt Germanium Overdrive
- 1962 British Overdrive
- 1962X 2-Mode Limited British Overdrive
- Sweet Spot Johnny Hiland Super Drive Overdrive
- Katana Blues Drive
- Drive For a Cure

Verification notes:
- Tube Drive is explicitly a dual overdrive on the current manufacturer site; the 12AT7WA RKFX.com edition is treated as a Tube Drive variant, not a separate pedal identity.
- Red Nectar, Manis, Mk3 Driver, Noble Screamer, Blues Disorder, Super Rodent, SUPER BASS, D&M Drive, DDR, DCR, Aria, El Rey Dorado, Oxblood, Red Dirt, Red Dirt Germanium, 1962, 1962X, Sweet Spot, Katana Blues Drive, and Drive For a Cure are explicitly described by the manufacturer as overdrive/drive products or as products containing an overdrive/drive section.
- The manufacturer describes the DDR drive section as two overdrive tones and describes DCR as a saturated tube-gain drive with crunch/lead profiles.
- The manufacturer explicitly describes Aria as compressor + overdrive and the D&M as Drive + Boost.

## Distortion
Explicit manufacturer-supported identities:
- SUPER BASS Overdrive and Distortion
- Blues Disorder Overdrive and Distortion
- Super Rodent Overdrive and Distortion
- Angry Orange Distortion and Fuzz
- OCTA PSI Transfigurating Fuzz
- Filaments High Gain Distortion
- Noble Screamer Overdrive and Boost
- Synth-1 Reverse Attack Fuzz Wave Generator

Verification notes:
- Blues Disorder explicitly contains both hard-clipping distortion and soft-clipping overdrive.
- Super Rodent explicitly combines an overdrive circuit and a distortion circuit, plus two hybrid combinations.
- SUPER BASS is explicitly labeled Overdrive and Distortion by the manufacturer.
- Angry Orange is identified by the manufacturer as Distortion and Fuzz in the current shop/product catalog.
- OCTA PSI's manufacturer specification explicitly lists Fuzz and Distortion among its effects.
- Filaments is explicitly a high-gain distortion pedal.
- Noble Screamer includes two amp-like distortion tones in addition to its overdrive/boost functions. It is retained in both categories because the manufacturer explicitly describes both behaviors.
- Synth-1 is a fuzz/synth product whose manufacturer title and product description explicitly use both distortion and fuzz language; it remains a conservative multi-effect dirt inclusion rather than a standalone conventional distortion design.

Conservative exclusions:
- Oxblood is not separately promoted into Distortion solely because its product page says the drive control can produce distortion; the named product identity remains Overdrive.
- DCR, DDR, Aria, El Rey Dorado, Red Dirt, 1962, and Sweet Spot remain Overdrive rather than being promoted into Distortion merely from generic saturation/crunch language.

## Fuzz
Explicit manufacturer-supported identities:
- OCTA PSI Transfigurating Fuzz
- Angry Orange Distortion and Fuzz
- SUNS Ultravoid Fuzz
- Fuzz Bender
- Moon Op Amp Fuzz
- Loomer
- Dark Side
- Monterey Rotary Fuzz Vibe
- Synth-1 Reverse Attack Fuzz Wave Generator
- Mk3 Driver — Andy Timmons Full Range Overdrive
- Germanium Super Phat Mod
- Fuzz Head

Verification notes:
- OCTA PSI is explicitly an analog transistor fuzz with octave/pitch functions and is also specified as including distortion.
- Angry Orange is explicitly identified by the manufacturer as Distortion and Fuzz.
- SUNS is explicitly identified as a fuzz product in the current manufacturer shop/catalog.
- Fuzz Bender is a three-transistor hybrid fuzz with silicon and germanium fuzz stages.
- Moon Op Amp Fuzz is explicitly described as a fuzz and as four gain/fuzz stages.
- Loomer is explicitly a “Wall-of-Fuzz” plus reverb workstation; the manufacturer repeatedly identifies its dirt section as fuzz.
- Dark Side explicitly includes a fuzz section and is categorized by the manufacturer in the dirt family.
- Monterey is explicitly a Rotary Fuzz Vibe with a dedicated vintage fuzz channel.
- Synth-1 is explicitly described as creating fuzzed-out synth waves.
- Mk3 Driver remains primarily an Overdrive identity, but the manufacturer explicitly documents a clean-boost-to-square-wave-fuzz range and a germanium fuzz generator, so it is retained as an overlap identity rather than inferred from generic gain language.
- Germanium Super Phat Mod is retained as a named dirt identity because the manufacturer explicitly documents a Germanium Fuzz added to the JFET overdrive design. This is a functional product identity, not a cosmetic finish.
- Fuzz Head is explicitly described by Keeley as a fuzz/overdrive design and is retained as its own underlying pedal identity; color-specific Red/Blue pages are not split.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Noble Screamer**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer describes it as a 4-in-1 overdrive/boost with two new amp-like distortion tones.
- **Blues Disorder**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer explicitly describes soft-clipping overdrive and hard-clipping distortion.
- **Super Rodent**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer explicitly combines classic overdrive and distortion circuits plus hybrid modes.
- **SUPER BASS**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer product identity explicitly uses both.
- **Angry Orange**: Distortion + Fuzz. Current manufacturer shop/product identity explicitly uses both.
- **OCTA PSI**: Fuzz + Distortion. Manufacturer explicitly specifies both fuzz and distortion effects.
- **Mk3 Driver**: Overdrive + Fuzz. Manufacturer's primary product identity is Full Range Overdrive, but its own feature text explicitly reaches square-wave fuzz and calls the circuit a germanium fuzz generator.
- **Synth-1**: Fuzz + Distortion. Manufacturer's title and description explicitly support both terms, while the product is fundamentally a synth/fuzz multi-effect.
- **Germanium Super Phat Mod**: Overdrive + Fuzz. Manufacturer explicitly describes the original JFET overdrive plus an added Germanium Fuzz stage.

## Historical / archive handling
The following are retained as named underlying products where manufacturer pages document them:
- Red Dirt Overdrive
- Red Dirt Germanium Overdrive
- 1962 British Overdrive
- 1962X 2-Mode Limited British Overdrive
- Sweet Spot Johnny Hiland Super Drive Overdrive
- Katana Blues Drive
- Drive For a Cure
- Fuzz Head
- Germanium Super Phat Mod

The following are not split into additional catalog identities:
- Tube Drive 12AT7WA RKFX.com Exclusive → consolidated under Tube Drive
- Custom Shop colorways/limited finishes → consolidated under the underlying pedal
- Fuzz Bender Army, Ghost Prism, Tuxedo, Bubblegum/Surf Green, and 20 for 20 editions → consolidated under Fuzz Bender
- Moon Op Amp Fuzz ‘Lunatrip’ edition → consolidated under Moon Op Amp Fuzz
- Red Dirt Professional, KE-808, and GE-808 limited variants are treated as Red Dirt-family variants rather than independent base identities unless a future archive pass establishes them as materially distinct product models
- Brad’s Batch and similar Custom Shop drops are not treated as new pedal identities when they merely re-finish existing Keeley designs

## Exclusions
- Katana Clean Boost / Mini Katana / Time Machine Boost / K201 JFET Booster: boost/preamp identities; the manufacturer explicitly distinguishes the Time Machine Boost from overdrive/distortion.
- Compressor Plus / Compressor Mini / Compressor Pro / GC-2 / Bassist: compressor/limiter products, not target dirt identities.
- Halo, ECCOS, Caverns, Hydra, Nocturne, Magnetic Echo, Memphis Sun, and other delay/reverb products: non-dirt effects.
- Seafoam Plus, DynaTrem, Bubble Tron, Rotary, Vibe-O-Verb, and other modulation-first products: excluded unless an explicit dirt identity is present.
- Repair/restoration services, cables, apparel, gift cards, and accessories: excluded.
- Cosmetic Custom Shop editions and B-stock listings: not separate builder/product identities.

## Primary source URLs
- https://robertkeeley.com/about-keeley-electronics/
- https://robertkeeley.com/shop-keeley-guitar-pedals/
- https://robertkeeley.com/product-category/distortion-drive-boost-fuzz/
- https://robertkeeley.com/product/tube-drive-twin-triode-dual-overdrive/
- https://robertkeeley.com/product/mk3-driver-andy-timmons-full-range-overdrive/
- https://robertkeeley.com/product/noble-screamer-overdrive-and-boost/
- https://robertkeeley.com/product/blues-disorder-overdrive-and-distortion/

- https://robertkeeley.com/product/super-bass-overdrive-and-distortion/
- https://robertkeeley.com/product/suns-ultravoid-fuzz/
- https://robertkeeley.com/product/octa-psi-transfigurating-fuzz/
- https://robertkeeley.com/product/fuzz-bender/

# Block 063 — Wampler Pedals — Brian Wampler

## Builder
**Wampler Pedals — Brian Wampler**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources reviewed:
- Wampler Pedals About page
- current Distortion / Overdrive catalog
- current Overdrive tag index
- current Distortion tag index
- current Fuzz catalog
- official Downloads / manuals archive
- individual manufacturer product pages and manuals for current, discontinued, and category-overlap identities

Wampler's manufacturer About page identifies Brian Wampler as founder, CEO, and chief engineer and says Wampler produces overdrive, distortion, and fuzz pedals. The current manufacturer site maintains separate Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz product groupings and an official archive of discontinued product manuals.

This block records builder/product relationships only. Current products are taken from current manufacturer category/tag pages and individual product pages. Historical identities are retained only when manufacturer-originated pages or manuals explicitly identify the product as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz. Cosmetic editions, Halloween/limited finishes, and substantially identical revisions are consolidated rather than split into duplicate pedal identities.

## Overdrive
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Golden Jubilee
- Brent Mason: ReWired
- Germanium Tumnus Deluxe
- Mofetta
- Moxie
- Belle Overdrive
- Pantheon Deluxe
- Ratsbane
- Andy Wood: Gearbox
- Germanium Tumnus
- Plexi Drive Mini
- Triumph Overdrive
- Pantheon Overdrive
- Tumnus
- Brad Paisley: Paisley Deluxe
- Euphoria Overdrive
- Tom Quayle: Dual Fusion
- Plexi Drive Deluxe
- Brad Paisley: Paisley Drive

Historical / discontinued manufacturer-supported identities:
- Black '65
- Clarksdale Overdrive
- Hot Wired
- Low Blow Bass Overdrive
- Plexi-Drive
- Thirty Something
- Tweed '57
- UnderDog

Verification notes:
- Golden Jubilee is a current combined Overdrive / Distortion product with dedicated OD and DS channels.
- Brent Mason: ReWired contains separate Overdrive and Distortion circuits and is tagged for both categories.
- Mofetta is explicitly presented as an overdrive / distortion pedal and is included in both categories.
- Pantheon Deluxe is explicitly described by the manufacturer as covering creamy gain, gritty overdrive, and heavy distortion, so it is retained in both categories.
- Ratsbane is a current Distortion / Overdrive + Fuzz identity and is retained in all three target categories because the manufacturer explicitly documents the full range from light overdrive through saturated fuzz and categorizes it in the dirt family.
- Andy Wood: Gearbox has a stand-alone overdrive channel and a Pinnacle-derived distortion channel, so it is retained in both categories.
- Plexi Drive Mini is currently exposed in the manufacturer's Overdrive and Distortion indexes and is retained in both categories.
- Brad Paisley: Paisley Deluxe is treated as Overdrive only despite a current combined-category placement because the manufacturer's product description and manual identify its two channels as overdrive tones, including a v2 UnderDog channel.
- Black '65 and Tweed '57 are retained as Overdrive because manufacturer manuals identify them as vintage OD products.
- Clarksdale is retained as Overdrive from the manufacturer's discontinued product/manual material.
- Hot Wired is retained as a historical two-channel dirt identity because Wampler's v2 documentation identifies the Hot Wired lineage as a two-in-one Overdrive / Distortion design; the archive does not split the v1/v2 family into unnecessary duplicate entries.
- Low Blow is explicitly documented by the manufacturer as Bass Overdrive / Distortion / Notch Filter and is retained in both dirt categories.
- Plexi-Drive is explicitly documented by the manufacturer as a British Overdrive.
- Thirty Something is explicitly documented by the manufacturer as Overdrive.
- UnderDog is explicitly documented by the manufacturer as Overdrive.

## Distortion
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Golden Jubilee
- the Compulsion Drive (tcd)
- Brent Mason: ReWired
- Mofetta
- Pantheon Deluxe
- Ratsbane
- Andy Wood: Gearbox
- Plexi Drive Mini
- Phenom Distortion
- Pinnacle Deluxe v2
- Dracarys Distortion

Historical / discontinued manufacturer-supported identities:
- cataPulp
- Hot Wired
- Low Blow Bass Overdrive
- Pinnacle Standard
- Plextortion
- SLOstortion
- Sovereign Distortion
- Triple Wreck

Verification notes:
- the Compulsion Drive (tcd) is tagged by the manufacturer as Distortion and its product description emphasizes distortion.
- Golden Jubilee, Brent Mason: ReWired, Mofetta, Ratsbane, Andy Wood: Gearbox, and Plexi Drive Mini are all exposed by the current manufacturer as distortion-category identities.
- Pantheon Deluxe is retained in Distortion because the manufacturer explicitly describes its heavy-distortion range even though it is not used to create a separate distortion-only identity.
- Pinnacle Deluxe v2 is retained as Distortion because the manufacturer explicitly tags it as distortion and describes its range from amp overdrive to screaming metal.
- cataPulp is explicitly titled by Wampler as British Distortion.
- Pinnacle Standard is a manufacturer-supported discontinued distortion identity. Its product description mentions overdrive capability, but the named product identity remains Distortion.
- Plextortion is explicitly titled by the manufacturer as Distortion.
- SLOstortion is explicitly titled by the manufacturer as American High Gain Distortion; its crunch/overdrive control does not create a separate Overdrive identity.
- Sovereign Distortion is a manufacturer-supported discontinued Distortion identity.
- Triple Wreck is explicitly documented as Modern Rectified Distortion.

## Fuzz
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Cryptid Fuzz
- Ratsbane
- Velvet Fuzz

Historical / discontinued manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fuzztration
- Leviathan Fuzz
- Triple Wreck

Verification notes:
- Cryptid Fuzz and Velvet Fuzz are current manufacturer Fuzz products.
- Ratsbane is explicitly categorized by the manufacturer under Fuzz as well as Distortion / Overdrive and is retained in all three target categories.
- Fuzztration is a manufacturer-supported discontinued fuzz/octave identity and is retained as Fuzz.
- Leviathan Fuzz is explicitly identified by the manufacturer as a fuzz product. Its internal controls reach aggressive/distortion-like sounds, but it is not promoted into Distortion without an explicit product-level classification.
- Triple Wreck is primarily a distortion identity, but the manufacturer's official manual explicitly describes its Boost mode as producing a crushing wall of fuzz / fuzz monster behavior, so it is retained as Distortion + Fuzz rather than creating an unsupported standalone Fuzz identity.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Golden Jubilee**: Overdrive + Distortion. Dedicated OD and DS channels are explicitly documented.
- **Brent Mason: ReWired**: Overdrive + Distortion. The two channels are separately identified as Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Mofetta**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer explicitly presents it as overdrive / distortion.
- **Pantheon Deluxe**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer explicitly documents gritty overdrive and heavy distortion.
- **Ratsbane**: Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Current manufacturer categories/tags and product description support all three.
- **Andy Wood: Gearbox**: Overdrive + Distortion. Channel 1 is stand-alone overdrive; channel 2 is Pinnacle-derived distortion.
- **Plexi Drive Mini**: Overdrive + Distortion. Current manufacturer indexes expose both categories.
- **Hot Wired**: Overdrive + Distortion. Wampler's Hot Wired lineage is documented as a two-in-one OD / Distortion design; versioning is consolidated.
- **Low Blow Bass Overdrive / Distortion / Notch Filter**: Overdrive + Distortion. Both target dirt functions are explicit in the manufacturer title.
- **Triple Wreck**: Distortion + Fuzz. Its primary identity is distortion, with explicit manufacturer fuzz behavior in Boost mode.

## Conservative exclusions / variant handling
- **The Fearbox**: manufacturer-labeled limited Halloween take on Gearbox. It is not recorded as a new base pedal identity; consolidated under Andy Wood: Gearbox because the manufacturer presents it as a special edition of that design.
- **Black '65 Limited Edition** and **Tweed '57 Limited Edition**: retained as their underlying Overdrive identities rather than split into finish/edition duplicates.
- Discontinued products such as dB+, Decibel+, Talent Booster, and other booster/utility identities are excluded from the target dirt taxonomy.
- Compression, chorus, delay, reverb, echo, tremolo, and other non-dirt products in the manufacturer's Downloads/archive are excluded.
- **SuperPlex** is retained only as a verification lead, not a categorized core identity, because the primary material reviewed did not provide sufficiently explicit target-category wording.
- **Cranked AC** and **Cranked OD** are retained only as verification leads. They are named in Wampler's historical material, but the manufacturer-originated material reviewed here did not provide enough explicit product-level category evidence to promote them into the core census.
- Generic references to gain, saturation, crunch, clipping, or distortion-like response are not sufficient to create an additional category when the named product identity is explicitly presented under another target category.

## Exclusions
- Boost-only / preamp utilities: dB+, Decibel+, Talent Booster and similar products.
- Compressor-only products and compressor sections without a named target dirt identity.
- Delay/reverb/echo products including Faux Tape Echo, Faux Spring Reverb, Reflection, and related non-dirt identities.
- Modulation and chorus products including Nirvana Chorus.
- Utility/equipment products and non-pedal items in the manufacturer archive.
- Cosmetic-only limited editions and B-stock listings are not separate builder/product identities.

## Primary source URLs
- https://www.wamplerpedals.com/about/
- https://www.wamplerpedals.com/products/c/distortion-overdrive/
- https://www.wamplerpedals.com/products/t/overdrive/

# Block 064 — Walrus Audio

## Builder
**Walrus Audio**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources reviewed:
- Walrus Audio About page
- current Overdrive, Fuzz, & Distortion collection
- current All Effect Pedals catalog
- current Fuzz collection
- official Product Manuals archive
- official Discontinued Pedals collection
- individual manufacturer product pages and manufacturer-authored release/demo pages for current and historical dirt identities

The manufacturer describes Walrus Audio as a guitar-effects company and organizes its catalog into an explicit “Overdrive, Fuzz, & Distortion” family. Current catalog material exposes named overdrive, distortion, and fuzz products, while the official Discontinued collection and manufacturer-authored product pages preserve older dirt identities. The manufacturer also publishes official manuals for current and discontinued dirt products.

This block records builder/product relationships only. Current products are taken from the manufacturer’s current catalog and product pages. Historical identities are retained only when manufacturer-originated pages explicitly identify the product as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz. Cosmetic editions, B-stock pages, and limited floral/Onyx/other finish variants are consolidated under the underlying product identity.

## Overdrive
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Voyager Preamp/Overdrive MKII
- 385 Overdrive MKII
- Ages Five-State Overdrive

Historical / discontinued manufacturer-supported identities:
- 385 Overdrive
- Warhorn Mid-Range Overdrive
- Voyager Preamp/Overdrive
- Mayflower Mid-Range Overdrive
- Messner Stage-One Overdrive
- Fundamental Series Drive
- Badwater Bass Pre-amp and D.I.

Verification notes:
- Voyager Preamp/Overdrive MKII is explicitly identified by the manufacturer as a mid-focused overdrive/preamp.
- 385 Overdrive MKII is explicitly identified as an Overdrive. The manufacturer says the MKII was a reworked version of the original 385 Overdrive, adding an A/B control scheme and a 385+ high-gain switch.
- Ages Five-State Overdrive is explicitly identified as an overdrive.
- 385 Overdrive is a distinct historical named identity documented by Walrus as the original pedal released in 2016 before the later MKII.
- Warhorn is explicitly titled Mid-Range Overdrive and is retained as Overdrive.
- Voyager is explicitly described as a gain/preamp overdrive and is retained as Overdrive.
- Mayflower is explicitly described by the manufacturer as a transparent, midrange overdrive.
- Messner is explicitly described as a low-gain transparent overdrive.
- Fundamental Series Drive is explicitly described as a versatile overdrive.
- Badwater is primarily a bass preamp/DI but the manufacturer explicitly documents an overdrive/drive section, so it is retained as an Overdrive identity rather than discarded as a non-dirt preamp.

## Distortion
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Iron Horse LM308 Distortion
- Eras Five-State Distortion
- Melee: Wall of Noise

Historical / discontinued manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fundamental Series Distortion

Verification notes:
- Iron Horse is explicitly titled Distortion by the manufacturer; its product description says it can be dialed toward clear overdriven sounds at lower settings, but the named product identity remains Distortion.
- Eras is explicitly identified as a high-gain distortion pedal.
- Melee: Wall of Noise is explicitly a distortion + reverb combination, with the joystick controlling the distortion amount.
- Fundamental Series Distortion is explicitly identified as a three-mode distortion pedal in the manufacturer’s discontinued archive.

## Fuzz
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Silt Harmonic Fuzz
- Eons Five-State Fuzz
- Janus Fuzz/Tremolo with Joystick Control
- Kangra Filter Fuzz

Historical / discontinued manufacturer-supported identities:
- Jupiter Multi-Clip Fuzz V2
- Fundamental Series Fuzz
- Contraband Fuzz

Verification notes:
- Silt Harmonic Fuzz is explicitly identified as a fuzz pedal with a tube-driven fuzz section and harmonic mode.
- Eons Five-State Fuzz is explicitly identified as a five-state fuzz.
- Janus is explicitly a Fuzz/Tremolo pedal and is retained as Fuzz; the tremolo portion does not create another target dirt identity.
- Kangra Filter Fuzz is explicitly identified by the manufacturer as an octave fuzz plus filter pedal. The underlying Kangra identity is retained despite later limited-edition finishes.
- Jupiter Multi-Clip Fuzz V2 is explicitly described as a true-bypass fuzz pedal with three clipping arrangements; the original Jupiter and V2 are consolidated under the underlying Jupiter identity rather than split into unnecessary duplicate models.
- Fundamental Series Fuzz is explicitly identified as a three-mode aggressive fuzz pedal.
- Contraband Fuzz is explicitly identified by the manufacturer as a single-knob fuzz machine.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **385 Overdrive MKII**: Overdrive only. The manufacturer says cranking the gain produces a “distortion-esque” drive, but the named product identity is Overdrive and no separate manufacturer Distortion categorization is used for the product.
- **Iron Horse LM308 Distortion**: Distortion only. The manufacturer notes that it can be tuned for clearer overdriven sounds, but its product identity is Distortion.
- **Melee: Wall of Noise**: Distortion only within the target dirt taxonomy. Reverb is an additional non-target effect, but the manufacturer explicitly identifies the dirt section as distortion.
- **Silt Harmonic Fuzz**: Fuzz only. The manufacturer describes its tube section as the drive section feeding the fuzz; generic “drive” language is not promoted into a separate Overdrive identity.
- **Janus Fuzz/Tremolo**: Fuzz only. Tremolo is excluded from the dirt taxonomy.
- **Kangra Filter Fuzz**: Fuzz only. The filter section is a secondary effect and does not create an additional target-category identity.
- **Badwater Bass Pre-amp and D.I.**: Overdrive only within the target dirt taxonomy. Compression and EQ/preamp functions are not added as dirt categories.

## Historical / variant handling
- **385 Overdrive → 385 Overdrive MKII**: retained as two distinct named product identities because Walrus explicitly describes the MKII as a later reworked version of the original 385 released in 2016.
- **Jupiter Multi-Clip Fuzz / Jupiter Multi-Clip Fuzz V2**: consolidated under the V2/currently documented Jupiter identity rather than splitting ordinary revision changes into separate base products.
- **Mayflower limited editions**: consolidated under Mayflower Mid-Range Overdrive.
- **Messner limited editions**: consolidated under Messner Stage-One Overdrive / Messner Overdrive.
- **Contraband limited editions**: consolidated under Contraband Fuzz.
- **385 floral/limited editions**: consolidated under the underlying 385 Overdrive identity or 385 Overdrive MKII identity as applicable.
- **Melee floral, Onyx, and blemished listings**: consolidated under Melee: Wall of Noise.
- **Fundamental Series B-stock listings**: not separate product identities.
- **Jupiter limited editions**: not separate product identities.

## Exclusions
- Boost-only products such as Emissary Parallel Boost and Plainsman Clean Boost: not target dirt identities.
- Compressor/EQ products such as Mira and Deep Six: not target dirt identities unless an explicit dirt identity is present.
- Delay/reverb products such as Bellwether, Fathom, Descent, Slö, and R-series units: excluded.
- Modulation products such as Julia, Lillian, Monument, and related units: excluded unless paired with an explicit target dirt identity.
- Amp/cab simulation and D.I. platforms without explicit target dirt identity: excluded.
- Cosmetic-only limited editions, B-stock listings, and artwork/finish variants: not separate builder/product identities.

## Primary source URLs
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/pages/about
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/overdrive-fuzz-distortion
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/all-effect-pedals
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/fuzz
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/pages/manuals
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/discontinued-pedals
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/385-overdrive-mkii
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/silt-harmonic-fuzz
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/melee-wall-of-noise
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/fundamental-series-fuzz
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/fundamental-series-distortion
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/fundamental-series-drive
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/warhorn-mid-range-overdrive
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/jupiter-multi-clip-fuzz-v2
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/badwater-bass-pre-amp-with-d-i
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/pages/kangrafilterfuzz
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/blogs/news/385-overdrive-now-shipping
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/blogs/news/dirt-drive-distortion-comparison
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/blogs/news/131632131-contraband-fuzz
- https://www.walrusaudio.com/blogs/news/83805507-messner-overdrive

## Checkpoint
Block 064 complete. Website untouched. No active-repository search match for “Walrus Audio” was returned before this block was added. The repository search result was empty, so Walrus Audio is treated as unrepresented in the active census. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–064.
