# Block 065 — ZVEX Effects — Zachary Vex

## Builder
**ZVEX Effects — Zachary Vex**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources reviewed:
- ZVEX Effects official site / Guitar Pedals catalog
- individual manufacturer product pages for current dirt products
- manufacturer Terms page listing ZVEX product trademarks
- manufacturer artist/product references for historical product existence
- manufacturer-authored Box of Metal instruction manual

ZVEX's current Guitar Pedals catalog explicitly exposes **Distortion** and **Fuzz** as product filters and does not expose an Overdrive filter. Current catalog listings place Box of Rock, Distortron, Double Rock, Basstortion, and '59 Sound in Distortion, while Fuzz Factory-family products, Woolly Mammoth-family products, Mastotron, Fuzzolo, Fuzz Probe, Octane 3, and Machine are placed in Fuzz. Historical manufacturer material also identifies the Box of Metal as a distortion product. This block therefore follows ZVEX's own current category structure rather than inferring Overdrive from circuit behavior or amp-model descriptions.

This block records builder/product relationships only. Current products are taken from the manufacturer's current catalog and individual product pages. Historical identities are retained where manufacturer-originated material explicitly supports the product and target dirt category. Vexter editions, anniversary editions, custom-art versions, and cosmetic variations are consolidated under their underlying product identity when the manufacturer says the parts are identical or otherwise presents the item as the same pedal.

## Overdrive
No explicitly manufacturer-categorized Overdrive identities were found in the reviewed ZVEX catalog.

Verification note:
- The current ZVEX Guitar Pedals catalog exposes Distortion and Fuzz categories but no Overdrive category. Box of Rock, Distortron, Double Rock, and '59 Sound are therefore not promoted into Overdrive merely because their descriptions involve amp-like breakup, gain, or overdrive-adjacent language. ZVEX's current taxonomy is used as the controlling category evidence.

## Distortion
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Box of Rock
- Distortron
- Double Rock
- Basstortion
- '59 Sound

Historical / discontinued manufacturer-supported identity:
- Box of Metal

Verification notes:
- Box of Rock is currently placed by ZVEX in Distortion. Its product page describes it as simulating a Marshall JTM45 pushed hard and includes a separate high-headroom boost section, but no separate Overdrive categorization is assigned by the current manufacturer catalog.
- Distortron is explicitly described by ZVEX as a distortion pedal and as a modified version of the Box of Rock's Distortron engine.
- Double Rock is currently placed by ZVEX in Distortion. The manufacturer describes it as containing two Box of Rock pedals and discusses using two Distortrons, while the product identity remains a Distortion-family product.
- Basstortion is currently placed by ZVEX in Distortion and is described around tube-amp-style bass distortion.
- '59 Sound is currently placed by ZVEX in Distortion. Manufacturer catalog material describes it as a distortion design based on the classic Fender 1959 Tweed Bassman.
- Box of Metal is retained historically because the manufacturer-authored instruction manual explicitly describes the pedal as an aggressive high-gain effect and labels the DRIVE control as setting the amount of distortion. The same manual lists both Vexter and hand-painted versions, which are consolidated into one Box of Metal identity.

## Fuzz
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fuzz Factory
- Fuzz Factory 7
- Fat Fuzz Factory
- Woolly Mammoth
- Woolly Mammoth 7
- Mastotron
- Fuzz Probe
- Fuzzolo
- Machine
- Octane 3

Verification notes:
- Fuzz Factory is explicitly identified by ZVEX as a fuzz product and remains a core current identity.
- Fuzz Factory 7 is a distinct named fuzz model with a nine-position rotary switch and expanded controls. It is retained separately from Fuzz Factory because this is a functional model distinction rather than a cosmetic edition.
- Fat Fuzz Factory is explicitly described as a version of the Fuzz Factory with a functional three-position low-frequency toggle, so it is retained as a distinct named fuzz identity.
- Woolly Mammoth is explicitly described by ZVEX as a fuzz; current Vexter and hand-painted versions share the underlying identity.
- Woolly Mammoth 7 is explicitly described as an expanded version of the original Woolly Mammoth with an additional Marshall-style tone stack, so it is retained as a distinct named fuzz model.
- Mastotron is explicitly described as a sub-heavy silicon fuzz.
- Fuzz Probe is explicitly described by ZVEX as a fuzz product derived from the Fuzz Factory concept and paired with its antenna/probe control.
- Fuzzolo is explicitly described as a fuzz pedal and as being derived in part from Mastotron and Woolly Mammoth.
- Machine is explicitly described by ZVEX as a unique fuzz.
- Octane 3 is currently placed in Fuzz; manufacturer material describes its fuzzy gain and high-octave character.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Box of Rock**: Distortion only. Its amp-simulation description and high-headroom boost do not create a separate Overdrive identity because the current ZVEX catalog classifies it under Distortion.
- **Distortron**: Distortion only. The manufacturer explicitly calls it a distortion pedal.
- **Double Rock**: Distortion only. The current manufacturer catalog places it in Distortion despite its internal relationship to Box of Rock and Distortron.
- **'59 Sound**: Distortion only. Current manufacturer catalog and manufacturer-authored catalog material identify it as distortion.
- **Box of Metal**: Distortion only. The manufacturer manual explicitly identifies the DRIVE control as setting the amount of distortion.
- **Fuzz Factory 7**: Fuzz only. Its expanded control set does not create an additional dirt category.
- **Fat Fuzz Factory**: Fuzz only. Its low-frequency toggle changes the fuzz behavior without creating an Overdrive or Distortion identity.
- **Woolly Mammoth 7**: Fuzz only. The added tone stack is a shaping feature around the fuzz circuit.
- **Fuzz Probe**: Fuzz only. The probe/antenna control is an interaction mechanism within the fuzz design.
- **Machine**: Fuzz only. The manufacturer explicitly uses the fuzz classification even though its timbre can cut through high distortion elsewhere in the signal chain.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Vexter editions**: consolidated with their corresponding hand-painted product when ZVEX states that the parts inside Vexters are identical to the hand-painted model. This applies to Fuzz Factory, Fuzz Factory 7 USA Vexter, Fat Fuzz Factory, Woolly Mammoth, Fuzz Probe, Box of Rock, Double Rock, and other equivalent Vexter listings.
- **Fuzz Factory 7 USA Vexter**: consolidated under Fuzz Factory 7. ZVEX explicitly states that its parts are identical to the hand-painted model.
- **Woolly Mammoth Vexter**: consolidated under Woolly Mammoth for the same reason.
- **Fat Fuzz Factory Vexter**: consolidated under Fat Fuzz Factory for the same reason.
- **Fuzz Factory Vertical**: consolidated under Fuzz Factory as a form-factor edition rather than a new underlying dirt identity.
- **Fuzz Factory Vexter**: consolidated under Fuzz Factory as the same underlying pedal with Vexter construction/art.
- **Box of Rock Vexter / Double Rock Vexter**: consolidated under their corresponding Box of Rock / Double Rock identities.
- **Sylvia Massy Woolly Mammoth**: consolidated under Woolly Mammoth because ZVEX presents it as a special-edition release of that fuzz identity rather than a separate base circuit/product family.
- **Custom-store and Mod Bench one-offs**: excluded from the core product census. ZVEX explicitly presents these as one-of-a-kind custom/artwork or experimental variants of existing pedals.
- **Silicon Fuzz Factory, Reverse Fuzz Factory, and similar Mod Bench experiments**: excluded from the core census because the manufacturer presents them as prototypes, hybrids, or experimental/custom variants rather than standard catalog identities.

## Exclusions
- Boost products such as Super Hard On and Super Duper: excluded because the active scope is Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz, and ZVEX categorizes these as Boost.
- Octave-only products such as Jonny Octave: excluded because the manufacturer categorizes them as Octave rather than a target dirt identity.
- Modulation, phaser, tremolo, sampler, meter, step-sequencer, volume, and other non-target products: excluded.
- Custom-store one-offs, engraved items, artwork editions, and Mod Bench experiments: excluded as variants/prototypes rather than separate core product identities.
- Generic gain, breakup, amp-emulation, or distortion-adjacent language is not enough to add a second dirt category when ZVEX's own product taxonomy does not do so.

## Primary source URLs
- https://www.zvex.com/
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/fuzz-factory-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/fuzz-factory-7-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/fat-fuzz-factory-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/woolly-mammoth-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/woolly-mammoth-7-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/fuzzolo-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/machine-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/distortron-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/box-of-rock-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/fuzz-factory-7-usa-vexter-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/woolly-mammoth-vexter-guitar-effects-pedal
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/fat-fuzz-factory-vexter-guitar-effects-pedal-25th-anniversary
- https://www.zvex.com/guitar-pedals/fuzz-probe-vexter-guitar-effects-pedal

- https://static1.squarespace.com/static/555e332ce4b0577e788c3a16/t/618442b190eb205f2796f06c/1636057777805/ZVEX%2BBox%2Bof%2BMetal%2BHP%2BInstructions.pdf

ZVEX is therefore treated as unrepresented in the active census. The current manufacturer taxonomy yields no explicit Overdrive group, with Distortion and Fuzz supplying the categorized identities. # Block 066 — MXR — Dunlop Manufacturing, Inc.

## Builder
**MXR — Dunlop Manufacturing, Inc.**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources reviewed:
- Dunlop / MXR current MXR catalog
- Dunlop / MXR legacy-product catalog
- individual manufacturer product pages
- manufacturer product manuals
- manufacturer-authored MXR history and product material

The official Dunlop site carries the MXR catalog and identifies MXR as a registered trademark of Dunlop Manufacturing, Inc. The manufacturer maintains a current MXR product catalog plus a separate MXR legacy-product catalog. This block therefore records MXR as the builder identity and Dunlop Manufacturing, Inc. as the corporate attribution rather than treating every artist-series, finish, or revision listing as a new builder.

This block records builder/product relationships only. Current products are taken from the live MXR catalog and individual product pages. Historical identities are retained when the manufacturer maintains them in its legacy catalog or provides manufacturer-authored product/manual material supporting the dirt identity. Limited editions, anniversary finishes, and cosmetic variants are consolidated with their underlying products unless the manufacturer presents a genuinely distinct named functional model.

## Overdrive
Current / live manufacturer-supported identities:
- Wylde Audio Zakk Sabbath Overdrive
- GT-OD
- Super Badass Dynamic O.D.
- Duke of Tone Overdrive
- Timmy Overdrive
- Wylde Audio Overdrive
- EVH 5150 Overdrive
- Raw Dawg Overdrive
- Tom Morello Power 50 Overdrive
- Sugar Drive
- Fat Sugar Drive
- Yngwie Malmsteen Overdrive
- Custom Badass Modified O.D.
- Dookie Drive
- Jail Guitar Doors Overdrive

Historical / legacy manufacturer-supported identities:
- Double-Double Overdrive
- CAE Boost/Overdrive
- Gran Torino Boost Overdrive
- FOD Drive
- Raijin Drive
- Bass Overdrive

Verification notes:
- GT-OD is explicitly described by MXR as an Overdrive.
- Super Badass Dynamic O.D. is explicitly named as an O.D. product in the current MXR catalog.
- Duke of Tone is explicitly named as an Overdrive; the manufacturer manual documents selectable OD, Boost, and Distortion modes, so the pedal is also retained in Distortion below.
- Timmy Overdrive is explicitly named as an Overdrive.
- EVH 5150 Overdrive is explicitly named as Overdrive and the manufacturer describes a range of OD and distortion tones; its named core identity remains Overdrive, with the manufacturer not using a separate Distortion product identity for it.
- Raw Dawg Overdrive is explicitly named and described as an OD based on a classic overdrive circuit.
- Sugar Drive is explicitly described as a transparent overdrive, and the manual identifies its Drive control as increasing overdrive.
- Fat Sugar Drive is explicitly described as providing transparent overdrive tones; its manual identifies the Drive control as increasing overdrive.
- Yngwie Malmsteen Overdrive is explicitly named and described as an Overdrive.
- Custom Badass Modified O.D. is explicitly named as an O.D. product.
- Dookie Drive is retained as Overdrive because MXR's manufacturer page describes its purpose as a full harmonic range of overdriven tones and the product is presented as a drive rather than a separately categorized distortion identity.
- Jail Guitar Doors Overdrive is explicitly named as Overdrive.
- FOD Drive is retained as Overdrive because MXR describes its two channels as recreating overdriven amp stacks and repeatedly characterizes the resulting gain as overdrive.
- Raijin Drive is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion because MXR explicitly describes its two selectable sides as Japanese overdrive and distortion tones.
- Double-Double Overdrive, CAE Boost/Overdrive, Gran Torino Boost Overdrive, and Bass Overdrive are explicitly supported by their manufacturer product names/descriptions.

## Distortion
Current / live manufacturer-supported identities:
- Randy Rhoads Distortion+
- Distortion+
- Distortion III
- Super Badass Distortion
- Fullbore Metal Distortion
- Custom Badass '78 Distortion
- Bass Distortion+
- Bass Distortion
- Duke of Tone Overdrive

Historical / legacy manufacturer-supported identities:
- Prime Distortion
- Dime Distortion
- Raijin Drive

Verification notes:
- Distortion+ is explicitly named and documented as a distortion product, even though MXR also describes usable overdriven blues settings.
- Randy Rhoads Distortion+ is a separately named signature/reissue identity but is a recreation of the MXR Distortion+; it is retained as a named product identity because the manufacturer presents it as a distinct current model with its own product page.
- Distortion III is explicitly named as Distortion. MXR describes its range from singing overdrive to massive distortion, but the named manufacturer identity remains Distortion.
- Super Badass Distortion is explicitly named as Distortion. MXR describes its range from low-gain overdrive to metal distortion, but it remains in Distortion.
- Fullbore Metal Distortion is explicitly named as a metal distortion product.
- Custom Badass '78 Distortion is explicitly named as Distortion; the manufacturer manual identifies its Distortion control and distortion output behavior.
- Bass Distortion+ and Bass Distortion are explicitly named bass-distortion products. Bass Distortion is marked legacy/no longer in production on the manufacturer page.
- Duke of Tone is retained in Distortion because the official MXR manual explicitly documents a Distortion mode using hard clipping; this is a functional category overlap, not an inference from tone alone.
- Raijin Drive is retained in Distortion because MXR explicitly describes a selectable distortion side.
- Prime Distortion and Dime Distortion are manufacturer-supported historical/legacy Distortion identities.

## Fuzz
Current / live manufacturer-supported identities:
- Blue Box Fuzz
- Slash Octave Fuzz
- Bass Fuzz Deluxe
- Super Badass Variac Fuzz
- Classic 108 Fuzz Mini

Historical / legacy manufacturer-supported identities:
- Classic 108 Fuzz
- Octavio Fuzz
- Hybrid Fuzz
- Sub Machine Fuzz
- Sub Octave Bass Fuzz

Verification notes:
- Blue Box Fuzz is explicitly named and described as octave fuzz.
- Slash Octave Fuzz is explicitly named as Fuzz; the official manual identifies main fuzz, sub-octave, and octave-up fuzz sections.
- Bass Fuzz Deluxe is explicitly named and described as a fuzz product with separate dry/wet controls.
- Super Badass Variac Fuzz is explicitly named as Fuzz and described as a variable-voltage fuzz design.
- Classic 108 Fuzz Mini is explicitly named Fuzz. It carries the same core vintage fuzz concept as the larger Classic 108 but is retained as a distinct named mini model.
- Classic 108 Fuzz is a manufacturer-supported legacy Fuzz identity based on BC108 Fuzz Face circuitry.
- Octavio Fuzz is a manufacturer-supported legacy Fuzz identity centered on octave-up fuzz.
- Hybrid Fuzz is a manufacturer-supported legacy Fuzz identity.
- Sub Machine Fuzz is a manufacturer-supported legacy Fuzz identity combining fuzz with sub-octave circuitry.
- Sub Octave Bass Fuzz is a manufacturer-supported legacy Fuzz identity.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Duke of Tone Overdrive**: Overdrive + Distortion. The MXR manual explicitly provides OD, Boost, and Distortion clipping modes. Boost remains outside the target taxonomy, while OD and Distortion are both retained.
- **Raijin Drive**: Overdrive + Distortion. MXR explicitly describes separate Japanese overdrive and distortion tones selectable by switch.
- **Distortion+**: Distortion only. Overdriven settings are documented, but the manufacturer names and categorizes the product as Distortion+ rather than creating a separate Overdrive identity.
- **Distortion III**: Distortion only. Its overdrive range does not change the named manufacturer category.
- **Super Badass Distortion**: Distortion only. Its lower-gain overdrive range does not create a second manufacturer identity.
- **EVH 5150 Overdrive**: Overdrive only. MXR describes both OD and distortion tones, but the manufacturer product identity is explicitly Overdrive.
- **Dookie Drive**: Overdrive only in the target taxonomy. The manufacturer characterizes the pedal around overdriven amp tones rather than assigning it a separate Distortion category.
- **FOD Drive**: Overdrive only. MXR's manufacturer descriptions repeatedly call the core behavior overdrive/overdriven amp stacks.
- **Blue Box Fuzz**: Fuzz only. Its octave content does not create a separate target category.
- **Slash Octave Fuzz**: Fuzz only. Sub-octave and octave-up sections are part of the fuzz design.
- **Classic 108 Fuzz Mini**: Fuzz only. The smaller housing is treated as a functional mini model, not a separate category.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Randy Rhoads Distortion+ / Randy Rhoads Special Edition Distortion+**: consolidated where the manufacturer is presenting the latter as a special-edition finish/iteration of the same Randy Rhoads Distortion+ identity; the base RR104 identity is retained in the census.
- **Dookie Drive V2 / V3 / V4 / 30th Anniversary**: consolidated under Dookie Drive rather than treating version numbers and anniversary finishings as separate base identities.
- **Classic 108 Fuzz / Classic 108 Fuzz Mini**: retained as distinct named functional models because the Mini is an independently named smaller-footprint product, while the larger Classic 108 remains a separate legacy model.
- **Sugar Drive / Fat Sugar Drive**: retained separately because MXR presents the Fat Sugar Drive as a distinct full-size housing/product identity rather than a purely cosmetic finish.
- **Vexter-style and custom-art logic is not applicable here**; MXR limited finishes and anniversary editions are consolidated under their underlying named models unless functionality creates a distinct named model.
- **Jail Guitar Doors Drive**: kept outside the categorized core census as a verification lead because the manufacturer page reviewed names it “Drive” and describes raw/gritty tones but does not provide sufficiently explicit Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz categorization at product level. The explicitly named Jail Guitar Doors Overdrive is categorized.

## Exclusions
- Boost-only products such as Booster Mini, Micro Amp, Micro Amp+, CAE Boost/Line Driver, and similar utility/boost identities are excluded unless the product has an explicit target dirt identity.
- Compression, EQ, noise gate, tuner, utility, power-supply, looper, modulation, delay, reverb, talk-box, synth, and octave-only products are excluded from the target dirt census.
- Products belonging to non-MXR Dunlop brands such as Cry Baby, Way Huge, and Authentic Hendrix are not merged into this MXR builder block.
- Cosmetic-only special editions, anniversary finishes, and B-stock listings are not separate base identities.

Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–066.

# Block 067 — Pigtronix

## Builder
**Pigtronix**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources reviewed:
- Pigtronix About page
- current All Pedals catalog
- current Micro Pedals catalog
- current Product Manuals archive
- current Legacy Products archive
- individual manufacturer product pages
- manufacturer-authored product manuals
- manufacturer FAQ for historical product relationships

Pigtronix identifies itself as a boutique pedal maker and maintains a current pedal catalog plus separate current/legacy support indexes. The reviewed manufacturer pages explicitly identify Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz functions for multiple products. This block records only builder/product relationships and uses manufacturer wording and product taxonomy rather than inferring additional dirt categories from general saturation language.

## Overdrive
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Gamma Drive
- Philosopher's Tone 2
- Philosopher's Gold
- Disnortion

Historical / verification-lead identities not promoted into the core census:
- FAT Drive
- Bass FAT Drive
- XL FAT Drive
- PolySaturator

Verification notes:
- Gamma Drive is explicitly identified by Pigtronix as an overdrive with 2-band EQ. Its manufacturer product data lists Overdrive as Category 1 and Distortion as Category 2, so both target categories are recorded below.
- Philosopher's Tone 2 is primarily a compressor/sustainer, but the manufacturer explicitly describes a footswitchable Grit circuit using an overdrive stage and says the engaged circuit can range from subtle overdrive to overflowing distortion. It is therefore retained in both target dirt categories while its compression function remains outside scope.
- Philosopher's Gold is explicitly described as a compressor plus germanium-enhanced distortion, and its manufacturer product data identifies the function as Compressor Sustainer Overdrive. It is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion because the product-level language explicitly supports both.
- Disnortion is explicitly identified by Pigtronix as an Analog Fuzz + Overdrive / Parallel Fuzz and Overdrive pedal. It is retained in Overdrive and Fuzz; the manual's generic use of “Distortion” to describe gain in the fuzz and overdrive stages is not treated as a separate Distortion category.
- Pigtronix's FAQ confirms the existence of the historical FAT Drive and Bass FAT Drive relationship, but the reviewed manufacturer material does not provide sufficiently explicit product-level target-category classification to promote them into the categorized census.

## Distortion
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Gamma Drive
- Philosopher's Tone 2
- Philosopher's Gold
- Octava
- Star Eater

Historical / verification-lead identities not promoted into the core census:
- FAT Drive
- XL FAT Drive
- PolySaturator

Verification notes:
- Gamma Drive's manufacturer model data explicitly lists Distortion as Category 2 alongside Overdrive.
- Philosopher's Tone 2 explicitly describes its Grit circuit as germanium/silicon distortion and says the nonlinear diode clipping ranges from subtle overdrive to overflowing distortion.
- Philosopher's Gold is explicitly described by Pigtronix as compressor + germanium distortion, with a germanium overdrive stage blended by its Grit control.
- Octava's manufacturer model data lists Distortion as Category 1 even though its function is Analog Octave Fuzz; the product description also explicitly describes full-wave rectified distortion. It is therefore recorded in both Distortion and Fuzz.
- Star Eater's manufacturer model data lists Distortion as Category 1 while the function is Super-Jumbo Fuzz with Filter. The manufacturer description repeatedly identifies the effect as fuzz, so it is retained in both Distortion and Fuzz based on explicit manufacturer taxonomy/function fields.
- FAT Drive and related historical drive products are retained only as verification leads because the reviewed current Pigtronix archive does not expose sufficiently strong manufacturer category data to classify them without relying on secondary sources.

## Fuzz
Current manufacturer-supported identities:
- Gas Giant
- Star Eater
- Octava
- Disnortion

Verification notes:
- Gas Giant is explicitly identified as an Analog Jumbo Fuzz and is current in the Pigtronix pedal catalog.
- Star Eater is explicitly identified as an Analog Dual Stage Super Jumbo Fuzz and its manufacturer manual describes it as a super-jumbo fuzz.
- Octava is explicitly identified as an Analog Octave Fuzz. The manufacturer describes its front-end drive plus fuzz circuit and identifies Distortion in its model taxonomy.
- Disnortion is explicitly identified as Analog Fuzz + Overdrive and as a Parallel Fuzz and Overdrive design. It is retained in both target categories without adding Distortion from generic gain terminology.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Gamma Drive**: Overdrive + Distortion. Pigtronix explicitly lists both categories in its product model data.
- **Philosopher's Tone 2**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer text explicitly documents an overdrive stage and distortion output range when Grit is engaged.
- **Philosopher's Gold**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer product data identifies an Overdrive function and the product description explicitly identifies germanium-enhanced distortion.
- **Disnortion**: Overdrive + Fuzz. Manufacturer function/category wording explicitly identifies both; the manual's use of “distortion” as a control quantity is not promoted into a separate Distortion identity.
- **Octava**: Fuzz + Distortion. Manufacturer function is Analog Octave Fuzz while model taxonomy explicitly lists Distortion.
- **Star Eater**: Fuzz + Distortion. Manufacturer function is Super-Jumbo Fuzz with Filter while model taxonomy explicitly lists Distortion.
- **Gas Giant**: Fuzz only. Its built-in noise gate and voicing switches are secondary features around the explicitly named fuzz circuit.

## Historical / variant handling
- Pigtronix current and legacy support pages can expose both current product pages and discontinued families. A product appearing in the current manual archive is not assumed to be currently manufactured if the Legacy Products page explicitly marks it discontinued; status is recorded conservatively in the relevant notes.
- **Disnortion / Disnortion Micro**: consolidated under the current Disnortion identity because Pigtronix presents the Micro product as the compact current expression of the Disnortion design rather than a separate dirt family.
- **Philosopher's Tone / Philosopher's Tone 2**: retained as distinct named products because the Tone 2 adds a footswitchable Grit circuit and is independently named by the manufacturer.
- **Philosopher's Gold**: retained as a distinct named product because it is a separately named compressor + germanium distortion/overdrive variant.
- FAT Drive, Bass FAT Drive, XL FAT Drive, and PolySaturator are not split into the categorized census without sufficiently explicit manufacturer product-category evidence in the reviewed current/legacy archive. They remain verification leads only.

## Exclusions
- Compressor-only Philosopher's Tone is excluded from the dirt census despite being historically related to the dirt-enabled Gold/Tone 2 variants.
- Class A Boost is excluded because the manufacturer classifies it as a clean boost/Utility, even though the page mentions saturation and pushing distortion pedals.
- Gatekeeper and other noise-gate/utility products are excluded.
- Delay, reverb, modulation, synth, looper, ring-modulator, and other non-dirt products are excluded.
- Generic references to gain, saturation, crunch, or distortion-like behavior do not create a new category unless Pigtronix explicitly supplies target-category support.

## Primary source URLs
- https://www.pigtronix.com/about/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/micro-pedals/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/support/product-manuals/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/support/legacy-products/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/gamma-drive/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/gas-giant/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/star-eater/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/octava/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/disnortion-micro/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/philosophers-tone-2/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/philosophers-gold/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/pedals/philosophers-tone/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/faq/
- https://www.pigtronix.com/uploads/pigtronix/2020/11/OFM2_Disnortion_Manual.pdf
- https://www.pigtronix.com/uploads/pigtronix/2020/11/OFM_Disnortion_Manual.pdf
- https://www.pigtronix.com/uploads/pigtronix/2020/11/OCT2_Octava_Manual.pdf
- https://www.pigtronix.com/uploads/pigtronix/2022/06/FUZ_Star_Eater_Manual.pdf
- https://www.pigtronix.com/uploads/pigtronix/2022/10/GGM2_Philosophers_Gold_Manual-2.pdf
- https://www.pigtronix.com/uploads/pigtronix/2021/07/GGM_Philosophers_Gold_Manual.pdf

## Checkpoint
Block 067 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository searches for “Pigtronix,” “Disnortion,” “Gamma Drive,” “Gas Giant,” “Star Eater,” “Octava,” and “Philosopher's Gold” returned no matches before this block was added, so Pigtronix is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–067.

# Block 068 — Foxgear

## Builder
**Foxgear**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer/distributor sources reviewed:
- Fox Gear Distribution official home page
- Fox Gear Product List
- Fox Gear Manuals archive
- individual Foxgear product pages
- manufacturer-authored Foxgear product manuals

The official Fox Gear Distribution site presents Foxgear as one of its product brands and separately identifies Gurus and Baroni-Lab as other brands. The Foxgear product list explicitly names several dirt products, and the manuals archive provides manufacturer-authored documentation for the relevant models. This block therefore records the **Foxgear** builder/brand only and does not merge the separately presented Gurus or Baroni-Lab brands.

This block records builder/product relationships only. Dirt categories are based on explicit manufacturer product names, descriptions, or manuals. General references to gain, saturation, clipping, or technical THD are not used to create additional target categories unless the manufacturer clearly identifies the product as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fenix
- Cream

Verification notes:
- Fenix is explicitly described by Foxgear as one of its most versatile **overdrive/distortion** pedals, covering tones from early-'70s overdrive through modern hard-rock sounds. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- Cream is explicitly titled **Screaming Overdrive** in the Foxgear product catalog, and its manufacturer manual says its gain control sets the level of overdrive and describes the pedal as a stand-alone overdrive or amp booster.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fenix
- Ryder
- Muffin
- Bass Muffin
- Sinister
- Rats

Verification notes:
- Fenix is explicitly described as an overdrive/distortion pedal by Foxgear and is retained in both target categories.
- Ryder is explicitly described in the manufacturer manual as Doug Aldrich's signature **distortion pedal** and as Foxgear's latest distortion pedal at the time of the manual.
- Muffin is explicitly titled **Gilmourish Russian Muff Distortion** in the Foxgear product list/product page.
- Bass Muffin is explicitly titled **Bass Distortion with Blend** in the Foxgear product list/product page, and its manual describes an aggressive bass-oriented muff/distortion with a distortion control.
- Sinister is explicitly titled **FET Metal Distortion** and its manufacturer manual identifies it as a powerful metal distortion pedal.
- Rats is explicitly titled **Classic Distortion** in the Foxgear product list, and the manufacturer manual archive includes RATSUSERMANUAL documentation for the product.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Manic

Verification notes:
- Manic is explicitly titled **Vintage Fuzz tone** in the Foxgear product list and is described on its manufacturer page as a Vintage Fuzz Face design. It is therefore recorded as Fuzz only.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Fenix**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer wording explicitly uses both target categories in the product description.
- **Cream**: Overdrive only. The manufacturer explicitly identifies it as an overdrive and amp booster; boost functionality does not create a second target dirt category.
- **Ryder**: Distortion only. The manufacturer explicitly calls it a distortion pedal; its Germanium clipping section and RAT foundation do not create a separate Overdrive or Fuzz identity.
- **Muffin**: Distortion only. The manufacturer names it as Russian Muff Distortion; generic saturation/fuzz-adjacent terminology is not used to add Fuzz without explicit manufacturer classification.
- **Bass Muffin**: Distortion only. The manufacturer identifies it as Bass Distortion with Blend; the blend control is a secondary clean-mix feature.
- **Sinister**: Distortion only. The manufacturer explicitly identifies it as FET Metal Distortion.
- **Rats**: Distortion only. The manufacturer product list explicitly categorizes it as Classic Distortion.
- **Manic**: Fuzz only. The manufacturer explicitly identifies it as Vintage Fuzz tone and a Vintage Fuzz Face design.

## Historical / variant handling
- The reviewed Foxgear product list and manuals archive contain the relevant Foxgear dirt identities, but they do not provide enough product-level evidence in the reviewed material to construct a reliable, separate historical/discontinued dirt catalog. This block therefore records the explicitly supported Foxgear identities presently exposed by the manufacturer sources reviewed.
- **Ryder / Rats**: retained as separate named functional products. Ryder is presented as a newer signature distortion based on the Foxgear Rats platform but with different filtering and Germanium clipping; it is not collapsed into Rats because the manufacturer gives it a separate product identity.
- **Bass Muffin / Muffin**: retained separately because Bass Muffin is a dedicated bass product with an independent product name and blend architecture.
- **Foxgear vs. Gurus vs. Baroni-Lab**: kept separate because the official site presents Gurus and Baroni-Lab under separate brand sections. Gurus products such as Sexy Drive MK II and Ninja are not merged into this Foxgear block.

## Exclusions
- Gurus products such as Sexy Drive MK II and Ninja: excluded from the Foxgear builder block because the official site presents Gurus as a separate brand.
- Baroni-Lab amplifiers and related products: excluded because Baroni-Lab is presented as a separate brand.
- QBoost and other boost-only products: excluded.
- Squeeze and other compressors: excluded.
- Chorus, flanger, tremolo, delay, reverb, echo, modulation, multi-effect, power-supply, interface, amp, and other non-target products: excluded.
- Generic references to gain, saturation, clipping, or THD are not used to expand a product into another target dirt category without explicit manufacturer support.

## Primary source URLs
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/fox-gear-product-list/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/manuals/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/product/fenix/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/product/cream/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/product/manic/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/product/muffin/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/product/bass-muffin/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/product/sinister/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/product/rider/
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/backend/wp/wp-content/uploads/simple-file-list/FENIXUSERMANUAL.pdf
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/backend/wp/wp-content/uploads/simple-file-list/CREAMUSERMANUAL.pdf
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/backend/wp/wp-content/uploads/simple-file-list/MANICUSERMANUAL.pdf
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/backend/wp/wp-content/uploads/simple-file-list/MUFFINUSERMANUAL.pdf
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/backend/wp/wp-content/uploads/simple-file-list/BASSMUFFINUSERMANUAL.pdf
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/backend/wp/wp-content/uploads/simple-file-list/SINISTERUSERMANUAL.pdf
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/backend/wp/wp-content/uploads/simple-file-list/RYDERUSERMANUAL.pdf
- https://www.foxgeardistribution.com/backend/wp/wp-content/uploads/simple-file-list/RATSUSERMANUAL.pdf

## Checkpoint
Block 068 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Foxgear” returned no matches before this block was added, so Foxgear is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The official Fox Gear site separately presents Gurus and Baroni-Lab as other brands, so their products remain outside this builder block. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–068.

# Block 069 — Lovepedal

## Builder
**Lovepedal / Love Pedal**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer sources reviewed:
- Lovepedal official home page and Pedals index
- Lovepedal official New Products index
- Lovepedal official History page
- individual Lovepedal product pages
- official Lovepedal press archive

The manufacturer presents Love Pedal / Lovepedal as the builder brand and currently exposes a mix of current and small-batch products alongside historical product information. This block records the Lovepedal builder relationship only. Hermida Audio products that the Lovepedal site now sells or documents are retained as Lovepedal product identities only where the Lovepedal manufacturer page itself presents them within the Lovepedal catalog; this does not create a separate Hermida Audio builder block here.

Dirt categories are based on explicit manufacturer product titles or descriptions. References to clipping, gain, saturation, or distortion as a sonic range do not automatically create an additional target category unless the manufacturer clearly identifies the product as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- 200lbs of Gold
- Purple Plexi
- Eternity Burst
- Zendrive
- Dover Drive
- Amp Eleven
- Kalamazoo
- Super Six
- 5E3 Deluxe
- Tchula

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- COT 50 / Church of Tone 50
- Eternity Overdrive
- Amp 50
- Mosferatu
- Zendrive 2
- Tiki Drive

Verification notes:
- **200lbs of Gold** is explicitly described as a modified vintage Fuzz Face feeding a TS808-style overdrive, so it is recorded as Overdrive and Fuzz.
- **Purple Plexi** is explicitly described as balanced, medium-gain overdrive through full JCM800-style roar.
- **Eternity Burst** is explicitly presented as a hand-wired Eternity Overdrive build.
- **Zendrive** is explicitly described as a dynamic overdrive and low-distortion pedal.
- **Dover Drive** is explicitly described as a wide-ranging overdrive, even though its upper range can become Fuzz Face-like.
- **Amp Eleven** is explicitly described as low-to-medium-gain overdrive with a separate boost channel.
- **Kalamazoo** is explicitly titled and described as an overdrive.
- **Super Six** exposes a Drive control and feedback-loop clipping, but the manufacturer does not explicitly label it as an Overdrive pedal on the current page; it is retained conservatively here because the product is presented as an amp-tone dirt device with dedicated Drive clipping.
- **5E3 Deluxe** explicitly says the GAIN/BIAS control selects the amount of overdrive.
- **Tchula** is explicitly described by Lovepedal as a 2-stage COT50-based “Dirt Box”; because the builder identifies its underlying COT50 circuit as Boost / Overdrive, it is retained as an Overdrive identity without adding Distortion or Fuzz.
- **COT 50 / Church of Tone 50** is explicitly called Boost / Overdrive by the Lovepedal history page.
- **Eternity Overdrive** is explicitly identified as an overdrive in the manufacturer history and press material.
- **Amp 50** is explicitly identified as Overdrive in Lovepedal's account of its 2009 Mini Line.
- **Mosferatu** is identified by the manufacturer as one of Alfonso Hermida's early overdrive pedals and is retained as an Overdrive identity.
- **Zendrive 2** is explicitly described as low-to-medium-gain tube overdrive.
- **Tiki Drive** is described as a multi-gain-stage pedal developed to reproduce overdriven and distorted sounds from clean amplifiers. It is retained in Overdrive and Distortion below.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Tiki Drive

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none separately promoted beyond Tiki Drive

Verification notes:
- **Tiki Drive** is the only Lovepedal identity promoted into the Distortion category in this block because the manufacturer explicitly describes its design as intended to produce overdriven/distorted sounds and discusses its multiple gain stages in those terms.
- Other Lovepedal products use phrases such as “low distortion,” “distortion amount,” or describe a transition into aggressive gain, but the manufacturer presents them as Overdrive products rather than as a separate Distortion category. They remain Overdrive only.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- 200lbs of Gold

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- FAB 50

Verification notes:
- **200lbs of Gold** is explicitly described as a modified vintage Fuzz Face feeding a TS808-style overdrive, so it is retained as Fuzz and Overdrive.
- **FAB 50** is identified by Lovepedal's official history as a one-knob design that simulated a cranked Marshall at lower gain settings and produced a “nasty fuzz tone” at the upper end. Because the manufacturer itself explicitly describes the product's high-gain result as fuzz, it is retained as a historical Fuzz identity.
- **Dover Drive** is not added to Fuzz despite its Fuzz Face-like upper range because the manufacturer explicitly presents it as an Overdrive product.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **200lbs of Gold**: Overdrive + Fuzz. Manufacturer description explicitly combines vintage fuzz-face circuitry with TS808-style overdrive.
- **Tiki Drive**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer description explicitly discusses both overdrive and distortion as target sounds of the multi-stage design.
- **FAB 50**: Fuzz only. Manufacturer explicitly describes the upper range as fuzz but does not identify a separate Overdrive category for the product.
- **Dover Drive**: Overdrive only. Manufacturer explicitly calls it an overdrive even though it reaches Fuzz Face-like textures.
- **Zendrive**: Overdrive only. “Low distortion” is treated as a range descriptor, not a second category.
- **Eternity Burst / Eternity Overdrive**: Overdrive only. Product identity is explicitly Overdrive.
- **Amp Eleven / Amp 50**: Overdrive only. Boost functionality is outside the target taxonomy and does not create an additional category.
- **COT 50**: Overdrive only. Boost/Overdrive is retained under the target Overdrive category.
- **Tchula**: Overdrive only. Lovepedal identifies it as a COT50-derived 2-stage dirt box, but does not explicitly classify it as Distortion or Fuzz.
- **Super Six**: Overdrive only for this census. The current page presents dedicated Drive clipping in an amp-tone pedal, but does not provide a separate Distortion or Fuzz identity.

## Historical / variant handling
- Lovepedal maintains a mixture of current products, custom small-batch builds, old-school builds, and historical references. This block consolidates cosmetic, hand-wired, NOS-component, and small-batch versions when the underlying product identity is clearly the same.
- **Eternity Burst** is retained as a separate current named build because the manufacturer gives it its own product identity, while it still belongs to the broader Eternity overdrive family.
- **Zendrive 2** remains separate from Zendrive because Lovepedal gives it a distinct product name and construction.
- **COT50 Eternity Stack / COT50 Eternity Stack Dual Unit** is not promoted as a new dirt identity. It is a two-product stack of COT50 and Eternity Overdrive, and the underlying identities are already represented.
- **Hermida Audio Zendrive / Dover Drive** are retained here only because the Lovepedal manufacturer site currently presents these named products within its own catalog and product pages. No separate Hermida builder block is created from this evidence alone.
- Cosmetic finishes, old-school versus current cosmetic editions, and limited small-run batches are consolidated unless Lovepedal presents a clearly distinct functional product name.

## Exclusions
- Rubber Chicken and Hermida EPH3: non-target filter/delay products excluded.
- COT50 Eternity Stack: no new identity; constituent COT50 and Eternity are already represented.
- Boost-only functionality and clean-boost sections: excluded from target categories.
- General amp-emulation language without explicit target dirt wording is not used to force a category.
- Generic “distortion amount,” “low distortion,” gain, clipping, saturation, or fuzz-adjacent wording does not add categories unless the manufacturer explicitly identifies the product as such.

## Primary source URLs
- https://www.lovepedal.com/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/pedals/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/new-products/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/history/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/200lbs-of-gold/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/pedals/tchula/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/purple-plexi/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/burst-eternity/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/zendrive/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/hermida-audio-dover-drive/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/pedals/amp-eleven/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/pedals/kalamazoo/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/pedals/super-six/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/5e3-deluxe/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/zendrive-2/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/n-o-s-tiki-drive-50-unit-build/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/cot50-eternity-stack/
- https://www.lovepedal.com/press/

## Checkpoint
Block 069 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Lovepedal” returned no matches before this block was added, so Lovepedal is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The manufacturer supports a substantial Overdrive catalog and a smaller number of explicit Fuzz/Distortion identities. No separate Distortion catalog was inferred from generic high-gain language. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–069.

# Block 071 — Maxon

## Builder
**Maxon / Nisshin Onpa**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer / authoritative sources reviewed:
- Maxon official effects catalog and product pages
- Nisshin Onpa / Maxon manufacturer documentation
- Godlyke's Maxon catalog and archived Maxon product documentation where manufacturer material was unavailable or no longer surfaced in the current Maxon site

The active repository contained no prior Maxon match before this block was added. Maxon is treated as a distinct builder/brand from Ibanez even where historically related products share designs or naming. This block records builder/product relationships only.

Dirt categories are based on explicit manufacturer or authoritative catalog titles and descriptions. Generic references to gain, clipping, saturation, or a product reaching another sound are not used to create an additional category unless the source explicitly identifies the product as overdrive, distortion, or fuzz.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- OD808
- OD-9
- OD820 Overdrive Pro+
- OD808X
- OD-9 Pro+
- OOD-9 Organic Overdrive
- OSD-9 Overdrive / Soft Distortion
- RTO700 Real Tube Overdrive
- ST9Pro+ Super Tube Overdrive Pro+
- TBO-9 True Tube Booster / Overdrive
- TOD-9 True Tube Overdrive
- RTD800 Real Tube Distortion
- ROD881 Real Overdrive / Distortion

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- ST-9 Super Tube Screamer
- SD-9 Sonic Distortion / Overdrive
- SM-9Pro Super Metal Pro+

Verification notes:
- **OD808** and **OD-9** are explicitly identified by Maxon as overdrive pedals.
- **OD820 Overdrive Pro+**, **OD808X**, **OD-9 Pro+**, and **OOD-9** are explicitly named as overdrive products.
- **OSD-9** is explicitly titled **Overdrive / Soft Distortion**, so it is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **RTO700** is explicitly a Real Tube Overdrive.
- **ST9Pro+** is explicitly a Super Tube Overdrive.
- **TBO-9** is explicitly a tube booster / overdrive product.
- **TOD-9** is explicitly a True Tube Overdrive.
- **RTD800** is a dual-stage tube unit with independently described overdrive and distortion circuits; it is therefore retained in both categories.
- **ROD881** is explicitly documented as Real Overdrive / Distortion and is retained in both categories.
- Historical **ST-9**, **SD-9**, and **SM-9Pro** are retained as Maxon-named historical dirt products where the Maxon/Godlyke documentation explicitly connects them to overdrive or distortion functions. Their primary target categorization follows the explicit product naming, with overlap noted below where supported.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- OSD-9
- D&S
- D&S II
- DS830 Distortion Master
- SD-9M Sonic Distortion Master
- SSD-9 Super Sonic Distortion
- SM-9Pro+ Super Metal Pro+
- RTD800 Real Tube Distortion
- ROD881 Real Overdrive / Distortion

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- SD-9 Sonic Distortion
- SM-9 Super Metal

Verification notes:
- **OSD-9** explicitly combines Overdrive and Soft Distortion.
- **D&S** and **D&S II** are Maxon's Distortion & Sustainer products.
- **DS830** is explicitly a Distortion Master.
- **SD-9M** and historical **SD-9** are explicitly Sonic Distortion products.
- **SSD-9** is explicitly Super Sonic Distortion.
- **SM-9Pro+** and historical **SM-9** are explicitly Super Metal distortion products.
- **RTD800** contains distinct overdrive and distortion stages and is retained in both categories.
- **ROD881** is explicitly named Real Overdrive / Distortion and is retained in both categories.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fuzz Elements Air
- Fuzz Elements Earth
- Fuzz Elements Ether
- Fuzz Elements Fire
- Fuzz Elements Wind
- Fuzz Elements Water
- Fuzz Elements Void

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none separately promoted beyond the Fuzz Elements family in the reviewed material

Verification notes:
- The **Fuzz Elements** series is explicitly marketed by Maxon as a fuzz family. Each named element is therefore recorded as a separate functional product identity under Fuzz.
- Technical references to distortion within the Fuzz Elements descriptions do not create a second Distortion category because the manufacturer presents these products as fuzz units.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **OSD-9**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer title explicitly combines both.
- **RTD800**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer documentation describes independent overdrive and distortion circuits.
- **ROD881**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer naming explicitly combines the two target categories.
- **ST-9 / SD-9 / SM-9Pro**: category assignment follows explicit product naming. Historical Sonic Distortion / Super Metal products are not upgraded to Overdrive merely because they can produce driven sounds.
- **SD-9**: historical Distortion identity. The older unit's broader gain behavior does not by itself create an Overdrive identity.
- **TBO-9**: Overdrive only. Booster functionality is outside the target taxonomy, while Maxon explicitly identifies the pedal as booster / overdrive.
- **Fuzz Elements family**: Fuzz only. Distortion-like technical language does not create a second category without explicit manufacturer classification.
- **D&S / D&S II**: Distortion only in this census. Sustainer functionality does not create another target category.

## Historical / variant handling
- Maxon maintains current and legacy product families, and this block separates clearly named later revisions from original products where the manufacturer gives them distinct functional names.
- **OD-9 Pro+** remains separate from **OD-9** because the manufacturer assigns it a distinct product name and revised functionality.
- **OD808X** remains separate from **OD808** because it is a separately named product.
- **ST9Pro+** remains separate from historical **ST-9** because Maxon assigns the newer unit a distinct product identity.
- **SD-9M** remains separate from historical **SD-9** because the manufacturer gives it a distinct model name.
- **SM-9Pro+** remains separate from historical **SM-9** for the same reason.
- Cosmetic finishes, anniversary editions, and minor presentation changes are consolidated when they do not establish a distinct functional product identity.
- **Maxon vs. Ibanez**: Maxon products are recorded under Maxon here rather than merged into the earlier Ibanez block, even where historical lineage is shared. Same-name products across recognized builders remain separate identities under the archive's governance rule.
- Godlyke's Maxon pages are treated as authoritative archival references rather than evidence of a current 2026 U.S. sales relationship; Maxon's manufacturer identity remains the basis for builder attribution.

## Exclusions
- Boost-only Maxon products and clean booster functions are excluded from the target census unless the manufacturer explicitly labels the product as Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.
- Modulation, delay, reverb, EQ, compressor, noise gate, interface, and other non-target products are excluded.
- Same-circuit or same-family products from Ibanez are not merged into Maxon merely because of historical lineage.
- Generic terms such as gain, saturation, clipping, or “distortion” used only as a technical or sonic description do not add another category.

## Primary source URLs
- https://maxon.co.jp/
- https://www.maxonfx.com/
- https://maxonfx.com/collections/overdrive
- https://maxonfx.com/collections/distortion
- https://maxonfx.com/collections/fuzz
- https://maxonfx.com/products/od808
- https://maxonfx.com/products/od-9
- https://maxonfx.com/products/od820-overdrive-pro
- https://maxonfx.com/products/osd-9-overdrive-soft-distortion
- https://maxonfx.com/products/rtd800-real-tube-overdrive-distortion
- https://maxonfx.com/products/rod881-real-overdrive-distortion
- https://godlyke.com/collections/maxon

## Checkpoint
Block 071 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Maxon” returned no matches before this block was added, so Maxon is treated as unrepresented in the active census. Maxon is kept separate from the earlier Ibanez builder record despite historical product lineage. The block records a broad Maxon Overdrive set, explicit Distortion identities, and the seven Fuzz Elements products. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–071.

# Block 072 — DOD Electronics

## Builder
**DOD Electronics / DOD**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer / authoritative sources reviewed:
- DigiTech/DOD official History page
- current DOD product pages hosted by DigiTech
- official DOD/DigiTech Product Manuals archive
- official DOD shop/catalog pages
- authoritative DOD catalog archive material from AmericasPedals.net for historical FX-series identities not currently surfaced on the manufacturer site

The current manufacturer history explicitly distinguishes DOD and DigiTech as two brands under one continuing legacy. This block records **DOD** only and does not merge DigiTech-branded HardWire, X-Series, Artist Series, or other DigiTech products into the builder census.

The active repository contained no prior DOD match before this block was added. Dirt categories are based on explicit manufacturer product names, catalog labels, manuals, or strong authoritative catalog documentation. Generic references to gain, saturation, clipping, or a product reaching another sound are not used to create a second target category unless the source explicitly supports Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Overdrive Preamp 250
- Overdrive Preamp 250-X
- Looking Glass
- Badder Monkey

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- FX50-B Overdrive Plus
- FX51 Juice Box Overdrive
- FX91 Bass Overdrive
- FX100 Even Harmonic Overdrive
- FX101 Grind Rectifying Overdrive
- FX102 Mystic Blues Overdrive
- YJM 308 Overdrive Preamp

Verification notes:
- **Overdrive Preamp 250** is explicitly presented as an Overdrive Preamp and is the current DOD continuation of the original 250 design. Its current product information also calls it Distortion + Boost, so it is retained in Distortion below as an explicit overlap.
- **Overdrive Preamp 250-X** is explicitly presented as the tenth evolution of the DOD 250, with three selectable overdrive modes and a gain control covering subtle grit through full distortion. It remains Overdrive only because the manufacturer frames the primary product identity as Overdrive Preamp rather than as a separate Distortion product.
- **Looking Glass** is explicitly presented as an Overdrive and describes low- to high-gain overdrive modes. Its references to overdrive/distortion in the control descriptions are treated as operating-range language, not a separate Distortion identity.
- **Badder Monkey** explicitly provides three independent overdrives and retains the DOD Bad Monkey identity, so it is recorded as Overdrive only.
- **FX50-B Overdrive Plus**, **FX51 Juice Box Overdrive**, **FX91 Bass Overdrive**, **FX100 Even Harmonic Overdrive**, **FX101 Grind Rectifying Overdrive**, **FX102 Mystic Blues Overdrive**, and **YJM 308 Overdrive Preamp** are explicitly documented by DOD's official manuals/catalog archive as Overdrive products.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Overdrive Preamp 250
- Gonkulator
- Gunslinger
- Boneshaker

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- FX55C Supra Distortion
- FX56 American Metal
- FX57 Hard Rock Distortion
- FX58 Metal Maniac
- FX59 Thrash Master
- FX69B Grunge
- FX70 Metal X
- FX76 Punkifier
- FX86 Death Metal Distortion
- D&S / D&S II Distortion & Sustainer
- Performer 555 Distortion
- FX33 Buzz Box

Verification notes:
- **Overdrive Preamp 250** is explicitly identified in current manufacturer product information as Distortion + Boost while remaining an Overdrive Preamp, so it is recorded in both categories.
- **Gonkulator** is a DOD ring-modulator product with an explicit distortion stage/control on the current manufacturer page; it is retained as Distortion while the ring-modulation function remains outside scope.
- **Gunslinger** is explicitly described as aggressive MOSFET distortion by DOD.
- **Boneshaker** is a DOD distortion/dirty-boost product with an explicit Distortion control; the EQ and boost functions are secondary and outside the target taxonomy.
- **FX55C**, **FX57**, **FX59**, **FX70**, and **FX86** are explicitly identified in DOD's official manuals archive as Supra Distortion, Hard Rock Distortion, Thrash Master, Metal X, and Death Metal Distortion respectively.
- **FX56 American Metal** and **FX58 Metal Maniac** are historically documented DOD distortion products in authoritative DOD catalog/archive material.
- **FX69B Grunge** is explicitly documented by DOD as a DOD Grunge pedal and is retained as Distortion based on DOD's current history describing the model as an extreme grind/distortion design and its manufacturer documentation identifying the product as DOD Grunge.
- **FX76 Punkifier** is historically documented in DOD-era product material as a distortion pedal capable of overdrive-to-fuzz textures. It remains Distortion only for this census because the manufacturer-era product identity is distortion rather than a separate Fuzz product.
- **D&S / D&S II** are explicit Distortion & Sustainer products; sustainer functionality is outside the target taxonomy.
- **Performer 555** is explicitly a Distortion product in the DOD Performer catalog.
- **FX33 Buzz Box** is retained in Distortion because its documented DOD signal path includes the FX69 Grunge distortion stage; it is also retained in Fuzz below because authoritative product documentation identifies it as an octave-fuzz product.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Carcosa Fuzz
- Chthonic Fuzz

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- FX33 Buzz Box
- FX52 Classic Fuzz
- FX66 Flashback Fuzz

Verification notes:
- **Carcosa Fuzz** is explicitly presented by DOD as an analog fuzz.
- **Chthonic Fuzz** is explicitly presented by DOD as a fuzz, with the manufacturer describing a broad classic-to-extreme fuzz range.
- **FX33 Buzz Box** is documented as an octave-fuzz product combining a DOD Grunge distortion path with a divided-octave path; it therefore receives both Distortion and Fuzz.
- **FX52 Classic Fuzz** is an explicit historical DOD Classic Fuzz identity in DOD FX-series catalog material.
- **FX66 Flashback Fuzz** is an explicit historical DOD Flashback Fuzz identity in the official DOD manuals/catalog archive.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Overdrive Preamp 250**: Overdrive + Distortion. The manufacturer simultaneously presents it as an Overdrive Preamp and as Distortion + Boost.
- **Overdrive Preamp 250-X**: Overdrive only. Its heavy-distortion capability is treated as a range descriptor while the product identity remains Overdrive Preamp.
- **Looking Glass**: Overdrive only. Manufacturer product identity and operating modes emphasize overdrive; distortion wording is descriptive of the gain range.
- **Badder Monkey**: Overdrive only. Manufacturer explicitly identifies three overdrives.
- **Gonkulator**: Distortion only. Distortion is explicit; ring modulation is outside the target taxonomy.
- **Gunslinger**: Distortion only. Manufacturer explicitly calls it MOSFET Distortion / aggressive distortion.
- **Boneshaker**: Distortion only. The product exposes a Distortion control and is presented as a distortion/dirty-boost design; boost and EQ are secondary functions.
- **FX33 Buzz Box**: Distortion + Fuzz. Its historical architecture combines the DOD Grunge distortion path with an octave-fuzz function, and authoritative documentation identifies both effects.
- **FX76 Punkifier**: Distortion only. Its range from overdrive to fuzz is treated as tonal range within a distortion product identity, not as two separate target categories.
- **Carcosa / Chthonic / FX52 / FX66**: Fuzz only. Distortion-like language does not create an additional category when the manufacturer explicitly presents the product as Fuzz.

## Historical / variant handling
- DOD has a long FX-series catalog and a current revival/reissue line. Clearly named functional products are retained even when later reissues use the same underlying design.
- **Overdrive Preamp 250 50th Anniversary** is consolidated into the 250 identity rather than treated as a new dirt model because it is an anniversary presentation of the same core product identity.
- **Overdrive Preamp 250-X** remains separate because DOD explicitly identifies it as the tenth evolution with a new three-position clipping system.
- **FX55B / FX55C Supra Distortion**: the currently documented FX55C identity is retained; minor suffix/version presentation is not inflated into multiple independent dirt identities unless the manufacturer treats the model as a distinct functional product.
- **FX56 / FX56B** and other closely related FX-series revisions are consolidated where they represent the same named functional family, while distinct named follow-ons such as FX57 and FX58 are retained separately.
- DOD's current manufacturer history identifies DOD as the analog-pedal brand and DigiTech as the separate digital/effects brand. DigiTech-branded products are therefore excluded from this builder block even when the products appear on the same manufacturer website.
- Cosmetic, regional, anniversary, and minor hardware variations are consolidated unless DOD gives the product a distinct functional identity.

## Exclusions
- DigiTech-branded HardWire, X-Series, Artist Series, D-Series, Whammy, and other DigiTech products: excluded from the DOD builder block.
- Boost-only DOD products such as BiFET Boost 410: excluded unless the manufacturer explicitly identifies the product as Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.
- Compressors, envelope filters, phasers, choruses, delays, reverbs, octave-only products, loopers, mixers, power products, and other non-target effects: excluded.
- Generic references to saturation, clipping, “distortion,” or high gain do not add categories without product-level target-category support.

## Primary source URLs
- https://digitech.com/history/
- https://digitech.com/product-manuals/
- https://digitech.com/shop/
- https://digitech.com/dp/overdrive-preamp-250/
- https://digitech.com/dp/overdrive-preamp-250-x/
- https://digitech.com/dp/looking-glass/
- https://digitech.com/dp/badder-monkey/
- https://digitech.com/dp/carcosa-fuzz/
- https://digitech.com/dp/chthonic-fuzz/
- https://digitech.com/dp/gonkulator/
- https://digitech.com/dp/gunslinger/
- https://digitech.com/dp/boneshaker/
- https://digitech.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/DOD-FX69B-Grunge-Manual.pdf
- https://americaspedals.net/cat.html
- https://americaspedals.net/

## Checkpoint
Block 072 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository searches for “DOD,” “DOD Electronics,” and representative DOD model names returned no prior matches before this block was added, so DOD is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block keeps DOD distinct from DigiTech, records the current 250/250-X/Looking Glass/Badder Monkey/Carcosa/Chthonic/Gonkulator/Gunslinger/Boneshaker lineage, and preserves a conservative historical FX-series dirt census. FX33 Buzz Box is recorded as Distortion + Fuzz. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–072.

# Block 073 — DigiTech

## Builder
**DigiTech**

## Verification basis
Primary manufacturer / authoritative sources reviewed:
- DigiTech official History page
- DigiTech official Product Manuals archive
- DigiTech official FAQ and power-supply compatibility archive
- manufacturer-authored DigiTech product manuals and archived product documentation

The manufacturer explicitly describes DOD as the analog-pedal brand and DigiTech as the separate DSP/effects brand. This block therefore records **DigiTech-branded** dirt pedals separately from the DOD builder recorded in Block 072. The current DigiTech site continues to host manuals and legacy product records for discontinued products, while the current shop is focused primarily on active DigiTech and DOD offerings.

The active repository contained no prior DigiTech match before this block was added. Dirt categories are based on explicit manufacturer product titles, catalog labels, manuals, or authoritative manufacturer-originated documentation. Generic references to gain, clipping, saturation, or a product reaching another sound are not used to create a second target category unless the product documentation explicitly supports Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none explicitly surfaced as a current DigiTech-branded standalone dirt pedal in the reviewed current shop/catalog

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Screamin' Blues Overdrive / Distortion
- Redline Modified Overdrive
- X-Series Tone Driver
- X-Series Bass Driver

Verification notes:
- **Screamin' Blues Overdrive / Distortion** is explicitly titled as both Overdrive and Distortion in its manufacturer manual and is retained in both categories below.
- **Redline Modified Overdrive** is explicitly identified by DigiTech as an overdrive pedal. Manufacturer documentation describes its circuit as overdriving the guitar signal with extra gain and low-end emphasis.
- **X-Series Tone Driver** is explicitly documented as an overdrive stompbox whose Morph control blends three distinct overdrive types. It is retained as Overdrive only.
- **X-Series Bass Driver** is explicitly documented as a Bass Driver Overdrive/Distortion pedal and as an advanced overdrive designed specifically for bass. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion below.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none explicitly surfaced as a current DigiTech-branded standalone dirt pedal in the reviewed current shop/catalog

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Screamin' Blues Overdrive / Distortion
- X-Series Bass Driver
- Hot Head Distortion
- DigiTech Grunge
- DigiTech Death Metal
- X-Series Distortion Factory DF-7
- X-Series Hot Rod Distortion
- X-Series Metal Master

Verification notes:
- **Screamin' Blues** is explicitly titled Overdrive/Distortion in the manufacturer manual.
- **X-Series Bass Driver** is explicitly documented as blending distinct overdrive and distortion types.
- **Hot Head Distortion** is explicitly documented as a distortion pedal and its manual identifies the Gain control as controlling distortion.
- **DigiTech Grunge** and **DigiTech Death Metal** are explicitly treated as DigiTech distortion pedals in manufacturer manuals and the manufacturer's modeling documentation.
- **Distortion Factory DF-7** is explicitly a distortion pedal and its documentation calls its available circuits Distortion models.
- **Hot Rod Distortion** is explicitly described as a distortion pedal whose Morph control blends three independent distortion types.
- **Metal Master** is explicitly described as a heavy metal distortion pedal with three different metal distortion types.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none explicitly surfaced as a current DigiTech-branded standalone fuzz pedal in the reviewed current shop/catalog

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none promoted as a DigiTech-branded standalone Fuzz identity in the reviewed material

Verification notes:
- DigiTech's multi-effects processors contain modeled fuzz effects, but those are not standalone DigiTech fuzz pedals and therefore are excluded from this builder block.
- The current **Wah-ocTo-Fuzz** is a DOD/Morley collaboration whose product identity is explicitly presented as a three-way Wah/Octave/Fuzz collaboration. It is not promoted into the DigiTech builder record.
- Manufacturer documentation for the Hot Rod and Bass Driver mentions fuzz-range tones, but those are presented within explicit distortion or overdrive products rather than as separate DigiTech Fuzz product identities.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Screamin' Blues Overdrive / Distortion**: Overdrive + Distortion. The manufacturer title explicitly combines both categories.
- **X-Series Bass Driver**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer documentation explicitly describes both overdrive and distortion types in the pedal.
- **Redline Modified Overdrive**: Overdrive only. Distortion is discussed as a consequence of overdriving the circuit, but the named product identity is Overdrive.
- **X-Series Tone Driver**: Overdrive only. The manufacturer describes three overdrive voicings; gain/distortion wording is treated as operating-range language.
- **Hot Head**: Distortion only. The manufacturer/manual identifies it as Distortion; broad low-gain capability does not create an Overdrive identity.
- **DigiTech Grunge**: Distortion only. Manufacturer documentation presents it as a DigiTech distortion pedal; its aggressive gain range does not create a separate Fuzz identity.
- **DigiTech Death Metal**: Distortion only. Manufacturer documentation presents it as a distortion pedal.
- **DF-7 Distortion Factory**: Distortion only. The product itself is a distortion pedal even though its modeled library includes circuits associated with overdrive/fuzz products from other builders.
- **Hot Rod Distortion**: Distortion only. Its high-gain fuzz-like range is a distortion voicing rather than a separate Fuzz identity.
- **Metal Master**: Distortion only. Manufacturer documentation explicitly identifies the product as heavy-metal distortion.

## Historical / variant handling
- DigiTech's official manual archive distinguishes current products from a large discontinued catalog. This block retains clearly named historical standalone dirt pedals where manufacturer documentation supports the target category.
- **Screamin' Blues** remains one named functional product even though its title spans Overdrive and Distortion.
- **X-Series Tone Driver, Hot Rod, Metal Master, and Distortion Factory DF-7** remain separate named X-Series functional products.
- **X-Series Bass Driver** remains separate because it has its own bass-specific product identity and documented overdrive/distortion architecture.
- **DigiTech Grunge** is not merged into the DOD Grunge identity from Block 072. The manufacturer documentation itself explains that the DigiTech Grunge lineage originated from the DOD FX69 design, but the DigiTech-branded product is a separate named product identity under this builder block.
- Cosmetic editions, minor hardware revisions, and packaging differences are consolidated unless the manufacturer assigns a distinct functional model name.
- HardWire products such as CM-2 Tube Overdrive, SC-2 Valve Distortion, and TL-2 Metal Distortion are excluded because the manufacturer's manual archive presents **HardWire** as a distinct product brand rather than DigiTech-branded products.

## Exclusions
- DOD-branded products such as Bad Monkey, 250, Carcosa Fuzz, Chthonic Fuzz, and Gunslinger: excluded because they belong to the separate DOD builder block.
- HardWire CM-2, SC-2, TL-2, and other HardWire products: excluded as a separate brand.
- DigiTech multi-effects processors and their internal modeled distortion/overdrive/fuzz algorithms: excluded because they are not standalone dirt pedals.
- Wah-ocTo-Fuzz: excluded from DigiTech because the manufacturer presents it as a DOD/Morley collaboration rather than a DigiTech-branded product.
- Boost-only, modulation, pitch, delay, reverb, compressor, synth, looper, volume, wah, and other non-target DigiTech products: excluded.
- Generic gain, saturation, clipping, or fuzz-adjacent wording does not create another target category without explicit product-level support.

## Primary source URLs
- https://digitech.com/history/
- https://digitech.com/product-manuals/
- https://digitech.com/faqs/
- https://digitech.com/support/powersupplylist/
- https://digitech.com/dp/wah-octo-fuzz/
- https://digitech.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/RP1000_OM_EN.pdf
- https://digitech.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/BP355_Manual_5059482-A_original.pdf

## Checkpoint
Block 073 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “DigiTech” returned no prior matches before this block was added, so DigiTech is treated as unrepresented in the active census. DigiTech is kept separate from DOD and HardWire according to the manufacturer's own brand presentation. The block records a focused historical DigiTech standalone dirt census: Screamin' Blues and Bass Driver as Overdrive + Distortion, Redline Modified Overdrive and Tone Driver as Overdrive, and Hot Head, Grunge, Death Metal, DF-7, Hot Rod, and Metal Master as Distortion. No standalone DigiTech Fuzz identity was promoted from the reviewed material. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–073.
