# Block 074 — Ramble FX

## Builder
**Ramble FX**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- archived Ramble FX website references and product identity references
- manufacturer/product documentation where available in archived dealer and manual records
- Effects Database Ramble FX brand catalog
- Guitar Pedal X archival profile of Ramble FX and Tim Krueger
- authoritative pedal documentation/manual reference for Twin Bender V3
- specialist retailer/product records for named Ramble FX models

Ramble FX is treated as a distinct builder identity. The active repository contained no prior Ramble FX match before this block was added.

The surviving documentation indicates a compact Ramble FX catalog centered on three clearly documented dirt pedals: Kismet, Marvel Drive, and Twin Bender. Dirt categories are assigned from explicit product descriptions or strong product-category documentation. Generic references to gain, clipping, harmonics, or fuzz-like behavior are not used to create unsupported secondary categories.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none currently produced

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Kismet
- Marvel Drive

Verification notes:
- **Kismet** is documented as an analog Overdrive/Distortion pedal with digital control. Its wide gain range runs from dirty-boost/overdrive territory into substantial distortion. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **Marvel Drive** is documented as a Plexi-style Overdrive / amp-in-a-box design recreating the blended four-input Marshall gain-stage behavior. It is retained as Overdrive only for this census because its named product identity and strongest product-category references identify it as an overdrive/drive pedal rather than a separate Distortion product.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none currently produced

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Kismet

Verification notes:
- **Kismet** is explicitly documented as Analog Overdrive/Distortion and as a wide-ranging distortion pedal. It therefore receives both target categories.
- **Marvel Drive** can reach aggressive distortion textures, but the surviving product references identify it as a Plexi-style overdrive/drive product. No separate Distortion identity is created from range language alone.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none currently produced

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Twin Bender

Verification notes:
- **Twin Bender** is explicitly documented as a Tone Bender-based Fuzz. The V3 manual describes MKII Pro and MKI.V modes, three-transistor/two-transistor fuzz stages, fuzz control, and germanium operation. It is therefore recorded as Fuzz only.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Kismet**: Overdrive + Distortion. Product descriptions explicitly use both categories.
- **Marvel Drive**: Overdrive only. The product is consistently presented as a Plexi-style overdrive/drive device; aggressive gain is treated as range rather than a separate Distortion identity.
- **Twin Bender**: Fuzz only. The product is explicitly a Tone Bender-style fuzz; amplifier-pushing or gain language does not create an Overdrive/Distortion identity.

## Historical / variant handling
- Ramble FX was a small catalog, and surviving documentation centers on three core dirt products: Kismet, Marvel Drive, and Twin Bender.
- **Marvel Drive V1 / V2 / V3** are treated as revisions of the same functional product identity unless the builder presents a distinct named model. The later **Marvel Drive 3** remains the named identity in the census rather than generating separate entries for every version.
- **Twin Bender MK I.V / II / V3** are retained as the Twin Bender functional identity rather than separate dirt products. Distinct mode names inside the pedal do not create new builder/product identities.
- **Kismet** remains a single functional identity despite its programmable presets and digital control layer; those are control features, not separate dirt models.
- Cosmetic colors and limited presentation variants are consolidated.
- Ramble FX is treated as historical/discontinued in this census. The builder's product lineage is still retained because the archive's scope includes historical dirt pedals.

## Exclusions
- Non-dirt utility functions and digital preset/MIDI control features: excluded as secondary functionality.
- Generic distortion/gain language on Marvel Drive: insufficient for a separate Distortion identity.
- Any unverified fourth model or ambiguous “Rainier” listing: excluded. The reviewed specialist references conflict on that name, and no sufficiently strong Ramble FX product-level evidence was found to promote it into the census.
- Cloning, schematics, internal construction, component details, and other technical reverse-engineering material: outside scope.

## Primary source URLs
- https://www.ramblefx.com/
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/ramblefx
- https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/gpx-blog/celebrating-the-works-of-ramble-fxs-tim-krueger
- https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/gpx-blog/2022-september-pedal-chain-update---episode-ix---a-ramble-through-history
- https://www.rockboard.de/en/pedalPedia/Ramble-FX/Twin-Bender-V3/68983657/
- https://www.rockboard.de/en/pedalPedia/Ramble-FX/Kismet/68983653/
- https://www.rockboard.de/en/pedalPedia/Ramble-FX/Marvel-3-Drive/384378765/

## Checkpoint
Block 074 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Ramble FX” returned no prior matches before this block was added, so Ramble FX is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records Kismet as Overdrive + Distortion, Marvel Drive as Overdrive, and Twin Bender as Fuzz. The ambiguous “Rainier” name was deliberately excluded rather than promoted without adequate product-level evidence. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–074.

# Block 075 — Voodoo Lab

## Builder
**Voodoo Lab**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Voodoo Lab official Effects catalog
- Voodoo Lab official product pages
- Voodoo Lab official manuals/download archive
- Voodoo Lab official Legacy product index
- Voodoo Lab official FAQ and manufacturer-authored manuals

The active repository contained no prior Voodoo Lab match before this block was added. Voodoo Lab is treated as the builder identity for the pedal products listed under the Voodoo Lab brand.

The reviewed manufacturer material supports five clearly documented dirt identities: GIGGITY, Sparkle Drive, Sparkle Drive MOD, Superfuzz, and Proctavia. Dirt categories are assigned from explicit manufacturer product descriptions or manufacturer category wording. Generic references to grit, saturation, or distortion-like behavior are not used to create unsupported secondary identities.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- GIGGITY
- Sparkle Drive MOD

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Sparkle Drive

Verification notes:
- **GIGGITY** is explicitly presented by Voodoo Lab as an Analog Overdrive + Preamp and is retained as Overdrive.
- **Sparkle Drive MOD** is explicitly described as carrying all the original Sparkle Drive overdrive tones and is retained as Overdrive. Voodoo Lab also explicitly calls its four selectable circuits overdrive/distortion circuits, so it receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **Sparkle Drive** is explicitly described as an overdrive pedal with a vintage-808-style overdrive circuit and is retained as historical Overdrive.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Sparkle Drive MOD

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none

Verification notes:
- **Sparkle Drive MOD** receives the Distortion category because Voodoo Lab's official product page explicitly describes its four selectable modes as "overdrive/distortion circuits." This is product-level category evidence rather than an inference from high-gain behavior alone.
- **GIGGITY** reaches gritty and compressed tones as its Loudness control is increased, but Voodoo Lab identifies the product as Overdrive + Preamp rather than a separate Distortion pedal. It therefore remains Overdrive only for this census.
- **Sparkle Drive** is retained as Overdrive only because the manufacturer identifies its effect as an overdrive circuit; its ability to increase saturation does not create a separate Distortion identity.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Superfuzz

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Proctavia

Verification notes:
- **Superfuzz** is explicitly documented by Voodoo Lab as an analog circuit for classic 1960s fuzz sounds and is retained as Fuzz.
- **Proctavia** is explicitly documented as a fuzz/octave pedal and as an exact replica of the Tycobrahe Octavia. It is retained as Fuzz only. The manufacturer's references to outrageous distortion describe its sound, but the named product identity is the octave-fuzz effect rather than a separate Distortion product.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **GIGGITY**: Overdrive only. Manufacturer taxonomy explicitly identifies Analog Overdrive + Preamp.
- **Sparkle Drive MOD**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer documentation explicitly uses both terms for its selectable circuits and its inherited overdrive tones.
- **Sparkle Drive**: Overdrive only. Manufacturer identifies it as an overdrive pedal with a vintage-808 overdrive circuit.
- **Superfuzz**: Fuzz only. Manufacturer explicitly identifies its circuit as fuzz.
- **Proctavia**: Fuzz only. Manufacturer explicitly identifies it as a fuzz/octave pedal.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Sparkle Drive** is retained as a historical identity because Voodoo Lab's current Legacy index lists it as a legacy/out-of-production product while the current catalog carries Sparkle Drive MOD.
- **Proctavia** is treated as historical/legacy for the census because it appears in Voodoo Lab's Legacy product index, while the current Effects catalog highlights GIGGITY, Sparkle Drive MOD, and Superfuzz.
- **Sparkle Drive MOD** is not merged into Sparkle Drive. The manufacturer presents MOD as a distinct named product with additional selectable circuits, so it remains a separate functional product identity.
- Product versions, finishes, stock status, and presentation variants do not create additional dirt identities.
- Preamp functionality on GIGGITY is secondary to its explicit Overdrive identity and does not create another target category.
- Octave functionality on Proctavia is secondary to its explicit Fuzz identity and does not create another target category.

## Exclusions
- Non-dirt Voodoo Lab products such as Tremolo, Micro Vibe, Analog Chorus, Wahzoo, switching, power, and pedalboard products: outside the target taxonomy.
- The Voodoo Lab Guitar Preamp is not promoted into the dirt census because the reviewed manufacturer material does not provide sufficiently clear target-category wording to establish it as an Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz pedal identity.
- Generic grit, compression, saturation, or distortion language without explicit product-category evidence: insufficient for secondary categorization.
- Cloning, schematics, internal construction, component details, and other technical reverse-engineering material: outside scope.

## Primary source URLs
- https://voodoolab.com/about-us/
- https://voodoolab.com/product-category/effects/
- https://voodoolab.com/product/giggity/
- https://voodoolab.com/product/sparkle-drive/
- https://voodoolab.com/product/sparkle-drive-mod/
- https://voodoolab.com/product/superfuzz/
- https://voodoolab.com/product/proctavia/
- https://voodoolab.com/product-category/legacy-products/
- https://voodoolab.com/manuals-dir/
- https://voodoolab.com/download/
- https://voodoolab.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/SparkleDriveMOD_manual-2.pdf
- https://voodoolab.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/proctavia_manual.pdf

## Checkpoint
Block 075 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Voodoo Lab” returned no prior match before this block was added, so Voodoo Lab is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records GIGGITY as Overdrive, Sparkle Drive MOD as Overdrive + Distortion, Sparkle Drive as historical Overdrive, Superfuzz as Fuzz, and Proctavia as historical Fuzz. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–075.

# Block 076 — Blackstar Amplification

## Builder
**Blackstar Amplification**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Blackstar official HT-series pedal catalog
- Blackstar official LT-series pedal catalog
- Blackstar official Dept. 10 product pages
- Blackstar manufacturer-authored product manuals
- Blackstar official product documentation / declarations

The active repository contained no prior Blackstar match before this block was added. Blackstar Amplification is treated as the builder identity for the pedals sold under the Blackstar brand, including its HT, LT, and Dept. 10 pedal families.

The reviewed manufacturer material supports a substantial historical dirt catalog. Dirt categories are assigned from explicit product names, manufacturer category descriptions, or clearly documented functional modes. Generic references to gain, saturation, breakup, or distortion-like behavior do not by themselves create an extra category.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Dept. 10 Dual Drive
- Dept. 10 Dual Distortion

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- HT-DRIVE
- HT-DUAL
- HT-METAL
- LT-DRIVE
- LT-DUAL

Verification notes:
- **HT-DRIVE** is explicitly presented by Blackstar as PURE VALVE OVERDRIVE and delivers gain from warm boost to screaming valve saturation. It is retained as Overdrive only.
- **HT-DUAL** is explicitly presented as a valve distortion pedal whose Channel 1 can be used as a boost or overdrive, while Channel 2 supplies distortion. It therefore receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **HT-METAL** is explicitly described as a valve distortion pedal with a Clean/Overdrive switch on Channel 1. It therefore receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **LT-DRIVE** is explicitly presented by Blackstar as a drive pedal whose range runs from warm boost to screaming lead saturation. It is retained as Overdrive only.
- **LT-DUAL** is explicitly described as a two-channel distortion pedal with Channel 1 moving from clean to boost to overdrive. It therefore receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **Dept. 10 Dual Drive** is explicitly presented by Blackstar as a valve overdrive pedal / preamp. Its manufacturer documentation also describes channels that move through overdrive and distortion. It therefore receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **Dept. 10 Dual Distortion** is explicitly presented by Blackstar as a valve distortion pedal and also as an overdrive pedal / preamp. Its Channel 2 uses Overdrive 1 and Overdrive 2 voices. It therefore receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Dept. 10 Dual Drive
- Dept. 10 Dual Distortion

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- HT-DIST
- HT-DISTX
- HT-DUAL
- HT-METAL
- LT-DIST
- LT-DUAL
- LT-METAL

Verification notes:
- **HT-DIST** is explicitly presented as PURE VALVE DISTORTION. It remains Distortion only despite manual language describing the Gain control as moving from overdrive to distortion, because the product-level manufacturer identity is a distortion pedal and the archive avoids promoting generic control-range wording to a second category.
- **HT-DISTX** is explicitly presented as PURE VALVE FILTH and as an ultra-high-gain pedal. It is retained as Distortion only.
- **HT-DUAL** is explicitly a valve distortion pedal and supplies distortion through Channel 2, while Channel 1 can operate as boost or overdrive. It receives both categories.
- **HT-METAL** is explicitly a valve distortion pedal with a Clean/Overdrive mode on Channel 1 and super-high-gain lead modes. It receives both categories.
- **LT-DIST** is explicitly described as classic high-gain distortion. It remains Distortion only.
- **LT-DUAL** is explicitly titled TWO CHANNEL DISTORTION and combines an overdrive channel with higher-gain distortion/lead operation. It receives both categories.
- **LT-METAL** is explicitly designed for aggressive high-gain tones and is presented within Blackstar's LT distortion family. It remains Distortion only.
- **Dept. 10 Dual Drive** is documented as a valve overdrive pedal with multiple clean and distortion voices and channels that move through overdrive to full distorted lead tones. It receives both categories.
- **Dept. 10 Dual Distortion** is documented as a valve distortion pedal with Clean, Crunch, and Overdrive voices and as an overdrive pedal / preamp on the current manufacturer page. It receives both categories.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- The reviewed Blackstar manufacturer catalogs and pedal manuals do not establish a standalone Blackstar-branded Fuzz pedal identity. Fuzz-like sound language is not promoted into the census without product-level category evidence.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **HT-DRIVE**: Overdrive only. Manufacturer category is explicit Overdrive.
- **HT-DIST**: Distortion only. Product-level identity is explicit Distortion; generic Gain-control overdrive wording is not sufficient for a second category.
- **HT-DISTX**: Distortion only. Manufacturer presents it as ultra-high-gain filth / distortion rather than as a separately named overdrive product.
- **HT-DUAL**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer explicitly says Channel 1 can be used as a boost or overdrive and Channel 2 delivers distortion.
- **HT-METAL**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer explicitly includes a Clean/Overdrive switch plus high-gain distortion operation.
- **LT-DRIVE**: Overdrive only. Manufacturer product category is Drive/overdrive.
- **LT-DIST**: Distortion only. Manufacturer presents it as classic high-gain distortion; generic overdrive control-range wording is not promoted separately.
- **LT-DUAL**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer explicitly describes Channel 1 as clean/boost/overdrive and Channel 2 as higher-gain distortion/lead.
- **LT-METAL**: Distortion only. Manufacturer presents it as aggressive high-gain and as part of the LT distortion family.
- **Dept. 10 Dual Drive**: Overdrive + Distortion. Current manufacturer material calls it a valve overdrive pedal while describing clean and distortion voices and an overdrive-to-distortion range.
- **Dept. 10 Dual Distortion**: Overdrive + Distortion. Current manufacturer material calls it a valve distortion pedal and also an overdrive pedal / preamp, with explicit Overdrive 1 / Overdrive 2 modes.

## Historical / variant handling
- HT, LT, and earlier valve-pedal generations are retained because the archive includes historical dirt products.
- Product generations and manufacturer revisions are consolidated when they represent the same named functional identity. Cosmetic finishes and stock/presentation variants do not create additional identities.
- **HT-DISTX** remains a separate identity from HT-DIST because Blackstar gave it a distinct product name and positioned it as the higher-gain "filth" model.
- **HT-DUAL** remains a separate identity because it is a distinct two-channel product rather than a revision of HT-DIST.
- **LT-DUAL** remains a separate identity because Blackstar presented it as a distinct two-channel product in the LT family.
- Dept. 10 Dual Drive and Dept. 10 Dual Distortion remain separate named products. The manufacturer currently lists both as distinct products and differentiates their voice sets.
- Internal mode selections, Cab Rig settings, effects-loop functions, USB audio, and preamp/direct-recording features remain secondary features and do not create additional dirt identities.

## Exclusions
- **HT-BOOST**: boost product, not promoted into the target Overdrive/Distortion/Fuzz census even though its Gain control can push an amplifier into overdrive and distortion.
- **Dept. 10 Boost**: boost product, excluded for the same reason.
- Amplifier models, footcontrollers, software/modeling effects, cabinet simulation, utility products, and other non-dirt Blackstar products: outside target taxonomy.
- Generic references to gain, saturation, breakup, grit, or distortion without sufficient product-level classification: excluded as secondary categories.
- Cloning, schematics, internal construction, component details, and reverse-engineering material: outside scope.

## Primary source URLs
- https://blackstaramps.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/09/blackstar-product-catalogue-2015.pdf
- https://blackstaramps.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/08/lt-drive-handbook.pdf
- https://blackstaramps.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/08/lt-dist-handbook.pdf
- https://www.blackstaramps.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/10/ht-drive-handbook.pdf
- https://blackstaramps.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/10/ht-dist-handbook.pdf
- https://blackstaramps.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/10/ht-distx-handbook.pdf
- https://blackstaramps.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/195401M-VB-1-DEPT.-10-DUAL-DRIVE-OWNERS-MANUAL-04-11-21.pdf
- https://blackstaramps.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/195401M-VA-1-DEPT.-10-DUAL-DISTORTION-OWNERS-MANUAL-04-11-21.pdf
- https://blackstaramps.us/products/dept10ddr
- https://blackstaramps.us/products/dept10dds

## Checkpoint
Block 076 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository searches for “Blackstar Amplification,” “HT-DRIVE,” “HT-DISTX,” and “HT-METAL” returned no prior matches before this block was added, so Blackstar is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records HT-DRIVE and LT-DRIVE as Overdrive, HT-DUAL and HT-METAL as Overdrive + Distortion, LT-DUAL as Overdrive + Distortion, Dept. 10 Dual Drive and Dept. 10 Dual Distortion as Overdrive + Distortion, the remaining documented HT/LT dirt products as Distortion where applicable, and no standalone Blackstar Fuzz identity. Next block should begin with another builder or builder group not already represented in Blocks 001–076.

# Block 077 — Bogner Amplification

## Builder
**Bogner Amplification**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Bogner-authored pedal manuals and product documentation where preserved in manufacturer-branded documents
- Bogner product descriptions reproduced in authoritative product documentation
- established specialist product catalogs and retailer records for model/category confirmation
- surviving Bogner pedal family references covering the Ecstasy, Uberschall, Transformer, and La Grange families

The active repository contained no prior Bogner match before this block was added. Bogner Amplification is treated as the builder identity for the Bogner-branded dirt pedals documented below.

The reviewed material supports seven clearly documented core dirt identities: Ecstasy Blue, Ecstasy Red, Uberschall, Wessex, Burnley, La Grange, and Oxford. Dirt categories are assigned from explicit product/category descriptions. Generic references to gain range, breakup, or aggressive tone are not used to manufacture additional categories.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- production status not established from reviewed current manufacturer material

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Ecstasy Blue
- Ecstasy Red
- Wessex
- La Grange

Verification notes:
- **Ecstasy Blue** is documented as an overdrive pedal based on the Ecstasy amplifier's Blue channel. Product descriptions emphasize classic-rock crunch, Plexi-style tones, and overdrive operation.
- **Ecstasy Red** is explicitly documented in surviving product material as an Overdrive/Distortion pedal and spans vintage overdrive through high-gain operation. It therefore receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **Wessex** is explicitly presented as an overdrive pedal, with a range from clean boost to moderate overdrive and a selectable higher-gain voicing.
- **La Grange** is consistently documented as an overdrive pedal recreating jumpered 1960s Plexi-style operation. Its wide gain range is retained within the single Overdrive identity rather than creating a separate Distortion identity.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- production status not established from reviewed current manufacturer material

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Ecstasy Red
- Uberschall
- Burnley

Verification notes:
- **Ecstasy Red** is explicitly described as an Overdrive/Distortion pedal and is retained in both target categories.
- **Uberschall** is explicitly presented as a high-gain distortion pedal derived from the Bogner Uberschall amplifier. The preserved Bogner manual describes its savage high-gain character while also noting lower and medium gain ranges. It remains Distortion only because the named product identity is a distortion pedal.
- **Burnley** is documented as a distortion pedal. High-gain and harmonic-range language does not create an additional Overdrive identity because the product-level category is Distortion.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- production status not established from reviewed current manufacturer material

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Oxford

Verification notes:
- **Oxford** is explicitly documented as a fuzz pedal. Its Gain switch spans amp-like breakup through extreme fuzz, but the named product identity and product description are centered on fuzz. It is therefore recorded as Fuzz only.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Ecstasy Blue**: Overdrive only. Product-level references identify it as an overdrive pedal.
- **Ecstasy Red**: Overdrive + Distortion. Surviving product specifications explicitly call it an Overdrive/Distortion pedal and describe its range from vintage overdrive to high gain.
- **Uberschall**: Distortion only. Manufacturer-authored material identifies it as a high-gain distortion pedal; lower gain range is treated as operating range rather than a second product category.
- **Wessex**: Overdrive only. Product-level descriptions identify it as an overdrive pedal.
- **Burnley**: Distortion only. Product-level descriptions identify it as a distortion pedal.
- **La Grange**: Overdrive only. Product-level descriptions identify it as an overdrive pedal; wide gain range does not by itself create a separate Distortion identity.
- **Oxford**: Fuzz only. Product-level descriptions identify it as a fuzz pedal; its low-gain breakup range does not create a separate Overdrive identity.

## Historical / variant handling
- The Bogner pedal lineup represented here spans multiple product generations. The archive records the functional named identities rather than treating every finish, production revision, or enclosure change as a new dirt product.
- **Ecstasy Blue Mini** and **Ecstasy Red Mini** are consolidated with their corresponding Ecstasy Blue and Ecstasy Red functional identities. The minis are reduced-size versions of the same named Ecstasy channel concepts rather than separate builder/product identities for this initial census.
- **Burnley V2** and **Wessex V2** are treated as revisions of the same named products rather than separate dirt identities.
- Current availability is not used as the identity boundary. Historical/discontinued dirt products remain in scope because the archive covers historical builders and products.
- Booster functions on Ecstasy pedals and the independent boost portions of La Grange are treated as secondary functions rather than standalone target products.

## Exclusions
- **Harlow**: boost/compressor-oriented product, excluded from the target dirt taxonomy.
- **Lyndhurst**: compressor, excluded.
- Generic gain, saturation, breakup, or high-gain references without explicit product-level category evidence: excluded as secondary categories.
- Amp models, preamp modules, and non-pedal Bogner products: outside this builder-to-pedal dirt census.
- Cloning, schematics, internal construction, component details, and reverse-engineering material: outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://manualzilla.com/doc/5946316/uberschall-pedal-manual
- https://jam.ua/files/files/BognerEcstasyRed_Guitar_Pedal_Manual.pdf
- https://www.bogneramplification.com/
- https://www.premierguitar.com/bogner-unveils-the-ecstasy-red-and-blue-minis
- https://www.pedalsatdawn.com/brand/bogner/
- https://www.sweetwater.com/store/detail/Oxford--bogner-oxford-fuzz-pedal
- https://www.sweetwater.com/store/detail/Wessex--bogner-wessex-overdrive-pedal
- https://www.sweetwater.com/store/detail/EcstasyRed--bogner-ecstasy-red-overdrive-pedal
- https://reverb.com/p/bogner-la-grange

## Checkpoint
Block 077 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Bogner” returned no prior match before this block was added, so Bogner Amplification is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records Ecstasy Blue, Ecstasy Red, Wessex, and La Grange as Overdrive; Ecstasy Red, Uberschall, and Burnley as Distortion; and Oxford as Fuzz. Ecstasy Blue/Red Mini versions, Burnley V2, and Wessex V2 are consolidated under their core functional identities. Harlow and Lyndhurst are excluded as non-target utility/effect categories.

# Block 078 — MESA/Boogie

## Builder
**MESA/Boogie**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- MESA/Boogie's official Drive Pedals catalog
- MESA/Boogie's official individual pedal pages and manuals archive
- MESA/Boogie's manufacturer-authored product announcement for the original Drive/Distortion lineup
- MESA/Boogie's official Boost Pedals catalog and Tone-Burst product page
- specialist product documentation used only where the manufacturer page does not state a target category as directly

The active repository contained no prior MESA/Boogie match before this block was added. MESA/Boogie is treated as the builder identity for the MESA-branded guitar dirt pedals documented below.

The reviewed material supports nine clearly documented core dirt identities: Cleo, DynaPlex, Gold Mine, Grid Slammer, Flux-Drive, Flux-Five, Throttle Box, Throttle Box EQ, and Tone-Burst. The functional product identity is recorded once even when the manufacturer later revised or expanded the same platform.

## Overdrive
Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Cleo
- DynaPlex
- Gold Mine
- Grid Slammer
- Flux-Drive
- Flux-Five
- Throttle Box
- Throttle Box EQ
- Tone-Burst

Verification notes:
- **Cleo** is explicitly presented as a transparent boost/overdrive pedal spanning subtle boost through harmonically rich overdrive.
- **DynaPlex** is explicitly presented as a medium-gain overdrive pedal.
- **Gold Mine** is explicitly presented within MESA's overdrive line and as the higher-gain member of the Cleo/DynaPlex/Gold Mine group.
- **Grid Slammer** is explicitly presented as an overdrive pedal.
- **Flux-Drive** is explicitly presented as an overdrive pedal, and MESA's launch announcement labels it an Overdrive/Distortion pedal. It therefore receives both categories below.
- **Flux-Five** is explicitly presented as a supercharged Flux-Drive overdrive and retains the original Flux-Drive low-gain mode.
- **Throttle Box** is a high-gain distortion product, but MESA's own product material also calls the platform an overdrive and describes its lower-gain mode as classic-rock/blues territory. It receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **Throttle Box EQ** is the expanded EQ version of the Throttle Box platform. Its manufacturer page explicitly describes its gain range as overdrive; the product is also directly derived from the Throttle Box distortion platform, so it receives both categories in this census.
- **Tone-Burst** is officially described by MESA as a Boost/Overdrive pedal with enough gain for subtle clipped overdrive. It is included under Overdrive despite appearing within the manufacturer's Boost Pedals section.

## Distortion
Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Flux-Drive
- Throttle Box
- Throttle Box EQ

Verification notes:
- **Flux-Drive** receives Distortion because MESA's manufacturer-authored launch material explicitly labels it an Overdrive/Distortion pedal while its dedicated product page identifies it primarily as an overdrive.
- **Throttle Box** is explicitly identified by MESA as a high-gain distortion pedal. Its low-gain mode is treated as operating range, but its manufacturer material also identifies the wider platform with overdrive language, so both target categories are retained.
- **Throttle Box EQ** is an expanded version of the Throttle Box gain platform. The manufacturer explicitly describes overdrive operation and its high-gain range, while authoritative product coverage identifies it as a distortion pedal. It therefore receives both categories.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- The reviewed MESA/Boogie pedal catalog, individual drive pages, boost catalog, and manuals do not establish a standalone MESA-branded Fuzz pedal identity. Generic saturated or fuzzy-sounding language is not promoted into the census without product-level category evidence.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Cleo**: Overdrive only.
- **DynaPlex**: Overdrive only.
- **Gold Mine**: Overdrive only. Its higher-gain saturation is treated as range within an overdrive product.
- **Grid Slammer**: Overdrive only.
- **Flux-Drive**: Overdrive + Distortion. MESA explicitly uses the phrase Overdrive/Distortion in manufacturer launch material.
- **Flux-Five**: Overdrive only. It is explicitly described by MESA as an overdrive and as the enhanced Flux-Drive platform.
- **Throttle Box**: Overdrive + Distortion. MESA identifies it as a high-gain distortion pedal while describing its lower-gain mode and broader platform with overdrive language.
- **Throttle Box EQ**: Overdrive + Distortion. MESA explicitly documents stinging overdrive and positions it as an expanded Throttle Box gain platform; authoritative product coverage classifies it as distortion.
- **Tone-Burst**: Overdrive only. MESA explicitly identifies it as Boost/Overdrive; no separate Distortion identity is created.

## Historical / variant handling
- The MESA Drive Pedals catalog presents Cleo, DynaPlex, Gold Mine, Grid Slammer, Flux-Drive, Throttle Box, Flux-Five, and Throttle Box EQ as a coherent pedal family; these are retained as distinct named products because the manufacturer gives each its own model identity.
- **Flux-Five** remains separate from Flux-Drive because MESA presents it as an expanded product with a five-band graphic EQ and additional switchable gain mode.
- **Throttle Box EQ** remains separate from Throttle Box because the manufacturer presents it as an expanded five-band-EQ version with additional controls and two independently levelled gain modes.
- Product finishes, production revisions, and stock/presentation variants do not create additional dirt identities.
- Boost, EQ, and preamp functions are treated as secondary functions unless the manufacturer explicitly places the named product in one of the target dirt categories.
- Historical/discontinued status does not exclude a product because the archive includes historical dirt products.

## Exclusions
- **Tone-Burst** is not excluded as a boost-only utility because MESA explicitly names it Boost/Overdrive and describes clipped overdrive operation. It is therefore included under Overdrive.
- MESA boost, EQ, switching, utility, amplifier, cabinet, and direct-interface products without explicit target-category dirt identity are outside the target census.
- Generic gain, saturation, clipping, or distortion language without product-level category evidence is not used to create secondary identities.
- Cloning, schematics, internal construction, component details, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/index.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/cleo.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/dynaplex.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/gold-mine.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/grid-slammer.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/flux-drive.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/flux-five.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/throttle-box.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/drive-pedals/throttle-box-eq.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/pedals--related/boost-pedals/tone-burst.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/amplitudes/2012/November/introducing-four-new-drive-and-distortion-pedals-from-mesa-engineering.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/amplitudes/2014/March/mesa-boogie-launches-three-new-guitar-pedals.html
- https://legacy.mesaboogie.com/support/user-manuals.html

## Checkpoint
Block 078 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “MESA/Boogie” returned no prior match before this block was added, so MESA/Boogie is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records Cleo, DynaPlex, Gold Mine, Grid Slammer, Flux-Drive, Flux-Five, Throttle Box, Throttle Box EQ, and Tone-Burst as Overdrive; Flux-Drive, Throttle Box, and Throttle Box EQ as Distortion; and no standalone MESA/Boogie Fuzz identity. Flux-Five and Throttle Box EQ remain distinct named products, while cosmetic and production variants are consolidated.

# Block 079 — TC Electronic

## Builder
**TC Electronic**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- TC Electronic official current product catalog
- TC Electronic official individual product pages
- TC Electronic manufacturer-authored Vintage Pedals manual
- TC Electronic manufacturer-authored VPD1 manual
- TC Electronic manufacturer-authored NOVA Drive manual
- authoritative product documentation for older TC Electronic dirt models where current product pages are archival or incomplete

The active repository contained no prior TC Electronic match before this block was added. TC Electronic is treated as the builder identity for TC-branded guitar dirt pedals documented below.

The reviewed material supports a broad dirt catalog spanning current/recent products and historical designs. Dirt categories are assigned from explicit product names, manufacturer category descriptions, or direct section/function descriptions in manufacturer manuals. Generic gain or distortion-like language is not used to create unsupported secondary categories.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- MojoMojo Overdrive
- MojoMojo Paul Gilbert Signature Overdrive
- Zeus Drive
- El Cambo Overdrive
- Cinders Overdrive
- Tube Pilot Overdrive

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Vintage Overdrive
- VPD1 Vintage Pre-Drive
- Nova Drive

Verification notes:
- **MojoMojo Overdrive** is explicitly identified by TC Electronic as Overdrive.
- **MojoMojo Paul Gilbert Signature Overdrive** is a distinct named MojoMojo-family overdrive product and remains separate from the standard MojoMojo identity.
- **Zeus Drive** is presented as a drive/overdrive product in TC Electronic's current guitar-effects catalog.
- **El Cambo Overdrive** is explicitly described as a vintage all-analog overdrive.
- **Cinders Overdrive** is explicitly named and cataloged as Overdrive.
- **Tube Pilot Overdrive** is explicitly named and cataloged as Overdrive.
- **Vintage Overdrive** is explicitly named in TC Electronic's manufacturer-authored Vintage Pedals manual.
- **VPD1 Vintage Pre-Drive** is explicitly described as a vintage pre-drive whose basic drive section delivers distinguished vintage overdrive.
- **Nova Drive** contains a dedicated analog overdrive section and is documented by TC Electronic as an overdrive/distortion pedal. It therefore receives both categories below.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Dark Matter Distortion
- Eyemaster Metal Distortion
- Grand Magus Distortion
- Magus Pro
- Fangs Metal Distortion
- Röttweiler Distortion

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Vintage Distortion
- Vintage Dual Distortion
- Nova Drive

Verification notes:
- **Dark Matter Distortion** is explicitly named and cataloged as Distortion.
- **Eyemaster Metal Distortion** is explicitly named and cataloged as Distortion.
- **Grand Magus Distortion** is explicitly presented by TC Electronic as a distortion pedal. Its broad gain range is retained within the Distortion identity rather than creating a secondary Overdrive identity.
- **Magus Pro** is a named Magus-family distortion product and remains under Distortion for the initial census.
- **Fangs Metal Distortion** is explicitly named and cataloged as a metal distortion pedal.
- **Röttweiler Distortion** is explicitly described by TC Electronic as an all-analog distortion circuit.
- **Vintage Distortion** and **Vintage Dual Distortion** are explicitly identified in TC Electronic's manufacturer-authored Vintage Pedals manual.
- **Nova Drive** has a separate distortion section in the manufacturer manual and is explicitly documented as an overdrive/distortion pedal, so it receives both categories.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Honey Pot Fuzz
- Rusty Fuzz

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **Honey Pot Fuzz** is explicitly described by TC Electronic as a vintage-flavored fuzz pedal. Its wide gain range reaches overdrive-like grind and thick distortion textures, but its product identity remains Fuzz only for this census.
- **Rusty Fuzz** is explicitly described as a silicon-based transistor fuzz pedal with vintage gated and velcro-like fuzz tones. It remains Fuzz only.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **MojoMojo Overdrive**: Overdrive only.
- **MojoMojo Paul Gilbert Signature Overdrive**: Overdrive only.
- **Zeus Drive**: Overdrive only based on its drive/overdrive product positioning.
- **El Cambo Overdrive**: Overdrive only.
- **Cinders Overdrive**: Overdrive only.
- **Tube Pilot Overdrive**: Overdrive only.
- **Vintage Overdrive**: Overdrive only.
- **VPD1 Vintage Pre-Drive**: Overdrive only. The boost section and speaker-simulation function do not create additional target identities.
- **Nova Drive**: Overdrive + Distortion. Manufacturer documentation explicitly provides separate overdrive and distortion sections and identifies the product as overdrive/distortion.
- **Dark Matter Distortion**: Distortion only.
- **Eyemaster Metal Distortion**: Distortion only.
- **Grand Magus Distortion**: Distortion only. Its Gain control may move through overdrive territory, but product identity and catalog wording are Distortion.
- **Magus Pro**: Distortion only.
- **Fangs Metal Distortion**: Distortion only.
- **Röttweiler Distortion**: Distortion only.
- **Vintage Distortion**: Distortion only.
- **Vintage Dual Distortion**: Distortion only.
- **Honey Pot Fuzz**: Fuzz only. Its overdrive-like lower-gain range does not create a separate Overdrive identity.
- **Rusty Fuzz**: Fuzz only.

## Historical / variant handling
- TC Electronic current/recent product names are retained as distinct identities when the manufacturer presents them as distinct named products.
- MojoMojo Paul Gilbert Signature Overdrive remains separate from standard MojoMojo Overdrive because it is a distinct named signature product.
- Nova Drive remains a single named identity despite its separately switchable overdrive and distortion sections; those sections determine category overlap but do not create duplicate products.
- Vintage Overdrive, Vintage Distortion, and Vintage Dual Distortion remain separate historical identities because TC Electronic's manufacturer-authored manual names them separately.
- VPD1 remains separate from Vintage Overdrive because the manufacturer presents it as a distinct vintage pre-drive product with a different control and boost architecture.
- Cosmetic editions, presentation variants, firmware/TonePrint variations, and stock status do not create additional functional dirt identities.

## Exclusions
- Boost-only and utility products such as Spark Booster, Mini Spark, Bonafide Buffer, and similar non-target products: outside the target taxonomy.
- Modulation, delay, tuner, reverb, chorus, and other non-dirt TC Electronic effects: outside scope.
- TonePrint artist presets and downloadable variations are control/preset content, not separate builder/product identities.
- Generic gain, saturation, clipping, or distortion language without product-level category evidence is not used to create secondary identities.
- Multi-effects/modeling platforms and their internal modeled dirt algorithms are not promoted into the standalone pedal census.
- Cloning, schematics, internal construction, component details, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.tcelectronic.com/en/products
- https://www.tcelectronic.com/en/products/0709-AHJ
- https://www.tcelectronic.com/en/products/0709-AHU
- https://www.tcelectronic.com/en/products/0709-AFJ
- https://www.tcelectronic.com/en/products/0709-AHE
- https://www.tcelectronic.com/en/products/0709-AHQ
- https://www.tcelectronic.com/en/products/0709-AFH
- https://service-tcgroup.tcelectronic.com/media/Vintage_pedals.pdf
- https://service-tcgroup.tcelectronic.com/media/VPD1_manual.pdf
- https://service-tcgroup.tcelectronic.com/media/NOVA_Drive_US.pdf
- https://service-tcgroup.tcelectronic.com/media/Rottweiler_US.pdf

## Checkpoint
Block 079 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for "TC Electronic" returned no prior match before this block was added, so TC Electronic is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records MojoMojo, MojoMojo Paul Gilbert Signature, Zeus Drive, El Cambo, Cinders, Tube Pilot, Vintage Overdrive, VPD1, and Nova Drive as Overdrive; Dark Matter, Eyemaster, Grand Magus, Magus Pro, Fangs, Röttweiler, Vintage Distortion, Vintage Dual Distortion, and Nova Drive as Distortion; and Honey Pot Fuzz and Rusty Fuzz as Fuzz. Nova Drive is the explicit Overdrive + Distortion overlap. Historical/manual-supported identities are retained, while boost-only utilities, TonePrint presets, and non-dirt effects remain excluded.

# Block 080 — Strymon

## Builder
**Strymon**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Strymon official current Drive product category
- Strymon official individual product pages
- Strymon manufacturer-authored Sunset user manual
- Strymon manufacturer-authored Fairfax quick-start/manual material
- Strymon manufacturer-authored product/support material for Deco V2 and Canoga
- active-repository search for “Strymon” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Strymon match before this block was added. Strymon is treated as the builder identity for Strymon-branded dirt pedals documented below.

The reviewed material supports five clearly documented core dirt identities: Sunset Dual Overdrive, Riverside Drive & Distortion, Fairfax Class A Output Stage Drive, Canoga Vintage Silicon Fuzz, and Deco / Deco V2 Tape Saturation & Doubletracker. Dirt categories are assigned from explicit product/category descriptions or direct manufacturer documentation. Generic saturation, gain, or sound-descriptor language is not used to manufacture additional categories.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Sunset Dual Overdrive
- Riverside Drive & Distortion
- Fairfax Class A Output Stage Drive
- Deco Tape Saturation & Doubletracker

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **Sunset Dual Overdrive** is explicitly presented by Strymon as a dual overdrive pedal with six classic overdrive/boost circuits. It remains Overdrive only despite one circuit approaching fuzz territory at maximum Drive.
- **Riverside Drive & Distortion** is explicitly presented by Strymon as a multistage drive pedal producing tones from clean overdrive through colossal distortion. It therefore receives both Overdrive and Distortion below.
- **Fairfax Class A Output Stage Drive** is explicitly presented as a drive pedal and Strymon's current page documents overdrive sounds from gentle push through higher-gain saturation. It remains Overdrive only because Strymon does not present it as a separate Distortion product.
- **Deco Tape Saturation & Doubletracker / Deco V2** is included under Overdrive because Strymon's current Drive category includes Deco V2 and the manufacturer explicitly describes the tape-saturation side as transparent overdrive and overdriven tape distortion. The tape/doubletracking functionality does not create a separate target product identity.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Riverside Drive & Distortion
- Canoga Vintage Silicon Fuzz

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **Riverside Drive & Distortion** receives Distortion from both its product title and manufacturer description of its clean-overdrive through colossal-distortion range.
- **Canoga Vintage Silicon Fuzz** receives Distortion because Strymon explicitly states that, when turned up, it works as a fuzz or distortion. This is product-level manufacturer wording, so the Distortion category is retained alongside Fuzz.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Canoga Vintage Silicon Fuzz

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **Canoga Vintage Silicon Fuzz** is explicitly presented by Strymon as a vintage-silicon fuzz pedal and as a tweaked vintage Silicon Fuzz Face-style design. It remains Fuzz and also receives Distortion because the manufacturer explicitly states that it works as fuzz or distortion.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Sunset Dual Overdrive**: Overdrive only. The product is explicitly a dual-overdrive pedal; approaching fuzz at maximum gain does not create a second category.
- **Riverside Drive & Distortion**: Overdrive + Distortion. Strymon explicitly describes its range from clean overdrive to colossal distortion and uses both terms in the product identity.
- **Fairfax Class A Output Stage Drive**: Overdrive only. Strymon documents softer overdrive through saturated amp-like gain but does not identify a separate Distortion category for the product.
- **Canoga Vintage Silicon Fuzz**: Fuzz + Distortion. Strymon explicitly identifies it as a vintage-silicon fuzz and states it can work as fuzz or distortion at higher settings.
- **Deco Tape Saturation & Doubletracker / Deco V2**: Overdrive only. Strymon explicitly describes transparent tape overdrive and includes Deco V2 in its Drive catalog. Tape saturation, compression, modulation, and delay functions remain secondary to the target dirt identity.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Deco V2** is consolidated with the Deco functional product identity rather than treated as a separate dirt family; Strymon states that V2 preserves the original sound and functions while expanding the interface.
- Cosmetic revisions, hardware revisions, firmware versions, and presentation variants do not create additional dirt identities.
- Sunset's individual circuit selections and stacking modes are internal functions of one named pedal and do not create additional products.
- Riverside's gain-stage architecture is internal product functionality and does not create separate identities.

## Exclusions
- Boost-only products such as Compadre Compressor & Boost and related clean-boost utilities: outside the target taxonomy.
- Strymon modulation, delay, reverb, tuner, interface, amp-modeling, and utility products without an explicit target dirt identity: outside scope.
- Product presets, MIDI patches, firmware variations, and internal circuit modes are not separate builder/product identities.
- Generic saturation, clipping, fuzz-like, or distortion-like sound descriptions without product-level classification are not used to create secondary identities.
- Cloning, schematics, internal construction, component details, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.strymon.net/product-category/effects-pedals/drive/
- https://www.strymon.net/product/sunset/
- https://www.strymon.net/product/riverside/
- https://www.strymon.net/product/fairfax/
- https://www.strymon.net/product/canoga/
- https://www.strymon.net/product/deco/
- https://www.strymon.net/manuals/Sunset_UserManual_RevC.pdf
- https://www.strymon.net/manuals/Fairfax_QuickStart_Web_RevA.pdf
- https://www.strymon.net/faq/whats-new-with-deco-v2/

## Checkpoint
Block 080 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Strymon” returned no prior match before this block was added, so Strymon is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records Sunset Dual Overdrive, Riverside Drive & Distortion, Fairfax Class A Output Stage Drive, and Deco Tape Saturation & Doubletracker as Overdrive; Riverside Drive & Distortion and Canoga Vintage Silicon Fuzz as Distortion; and Canoga Vintage Silicon Fuzz as Fuzz. Deco V2 is consolidated with the Deco identity. Boost-only utilities and non-dirt Strymon products remain excluded.

# Block 081 — Marshall Amplification

## Builder
**Marshall Amplification**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Marshall official current Pedals catalog
- Marshall official individual product pages for the current Overdrive Pedal series
- Marshall official individual pages for Bluesbreaker, Guv'nor, Drivemaster, and Shredmaster Vintage Reissue pedals
- Marshall manufacturer-authored pedal-history material and official Marshall pedal-history video
- Marshall-authored / preserved Jackhammer JH-1 manual
- manufacturer-authored / preserved Bluesbreaker II BB-2 manual
- authoritative product catalog/database material for historical Guv'nor Plus GV-2
- active-repository search for “Marshall” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Marshall match before this block was added. Marshall is treated as the builder identity for Marshall-branded dirt pedals documented below. Park-branded products and other separate brand identities are not merged into Marshall merely because Marshall has historical or corporate relationships with them.

The reviewed material supports current and historical Marshall dirt identities across Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Cosmetic editions, reissue presentation, and hardware revisions are consolidated under the functional product identity unless the product is a separately named model.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- 1959 Overdrive Pedal
- JCM800 Overdrive Pedal
- JCM900 Overdrive Pedal
- DSL Overdrive Pedal
- JVM Overdrive Pedal
- Bluesbreaker Vintage Reissue Pedal
- Guv'nor Vintage Reissue Pedal
- Drivemaster Vintage Reissue Pedal

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Bluesbreaker II BB-2
- Guv'nor Plus GV-2
- Jackhammer JH-1

Verification notes:
- **1959 Overdrive Pedal** is explicitly titled and described by Marshall as delivering classic Plexi crunchy overdrive.
- **JCM800 Overdrive Pedal**, **JCM900 Overdrive Pedal**, **DSL Overdrive Pedal**, and **JVM Overdrive Pedal** are explicitly named Overdrive products in Marshall's current catalog. Descriptions of saturated or aggressive distortion at higher settings are treated as operating range rather than automatic secondary categories unless the product is explicitly presented as a separate target identity.
- **Bluesbreaker Vintage Reissue Pedal** is explicitly described by Marshall as a classic low-gain drive / overdrive pedal.
- **Guv'nor Vintage Reissue Pedal** is explicitly described by Marshall as a smooth overdrive / overdrive effect pedal.
- **Drivemaster Vintage Reissue Pedal** is explicitly described by Marshall as a guitar overdrive pedal with a range extending from subtle crunch upward.
- **Bluesbreaker II BB-2** is retained as Overdrive from the manufacturer-authored manual's explicit Blues-mode overdrive description. Its Boost mode is a clean boost and is not separately counted.
- **Guv'nor Plus GV-2** is retained as Overdrive from authoritative catalog/database classification and its documented expanded gain structure.
- **Jackhammer JH-1** is Overdrive + Distortion because its manufacturer-authored manual explicitly documents two operating modes named Overdrive and Distortion.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Drivemaster Vintage Reissue Pedal
- Shredmaster Vintage Reissue Pedal

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Guv'nor Plus GV-2
- Jackhammer JH-1

Verification notes:
- **Drivemaster Vintage Reissue Pedal** is retained as Distortion as well as Overdrive because Marshall's current pedal catalog describes it as offering “vintage overdrive and distortion,” while its product page identifies the same named pedal as an overdrive pedal spanning substantial gain.
- **Shredmaster Vintage Reissue Pedal** is explicitly presented by Marshall as a high-gain distortion pedal.
- **Guv'nor Plus GV-2** is retained as Overdrive + Distortion because authoritative historical product classification identifies it under both categories and its documented gain range extends from vintage breakup through super-saturated distortion.
- **Jackhammer JH-1** is retained as Overdrive + Distortion because the manufacturer-authored manual explicitly states that the pedal has separate Overdrive and Distortion modes.
- **Guv'nor Vintage Reissue Pedal** remains Overdrive only in this census. Marshall's current product presentation emphasizes its overdrive identity, while generic historical descriptions of “distortion” are not enough to create an additional category here.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Supa Fuzz

Verification notes:
- **Supa Fuzz** is explicitly documented by Marshall as an early Marshall fuzz pedal. Marshall's official pedal-history material identifies the Supa Fuzz as the company's first pedal and describes it as a fuzz developed in the 1960s, with production continuing into the early 1970s.
- No current Marshall-branded standalone Fuzz pedal was identified in the reviewed current catalog.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Drivemaster Vintage Reissue Pedal**: Overdrive + Distortion. Marshall's current catalog uses both terms for this named pedal.
- **Jackhammer JH-1**: Overdrive + Distortion. The manufacturer manual explicitly gives it two modes with those names.
- **Guv'nor Plus GV-2**: Overdrive + Distortion. Historical product classification supports both categories; the product's broad gain structure does not require splitting it into multiple identities.
- **Bluesbreaker II BB-2**: Overdrive only. Boost mode is a clean boost and is excluded from the target taxonomy.
- **Guv'nor Vintage Reissue Pedal**: Overdrive only. Current Marshall product wording is explicitly centered on overdrive.
- **Shredmaster Vintage Reissue Pedal**: Distortion only. Marshall explicitly describes it as a high-gain distortion pedal.
- **Bluesbreaker Vintage Reissue Pedal**: Overdrive only. Marshall explicitly calls it a low-gain drive / overdrive pedal.
- **1959, JCM800, JCM900, DSL, and JVM Overdrive Pedals**: Overdrive only from their explicit product naming and current catalog placement. Saturated distortion language in descriptive copy is treated as tonal range rather than a second catalog identity.
- **Supa Fuzz**: Fuzz only. Historical product identity is unambiguous.

## Historical / variant handling
- Marshall's 2023 Vintage Reissue versions of the Guv'nor, Bluesbreaker, Drivemaster, and Shredmaster are consolidated with their named functional identities rather than counted as separate products from their originals.
- **Drivemaster** remains a separate product identity from **Guv'nor** even though authoritative historical sources describe the Drivemaster as the later rebranded/re-housed evolution of the Guv'nor design. They are separately named Marshall products and therefore remain separate identities in the census.
- **Guv'nor Plus GV-2**, **Bluesbreaker II BB-2**, and **Jackhammer JH-1** are kept distinct from the original black-box reissue models because they are separately named products with distinct product identities.
- Hardware revisions, finish changes, and authentic-reissue presentation do not create extra dirt identities.

## Exclusions
- Marshall Supa Wah and other wah/modulation effects: outside target taxonomy.
- Marshall clean boosts and boost-only modes: outside target taxonomy unless the named pedal also has an explicitly documented target dirt mode.
- DRP-1 Direct Recording Preamp and similar recording/preamp utilities: excluded because the reviewed evidence does not establish them as dedicated Marshall overdrive, distortion, or fuzz pedal identities.
- Park-branded effects and products from other explicitly separate brands: not merged into Marshall.
- Mark I/Mark II pedal-series names and other historical references without sufficiently clear product-level target-category evidence: not promoted into the categorized census.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material: outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.marshall.com/us/en/amplifiers/pedals
- https://www.marshall.com/us/en/product/1959-overdrive-pedal?pid=1008154
- https://www.marshall.com/us/en/product/jcm800-overdrive-pedal?pid=1008155
- https://www.marshall.com/us/en/product/jcm900-overdrive-pedal?pid=1008156
- https://www.marshall.com/us/en/product/dsl-overdrive-pedal?pid=1008158
- https://www.marshall.com/ca/en/product/jvm-overdrive-pedal?color=black%2Band%2Bgold
- https://www.marshall.com/us/en/product/bluesbreaker-vintage-reissue-pedal?pid=1007074
- https://www.marshall.com/pe/en/product/guvnor-vintage-reissue-pedal
- https://www.marshall.com/us/en/product/drivemaster-vintage-reissue-pedal?color=black%2Band%2Bred&pid=1007077
- https://www.marshall.com/cw/en/product/shredmaster-vintage-reissue-pedal?pid=1007076
- https://marshallamps.jp/products/pedals/jh_1_jackhammer/
- https://www.marshallamps.jp/live_for_music/tips/history/history-of-pedals-part-1.html

## Checkpoint
Block 081 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Marshall” returned no prior match before addition, so Marshall is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records 11 Overdrive identities, 4 Distortion identities, and 1 Fuzz identity, with explicit overlaps for Drivemaster, Guv'nor Plus, and Jackhammer. Supa Fuzz is the only standalone Marshall Fuzz identity established in the reviewed material. Boost-only utilities, DRP-1, and weakly documented historical series names remain excluded.

# Block 082 — Orange Amplification

## Builder
**Orange Amplification**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Orange official current Pedals catalog
- Orange official individual product pages for Fur Coat, Getaway Driver, Guitar Butler, Bass Butler, and Bax Bangeetar
- Orange manufacturer-authored Fur Coat manual
- Orange product/support material describing Guitar Butler's Dirty-channel overdrive behavior
- active-repository search for “Orange Amplification” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Orange Amplification match before this block was added. Orange is treated as the builder identity for Orange-branded dirt pedals documented below.

The reviewed material supports a focused Orange dirt catalog spanning current/recent Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz products. Preamp pedals are included where Orange explicitly documents them as delivering the target dirt function. Amplifier products are not treated as pedals, even where their manuals describe overdrive or distortion.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Getaway Driver
- Guitar Butler
- Bax Bangeetar

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **Getaway Driver** is an explicitly named Orange Overdrive pedal. Orange describes it as a hot-wired 1970s Orange amp-in-a-box with bold gain and edge-of-breakup response.
- **Guitar Butler** is retained as Overdrive because Orange's product/support material describes its Dirty channel as a classic overdrive channel and the current product page identifies it as a dual-channel preamp pedal with a dedicated Dirty channel. The clean channel, compressor function, CabSim, and routing features do not create additional target identities.
- **Bax Bangeetar** is retained as Overdrive because Orange explicitly states that the pedal provides a wide range of overdriven and distorted tones and describes it as a standalone drive pedal. The product is therefore recorded in both Overdrive and Distortion below.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Bax Bangeetar
- Bass Butler

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **Bax Bangeetar** receives Distortion as well as Overdrive because Orange explicitly describes its gain structure as producing overdriven and distorted tones.
- **Bass Butler** receives Distortion because Orange explicitly describes its Guitar/Dirty channel as delivering gritty, guitar-style distortion and searing distortion inspired by classic Orange guitar amps. It remains Distortion only because the reviewed manufacturer material does not explicitly present it as a separate Overdrive product.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fur Coat

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **Fur Coat** is explicitly presented by Orange as a vintage-style fuzz pedal inspired by the Foxx Tone Machine. Its switchable octave-up and active EQ are secondary functions and do not create separate dirt identities.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Bax Bangeetar**: Overdrive + Distortion. Orange's manufacturer page explicitly describes an enormous range of overdriven and distorted tones.
- **Getaway Driver**: Overdrive only. Orange explicitly names it an Overdrive pedal and describes 1970s amp-like gain. Higher saturation is treated as operating range rather than a second catalog identity.
- **Guitar Butler**: Overdrive only. Orange/support material explicitly describes the Dirty channel as a classic overdrive channel. Its preamp, compression, CabSim, and routing functions remain secondary.
- **Bass Butler**: Distortion only. Orange explicitly describes the guitar-style channel as distortion. No separate Overdrive identity is promoted from generic gain language.
- **Fur Coat**: Fuzz only. Orange explicitly identifies it as a vintage fuzz pedal. Octave-up is a secondary effect.

## Historical / variant handling
- No separate historical Orange dirt pedal identity was established from the reviewed manufacturer material. Orange's current site states that the Bax Bangeetar was its first pedal since the 1960s, so speculative or weakly sourced 1960s pedal names are not promoted into the census.
- Product revisions, regional pages, availability states, and presentation variants do not create additional dirt identities.
- Guitar Butler and Bass Butler remain separate named products despite their shared “Butler” naming.

## Exclusions
- Orange amplifiers, combo amps, heads, and speaker products are outside the pedal census even where their manuals describe overdrive or distortion behavior.
- Terror Stamp is excluded as an amp/power-amp product rather than a dedicated dirt-effect identity.
- King Comp, Two Stroke, Amp Detonator, Acoustic Pedal, and other current Orange products without an explicit target dirt identity are excluded.
- Generic saturation, gain, grit, or distortion-like descriptions are not used to manufacture extra categories where the product-level classification is unclear.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://orangeamps.com/en-us/collections/pedals
- https://orangeamps.com/en-us/products/getaway-driver-overdrive-pedal
- https://orangeamps.com/en-us/products/guitar-butler
- https://orangeamps.com/en-es/products/bass-butler
- https://orangeamps.com/en-us/products/bax-bangeetar
- https://orangeamps.com/products/fur-coat
- https://orangeamps.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/08/FUR-COAT-MANUAL-V1.pdf

## Checkpoint
Block 082 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Orange Amplification” returned no prior match before addition, so Orange Amplification is treated as unrepresented in the active census. The block records Getaway Driver, Guitar Butler, and Bax Bangeetar as Overdrive; Bax Bangeetar and Bass Butler as Distortion; and Fur Coat as Fuzz. Bax Bangeetar is the explicit Overdrive + Distortion overlap. Amplifiers, Terror Stamp, non-dirt pedals, and weakly sourced historical pedal names remain excluded.

# Block 083 — HardWire

## Builder
**HardWire**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- DigiTech official Product Manuals archive under the HARDWIRE heading
- DigiTech official FAQ/power-supply documentation listing HardWire models
- manufacturer-era HardWire product material preserved through authoritative pedal references
- Premier Guitar's historical review identifying the HardWire CM-2, SC-2, and line context
- active-repository search for “HardWire” before addition

The active repository contained no prior HardWire match before this block was added. HardWire is treated as a distinct builder/product-line identity for the HardWire-branded dirt pedals documented below, even though DigiTech manufacturer documentation now hosts the manuals. The repository's earlier DigiTech block deliberately kept HardWire separate, and this block preserves that distinction.

The reviewed material supports three clearly documented HardWire dirt identities: one Overdrive and two Distortion products. No HardWire-branded Fuzz identity was established in the reviewed manufacturer material.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- CM-2 Tube Overdrive

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **CM-2 Tube Overdrive** is explicitly named and documented by DigiTech's HardWire manual archive and historical manufacturer-era material as an overdrive pedal. Its Classic and Modified voicings remain internal modes of one named product and do not create additional identities.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- SC-2 Valve Distortion
- TL-2 Metal Distortion

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- **SC-2 Valve Distortion** is explicitly named and documented as a distortion pedal. Its Crunch and Saturated voicings remain internal modes of one product rather than separate identities.
- **TL-2 Metal Distortion** is explicitly named and documented as a metal distortion pedal. Its Tight and Loose settings are internal tone-shaping modes rather than separate products.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- none identified

Verification notes:
- No HardWire-branded fuzz product was established in the reviewed HardWire catalog/manual material.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **CM-2 Tube Overdrive**: Overdrive only from explicit product naming and manufacturer documentation.
- **SC-2 Valve Distortion**: Distortion only. Historical descriptions of lower-gain Crunch tones do not create a separate Overdrive identity.
- **TL-2 Metal Distortion**: Distortion only. Tight/Loose controls affect EQ/response and do not create another dirt category.

## Historical / variant handling
- HardWire product-line documentation is now hosted within DigiTech's support ecosystem, but HardWire remains a separate builder/product-line identity in this census because the pedals were explicitly branded and marketed as HardWire products.
- Product voicings, control modes, hardware revisions, and cosmetic presentation do not create additional dirt identities.
- The HardWire line's non-dirt pedals are not included.

## Exclusions
- CR-7 Stereo Chorus, DL-8 Delay/Looper, HT-2 Chromatic Tuner, HT-6 Polyphonic Tuner, RV-7 Stereo Reverb, SP-7 Stereo Phaser, TR-7 Tremolo/Rotary, and Supernatural Ambient Reverb: outside target dirt taxonomy.
- DigiTech-branded and DOD-branded products remain separate builder/brand identities and are not absorbed into HardWire.
- Generic gain, saturation, or “amp-like” wording without explicit product-level target-category naming is not used to manufacture extra Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz identities.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material: outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://digitech.com/product-manuals/
- https://digitech.com/faqs/
- https://www.premierguitar.com/gear/hardwire-cm-2-tube-overdrive-dl-8-delay-looper-and-sc-2-valve-distortion-pedal-review?page=2

## Checkpoint
Block 083 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “HardWire” returned no prior match before addition, so HardWire is treated as an unrepresented builder/product-line identity in the active census. The block records CM-2 Tube Overdrive as Overdrive; SC-2 Valve Distortion and TL-2 Metal Distortion as Distortion; and no HardWire Fuzz identity. HardWire remains separate from the previously documented DigiTech builder identity despite current DigiTech-hosted documentation.
