# Block 084 — VOX

## Builder
**VOX**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- VOX official current Single Effects catalog
- VOX official current Valvenergy product pages and manufacturer-authored Valvenergy manual
- VOX official current VFZ-1 Fuzz product page and 2026 launch material
- VOX manufacturer-authored Tone Garage manuals
- VOX Showroom archival product histories for historical VOX pedals
- historical product/manual coverage for the Joe Satriani signature pedal line
- active-repository search for “VOX” before addition

The active repository contained no prior VOX match before this block was added. VOX is treated as the builder/brand identity for VOX-branded dirt pedals documented below. The census stays at the pedal/product level and does not turn ToneLab/StompLab modeled effects into separate physical pedal identities.

The reviewed material supports a broad VOX dirt catalog spanning Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz from the 1960s through the current day. Current and historical product identities are retained where the VOX relationship and target dirt function are sufficiently explicit. Internal modes, preset models, and non-dirt functions do not create additional product identities.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Copperhead Drive
- Mystic Edge
- Cutting Edge
- Silk Drive
- Fuel Injector
- VFZ-1 Fuzz

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Big Ben Overdrive
- Straight 6 Drive
- V810 Valvetone
- Ice 9 Overdrive

Verification notes:
- **Copperhead Drive**, **Mystic Edge**, and **Cutting Edge** are VOX Valvenergy valve-distortion pedals whose manufacturer documentation explicitly says they provide genuine overdrive and distortion tones. They therefore receive both categories below.
- **Silk Drive** is explicitly presented by VOX as a valve overdrive pedal and remains Overdrive only.
- **Fuel Injector** is explicitly presented by VOX as an overdrive pedal. Generic references to its organic tube-like distortion remain operating range rather than a second catalog identity.
- **VFZ-1 Fuzz** is included under Overdrive because VOX explicitly documents its Crunch switch as producing lower-gain overdrive tones while the main product remains a Fuzz pedal.
- **Big Ben Overdrive**, **Straight 6 Drive**, **V810 Valvetone**, and **Ice 9 Overdrive** are explicitly identified as VOX overdrive products in VOX historical/catalog material.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Copperhead Drive
- Mystic Edge
- Cutting Edge

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- V8 Distortion
- Bulldog Distortion
- Satchurator
- VOX 7001 Distortion

Verification notes:
- **Copperhead Drive**, **Mystic Edge**, and **Cutting Edge** receive Distortion from their manufacturer presentation as valve distortion pedals and their explicit documentation of overdrive/distortion tones.
- **V8 Distortion** is retained as Distortion only. Its ability to produce lower-gain overdriven sounds is treated as range within the named distortion product rather than a separate catalog category.
- **Bulldog Distortion** is retained as Distortion only. Its historical descriptions of fuzz-like channel voices do not create a separate Fuzz identity because the named product is explicitly a distortion pedal.
- **Satchurator** is explicitly documented as a VOX Joe Satriani signature distortion pedal.
- **VOX 7001 Distortion** is retained as a distinct historical VOX distortion pedal identity from the early-1980s 7000-series effects.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- VFZ-1 Fuzz

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Trike Fuzz
- V828 Tone Bender
- V829 Tone Bender
- V830 Distortion Booster

Verification notes:
- **VFZ-1 Fuzz** is explicitly presented by VOX as a current vintage-style fuzz pedal inspired by the late-1960s Tone Bender Mk 1.5 family. VOX also documents its Crunch mode as a lower-gain overdrive function, supporting the Overdrive overlap above.
- **Trike Fuzz** is a historical VOX Tone Garage octave-fuzz pedal.
- **V828 Tone Bender** is a VOX-branded historical Tone Bender fuzz product from the 1960s. The pedal was sourced through VOX's relationship with Sola Sound/JEN, but the product was marketed under the VOX identity and is therefore recorded under VOX here.
- **V829 Tone Bender** is the later Korg/VOX Tone Bender fuzz product and is kept distinct from the V828 because it is a separately named historical VOX model.
- **V830 Distortion Booster** is recorded as Fuzz because VOX archival material documents “Distortion Booster” as period VOX terminology for fuzz and describes its Drive control as adjusting fuzz intensity.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Copperhead Drive**: Overdrive + Distortion. VOX describes it as a valve distortion pedal with genuine overdrive and distortion tones.
- **Mystic Edge**: Overdrive + Distortion. VOX describes it as a valve distortion pedal and explicitly documents genuine overdrive and distortion tones.
- **Cutting Edge**: Overdrive + Distortion. VOX describes it as a valve distortion pedal and explicitly documents genuine overdrive and distortion tones.
- **Silk Drive**: Overdrive only. Explicit VOX product identity is valve overdrive.
- **Fuel Injector**: Overdrive only. Explicit VOX product identity is overdrive.
- **VFZ-1 Fuzz**: Fuzz + Overdrive. VOX explicitly gives the Crunch switch a lower-gain overdrive function, but the named product remains Fuzz.
- **V8 Distortion**: Distortion only. Lower-gain overdrive-like sounds are treated as tonal range, not a second identity.
- **Bulldog Distortion**: Distortion only. Fuzz-like internal channel voicing does not create a separate Fuzz category.
- **V830 Distortion Booster**: Fuzz only. Period VOX terminology and product documentation identify the dirt function as fuzz.

## Historical / variant handling
- **V828 Tone Bender** and **V829 Tone Bender** remain separate named identities because they are distinct VOX-branded models from different eras.
- VOX's **Valvenergy 1st Series** products are recorded as individual pedal identities: Copperhead Drive, Cutting Edge, Mystic Edge, and Silk Drive. Their STANDARD, PREAMP, and CAB-SIM output modes are internal functions.
- **VFZ-1 Fuzz** and **VTB-1 Treble Booster** are separate 2026 VOX products. VTB-1 is excluded because it is a boost-only product even though VOX describes it as capable of pushing an amplifier into overdrive.
- Cosmetic editions, regional presentation, internal voicing modes, and hardware/presentation revisions do not create extra dirt identities.
- ToneLab and StompLab modeled effects are excluded as virtual effect models inside multi-effects products rather than standalone named VOX dirt pedals.

## Exclusions
- Flat 4 Boost and Black 9 Boost: boost-focused products; no separate named dirt identity is promoted despite available gain range.
- VTB-1 Treble Booster: boost-only product; excluded from the target taxonomy.
- Brit Boost and other boost-only VOX products: outside target taxonomy.
- VOX ToneLab, StompLab, and related modeled multi-effects products: their modeled Drive effects are not treated as separate physical pedal identities.
- Wah, delay, chorus, flanger, phaser, compressor, looper, EQ, and other non-dirt VOX products: outside scope.
- Amplifiers, cabinets, and amp models are not pedal identities for this census.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material: outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://voxamps.com/en-gb/type/single-effects-en-gb/
- https://voxamps.com/product/copperhead-drive/
- https://voxamps.com/product/mystic-edge/
- https://voxamps.com/product/cutting-edge/
- https://voxamps.com/product/silk-drive/
- https://voxamps.com/product/fuel-injector-tube-overdrive-valvenergy-guitar-effects-pedal/
- https://voxamps.com/product/vfz-1-fuzz/
- https://voxamps.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/09/ME_CD_CE_SD_OM_EFGSJ2.pdf
- https://voxamps.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/02/FL4_ST6_V8_OM_efgsj1.pdf
- https://voxamps.com/wp-content/uploads/support/tone_garage.pdf
- https://www.voxshowroom.com/equipment/effects.html
- https://www.voxshowroom.com/us/misc/satriani.html
- https://www.voxshowroom.com/us/misc/810830.html
- https://www.voxshowroom.com/us/misc/v828.html
- https://www.voxshowroom.com/us/misc/v829.html

## Checkpoint
Block 084 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “VOX” returned no prior match before addition, so VOX is treated as an unrepresented builder/brand identity in the active census. The block records Copperhead Drive, Mystic Edge, Cutting Edge, Silk Drive, Fuel Injector, and VFZ-1 Fuzz as Overdrive; Copperhead Drive, Mystic Edge, Cutting Edge, V8 Distortion, Bulldog Distortion, Satchurator, and VOX 7001 Distortion as Distortion; and VFZ-1 Fuzz, Trike Fuzz, V828 Tone Bender, V829 Tone Bender, and V830 Distortion Booster as Fuzz. VFZ-1 is the explicit Overdrive + Fuzz overlap, while the first three Valvenergy pedals are Overdrive + Distortion. ToneLab/StompLab virtual models and boost-only products remain excluded.

# Block 085 — Fender

## Builder
**Fender Musical Instruments Corporation / Fender**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Fender official current Effects Pedals support/catalog
- Fender official current individual product pages for Santa Ana V2, Pugilist V2, The Pelt V2, Full Moon Distortion, MTG Tube Distortion, Smolder Acoustic Overdrive, The Trapper Dual Fuzz, Hammertone Overdrive, Hammertone Distortion, Hammertone Metal, Hammertone Fuzz, and Fender Shields Blender
- Fender official archive of Effects Pedals
- Fender official historical effects article covering Fender effects from the 1950s–1980s
- Fender official historical/news material describing the 2013 Competition Series and the later original Fender effects line
- active-repository search for “Fender” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Fender match before this block was added. Fender is treated as the builder identity for Fender-branded dirt pedals that Fender itself documents as Fender-built/designed or as Fender's own historical effects. The block consolidates ordinary product revisions such as V2 under their underlying product identity rather than inflating the census.

The reviewed material supports a substantial Fender dirt catalog spanning historical Fuzz/Distortion/Drive products, the 2012–2019 Yngwie Malmsteen Overdrive, the 2013 Competition-series Drive and Distortion, the 2018-forward Fender-designed pedal family, the Hammertone series, and the Fender Shields Blender collaboration.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Santa Ana Overdrive
- Smolder Acoustic Overdrive
- Hammertone Overdrive

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fender Drive Pedal
- Yngwie Malmsteen Overdrive Pedal

Verification notes:
- **Santa Ana Overdrive** is explicitly identified by Fender as a JFET-based class-A overdrive pedal. The current V2 is consolidated under the underlying Santa Ana Overdrive identity.
- **Smolder Acoustic Overdrive** is explicitly named and presented by Fender as an overdrive pedal for acoustic instruments. Its Drive control's distortion range does not create a second named Distortion identity.
- **Hammertone Overdrive** is explicitly identified by Fender as an analog overdrive circuit and remains Overdrive only.
- **Fender Drive Pedal** is the historical Competition-series drive product. Fender's 2013 release identifies it as creating warm overdrive and harmonic crunch.
- **Yngwie Malmsteen Overdrive Pedal** is retained from Fender's official archive as a historical Fender overdrive identity.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Pugilist Distortion
- Full Moon Distortion
- MTG Tube Distortion Pedal
- MTG:LA Tube Distortion
- Compugilist Compressor/Distortion
- Hammertone Distortion
- Hammertone Metal

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fender Distortion Pedal
- Fender Blender

Verification notes:
- **Pugilist Distortion** is explicitly described by Fender as a distortion pedal with two independent distortion engines. The current V2 is consolidated under the Pugilist Distortion identity.
- **Full Moon Distortion** is explicitly described by Fender as a high-gain distortion effect pedal. Its switchable post-gain boost is secondary and does not create another target identity.
- **MTG Tube Distortion Pedal** and **MTG:LA Tube Distortion** are explicitly identified by Fender as tube-driven distortion pedals and remain Distortion only.
- **Compugilist Compressor/Distortion** is recorded as Distortion because Fender describes the unit as a combined compressor/distortion pedal with independently switchable circuits. Compression remains secondary.
- **Hammertone Distortion** and **Hammertone Metal** are explicitly identified by Fender as distortion circuits. Their lower-gain or high-gain operating ranges do not create additional Overdrive identities.
- **Fender Distortion Pedal** is retained from Fender's historical Competition-series archive and 2013 launch material, which explicitly describes the product as a distortion pedal.
- **Fender Blender** is recorded as Distortion because Fender's historical effects article explicitly calls the original Blender a distortion unit. Fender later used the Blender circuit as the basis for the Shields Blender fuzz product, but that does not retroactively change the original named product's archived category.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- The Pelt Fuzz
- The Trapper Dual Fuzz
- Hammertone Fuzz
- Fender Shields Blender

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fender Fuzz Wah Pedal

Verification notes:
- **The Pelt Fuzz** is explicitly identified by Fender as a fuzz pedal. The current V2 is consolidated under The Pelt Fuzz identity.
- **The Trapper Dual Fuzz** is explicitly identified by Fender as a dual-fuzz pedal. Its two discrete fuzz voices, octave switch, and noise gate remain internal functions.
- **Hammertone Fuzz** is explicitly identified by Fender as a fuzz circuit. Its octave-fuzz switch remains secondary.
- **Fender Shields Blender** is explicitly presented by Fender as a signature fuzz pedal and current Fender product. Fender states that it is built around the vintage Fender Blender circuit and adds blendable octave/suboctave fuzz and reactive sag functions.
- **Fender Fuzz Wah Pedal** is retained from Fender's historical archive and historical effects article, which explicitly identify its fuzz and wah functions. It is recorded under Fuzz because the target dirt function is explicit.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Santa Ana Overdrive**: Overdrive only. Explicit Fender product identity is overdrive.
- **Smolder Acoustic Overdrive**: Overdrive only. Fender explicitly names it as an overdrive pedal; its Drive control and acoustic distortion application are operating range and context.
- **Hammertone Overdrive**: Overdrive only. Explicit Fender product identity is an overdrive circuit.
- **Pugilist Distortion**: Distortion only. Product-level Fender naming is explicit despite lead-drive tones within its range.
- **Full Moon Distortion**: Distortion only. Product-level Fender naming is explicit; onboard boost is secondary.
- **Compugilist Compressor/Distortion**: Distortion only. Fender explicitly identifies its distortion circuit; compressor remains secondary.
- **Hammertone Distortion**: Distortion only.
- **Hammertone Metal**: Distortion only.
- **Fender Blender**: Distortion only. Fender's historical article explicitly labels the original unit a distortion effect.
- **The Pelt Fuzz**: Fuzz only.
- **The Trapper Dual Fuzz**: Fuzz only.
- **Hammertone Fuzz**: Fuzz only.
- **Fender Shields Blender**: Fuzz only. Fender explicitly calls it a signature fuzz pedal; octave/suboctave and sag features remain secondary.
- **Fender Fuzz Wah Pedal**: Fuzz only for this taxonomy. Wah is a secondary non-target effect.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Santa Ana Overdrive V2**, **Pugilist Distortion V2**, and **The Pelt V2 Fuzz** are consolidated with their underlying Santa Ana, Pugilist, and Pelt identities rather than treated as separate products.
- **Fender Blender Custom** is not promoted as a separate underlying dirt identity. Fender's archive places it as a later Fender Blender product/variant, so it is consolidated under Fender Blender for the initial census.
- **Fender Shields Blender** remains a distinct named product from the historical Fender Blender because Fender presents it as a new signature pedal with a different product identity, even though it is explicitly based on the vintage Blender circuit.
- Fender's 2018/2019 original pedal line and the Hammertone series are retained as separate named product families where Fender gives them distinct product names.
- Cosmetic editions, V2 revisions, and hardware/presentation variants do not create additional dirt identities.

## Exclusions
- Fender's 1986–1987 imported effects that merely carried the Fender name are not promoted when Fender's own historical article states that they were not actually built by Fender. This keeps builder attribution strict.
- Engager Boost and other boost-only products are excluded even when they can push an amplifier into breakup.
- Compression, buffer, wah, modulation, delay, reverb, filter, tuner, and other non-dirt products are outside target scope.
- Tone Master Pro, Mustang, and other multi-effects/modeling products are not mined for virtual effect-model identities.
- Generic distortion language inside an explicitly named overdrive or fuzz product does not create a second catalog identity unless Fender provides product-level support for the additional target category.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/42585135309851-Effects-Pedals-Current
- https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/42585132488987-Effects-Pedals-Archive
- https://www.fender.com/articles/behind-the-scenes/vintage-fender-effects-from-the-1950s-1980s
- https://spotlight.fender.com/newsroom/news/284
- https://www.fender.com/products/santa-ana-v2-overdrive
- https://www.fender.com/products/pugilist-v2-distortion
- https://www.fender.com/products/the-pelt-v2-fuzz
- https://www.fender.com/products/full-moon-distortion
- https://www.fender.com/products/mtg-tube-distortion-pedal
- https://www.fender.com/products/compugilist-compressor-distortion
- https://www.fender.com/products/smolder-acoustic-overdrive
- https://www.fender.com/products/the-trapper-dual-fuzz
- https://www.fender.com/products/hammertone-overdrive
- https://www.fender.com/products/hammertone-distortion
- https://www.fender.com/products/hammertone-metal
- https://www.fender.com/products/hammertone-fuzz
- https://www.fender.com/products/fender-shields-blender

## Checkpoint
Block 085 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Fender” returned no prior match before addition, so Fender is treated as an unrepresented builder identity in the active census. The block records Santa Ana Overdrive, Smolder Acoustic Overdrive, and Hammertone Overdrive as Overdrive, plus historical Fender Drive Pedal and Yngwie Malmsteen Overdrive Pedal; Pugilist, Full Moon, MTG Tube, MTG:LA, Compugilist, Hammertone Distortion, Hammertone Metal, Fender Distortion Pedal, and Fender Blender as Distortion; and The Pelt, The Trapper, Hammertone Fuzz, Fender Shields Blender, and historical Fender Fuzz Wah as Fuzz. Santa Ana/Pugilist/Pelt V2 versions are consolidated with their underlying products. Fender Blender Custom is consolidated with Fender Blender. Imported 1986–1987 Fender-logo effects are excluded where Fender itself states they were not built by Fender.

# Block 086 — MOOER Audio

## Builder
**MOOER Audio / MOOER**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- MOOER official product catalog and product pages
- MOOER official product pages/manuals for Green Mile, Blues Crab, Blues Mood, Black Secret, Hustle Drive, Cruncher, Blade, Solo, Rage Machine, and Ultra Drive MKII
- MOOER official/current product-index pages identifying dirt products by category
- MOOER manufacturer promotional material for Blue Faze and core Micro Series pedals
- authoritative Effects Database catalog coverage for the broader Micro Series, including Blue Faze, Grey Faze, Triangle Buff, Fog, Thunderball, Greenwood, Eight Hands, and original Ultra Drive
- 2020 MOOER retail catalog material for the established Micro Series dirt lineup
- active-repository search for “Mooer” and “MOOER” before addition

The active repository contained no prior MOOER match before this block was added. MOOER is treated as the builder/brand identity for MOOER-branded dirt pedals documented below. The census stays focused on standalone physical dirt pedals and does not convert modeled effects inside MOOER multi-effects into separate product identities.

The reviewed material supports a substantial MOOER Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz catalog, especially within the Micro Series. Product names and manufacturer category labels are treated as authoritative where explicit; broader historical catalog entries are retained when the builder relationship and dirt function are well documented.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Blues Crab
- Blues Mood
- Green Mile
- Rumble Drive

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Ultra Drive
- Greenwood

Verification notes:
- **Blues Crab** is explicitly labeled by MOOER as an Overdrive Pedal.
- **Blues Mood** is explicitly labeled by MOOER as Overdrive and described as a blues overdrive.
- **Green Mile** is explicitly labeled by MOOER as Overdrive.
- **Rumble Drive** is documented by MOOER as a Dumble-style overdrive with round, smooth overdrive response.
- **Ultra Drive** is retained as the original Micro Series Overdrive identity in authoritative catalog coverage. The later **Ultra Drive MKII** is kept as a separate named product and is classified as Distortion because MOOER explicitly labels the MKII that way.
- **Greenwood** is retained as the historical Micro Series Mix Overdrive identity documented in authoritative MOOER catalog coverage.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Black Secret
- Hustle Drive
- Cruncher
- Blade
- Solo
- Rage Machine
- Ultra Drive MKII
- Thunderball

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Eight Hands

Verification notes:
- **Black Secret** is explicitly labeled by MOOER as a Distortion Pedal.
- **Hustle Drive** is explicitly labeled by MOOER as a Distortion Pedal despite its open, tube-like drive character and lower-gain LP setting.
- **Cruncher** is explicitly labeled by MOOER as a Distortion Pedal.
- **Blade** is explicitly presented by MOOER as Distortion.
- **Solo** is explicitly presented by MOOER as high-gain Distortion.
- **Rage Machine** is explicitly labeled by MOOER as distortion and is described as a heavy-metal distortion pedal.
- **Ultra Drive MKII** is explicitly labeled by MOOER as a Distortion Pedal. Its Ultra mode is described as a fatter drive tone, but the product remains Distortion rather than gaining an additional Overdrive identity.
- **Thunderball** is retained as Distortion because authoritative MOOER Micro Series catalog coverage identifies it as Bass Fuzz/Distortion.
- **Eight Hands** is retained as a historical MOOER signature distortion identity from authoritative product catalog coverage. It remains distinct from later Eight Hands digital preamp products.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Triangle Buff
- MooerFog / Fog
- Thunderball

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Blue Faze
- Grey Faze

Verification notes:
- **Triangle Buff** is explicitly identified by MOOER as a Fuzz Pedal and is part of the established Micro Series catalog.
- **MooerFog / Fog** is documented as a bass fuzz pedal in authoritative MOOER catalog coverage. The archive uses the product's catalog identity rather than treating the bass orientation as a separate builder relationship.
- **Thunderball** is retained as Fuzz as well as Distortion because authoritative catalog coverage describes it as Bass Fuzz/Distortion.
- **Blue Faze** is documented by MOOER manufacturer promotional material as a vintage fuzz pedal and by authoritative catalog coverage as the Micro Series MFZ1 Blue Faze.
- **Grey Faze** is documented in authoritative MOOER catalog coverage as MFZ4 Grey Faze, a vintage-style fuzz pedal.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Thunderball**: Fuzz + Distortion. Authoritative MOOER catalog coverage explicitly identifies it as Bass Fuzz/Distortion.
- **Ultra Drive** vs. **Ultra Drive MKII**: kept as separate named identities because MOOER's historical catalog distinguishes the original Ultra Drive Overdrive from the later Ultra Drive MKII Distortion.
- **Hustle Drive**: Distortion only. MOOER explicitly categorizes it as Distortion even though it has a lower-gain clean-boost/drive mode.
- **Blues Mood**: Overdrive only. MOOER explicitly categorizes it as Overdrive.
- **Green Mile**: Overdrive only. MOOER explicitly categorizes it as Overdrive.
- **Blue Faze / Grey Faze / Triangle Buff / MooerFog**: Fuzz only. Their lower-output, cleanup, or bass-oriented behavior does not create additional target categories.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Ultra Drive** and **Ultra Drive MKII** remain distinct named products because MOOER's catalog presents them as separate generations with different product-level classifications.
- Cosmetic presentation, catalog-language changes, and ordinary production revisions do not create additional dirt identities.
- Product naming such as MFZ1, MFZ2, MFZ4, MDS1, MDS2, and related SKU/model codes are supporting identifiers rather than separate products.
- Blue Faze, Grey Faze, Triangle Buff, Black Secret, Hustle Drive, Cruncher, Solo, and other Micro Series products remain individual physical pedal identities even when their historical marketing describes the inspiration for the circuit.

## Exclusions
- MOOER Micro Preamp products are excluded from the dirt census as digital amplifier/preamp modeling products rather than standalone named Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz effects. Their modeled amplifier voices are not mined into additional pedal identities.
- GE/Prime/Black Truck/Red Truck and other MOOER multi-effects products are excluded from the individual dirt-pedal census even where their internal modules contain drive, distortion, or fuzz effects.
- Pure Boost and Flex Boost are excluded as boost-focused products.
- Tone Capture, cab simulations, EQ, modulation, delay, reverb, tuner, octave, looper, wireless, and other non-dirt products are outside target scope.
- Generic references to “drive,” “gain,” saturation, or amp-like response do not create a second category when MOOER's named product category is explicit.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.mooeraudio.com/
- https://www.mooeraudio.com/pro/1087702213854908416-168-12.html
- https://www.mooeraudio.com/product/Blues-Crab--141.html

Block 086 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Mooer” and “MOOER” returned no prior match before addition, so MOOER is treated as an unrepresented builder/brand identity in the active census. The block records Blues Crab, Blues Mood, Green Mile, Rumble Drive, Ultra Drive, and Greenwood as Overdrive; Black Secret, Hustle Drive, Cruncher, Blade, Solo, Rage Machine, Ultra Drive MKII, Thunderball, and Eight Hands as Distortion; and Triangle Buff, MooerFog/Fog, Thunderball, Blue Faze, and Grey Faze as Fuzz. Thunderball is the explicit Fuzz + Distortion overlap. Ultra Drive and Ultra Drive MKII remain separate named identities. Micro Preamp models, multi-effects modules, boosts, and non-dirt products remain excluded.

# Block 087 — NUX Audio

## Builder
**NUX Audio / NUX**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- NUX official current Effects catalog and product pages
- NUX official current Mini Core, Core, Reissue, and Verdugo product pages
- NUX official legacy-product pages
- NUX official product/manual material for Drive Core Deluxe MKII, Steel Singer Drive, XTC OD, REC TO Distortion, Fireman, Horseman, Morning Star, Tube Man MKII, Brownie, and 6ixty5ive OD
- authoritative Effects Database catalog coverage for earlier NUX die-cast, Core, Mini, Mini Core, Reissue, and Verdugo products
- active-repository search for “NUX” and “Cherub” before addition

The active repository contained no prior NUX or Cherub match before this block was added. NUX is treated as the builder/brand identity for NUX-branded dirt pedals documented below. The census stays focused on standalone physical dirt pedals and does not convert modeled effects inside NUX multi-effects, amp-modelers, or integrated processors into separate product identities.

The reviewed material supports a broad NUX Overdrive and Distortion catalog and one clearly documented standalone Fuzz product. Current NUX pages provide the strongest category evidence, while authoritative historical catalog coverage is used for discontinued die-cast and earlier stompbox identities.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Queen of Tone
- Horseman
- Morning Star
- Tube Man MKII
- Drive Core Deluxe MKII
- ’59 Bassguy
- ’63 Diamond
- ‘6ixty5ive OD
- Steel Singer Drive
- XTC OD

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Ace of Tone
- Drive Core
- Drive Core Deluxe
- BC-2 Vintage Blues Core
- Scream Bass / Bass Overdrive
- OD-2 Vintage Overdrive
- OD-3 Overdrive
- Mini Supa Drive

Verification notes:
- **Queen of Tone** is explicitly presented by NUX as a dual overdrive combining Horseman and Morning Star circuits.
- **Horseman** is presented by NUX as an overdrive pedal; its distortion capability does not create a second target category because NUX's named product category is Overdrive.
- **Morning Star** and **Tube Man MKII** are explicitly categorized by NUX as Overdrive products.
- **Drive Core Deluxe MKII** is explicitly an 8-way overdrive pedal. The earlier Drive Core Deluxe is treated as the same named product family rather than spawning a variant-only duplicate identity.
- **’59 Bassguy** and **’63 Diamond** are identified by NUX's Reissue catalog as overdrive products; the English pages emphasize amp-in-a-box behavior while NUX's Chinese catalog explicitly labels both as 过载 (overdrive).
- **‘6ixty5ive OD**, **Steel Singer Drive**, and **XTC OD** are explicitly identified by NUX as Overdrive products.
- **Ace of Tone** is retained as a historical dual-overdrive identity; NUX's legacy catalog lists it as a Verdugo stompbox and the product page describes it as dual overdrive.
- **Drive Core** is documented by authoritative catalog coverage as Overdrive. **BC-2 Vintage Blues Core**, **OD-2 Vintage Overdrive**, **OD-3 Overdrive**, **Scream Bass**, and **Mini Supa Drive** are likewise documented as Overdrive stompboxes in authoritative catalog coverage.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Fireman
- Plexi Crunch
- REC TO Distortion
- Brownie

Historical / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Metal Core
- Metal Core Deluxe
- DS-3 Classic Distortion
- HG-5 Modern High Gain
- HG-6 High Gain Distortion
- MX-6 Extreme Distortion
- Mini Distortion

Verification notes:
- **Fireman** is explicitly presented by NUX as a dual-channel distortion pedal.
- **Plexi Crunch** is explicitly described by NUX as high-gain distortion.
- **REC TO Distortion** is explicitly identified by NUX as a heavy distortion pedal.
- **Brownie** is explicitly categorized by NUX as Distortion in the Mini Core catalog and product page.
- **Metal Core** and **Metal Core Deluxe** are documented by authoritative catalog coverage as Distortion products. The newer **Metal Core Deluxe MKII** is excluded because NUX currently classifies it as a preamp pedal rather than a standalone target-category distortion identity.
- **DS-3**, **HG-5**, **HG-6**, and **MX-6** are documented as Distortion products in authoritative NUX catalog coverage; DS-3 is also supported by NUX-era manuals/product material identifying it as Classic Distortion.
- **Mini Distortion** is documented as a standalone NUX Mini Series distortion pedal and remains distinct from the later Mini Core **Brownie**.

## Fuzz
Historical / manufacturer-supported identity:
- FZ-2 Vintage Fuzz

Verification notes:
- **FZ-2 Vintage Fuzz** is documented in authoritative NUX catalog coverage as a standalone fuzz pedal from the earlier die-cast series.
- **Scream Bass** is not separately promoted into Fuzz despite its published range from overdrive to fuzz because its builder-level catalog classification is Overdrive.
- Modeled fuzz effects inside MG/Mighty/Multi-Effects products are excluded and are not treated as physical NUX fuzz-pedal identities.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Scream Bass**: Overdrive only. Authoritative catalog wording says it can range from overdrive to fuzz, but the product itself is categorized as Bass Overdrive.
- **Horseman**: Overdrive only. NUX describes a natural distortion capability, but the product category is Overdrive.
- **Drive Core Deluxe MKII**: Overdrive only. NUX explicitly describes it as an 8-way overdrive pedal with a post boost; the internal circuit choices do not create separate physical product identities.
- **’59 Bassguy / ’63 Diamond**: Overdrive only. NUX's Reissue catalog provides the target category; amp-in-a-box framing does not create an additional Distortion identity.
- **Metal Core Deluxe MKII**: excluded from Distortion because NUX currently presents it as a preamp pedal, not as a standalone target-category distortion product.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Drive Core Deluxe** and **Drive Core Deluxe MKII** are treated as one product-family identity for the initial census, with MKII retained in the product naming because it is a substantial named revision rather than an incidental cosmetic change.
- **Tube Man** and **Tube Man MKII** are consolidated under the current Tube Man MKII identity for the same reason.
- NUX model numbers such as NRO, NDO, NOD, NDS, and earlier die-cast XX-series codes are supporting identifiers rather than separate products.
- Cosmetic revisions, packaging changes, and routine production updates do not create additional dirt identities.

## Exclusions
- NUX MG, Mighty, and other multi-effects/modeling processors are excluded even when they contain modeled overdrive, distortion, or fuzz effects.
- Cerberus is excluded as an integrated multi-effects pedal even though it contains analog overdrive and distortion channels.
- Amp Academy, Solid Studio, Metal Core Deluxe MKII, Micro/amp-modeling products, and other preamp/modeler products are excluded when their product-level identity is not a standalone target dirt category.
- Pure boost products such as Lacerate, Boost Core Deluxe, and Masamune are excluded.
- Compressors, modulation, delay, reverb, EQ, tuner, octave, loop, cab/IR, wireless, power supply, and other non-dirt products are outside target scope.
- Modeled effects inside NUX digital products are not mined into separate physical pedal identities.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://nuxaudio.com/products/
- https://nuxaudio.com/products/effects/
- https://nuxaudio.com/products/effects/reissueseriesstompboxes/
- https://nuxaudio.com/products/effects/minicoreseriesstompboxes/
- https://nuxaudio.com/products/effects/coreseriesstompboxes/
- https://nuxaudio.com/products/legacyproducts/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/queenoftone/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/horseman/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/morningstar/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/tubemanmkii/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/drivecoredeluxemkii/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/steelsingerdrive/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/xtcod/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/plexicrunch/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/rectodistortion/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/fireman/
- https://nuxaudio.com/product/brownie/
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/nux
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/nux/core
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/nux/minicore
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/nux/reissue

## Checkpoint
Block 087 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository searches for “NUX” and “Cherub” returned no prior match before addition, so NUX is treated as an unrepresented builder/brand identity in the active census. The block records a broad Overdrive catalog spanning current Queen of Tone, Horseman, Morning Star, Tube Man MKII, Drive Core Deluxe MKII, ’59 Bassguy, ’63 Diamond, ‘6ixty5ive OD, Steel Singer Drive, XTC OD, plus historical Ace of Tone, Drive Core, Drive Core Deluxe, BC-2, Scream Bass, OD-2, OD-3, and Mini Supa Drive. Distortion includes Fireman, Plexi Crunch, REC TO Distortion, Brownie, and historical Metal Core, Metal Core Deluxe, DS-3, HG-5, HG-6, MX-6, and Mini Distortion. Fuzz includes the historical FZ-2 Vintage Fuzz. Multi-effects/modeling/preamp products and boost-only products remain excluded.

# Block 088 — Visual Sound / Truetone

## Builder
**Visual Sound / Truetone**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Truetone official About/history material establishing Visual Sound as the original company name and Truetone as the 2015 rebrand
- Truetone official archive pages and manuals for Jekyll & Hyde, Route 66, Open Road, VS-XO, Son of Hyde, Angry Fuzz, and GarageTone Drivetrain
- Truetone official manuals for Route 808, Double Trouble, Vans Warped Distortion, and GarageTone Chainsaw
- authoritative Effects Database catalog coverage for the Visual Sound dirt catalog
- active-repository search for “Visual Sound”, “Truetone”, and related names before addition

The active repository contained no prior Visual Sound or Truetone match before this block was added. Visual Sound and Truetone are treated as one builder/company lineage because Truetone's own history states that Visual Sound became Truetone in 2015. The census records the standalone physical dirt products associated with that lineage and does not turn compressor, chorus, delay, power, or buffer products into dirt identities.

The reviewed material supports a substantial Visual Sound / Truetone Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz catalog. Product-level labels are treated as authoritative where available; version-number and cosmetic changes are consolidated unless the product was a genuinely distinct named design.

## Overdrive
Current / archive-supported identities:
- Jekyll & Hyde
- Route 66 American Overdrive
- Open Road
- VS-XO
- Route 808
- Double Trouble
- Drivetrain / GarageTone Drivetrain
- Custom Shop Overdrive

Historical / archive-supported identities:
- Route 66

Verification notes:
- **Jekyll & Hyde** is an explicit dual-channel Overdrive + Distortion product. The Jekyll channel is Overdrive and the Hyde channel is Distortion.
- **Route 66 American Overdrive** is explicitly documented by Truetone as a two-effect housing with an Overdrive channel; the compressor side is excluded from the dirt census.
- **Open Road** is explicitly categorized by Truetone as Overdrive.
- **VS-XO** is explicitly presented as a dual-channel Overdrive.
- **Route 808** is explicitly categorized as Overdrive and is the single-channel form of Visual Sound's overdrive circuit used in Jekyll & Hyde and Route 66.
- **Double Trouble** is explicitly categorized as dual Overdrive.
- **Drivetrain / GarageTone Drivetrain** is explicitly categorized by Truetone as Overdrive. The archive keeps the Reverend-origin design as the same product identity when recorded through Visual Sound's GarageTone line because the builder/company documentation explicitly connects the reintroduced pedal to the earlier design.
- **Custom Shop Overdrive** is documented by Truetone as a dedicated Visual Sound Overdrive product and is retained as its own named identity.
- Earlier **Route 66** and later V2/V3 versions are handled as one Route 66 product identity rather than separate cosmetic/version duplicates.

## Distortion
Current / archive-supported identities:
- Jekyll & Hyde
- Son of Hyde
- GarageTone Chainsaw Distortion
- Vans Warped Distortion

Historical / archive-supported identities:
- Jekyll & Hyde (original)

Verification notes:
- **Jekyll & Hyde** explicitly contains an independent Distortion channel.
- **Son of Hyde** is explicitly documented by Truetone as the complete Hyde distortion circuit in a standalone pedal.
- **GarageTone Chainsaw Distortion** is explicitly identified in the official manual as a Visual Sound distortion pedal.
- **Vans Warped Distortion** is explicitly identified by Visual Sound/Truetone as a limited-edition distortion pedal made in partnership with the Vans Warped Tour.

## Fuzz
Current / archive-supported identity:
- Angry Fuzz

Verification notes:
- **Angry Fuzz** is explicitly identified by Truetone as Fuzz with an integrated octave circuit. The octave feature is secondary to the named Fuzz identity and does not create another dirt product.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Jekyll & Hyde**: Overdrive + Distortion. Both channels are independently labeled by the builder and documented in the same physical pedal.
- **Route 66**: Overdrive only for the target census. Its second channel is compression, which is outside scope.
- **Double Trouble**: Overdrive only. Its two internal channels are both overdrives.
- **VS-XO**: Overdrive only. Both channels are explicitly described as overdrives; higher gain does not create a separate Distortion identity.
- **Angry Fuzz**: Fuzz only. Its integrated octave circuit does not create a separate target category.
- **GarageTone Chainsaw Distortion**: Distortion only. Despite lower-gain use cases discussed by reviewers, the builder's product category is Distortion.
- **Vans Warped Distortion**: Distortion only. It is retained as a distinct named limited-edition product rather than merged into another Visual Sound distortion.

## Historical / variant handling
- Visual Sound became **Truetone** in 2015. The archive treats this as one continuous builder/company lineage rather than separate unrelated builders.
- V1/V2/V3 versions of **Jekyll & Hyde**, **Route 66**, and related product revisions are not multiplied into separate identities unless a substantially different named design exists.
- **Route 808** and **Double Trouble** remain separate named products because each was marketed as its own standalone pedal design, even though they use the same underlying Visual Sound overdrive lineage.
- **GarageTone Drivetrain** and **GarageTone Chainsaw Distortion** remain separate GarageTone named products.
- The limited **Vans Warped Distortion** run remains distinct because it was a separately named, explicitly limited-edition product.

## Unpromoted leads
- **Visual Blues** and **Visual Metal** are documented by Visual Sound/Truetone history as early test-market pedals, but the reviewed primary material does not provide enough product-level category evidence to confidently place them into the Overdrive or Distortion census. They are therefore not promoted into a target category in this block.

## Exclusions
- H2O / Liquid Chorus, Oil Can, Axle Grease, Dual Tap Delay, Time Bandit, Visual Volume, and other non-dirt effects remain outside the target taxonomy.
- Comp 66 and Route 66's compressor channel are excluded as compression products.
- Truetone Clean Boost, Pure Tone buffer products, 1 SPOT power products, and other boost/power/accessory products are excluded.
- No modeled multi-effects identities are created from non-standalone products.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.truetone.com/About/
- https://www.truetone.com/V2-Jekyll-Hyde/
- https://www.truetone.com/V3-Jekyll-Hyde/
- https://www.truetone.com/V3-VS-XO/
- https://www.truetone.com/V2-Open-Road/
- https://www.truetone.com/V2-Son-of-Hyde/
- https://www.truetone.com/V2-Angry-Fuzz/
- https://www.truetone.com/GarageTone-Drivetrain/
- https://truetone.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/V3RT66-Sample-Settings.pdf
- https://truetone.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/V2DT-Sample-Settings.pdf
- https://truetone.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/VansWarped_inst.pdf
- https://truetone.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/GT-Chainsaw-Instructions.pdf
- https://truetone.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/GTDRIVE_Instructions.pdf
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/visualsound
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/visualsound/route808
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/visualsound/doubletrouble
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/visualsound/sonofhyde
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/visualsound/angryfuzz
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/visualsound/openroad

## Checkpoint
Block 088 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository searches for “Visual Sound” and “Truetone” returned no prior match before addition, so Visual Sound / Truetone is treated as an unrepresented builder/company lineage in the active census. The block records Jekyll & Hyde, Route 66, Open Road, VS-XO, Route 808, Double Trouble, Drivetrain, and Custom Shop Overdrive as Overdrive; Jekyll & Hyde, Son of Hyde, GarageTone Chainsaw Distortion, and Vans Warped Distortion as Distortion; and Angry Fuzz as Fuzz. Jekyll & Hyde is the explicit Overdrive + Distortion overlap. Visual Blues and Visual Metal remain unpromoted historical leads because category evidence was insufficient.

# Block 089 — Danelectro

## Builder
**Danelectro**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Danelectro official pedal pages and current pedal catalog
- Danelectro official history material
- Danelectro official product pages for Nichols 1966, The Breakdown, Eisenhower Fuzz, 3699 fUZZ, and Roebuck
- authoritative Effects Database catalog coverage for Danelectro Cool Cat, FAB, mini, Paisley, Wasabi, Billionaire, and other stompbox lines
- historical Danelectro catalogs / dealer catalog material for FAB and earlier stompbox products
- current and historical manual archives for Danelectro dirt products
- active-repository search for “Danelectro” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Danelectro match before this block was added. Danelectro is treated as the builder/brand identity for Danelectro-branded standalone dirt pedals. The census does not turn modeled effects, amplifier products, or non-dirt accessories into separate identities.

The reviewed material supports a broad Danelectro Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz catalog spanning current vintage-series products and several historical stompbox families.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- The Breakdown

Historical / catalog-supported identities:
- Daddy O
- Cool Cat Drive
- Cool Cat Transparent Overdrive
- FAB Overdrive
- Pastrami Overdrive
- Blue Paisley PureDrive
- Wasabi Overdrive
- Cash Cow Overdrive
- Pride of Texas Overdrive

Verification notes:
- **The Breakdown** is presented by Danelectro as a vintage-style drive/boost product. The company describes the Break Up control as progressively increasing drive into the amplifier; authoritative catalog coverage places it in Overdrive.
- **Daddy O**, **Cool Cat Drive**, **Cool Cat Transparent Overdrive**, **FAB Overdrive**, **Pastrami Overdrive**, **Blue Paisley PureDrive**, **Wasabi Overdrive**, **Cash Cow Overdrive**, and **Pride of Texas Overdrive** are documented as standalone Danelectro overdrive products in authoritative catalog coverage.

## Distortion
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Roebuck
- Nichols 1966

Historical / catalog-supported identities:
- Cool Cat Distortion
- Cool Cat Metal
- Cool Cat Metal II
- FAB Distortion
- FAB Metal
- Fab Tone / FAB Tone Distortion
- T-Bone Distortion
- Grilled Cheese Distortion
- Black Coffee Metal Distortion
- French Toast Octave Distortion
- Wasabi Distortion
- Black Paisley Liquid Metal

Verification notes:
- **Roebuck** is explicitly presented by Danelectro as Distortion, based on the 1990s Ibanez Mostortion concept with two clipping options.
- **Nichols 1966** is explicitly described by Danelectro as halfway between distortion and fuzz, so it is retained in both Distortion and Fuzz.
- **Cool Cat Distortion**, **Cool Cat Metal**, **Cool Cat Metal II**, **FAB Distortion**, **FAB Metal**, **Fab Tone**, **T-Bone Distortion**, **Grilled Cheese**, **Black Coffee Metal**, **French Toast Octave Distortion**, **Wasabi Distortion**, and **Black Paisley Liquid Metal** are documented as Danelectro distortion products across the historical catalog families.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Eisenhower Fuzz
- 3699 fUZZ
- Nichols 1966

Historical / catalog-supported identities:
- Cool Cat Fuzz
- FAB Fuzz
- French Toast

Verification notes:
- **Eisenhower Fuzz** is explicitly presented by Danelectro as an octave fuzz pedal.
- **3699 fUZZ** is explicitly presented by Danelectro as the current revival of Steve Ridinger's FOXX Tone Machine-era fuzz design, with octave and EQ changes.
- **Nichols 1966** is explicitly described by Danelectro as a fuzz/distortion hybrid. It therefore remains in both Fuzz and Distortion.
- **Cool Cat Fuzz** is documented as a Danelectro fuzz pedal; its V2 revision is treated as the same product identity rather than a separate design.
- **FAB Fuzz** is documented in Danelectro's FAB catalog and historical dealer material.
- **French Toast** is documented by authoritative catalog coverage as an octave-fuzz/octave-distortion product. The archive records it under Fuzz and Distortion rather than inventing additional identities for the octave function.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Nichols 1966**: Distortion + Fuzz. Danelectro explicitly describes it as a fuzz/distortion hybrid. Published material also demonstrates smooth overdrive behavior, but the builder's own product description does not make Overdrive the named product category, so it is not promoted into the primary Overdrive set.
- **The Breakdown**: Overdrive only. Its six-position Break Up control reaches aggressive drive, but Danelectro does not name it a separate Distortion product.
- **Roebuck**: Distortion only. Secondary descriptions may discuss overdrive-style use, but Danelectro's own product identity is Distortion.
- **French Toast**: Fuzz + Distortion. It is an octave-fuzz/octave-distortion design; octave is a secondary function and does not create a separate product identity.
- **Eisenhower Fuzz**: Fuzz only. The octave feature is secondary to the named Fuzz identity.
- **Cool Cat Drive V2 / Transparent Overdrive V2**: consolidated with their respective underlying Cool Cat Drive / Transparent Overdrive identities rather than creating duplicate production identities.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Cool Cat Drive V2** remains the same product family as Cool Cat Drive for the initial census. **Cool Cat Transparent Overdrive V2** is likewise consolidated with the original Transparent Overdrive.
- **Cool Cat Fuzz V2** is retained as a revision of the Cool Cat Fuzz identity rather than a separate design.
- FAB product numbers such as D-1, D-2, and D-7 are supporting identifiers, not separate products.
- The historical mini “food” pedals remain separate named product identities because they were individually marketed stompboxes.
- Current vintage-series revivals such as **3699 fUZZ**, **Eisenhower Fuzz**, **The Breakdown**, and **Roebuck** remain distinct named products even when their inspiration traces to earlier designs.

## Unpromoted / cautious leads
- Danelectro's historical product families include additional dirt-adjacent mini and specialty products whose builder-level category wording is inconsistent across surviving catalogs. Where the available primary material did not clearly establish a target category, the product was not promoted beyond the supported category.
- **Nichols 1966** is intentionally treated as a category-boundary product rather than forcing it into a single historical taxonomy because Danelectro itself describes it as a fuzz/distortion hybrid.

## Exclusions
- Back Talk, Dan-Echo, Spring King, tremolo, chorus, flanger, vibe, wah, EQ, compressor, tuning, and other non-dirt products are outside target scope.
- Honeytone Mini Amp and other amplifier products are excluded.
- Billionaire boost-only products are excluded, except dirt products explicitly categorized as Overdrive such as Cash Cow and Pride of Texas.
- Modeled or multi-effect products are not mined into separate dirt identities.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://danelectro.com/pedals/
- https://danelectro.com/history/
- https://danelectro.com/pedals/nichols-1966/
- https://danelectro.com/pedals/breakdown-eisenhower/
- https://danelectro.com/pedals/3699-fuzz/
- https://danelectro.com/pedals/back-talk-roebuck/
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/danelectro/coolcat
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/danelectro
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/type/overdrive
- https://media.rainpos.com/9167/2019_may_fineartsmt_catalog.pdf

## Checkpoint
Block 089 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Danelectro” returned no prior match before addition, so Danelectro is treated as an unrepresented builder/brand identity in the active census. The block records a broad historical and current Danelectro dirt catalog across Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz families, with Nichols 1966 handled as the clearest Distortion + Fuzz boundary case and French Toast handled as Fuzz + Distortion. Cool Cat, FAB, mini, Paisley, Wasabi, Billionaire, and vintage-series dirt products are included where builder/product category evidence is sufficiently strong. Non-dirt effects, amplifier products, boosts, and modeled products remain excluded.

# Block 090 — Radial Engineering / Tonebone

## Builder
**Radial Engineering / Tonebone**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Radial Engineering official product pages and legacy-product archive
- Radial official pages for Tonebone Classic, Hot British, Trimode, Plexitube, Regency, Bones Texas, Bones London, Bones Hollywood, and Bassbone OD
- Radial official manuals / FAQs where product-level category language is explicit
- active-repository search for “Radial” and “Tonebone” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Radial or Tonebone match before this block was added. Radial Engineering and Tonebone are treated as one builder/product lineage because Tonebone is Radial's established guitar-pedal line and Radial's own legacy archive groups the later Bones pedals with their Tonebone predecessors.

The reviewed material supports a focused Radial overdrive/distortion catalog. No standalone Radial Fuzz identity with sufficiently strong builder-level evidence was found in the reviewed material.

## Overdrive
Current / archive-supported identities:
- Regency Overdrive and Boost
- Bones Texas Dual Mode Overdrive
- Bassbone OD
- Bones Hollywood Distortion & Overdrive

Historical / archive-supported identities:
- Tonebone Classic
- Tonebone Trimode

Verification notes:
- **Regency** is explicitly identified by Radial as an Overdrive and Boost pedal. Its pre-drive section is a dedicated overdrive circuit; the separate clean boost remains secondary and outside the target category.
- **Bones Texas** is explicitly presented by Radial as a Dual Mode Overdrive pedal with multiple overdrive voicings.
- **Bassbone OD** is explicitly identified by Radial as a Bass Preamp & Overdrive with a dedicated onboard overdrive section.
- **Bones Hollywood** is explicitly identified by Radial as a dual-mode Distortion & Overdrive pedal and therefore belongs in both target categories.
- **Tonebone Classic** is a tube distortion product whose gain range begins at slight overdrive and extends to high-gain saturation. Because Radial's primary product identity is Tube Distortion rather than Overdrive, it is not promoted into the main Overdrive category.
- **Tonebone Trimode** is likewise primarily identified by Radial as a dual-channel tube distortion pedal. Its rhythm channel provides overdrive-range operation, but the builder's primary product category is Distortion, so it is not separately promoted as Overdrive.

## Distortion
Current / archive-supported identities:
- Bones London Dual-Mode Distortion
- Bones Hollywood Distortion & Overdrive
- Hot-British V9
- Classic V9

Historical / archive-supported identities:
- Tonebone Classic
- Tonebone Hot British
- Tonebone Trimode
- Tonebone Plexitube

Verification notes:
- **Bones London** is explicitly identified by Radial as a Dual-Mode Distortion pedal and a solid-state version of the Tonebone Hot British.
- **Bones Hollywood** is explicitly identified by Radial as Distortion & Overdrive.
- **Hot British / Hot-British V9** is a high-gain Tonebone distortion lineage. Radial's original product is labeled Tube Distortion; the V9 is labeled Overdrive in the page title but described throughout as high-gain Plexi-style distortion. The archive keeps the lineage under Distortion rather than multiplying the V9 into an additional category.
- **Classic / Classic V9** belongs to the same Tonebone Classic distortion lineage. Radial identifies the original Classic as a Tube Distortion pedal, while its range extends from slight overdrive to high-gain saturation. The archive keeps the identity under Distortion.
- **Trimode** is explicitly described by Radial as a Tube Distortion pedal.
- **Plexitube** is explicitly described by Radial as a dual-channel Tube Distortion pedal with two distortion channels.

## Fuzz
- No standalone Radial/Tonebone fuzz pedal was established with sufficient product-level evidence in the reviewed authoritative material.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Bones Hollywood**: Overdrive + Distortion. Radial explicitly names both functions in the product title and description.
- **Regency**: Overdrive only. The clean booster is a secondary utility and the builder's FAQ explicitly distinguishes the pedal as power booster + overdrive, not as a separate distortion product.
- **Bones Texas**: Overdrive only. Despite very high-gain settings, Radial explicitly classifies the product as Overdrive.
- **Bassbone OD**: Overdrive only. Its dedicated onboard dirt section is explicitly an overdrive circuit; the larger preamp/DI architecture is not treated as a separate product category.
- **Tonebone Classic / Trimode**: Distortion only. Their gain ranges enter overdrive territory, but Radial's primary product identities are tube distortion pedals.
- **Hot British / Plexitube / Bones London**: Distortion only. Their documented product identities are distortion-focused even where the controls permit lower-gain operation.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Classic V9** is consolidated into the **Tonebone Classic / Classic** product lineage rather than creating a separate underlying identity solely for the later solid-state V9 revision.
- **Hot-British V9** is consolidated into the **Tonebone Hot British** lineage rather than creating a second underlying product solely for the V9 generation.
- The later **Bones Hollywood** and **Bones London** are retained as distinct named products because Radial marketed them as separate compact designs rather than mere cosmetic revisions of the Tonebone pedals.
- Tonebone and Bones are treated as one continuous Radial pedal lineage for builder attribution.
- Product revisions, power-supply changes, and cosmetic differences are not multiplied into separate dirt identities unless Radial presented them as a distinct named design.

## Unpromoted / cautious leads
- Radial's broader product catalog contains many preamps, direct boxes, speaker/interface products, and amp-emulation devices with distortion-adjacent language. These were not promoted unless Radial explicitly identified the standalone product as an Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz pedal.
- **JDX Direct-Drive** is excluded because Radial describes it as an amp-emulation/direct interface product rather than a standalone target-category dirt pedal.

## Exclusions
- JDX Direct-Drive, JDX 500, and other amp-emulation/direct products are outside the target dirt taxonomy.
- PZ-Pre, PZ-Deluxe, AC-Driver, and other acoustic/instrument preamps are excluded when they are not explicitly standalone dirt products.
- DI boxes, switchers, buffers, boosters without a dedicated target-category dirt section, power products, and non-dirt effects are excluded.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/classic
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/hot-british-legacy
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/trimode
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/plexitube
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/regency
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/bones-texas
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/bones-london
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/bones-hollywood
- https://www.radialeng.com/product/bassbone-od
- https://www.radialeng.com/product_category/legacy
- https://www.radialeng.com/blog/what-makes-a-great-overdrive

## Checkpoint
Block 090 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository searches for “Radial” and “Tonebone” returned no prior match before addition, so Radial Engineering / Tonebone is treated as an unrepresented builder/product lineage in the active census. The block records Regency, Bones Texas, Bassbone OD, and Bones Hollywood as Overdrive; Bones London, Bones Hollywood, Hot British, Classic, Trimode, and Plexitube as Distortion; and no standalone Fuzz identity. Variant handling consolidates Classic V9 with Classic and Hot-British V9 with Hot British. Amp-emulation, direct, preamp-only, boost-only, and non-dirt products remain excluded.

# Block 091 — Mid-Fi Electronics

## Builder
**Mid-Fi Electronics / Doug Tuttle**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Mid-Fi Electronics official pedal catalog and individual product pages
- Mid-Fi Electronics official purchase catalog
- Mid-Fi Electronics official pages for Magick "i", Full Frequency Overdrive, Hieracium, Cocoon, House Amp, Yard Sale, Demo Tape Fuzz, Psych Byke, Peace Gun, Glitch Computer, and Random Number Generator
- authoritative Effects Database catalog coverage for Mid-Fi Electronics dirt products
- contemporary retailer/catalog documentation for Clari(not) Fuzz and Country Fuzz
- contemporary product documentation for Another/An-other Fuzz Version and Rise/OverRun
- active-repository search for “Mid-Fi” and “Mid Fi” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Mid-Fi match before this block was added. Mid-Fi Electronics is treated as the builder/brand identity, with **Doug Tuttle** used as the associated designer/builder name where useful for identification. The census stays focused on standalone physical Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz pedals and does not convert modulation, pitch, delay, or other non-dirt functions into target identities.

The reviewed material supports a primarily fuzz-oriented Mid-Fi catalog with several explicit Overdrive products and no separately established standalone Distortion product identity beyond pedals already documented as fuzz or overdrive hybrids.

## Overdrive
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Magick "i"
- Hieracium
- Full Frequency Overdrive
- Yard Sale

Historical / manufacturer-supported identity:
- Overdrive

Verification notes:
- **Magick "i"** is explicitly described by Mid-Fi Electronics as an envelope-controlled overdrive. It is the developed successor to the earlier **Overdrive** pedal described in the builder interview material.
- **Hieracium** is explicitly described by Mid-Fi Electronics as envelope-controlled overdrive with a small tweed-amp character.
- **Full Frequency Overdrive** is explicitly described by Mid-Fi Electronics as an uncompressed, transparent overdrive.
- **Yard Sale** is documented by the builder as a low-impedance, blown-out consumer-electronics-inspired gain pedal; contemporary product listings classify it as Overdrive and Fuzz. The archive records it in both categories because the fuzz/overdrive boundary is part of the product's documented identity.
- Historical **Overdrive** is retained as a separate dated predecessor identity only where needed to document Mid-Fi's early product lineage. Mid-Fi's own interview material states that Overdrive later evolved into Magick "i".

## Distortion
- No standalone Mid-Fi Electronics product was established with sufficiently explicit builder-level product-category wording for Distortion as its primary identity.

Verification notes:
- Mid-Fi products often reach aggressive, blown-out, or highly saturated sounds, but generic use of “distortion” in descriptions does not automatically create a separate Distortion category.
- **Demo Tape Fuzz**, **House Amp**, **Glitch Computer**, **Peace Gun**, **Psych Byke**, and related products remain under Fuzz where the builder or authoritative catalog explicitly identifies them as fuzz products.

## Fuzz
Current / manufacturer-supported identities:
- Rise/OverRun
- Cocoon
- House Amp
- Faulty Channel
- Demo Tape Fuzz
- Another/An-other (Fuzz Version)
- Country Fuzz
- Peace Gun
- Psych Byke
- Glitch Computer
- Random Number Generator

Current / historical / catalog-supported identities:
- Fuzz Wall
- What?
- Clari(not) Fuzz
- Yard Sale

Verification notes:
- **Rise/OverRun** is a current Mid-Fi pedal whose documented core is a gritty fuzz foundation feeding reactive filtering; it is retained as Fuzz rather than treated as a separate modulation product.
- **Cocoon** is explicitly described by Mid-Fi as low-to-mid gain spongy/gritty fuzz.
- **House Amp** uses a dedicated fuzz-amount control and is documented as a busted-amp fuzz pedal.
- **Faulty Channel** is explicitly sold/documented as Fuzz.
- **Demo Tape Fuzz** is explicitly identified by Mid-Fi as a fuzz pedal designed around the sound of guitar driven directly into a 4-track recorder.
- **Another/An-other (Fuzz Version)** is a separately named fuzz version of the Another/An-other design and is retained because the builder's purchase catalog explicitly lists the Fuzz Version.
- **Country Fuzz** is documented by Mid-Fi-related retail material as a fuzz inspired by 1960s country recordings.
- **Peace Gun** is documented as an intermodulating fuzz with a resonant filter.
- **Psych Byke** is explicitly described by Mid-Fi as a buzzy 1960s fuzz.
- **Glitch Computer** is explicitly described as high-gain fuzz with poorly tracking two-octave-down harmony.
- **Random Number Generator** is explicitly described by Mid-Fi as fuzz with random harmonically related low-frequency arpeggiation.
- **Fuzz Wall**, **What?**, and **Clari(not) Fuzz** are supported by authoritative catalog/retail coverage as Mid-Fi fuzz products.
- **Yard Sale** is retained as Fuzz as well as Overdrive because contemporary product documentation classifies it as Overdrive and Fuzz.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Yard Sale**: Overdrive + Fuzz. The builder describes a blown-out, consumer-electronics-inspired gain circuit that cleans up strongly from the instrument volume, while contemporary product listings classify it under both Overdrive/Boost and Fuzz.
- **Rise/OverRun**: Fuzz only for the target taxonomy. Its pitch-reactive filters and modulation are secondary to the fuzz foundation and do not create a separate target identity.
- **House Amp**: Fuzz only. The broken-amp character includes aggressive drive tones, but Mid-Fi's own control description identifies the core gain control as fuzz amount.
- **Demo Tape Fuzz**: Fuzz only. Its saturation can move toward overdrive-like textures, but the builder's named product category is Fuzz.
- **Cocoon**: Fuzz only. Its relatively low gain does not justify a second Overdrive category when the builder explicitly identifies it as Fuzz.
- **Faulty Channel**: Fuzz only. Milder grit settings do not create a second category.
- **Magick "i" / historical Overdrive**: Overdrive only. Generic references to gain or clipping intensity do not create a separate Distortion identity.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Overdrive** and **Magick "i"** are documented as a builder lineage, with Magick "i" representing the later developed form of the earlier Overdrive design. The archive records both only when chronology/product identity requires it, not as cosmetic variants.
- **Clari(not) Clean** and **Clari(not) Fuzz** are treated as functionally distinct named versions because Mid-Fi's catalog explicitly offers separate clean and fuzz versions. The clean version is excluded; the Fuzz version is retained.
- **Another/An-other Clean Version** is excluded from the dirt census; **Another/An-other (Fuzz Version)** is retained as the explicitly dirt-oriented named version.
- Hand-painted artwork, enclosure colors, and normal production changes do not create additional dirt identities.
- Contemporary variants and limited colorways are consolidated unless the builder explicitly names a functionally distinct product version.

## Unpromoted / cautious leads
- **Wasteoid** and **Yard Sale** have dirt-adjacent descriptions in current or secondary material, but Yard Sale has enough explicit dual-category evidence to promote while Wasteoid does not have sufficient product-level category wording in the reviewed sources.
- **Faulty Channel**, **House Amp**, and **Rise/OverRun** can produce sounds commonly described as distortion or overdrive, but those descriptors are not used to create extra categories when the product's named identity is Fuzz.
- The historical Mid-Fi catalog contains one-off/custom combinations and crossover designs. They are not promoted into the main census unless a distinct named product identity is documented.

## Exclusions
- Wave Folder, Electric Yggdrasil, Standard Vibrato, Thought Form, Scrape Flutter, Random Phase & Vibrato, Random Vibrato, Deluxe Pitch Pirate, Clari(not) Clean, Audio Frequency Sample Rate Converter, Pitch Pirate, and other modulation, pitch, delay, or processing products are outside target scope.
- LSD1820 Propaganda Module and other synth/processing-oriented devices are excluded unless the builder presents them as standalone dirt pedals.
- One-off custom combinations, Franken-pedals, and customer-specific builds are not converted into new catalog identities.
- Purely descriptive references to clipping, saturation, broken electronics, or “distortion” do not create a separate category without product-level support.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/pedals
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/purchase
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/magicki
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/full-frequency-overdrive
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/hieracium-
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/cocoon
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/house-amp-
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/yard-sale-
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/demo-tape-fuzz
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/psychbyke
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/randomnumbergenerator
- https://www.midfielectronics.com/glitchcomputer
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/midfi
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/interviews/brands/midfi
- https://www.equipboard.com/brands/mid-fi-electronics
- https://umbrella-company.jp/contents/mid-fi-electronics-country-fuzz/
- https://umbrella-company.jp/contents/mid-fi-electronics-fuzz2018/
- https://www.reverb.com/item/52423280-mid-fi-electronics-another-an-other-fuzz-version

## Checkpoint
Block 091 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository searches for “Mid-Fi” and “Mid Fi” returned no prior match before addition, so Mid-Fi Electronics / Doug Tuttle is treated as an unrepresented builder identity in the active census. The block records Magick "i", Hieracium, Full Frequency Overdrive, Yard Sale, and historical Overdrive in the Overdrive set; no standalone Distortion identity; and a substantial Fuzz set including Rise/OverRun, Cocoon, House Amp, Faulty Channel, Demo Tape Fuzz, Another/An-other (Fuzz Version), Country Fuzz, Peace Gun, Psych Byke, Glitch Computer, Random Number Generator, Fuzz Wall, What?, Clari(not) Fuzz, and Yard Sale. Yard Sale is the explicit Overdrive + Fuzz overlap. Historical Overdrive is documented as the predecessor to Magick "i". Variant and one-off handling remains conservative.
