# Block 092 — VFE Pedals

## Builder
**VFE Pedals / Peter Rutter**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- VFE Pedals official current shop and product pages
- VFE Pedals official catalog/design-file index identifying the standalone pedal designs and their product-level categories
- VFE Pedals official pages for Tiamat, RUP mkII, and Killer Rabbit
- authoritative Effects Database / pedal catalog coverage for historical VFE dirt products
- active-repository search for “VFE Pedals” before addition

The active repository contained no prior VFE match before this block was added. VFE Pedals is treated as the builder identity, with Peter Rutter as the associated founder/designer. The census remains focused on standalone physical Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz products and does not convert modulation, delay, EQ, utility, or other non-dirt products into target identities.

VFE's own catalog explicitly separates Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz designs. The builder also documents several multi-function dirt pedals with more than one target category. Product-level category wording is treated as authoritative where explicit, while generic descriptions of gain or saturation do not automatically create additional categories.

## Overdrive
Current / current-catalog-supported identities:
- Tiamat

Historical / catalog-supported identities:
- Blues King
- Dragon
- Dragon Hound
- Merman
- Pale Horse
- The Scream
- Distortion3

Historical predecessor identity:
- Proto OD

Verification notes:
- **Tiamat** is explicitly described by VFE as its final/definitive overdrive design and is currently offered as a limited pre-order product.
- **Blues King** is explicitly identified in VFE's catalog as a low-gain overdrive.
- **Dragon** is explicitly identified as a dynamic overdrive.
- **Dragon Hound** is explicitly identified as a parallel OD/dist design and therefore belongs in both target categories.
- **Merman** is explicitly identified as a transparent overdrive.
- **Pale Horse** is explicitly identified as a dynamic overdrive.
- **The Scream** is explicitly identified as a classic overdrive.
- **Distortion3** is explicitly cataloged as boost/OD/dist and therefore belongs in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Proto OD** is retained as a historical named predecessor only because VFE documentation identifies it as the earliest form of the Pale Horse lineage. It is not treated as a cosmetic or version duplicate.

## Distortion
Current / current-catalog-supported identities:
- RUP mkII
- Killer Rabbit

Historical / catalog-supported identities:
- Alpha Dog
- Dark Horse
- Distortion3
- Dragon Hound
- Fiery Red Horse
- The Triplet
- Triumvirate

Verification notes:
- **RUP mkII** is explicitly presented by VFE as a multiband distortion pedal. Its individual bands use clipping sections derived from VFE overdrive and distortion designs, but the product-level identity is Distortion.
- **Killer Rabbit** is currently offered by VFE as a stereo distortion machine containing separate overdrive, distortion, and fuzz circuits. It therefore belongs in all three target categories.
- **Alpha Dog** is explicitly identified by VFE as a vintage distortion.
- **Dark Horse** is explicitly identified as a hard-edge distortion.
- **Distortion3** is explicitly cataloged as boost/OD/dist and therefore belongs in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Dragon Hound** is explicitly cataloged as parallel OD/dist and belongs in both categories.
- **Fiery Red Horse** is explicitly described by VFE as a fuzzstortion pedal with large dynamic fuzz/distortion territory. It belongs in both Fuzz and Distortion.
- **The Triplet** is a standalone physical pedal containing a dedicated Blue Clipper distortion section plus compressor and octave functions. It is recorded as Distortion only because the target taxonomy tracks the dirt section rather than secondary effects.
- **Triumvirate** is explicitly described as a multiband distortion pedal and is recorded as Distortion only.

## Fuzz
Current / current-catalog-supported identity:
- Killer Rabbit

Historical / catalog-supported identities:
- Fiery Red Horse
- Fuzz Duo
- Woodchipper

Verification notes:
- **Killer Rabbit** contains a dedicated fuzz circuit derived from VFE's latest Fiery Red Horse and is therefore a Fuzz identity as well as Distortion and Overdrive.
- **Fiery Red Horse** is explicitly described by VFE as a versatile fuzz pedal and fuzzstortion design.
- **Fuzz Duo** is explicitly identified by VFE as a germanium-silicon fuzz.
- **Woodchipper** is explicitly identified as a vintage bass octave fuzz.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Killer Rabbit**: Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. VFE explicitly documents two overdrive/boost circuits, one distortion circuit, and one fuzz circuit inside the standalone pedal.
- **Distortion3**: Overdrive + Distortion. VFE's own catalog identifies it as boost/OD/dist; the boost function is secondary and no separate boost identity is created.
- **Dragon Hound**: Overdrive + Distortion. The builder explicitly identifies the design as parallel OD/dist.
- **Fiery Red Horse**: Fuzz + Distortion. VFE describes it as fuzzstortion with both saturated fuzz and dynamic fuzz/distortion tones.
- **Tiamat**: Overdrive only. VFE identifies it as an overdrive and its gain/compression controls do not justify adding a separate Distortion category.
- **RUP mkII**: Distortion only. Its gain stages include soft clipping, but the builder's product identity is multiband distortion rather than a separate OD identity.
- **The Triplet**: Distortion only. Compressor and octave sections are outside the target taxonomy.
- **Woodchipper**: Fuzz only. The octave function is secondary to the named octave-fuzz identity.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Pale Horse** versions are consolidated into one Pale Horse product identity. VFE's current catalog notes multiple versions of the design.
- **Dragon** versions are consolidated into one Dragon identity; the later Tiamat is retained as a distinct named design because VFE presents it as the final overdrive product.
- **Triumvirate** versions are consolidated into one product identity.
- **Fiery Red Horse** versions are consolidated into one product identity while preserving the named design itself.
- **Killer Rabbit** is retained as a distinct named current product rather than being reduced to its component circuits.
- No enclosure artwork, color, normal production revision, or series-format variation creates a new underlying dirt identity.

## Unpromoted / cautious leads
- **Fuzz Cocktail** appears in VFE's current “ideas I'm tinkering with” section and is not promoted because it is explicitly an idea rather than an established standalone product.
- Other VFE design-file experiments and non-pedal educational/Eurorack projects are not promoted into the dirt census.
- The broad use of “drive,” “saturation,” or “distortion” in descriptions is not enough by itself to create a second target category when VFE gives the product a specific primary taxonomy.

## Exclusions
- Blueprint, Choral Reef, Bumblebee, Mini Mu, Mobius Strip, Old School, Pinball, Rocket EQ, Springboard, Standout/Focus, Tractor Beam, White Horse, Yodeler, Klein Bottle and other non-dirt products remain outside target scope.
- Multi-band or multi-function dirt pedals remain included when they are standalone physical VFE pedal products with explicit target-category circuits.
- DIY documentation itself is not treated as a separate product identity from the assembled pedal design.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.vfepedals.com/
- https://www.vfepedals.com/shop
- https://www.vfepedals.com/product-page/final-overdrive
- https://www.vfepedals.com/product-page/rup
- https://www.vfepedals.com/product-page/killer-rabbit
- https://www.vfepedals.com/product-page/the-motherload-all-vfe-design-files
- https://www.vfepedals.com/product-page/fiery-red-horse
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/vfe/palehorse
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/vfe/thescream
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/vfe/triumvirate
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/vfe/woodchipper

## Checkpoint
Block 092 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “VFE Pedals” returned no prior match before addition, so VFE Pedals / Peter Rutter is treated as an unrepresented builder identity in the active census. The block records Tiamat, Blues King, Dragon, Dragon Hound, Merman, Pale Horse, The Scream, Distortion3, and historical Proto OD as Overdrive; RUP mkII, Killer Rabbit, Alpha Dog, Dark Horse, Distortion3, Dragon Hound, Fiery Red Horse, The Triplet, and Triumvirate as Distortion; and Killer Rabbit, Fiery Red Horse, Fuzz Duo, and Woodchipper as Fuzz. Killer Rabbit is the explicit three-category overlap; Distortion3 and Dragon Hound are Overdrive + Distortion; Fiery Red Horse is Fuzz + Distortion. VFE design-file ideas, non-dirt effects, and non-product experiments remain excluded.

# Block 093 — Empress Effects

## Builder
**Empress Effects Inc.**

## Verification basis
Primary and authoritative sources reviewed:
- Empress Effects official current All Products catalog
- Empress Effects official Distortion & Overdrive collection
- Empress Effects official product pages for Drive, Heavy Menace, Multidrive, Distortion, Fuzz, and Germ Drive
- Empress Effects official Heavy announcement/support material
- active-repository search for “Empress Effects” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Empress Effects match before this block was added. Empress Effects is treated as the builder/brand identity. The census remains focused on standalone physical Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz pedals and does not convert ZOIA modules, modeled patches, reverb/delay modulation features, or other non-dirt products into separate target identities.

The reviewed Empress catalog supports a compact but clearly documented dirt lineup spanning all three target categories. Product-level wording is followed conservatively: explicit overdrive, distortion, and fuzz identities are promoted, while generic references to saturation or clipping do not create extra categories.

## Overdrive
Current / current-catalog-supported identities:
- Drive

Discontinued / historical identities:
- Germ Drive
- Multidrive

Verification notes:
- **Drive** is explicitly described by Empress as a tube-like overdrive with advanced tone control. It appears in the current Distortion & Drive catalog and current All Products catalog.
- **Germ Drive** is explicitly described by Empress as a warm, harmonically rich overdrive with tube-like breakup and is currently listed as discontinued.
- **Multidrive** contains an independently controllable overdrive circuit in parallel with its distortion and fuzz sections. Empress describes the unit as three pedals in one and explicitly identifies its overdrive section.

## Distortion
Current / current-catalog-supported identities:
- Heavy
- Heavy Menace

Discontinued / historical identities:
- Distortion
- Multidrive

Verification notes:
- **Heavy** is explicitly described by Empress as a 2-channel distortion pedal. Empress's historical product announcement identifies high-gain distortion as one of the product's central functions.
- **Heavy Menace** is explicitly described by Empress as its most versatile distortion pedal and as an evolution of the original Heavy. It remains a current product.
- **Distortion** is the named Empress distortion product and is currently listed as discontinued.
- **Multidrive** explicitly blends overdrive, distortion, and fuzz in parallel, so it belongs in all three target categories.

## Fuzz
Discontinued / historical identities:
- Fuzz
- Multidrive

Verification notes:
- **Fuzz** is explicitly identified by Empress as a classic fuzz pedal and is listed as discontinued.
- **Multidrive** contains a dedicated fuzz circuit and is explicitly described by Empress as combining fuzz, overdrive, and distortion in one box.
- No standalone Empress product besides Multidrive and Fuzz was established with sufficiently explicit builder-level Fuzz identity. ZOIA's fuzz module and factory patches are excluded because they are modular/software-like functions inside a multi-effect platform rather than standalone named dirt pedals.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **Multidrive**: Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Empress explicitly states that the pedal blends all three circuits in parallel.
- **Heavy**: Distortion only. Empress's own product announcement calls it a 2-channel distortion pedal. Lower-gain behavior is not enough by itself to create a separate Overdrive identity.
- **Heavy Menace**: Distortion only. Empress explicitly says the Lite(ish) mode remains distortion rather than a transparent boost or edge-of-breakup drive.
- **Drive**: Overdrive only. Its high-saturation range remains part of the overdrive design rather than a separate Distortion identity.
- **Germ Drive**: Overdrive only. Its natural breakup and sustain remain within the builder's explicit Overdrive identity.
- **Distortion**: Distortion only. Although Empress describes its mild setting as gentle breakup that cleans up with the volume knob, the product-level identity is explicitly Distortion. The archive does not manufacture a second Overdrive category from that lower-gain behavior.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Heavy Menace Limited Edition** is consolidated into **Heavy Menace** because Empress describes it as a limited colourway of the same product rather than a distinct circuit or named design.
- Scratch-and-Dent listings are not separate product identities.
- **Heavy** and **Heavy Menace** remain distinct named products because Empress explicitly presents Heavy Menace as an evolution of the original Heavy with a different enclosure and feature set.
- Normal production revisions, artwork, colourways, and sales-condition designations do not create new dirt identities.

## Unpromoted / cautious leads
- **ZOIA** contains OD & Distortion and Fuzz modules, and its factory patches include drive/fuzz combinations, but it is a modular multi-effect platform rather than a standalone named dirt pedal. It is excluded from the builder-to-pedal dirt census.
- Reverb pages that mention overdriven spring modes do not create separate Empress Overdrive products because the dirt is part of a reverb algorithm rather than a standalone dirt pedal.
- Generic mentions of clipping, saturation, breakup, or high gain are not used to create additional target categories when the product-level identity is explicit.

## Exclusions
- ZOIA, ZOIA Euroburo, Echosystem, Reverb, Nebulus, ParaEq, Compressor, Buffer, Phaser, Tremolo, delay products, and other non-dirt Empress products remain outside the target census.
- Scratch-and-Dent products, Limited Edition colourways, and cosmetic variants do not create additional underlying dirt identities.
- ZOIA factory patches are excluded because they are software-defined patches within the ZOIA platform rather than standalone physical dirt pedals.
- Cloning, schematics, components, internal construction, and reverse-engineering material are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://empresseffects.com/pages/all-products
- https://empresseffects.com/collections/distortion-drive
- https://empresseffects.com/products/drive
- https://empresseffects.com/products/heavy-menace
- https://empresseffects.com/products/multidrive
- https://empresseffects.com/products/distortion
- https://empresseffects.com/products/fuzz
- https://empresseffects.com/products/germ-drive
- https://empresseffects.com/blogs/empress-blog/the-heavy-menace-celebrating-10-years-of-heavy
- https://empresseffects.com/pages/product-support

## Checkpoint
Block 093 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Empress Effects” returned no prior match before addition, so Empress Effects Inc. is treated as an unrepresented builder identity in the active census. The block records Drive, Germ Drive, and Multidrive as Overdrive; Heavy, Heavy Menace, Distortion, and Multidrive as Distortion; and Fuzz plus Multidrive as Fuzz. Multidrive is the explicit three-category overlap. Heavy Menace Limited Edition and scratch-and-dent listings are consolidated as variants/conditions rather than new dirt identities. ZOIA modules and factory patches remain excluded.


============================================================
SOURCE FILE: research/builders/BLOCK-2026-09-17-094.md
============================================================
# Block 094 — Dwarfcraft Devices

## Builder
**Dwarfcraft Devices / Benjamin Hinz and Louise Woletz Hinz**

## Verification basis
Primary / authoritative and strong historical sources reviewed:
- Effects Database Dwarfcraft Devices catalog and individual product records
- Dwarfcraft product/press-release material reproduced by Effects Database and contemporary gear publications
- Premier Guitar coverage for Gears
- contemporary retailer/catalog coverage for The Internet, Hair of the Dog, Silver Rose v2, and Dream Mangler
- active-repository search for “Dwarfcraft” before addition

The active repository contained no prior Dwarfcraft match before this block was added. Dwarfcraft Devices is treated as the builder identity. The census remains focused on standalone physical Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz products and excludes pure synth, oscillator, filter, delay, pitch, utility, and other non-dirt devices even when those products can produce harsh or noisy sounds.

Dwarfcraft's catalog is unusually fuzzy about the boundary between fuzz, distortion, and overdrive. Product-level category labels and builder descriptions are therefore used together, with conservative overlap decisions. Cosmetic editions and normal revisions are consolidated unless the named product identity is functionally distinct.

## Overdrive
- Gears
- The Internet
- Spectacular Aenima
- Robot Devil

Verification notes:
- **Gears** is explicitly described as an overdrive with fixed drive character feeding its sub-octave and filter sections.
- **The Internet** is cataloged as an overdrive; contemporary product documentation also identifies its third gain stage as additional distortion, so it is recorded in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Spectacular Aenima** is categorized by Effects Database as Overdrive and its product description repeatedly calls it a fuzz pedal while describing a feedback mode capable of metallic distortion. It is retained as Overdrive + Fuzz rather than creating a separate distortion identity from its feedback behavior.
- **Robot Devil** is cataloged as a Fuzz pedal but its documented sound range explicitly includes overdrive. It is recorded as Fuzz + Overdrive because the product description directly identifies both.

## Distortion
- Abaddon
- Baby Thundaa / Baby Thunder
- Eau Claire Thunder
- The Internet
- The Great Destroyer

Verification notes:
- **Abaddon** is explicitly categorized as Distortion by Effects Database.
- **Baby Thundaa / Baby Thunder** is explicitly described as a full-frequency fuzz/distortion pedal. The later size/name presentation is consolidated into one product identity.
- **Eau Claire Thunder** is cataloged as Fuzz and described as a classically styled distortion-and-sustain pedal with a dedicated Distortion control. It is therefore retained as Fuzz + Distortion.
- **The Internet** is an Overdrive product with a third gain stage providing additional distortion; it is retained in both categories.
- **The Great Destroyer** is cataloged as Fuzz but its builder/authoritative description also calls it a distortion pedal. It is recorded as Fuzz + Distortion because both target identities are directly documented for the named product.

## Fuzz
- Baby Thundaa / Baby Thunder
- Bit Mangler
- Dream Mangler
- Eau Claire Thunder
- HAX
- Hair of the Dog
- Necromancer
- Reese Lightning
- Robot Devil
- Rot Yr Brain
- SheFuzz / formerly Shiva
- Silver Rose v2
- Spectacular Aenima
- Surrender Dorothy
- The Great Destroyer
- The Secret Destroyer
- Zhago

Verification notes:
- **Bit Mangler** is categorized as Fuzz and described as a combination of two fuzz circuits.
- **Dream Mangler** is explicitly documented as a pair of fuzz circuits, including a Disaster Fuzz and Vintage Fuzz Machine section.
- **HAX** is cataloged as Fuzz alongside its ring-modulator function; the fuzz/noise character is part of the named product's identity.
- **Hair of the Dog** is documented and sold as a Fuzz pedal, with a clean/low blend and strong distortion available at high settings; the archive retains it as Fuzz only.
- **Necromancer** is explicitly identified as Dwarfcraft's Super Fuzz-style pedal with EQ.
- **Reese Lightning** is explicitly cataloged as Fuzz.
- **Rot Yr Brain** is documented as a Fuzz pedal in contemporary catalog coverage and is retained as Fuzz only.
- **SheFuzz** is the later name for **Shiva**; the builder documentation explicitly states that Shiva was renamed She Fuzz, so the archive consolidates them into one named lineage rather than creating duplicate dirt identities.
- **Silver Rose v2** is explicitly cataloged as Fuzz and combines Super Fuzz and Eau Claire-style fuzz sections. Its ability to dial one section back to overdrive range does not create a second Overdrive identity.
- **Surrender Dorothy** is explicitly cataloged as Fuzz.
- **The Secret Destroyer** is explicitly cataloged as Fuzz and described as a deluxe Great Destroyer-derived oscillating fuzz.
- **Zhago** is explicitly cataloged as Fuzz.

## Category-overlap decisions
- **The Internet**: Overdrive + Distortion. The product is categorized as an overdrive while its third gain stage is explicitly described as additional distortion.
- **Robot Devil**: Fuzz + Overdrive. The product is sold/documented as a fuzz pedal while its description explicitly states that it covers both fuzz and overdrive territory.
- **Spectacular Aenima**: Overdrive + Fuzz. Effects Database classifies it as Overdrive while the builder description explicitly calls it a fuzz pedal. Metallic distortion appears as a feedback-loop behavior, but no separate Distortion category is created from that secondary effect.
- **Baby Thundaa / Baby Thunder**: Fuzz + Distortion. Dwarfcraft documentation directly identifies it as full-frequency fuzz/distortion.
- **Eau Claire Thunder**: Fuzz + Distortion. It is a named fuzz pedal with an explicit distortion-and-sustain description and Distortion control.
- **The Great Destroyer**: Fuzz + Distortion. The product is categorized as Fuzz while the builder/authoritative description also calls it a distortion pedal.
- **Silver Rose v2**: Fuzz only. One internal fuzz section can be backed down into overdrive range, but the named pedal identity is Fuzz and its overall design is a dual-fuzz machine.
- **Hair of the Dog**: Fuzz only. Strong overdrive/distortion language describes operating range rather than a separate named dirt identity.
- **Necromancer**: Fuzz only. Its higher-gain range remains within the builder's explicit Super Fuzz identity.

## Historical / variant handling
- **Baby Thundaa / Baby Thunder** is consolidated as one product identity; the compact Baby version is a named smaller form of Eau Claire Thunder's design.
- **Shiva** is consolidated into **SheFuzz** because Dwarfcraft explicitly states that She Fuzz used to be called Shiva.
- **Silver Rose v2** is retained as the Dwarfcraft product identity; its relationship to the earlier Devi Ever Silver Rose is not used to create a second Dwarfcraft product entry beyond the named Dwarfcraft release.
- **Dream Mangler** remains distinct from **Bit Mangler** because Dwarfcraft presented Dream Mangler as a separate dual-fuzz product with a Disaster Fuzz section and a Vintage Fuzz Machine section.
- Normal artwork, enclosure, edition, and production changes do not create additional identities.

## Unpromoted / cautious leads
- **The Hurricane** is present in the historical Dwarfcraft catalog but the reviewed source material does not establish a target dirt category strongly enough for promotion.
- **SOMMS**, **Rot Yr Brain**, **Thumping Double Squaresnakes**, and similar experimental devices can create distortion/noise textures, but only products with sufficient explicit target-category evidence are promoted. Rot Yr Brain is retained because contemporary product coverage explicitly identifies it as Fuzz.
- **Super Wizard**, **Wizard of Pitch**, **Pitch Grinder**, **Witch Shifter**, and related pitch-focused devices are excluded even when they can become noisy because their primary identities are pitch/multi-effect products.
- **ARF**, filters, delays, reverbs, tremolos, utility products, and the pure oscillator/synth designs are outside target scope.

## Exclusions
- Afterlife of Pitch, ARF, Body Mod, Dream Mangler's non-dirt behavior, Ghost Fax, Happiness, Loop Mangler, Memento, Minivan Echo, Paraloop, Pitch Grinder, Power Struggle, SOMMS, Stomping Double Squaresnakes, Super Wizard, Synth Mangler, The Curse, The Grazer, Thumping Double Squaresnakes, Total Spack Vibes, Treeverb, Twin Stags, Witch Shifter, Wizard of Pitch, and other non-dirt products remain outside target scope unless their named product identity independently satisfies the target taxonomy.
- Fuzzrocious/Dwarfcraft collaborations are not converted into standalone Dwarfcraft identities unless the product is explicitly presented as a Dwarfcraft product in the builder lineage.
- DIY layouts, schematics, components, cloning information, and internal construction are outside scope.

## Primary source URLs / authoritative references
- https://www.dwarfcraft.com/
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/abaddon
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/babythundaa
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/bitmangler
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/destroyer
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/eauclairethunder
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/gears
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/hax
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/necromancer
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/reeselightning
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/robotdevil
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/shefuzz
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/silverrose/v2
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/spectacularaenima
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/surrenderdorothy
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/secretdestroyer
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/zhago
- https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/dwarfcraft/internet

## Checkpoint
Block 094 complete. Website untouched. Active-repository search for “Dwarfcraft” returned no prior match before addition, so Dwarfcraft Devices is treated as an unrepresented builder identity in the active census. The block records Gears, The Internet, Spectacular Aenima, and Robot Devil in Overdrive; Abaddon, Baby Thundaa / Baby Thunder, Eau Claire Thunder, The Internet, and The Great Destroyer in Distortion; and a broad historical Fuzz catalog including Baby Thundaa / Baby Thunder, Bit Mangler, Dream Mangler, Eau Claire Thunder, HAX, Hair of the Dog, Necromancer, Reese Lightning, Robot Devil, Rot Yr Brain, SheFuzz / formerly Shiva, Silver Rose v2, Spectacular Aenima, Surrender Dorothy, The Great Destroyer, The Secret Destroyer, and Zhago. The most explicit overlaps are The Internet (OD + Distortion), Robot Devil (Fuzz + OD), Spectacular Aenima (OD + Fuzz), Baby Thundaa / Baby Thunder (Fuzz + Distortion), Eau Claire Thunder (Fuzz + Distortion), and The Great Destroyer (Fuzz + Distortion). Pure synth, oscillator, pitch, modulation, utility, and other non-dirt products remain excluded.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 095

## Builder
**Mask Audio Electronics — Alec Breslow**

## Verification basis
Mask Audio Electronics' official About page identifies Alec Breslow as the person behind the company and describes the move from DIY projects into a line of production pedals. The official pedal collection identifies a separate production-pedal catalog, while the site also maintains a distinct DIY Projects catalog. This block limits the census to named physical production pedals and clearly documented former production/limited pedals, excluding DIY-only projects, utilities, modulation, and unresolved catalog names.

The builder's own product descriptions are unusually explicit about fuzz identity and occasionally describe usable overdrive territory. Category overlaps are retained when the manufacturer clearly presents the target function as part of the product's design, but gain behavior alone is not promoted into a second category. Normal revisions, finishes, and small-batch germanium selections are not split into separate identities.

## Overdrive
- Germanium Part Garden
- Part Garden
- LARAN (prebuilt)

## Distortion
- TRUST YOURSELF

## Fuzz
- Germanium Part Garden
- Little Acorn
- MAYBE
- CLUBBER LANG
- Hot Water
- BSRI x MAE | MAYBE THE REAL TREASURE...
- CIVIL MATH
- BLACK MATH
- PART GARDEN
- CASCADER
- Screws
- LARAN (prebuilt)
- YES! (historical / discontinued)
- NO. (historical / discontinued)
- Ears Come To Die (historical limited-edition Fuzz Lab)

## Product notes
- **Germanium Part Garden:** Mask Audio explicitly describes this as an overdrive that can become a fuzz and separately describes fuzz gain/compression behavior. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Fuzz.
- **Part Garden:** the official page describes it as two fuzzes in one pedal and explicitly says it can work as a smooth overdrive, wild fuzz, or anything in between. It is retained in both Overdrive and Fuzz. The manufacturer currently marks the product discontinued as of December 10, 2025, with remaining stock on clearance; this does not create a separate product identity.
- **LARAN (prebuilt):** the official MAE page identifies this as a fully built fuzz made in collaboration with Gorva Design and describes a range from mild, crisp overdrive to full-on fuzz. It is retained in both Overdrive and Fuzz. The DIY kit is not separately counted because the archive is tracking physical pedal identities rather than kit listings.
- **TRUST YOURSELF:** MAE's official page directly identifies the design as a Rat-based pedal. The Stompbox Sonic reference library independently classifies Trust Yourself as Distortion, and a current product reference also lists it as a RAT-style distortion. It is therefore retained under Distortion rather than forcing an Overdrive label from generic gain behavior.
- **Little Acorn:** the official product page describes it as a no-knob Big Muff-style design, and MAE's official Fuzz-tagged production collection includes Little Acorn. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **MAYBE:** the official page explicitly describes the pedal as combining the YES! and NO. circuits and says it can provide full-bodied fuzz; one side can also be pushed through an overdrive. Because the core named identity is a dual-fuzz design, it is retained under Fuzz only rather than promoting its secondary overdrive behavior into a separate category.
- **CLUBBER LANG:** MAE describes this as a Harmonic Percolator-inspired design with a dedicated fuzz stage and increased gain. It is retained under Fuzz only.
- **Hot Water:** MAE describes the pedal as using a Big Muff-style transistor count with four times the gain and explicitly says higher gain increases fuzz compression. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **BSRI x MAE | MAYBE THE REAL TREASURE...:** the official collaboration page explicitly calls this a fuzz pedal and describes its gain staging as moving toward a controllable distortion vibe. It is retained under Fuzz only because the manufacturer presents the product identity as fuzz rather than a separate distortion product.
- **CIVIL MATH:** the manufacturer describes the design as a Russian Big Muff adaptation with a modified boost and muff section. It is retained under Fuzz because the product is explicitly framed around the Russian Big Muff family. The aggressive boost/saturation behavior does not create a separate Distortion identity.
- **BLACK MATH:** the manufacturer describes the design as based on NYC Big Muff reissues and focuses on the muff gain/tone structure. It is retained under Fuzz. Stompbox Sonic's reference library also independently categorizes Black Math as Fuzz.
- **CASCADER:** MAE calls Cascader a one-knob fuzz and describes it as combining a smooth fuzz with a low-gain gated fuzz, while noting the resulting mix can cross into distortion-like saturation. It is retained under Fuzz only.
- **Screws:** the official product page explicitly identifies it as an octave fuzz with an ever-present octave. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **YES!:** MAE's DIY page identifies YES! as the DIY version of the now-discontinued YES! gated fuzz and describes the production identity in explicitly fuzz terms. It is retained as a historical Fuzz identity. The DIY version itself is not separately counted.
- **NO.:** MAE's DIY page identifies NO. as the DIY version of the discontinued NO. octave fuzz. It is retained as a historical Fuzz identity. The DIY version itself is not separately counted.
- **Ears Come To Die:** MAE's official DIY page states that this fuzz was originally released as a limited-edition Fuzz Lab pedal and is now offered as a DIY project. The historical physical pedal identity is therefore retained under Fuzz, while the DIY project listing is excluded as a separate identity.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **DIY-only projects and kits:** excluded from the production-pedal census, including Dark Grime DIY Project, Small Format Drive (SFD) DIY Project, Big Clang DIY Project, Modern Revolt Octave Fuzz DIY Project, YES DIY, NO DIY, LARAN DIY KIT, Titan, Fragments, Ears Come To Die DIY, and other DIY listings. Where a DIY listing directly documents a former physical production pedal (YES!, NO., Ears Come To Die), only the former pedal identity is retained.
- **ALONE:** excluded as modulation/flanger rather than dirt.
- **NECKBRACE:** excluded as a phaser; its internal output-gain trim does not create a target dirt identity.
- **SEND-IN MODS:** excluded as a service rather than a standalone pedal identity.
- **???**: excluded because the official catalog does not provide a resolved product identity suitable for the census.
- **TRUST YOURSELF:** retained under Distortion only. Its Rat association is clear enough to support the target category, but no separate Overdrive entry is created merely from its gain control.
- **MAYBE and BSRI x MAE | MAYBE THE REAL TREASURE...:** fuzz identities retained despite descriptions of distortion/overdrive territory; those descriptions are treated as operating range rather than separate builder-level product categories.
- **Germanium Part Garden variants:** Chef's Choice germanium transistor selections are treated as configurations of one Germanium Part Garden identity, not separate products.
- **Cosmetic/finish variations:** not split into separate identities.
- **Collaborations:** BSRI x MAE | MAYBE THE REAL TREASURE... and LARAN (prebuilt) are retained as cataloged physical pedal identities because MAE presents them in its production pedal catalog; their collaborator relationships do not create duplicate builder identities in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Mask Audio Electronics official site: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/
- About / Alec Breslow: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/pages/about
- Production pedal catalog: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/collections/pedals
- Fuzz-tagged production pedals: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/collections/pedals/fuzz
- All products / DIY separation: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/collections/all
- Germanium Part Garden: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/ge-part-garden
- Part Garden: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/part-garden
- Little Acorn: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/little-acorn
- Trust Yourself: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/trust-yourself
- MAYBE: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/maybe
- CLUBBER LANG: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/clubber-lang
- Hot Water: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/hot-water
- BSRI x MAE | MAYBE THE REAL TREASURE...: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/bsri-x-mae-maybe-the-real-treasure
- CIVIL MATH: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/civil-math
- BLACK MATH: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/black-math
- CASCADER: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/cascader
- Screws: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/screws
- LARAN (prebuilt): https://maskaudioelectronics.com/collections/all/products/laran-diy-kit-1
- YES DIY / discontinued YES!: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/yes-diy
- NO DIY / discontinued NO.: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/no-diy
- Ears Come To Die DIY / historical Fuzz Lab identity: https://maskaudioelectronics.com/products/ears-come-do-die-diy
- Stompbox Sonic reference library: https://stompboxsonic.com/pages/reference-library

## Status
**Verified research block.** Mask Audio Electronics is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The official catalog documents a strongly fuzz-oriented physical pedal lineup, with explicit historical/discontinued fuzz identities and one clear Rat-style distortion identity. Germanium Part Garden and Part Garden, plus the prebuilt LARAN collaboration, have sufficiently explicit manufacturer wording to retain both Overdrive and Fuzz identities. DIY-only listings, modulation, services, unresolved names, and category-inflating gain descriptions were deliberately excluded.


============================================================
SOURCE FILE: research/builders/BLOCK-2026-09-17-096.md
============================================================
# Builder → Pedals Research Block 096

## Builder
**Death By Audio — Oliver Ackermann**

## Verification basis
Death By Audio's official About page identifies Oliver Ackermann as the founder and describes Death By Audio as a handmade effects-pedal builder operating in Queens, NYC. The current official site maintains separate pedal collections for Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz, along with separate collections for delay/reverb, filters, modulation, and extreme/experimental effects. This block limits the census to physical pedals with an explicit target dirt identity in the manufacturer material.

The manufacturer frequently describes a fuzz or distortion pedal as capable of producing neighboring gain ranges. Those operating ranges are not automatically promoted into additional archive categories. A second category is retained only where the product itself is explicitly presented by Death By Audio as having that target dirt identity, rather than merely being able to reach it. Normal reissues/revivals and version naming are consolidated when the manufacturer presents them as the same underlying product identity.

## Overdrive
- Interstellar Overdriver

## Distortion
- Germanium Filter
- Absolute Destruction

## Fuzz
- Fuzz War
- Bass War
- Supersonic Fuzz Gun
- Apocalypse
- Octave Clang V2
- Crossover Fuzz
- Absolute Destruction

## Product notes
- **Interstellar Overdriver:** Death By Audio explicitly calls this a true overdrive pedal and describes its gain control as ranging from subtle boost into harmonically rich overdrive. It is retained under Overdrive only.
- **Fuzz War:** the manufacturer explicitly presents Fuzz War as a fuzz pedal and describes a sweep from booster through overdrive and thick distortion into full fuzz. Following the archive rule, those operating ranges are treated as behavior inside the fuzz identity rather than additional OD/Distortion identities.
- **Bass War:** Death By Audio explicitly calls Bass War a bass fuzz and describes a range from grinding drive through thick distortion to extreme fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Supersonic Fuzz Gun:** the manufacturer directly identifies it as a fuzz pedal built around gated and oscillating fuzz behavior. Distorted guitar sounds are described as part of its range, not as a separate distortion identity.
- **Apocalypse:** Death By Audio explicitly calls Apocalypse a fuzz pedal and describes selectable post-amplifier voices including a War Fuzz setting described as full dynamic distortion. The core product identity remains Fuzz rather than being split into multiple dirt categories.
- **Octave Clang V2:** the current manufacturer page explicitly describes a blend of octave-up fuzz and clangorous tones. Its gain can drive the circuit into overdrive and fuzz, and the internal bias concerns the distortion circuitry, but the named product identity is retained under Fuzz only.
- **Crossover Fuzz:** Death By Audio explicitly presents this as a dual-band fuzz and describes the gain range as clean, overdrive, and towering fuzz. Because the product is explicitly a fuzz pedal, the neighboring drive range is not separately counted.
- **Germanium Filter:** Death By Audio titles this product as “TRUE VINTAGE DISTORTION” and describes a gain range from mild overdrive to blasted-out fuzz. It is retained under Distortion only because the manufacturer gives it an explicit distortion identity; its fuzz/overdrive sweep is treated as operating range rather than additional categories.
- **Absolute Destruction:** the manufacturer's product page explicitly describes both thick, gnarly fuzz and crushing distortion. It is therefore retained in both Fuzz and Distortion.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Evil Filter:** excluded from the core dirt census because the product is fundamentally a multimode filter pedal. Although it contains a dedicated Evil Fuzz section that can operate as a standalone fuzz, Death By Audio's product identity is presented as a filter rather than a target dirt pedal.
- **Echo Dream 2:** excluded because the target identity is delay/echo with a fuzz control rather than a standalone dirt product.
- **Time Shadows II:** excluded because it is a multi-delay/filter/pitch-morphing pedal with a digital-distortion component rather than a standalone dirt identity.
- **Amp Crash:** excluded from the core builder-to-dirt catalog because it is a real amplifier/speaker/microphone recording and multi-effects platform, even though its internal amplifier can produce overdrive and fuzz.
- **Thee Treble Overload:** excluded because Death By Audio presents it as a treble-boost pedal; its ability to hit distortion territory does not create a standalone distortion identity.
- **Space Bender, Moonbeam Phaser, Reverberation Machine, Rooms, vocal effects, utilities, and other non-target products:** excluded.
- **Historical leads:** older names such as Armageddon and Waveformer Destroyer were not promoted here because the reviewed current manufacturer material did not establish a sufficiently direct product-level dirt classification for the present block. They remain outside the categorized census until directly verified.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Death By Audio official About: https://deathbyaudio.com/pages/about-timeline
- Death By Audio collections index: https://deathbyaudio.com/collections
- Interstellar Overdriver: https://deathbyaudio.com/collections/1/products/interstellar-overdriver
- Fuzz War: https://deathbyaudio.com/collections/1/products/fuzz-war
- Bass War: https://deathbyaudio.com/products/bass-war
- Supersonic Fuzz Gun: https://deathbyaudio.com/products/supersonic-fuzz-gun
- Apocalypse: https://deathbyaudio.com/collections/fuzz/products/apocalypse
- Octave Clang V2: https://deathbyaudio.com/products/octave-clang
- Crossover Fuzz: https://deathbyaudio.com/collections/all-pedals/products/crossover-fuzz
- Germanium Filter: https://deathbyaudio.com/products/germanium-filter
- Absolute Destruction: https://deathbyaudio.com/collections/homepage-wholesale/products/absolute-destruction

## Status
**Verified research block.** Death By Audio is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The reviewed manufacturer material establishes a broad dirt footprint centered on fuzz, with a clear overdrive identity in Interstellar Overdriver and explicit distortion identities in Germanium Filter and Absolute Destruction. Fuzz War, Bass War, Supersonic Fuzz Gun, Apocalypse, Octave Clang V2, and Crossover Fuzz are retained as fuzz identities without manufacturing extra OD/Distortion entries from mere gain range descriptions. Non-dirt and multifunction products were deliberately excluded.


============================================================

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 097

## Builder
**Fjord Fuzz — Daniel Thornhill**

## Verification basis
Fjord Fuzz's official site maintains a dedicated Fuzz section and currently lists multiple standalone fuzz products. The builder's own product pages and official Fjord Fuzz videos provide product-level descriptions; Guitar Pedal X and other authoritative pedal references were used only to corroborate historical/current identities where the official site no longer exposes an older product page prominently. This block is limited to physical pedals and the archive's three target dirt categories.

The builder is strongly fuzz-focused. The archive does not promote neighboring overdrive, preamp, distortion, or boost behavior into separate categories unless the product itself is explicitly identified that way. Normal revisions and enclosure/finish changes are consolidated into the underlying product identity.

## Overdrive
- Embla — historical / discontinued identity

## Distortion
- None established with sufficient product-level evidence

## Fuzz
- Odin
- Midgard
- Thor
- Midnattsol
- Dovre
- Hedda
- Måne
- SOL
- Embla — historical / discontinued identity
- Bifrost — historical / discontinued identity
- Gjallarhorn — historical identity
- Berserk — consolidated identity across V1/V2/V3
- Freia — historical / discontinued identity
- Kvasir — historical / discontinued identity
- Mjølner — historical identity
- Bjørn — historical identity
- Bufo — historical identity
- Fjord-Fuzz Super-Fuzz — historical identity

## Product notes
- **Odin:** Fjord Fuzz's official product page explicitly identifies the core circuit as an octave fuzz and says the regular mode is a classic-style fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Midgard:** the official product page explicitly calls MIDGARD a warm fuzz pedal and describes its pickup-simulation/signal-translator section as an auxiliary feature. It remains Fuzz only; the translator/preamp functionality is not treated as a separate dirt identity.
- **Thor:** Fjord Fuzz's official 2025 video explicitly identifies THOR as a gated, bass-forward fuzz pedal. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Midnattsol:** the official product page explicitly describes three modes of fuzz plus vibe/filter-style expression. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Dovre:** Fjord Fuzz's official 2024 product video calls DOVRE a fuzz + reverb pedal and describes the fuzz as the primary dirt section. It is retained under Fuzz; the reverb is outside scope.
- **Hedda:** builder-level descriptions identify HEDDA as a vintage fuzz with high dynamic range and a chaos-oscillation switch. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Måne:** the official Fjord Fuzz video calls MÅNE a dual fuzz / dual filter pedal. It is retained under Fuzz only; the filter is not a target dirt category.
- **SOL:** Fjord Fuzz's published catalog materials identify SOL as a Fuzz & Vibe design. It is retained under Fuzz; the vibe function is outside scope.
- **Embla:** the official constructor description says EMBLA was designed as a fuzz that behaves like an overdrive. Because the manufacturer explicitly frames the design around both fuzz and overdrive behavior, it is retained in both Fuzz and Overdrive. The identity is historical/discontinued in the current range.
- **Bifrost:** Fjord Fuzz's official 2020 video describes a fuzz section that can clean up to preamp drive or clean signal inside a combined fuzz/delay pedal. It is retained under Fuzz only because the named dirt identity is fuzz; the delay is outside scope.
- **Gjallarhorn:** period builder-description material identifies it as a reworked FY-6 / Super-Fuzz style fuzz and notes that lower gain yields a less-saturated drive. The archive retains Fuzz only rather than inferring a second Overdrive identity from that gain range.
- **Berserk:** the product family is described as a combined take on classic Fuzz Face and Tone Bender approaches. V1/V2/V3 are consolidated as one Berserk identity; descriptive references to lower-gain grit do not create an additional target category.
- **Freia:** the builder's own 2022 video explicitly identifies FREIA as a fuzz created from the Uni-Vibe preamp and describes the range from clean preamp tones to thick gated fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz only.
- **Kvasir:** manufacturer-originated description reproduced by specialist references identifies Kvasir as a vintage-style transistor fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Mjølner:** historical Fjord Fuzz catalog coverage identifies Mjølner as a high-gain fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Bjørn:** historical catalog coverage identifies Bjørn as a vintage dark gated fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Bufo:** historical catalog coverage identifies Bufo as a croaky fuzz-drive design. The archive retains it under Fuzz because the documented product identity is fuzz-oriented and the drive wording describes behavior rather than a separately named overdrive product.
- **Fjord-Fuzz Super-Fuzz:** historical catalog coverage identifies this as a Super-Fuzz family pedal. It is retained under Fuzz.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Frei / Frei 2:** excluded because the product identity is Uni-Vibe/vibe modulation rather than a dirt pedal. The separate Freia is retained because Fjord Fuzz explicitly transformed that preamp into a fuzz product.
- **Loke:** excluded because it is a delay/tape-manipulation multi-effect. Its preamp drive capability does not create a standalone dirt identity.
- **FJÆR / Mime:** excluded because they are reverb products.
- **Njord / Frøy:** excluded because their primary identity is modulation/rotary/vibe rather than dirt.
- **Bifrost:** delay is not separately counted; only its explicit fuzz section contributes to the dirt census.
- **Version handling:** Odin II/III/IV, Berserk V1/V2/V3, and other normal revisions are consolidated unless the manufacturer presents a materially different product identity.
- **Gain-range inflation:** products described as reaching clean boost, overdrive, or distortion territory remain Fuzz-only unless the builder establishes that target function as a distinct product identity.
- **No standalone distortion identity:** reviewed material did not establish a sufficiently explicit Fjord Fuzz product whose primary product identity is Distortion.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Fjord Fuzz official site: https://fjordfuzz.no/
- Main Line-Up: https://fjordfuzz.no/main/
- Fuzz catalog: https://fjordfuzz.no/main-line-up/fuzz/
- Odin: https://fjordfuzz.no/odin-iii/
- Midgard: https://fjordfuzz.no/midgard/
- Midnattsol: https://fjordfuzz.no/midnattssol/
- Loke: https://fjordfuzz.no/loke/
- Fjord Fuzz official THOR video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RfOKIDUgzJw
- Fjord Fuzz official DOVRE video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HME3oU9R1wI
- Fjord Fuzz official MÅNE video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MYpYA7TyFgA
- Fjord Fuzz official FREIA video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Iy1NgwTWDq4
- Fjord Fuzz official BIFROST video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ys0MBZYn2kY
- Guitar Pedal X — Fjord Fuzz historical/current catalog: https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/fjord-fuzz---daniel-thornhills-cutting-edge-futuristic-fuzz-pedals-from-the-west-coast-of-norway
- Guitar Pedal X — 15 Favourite Fjord Fuzz Pedals: https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/gpx-blog/15-favourite-fjord-fuzz-pedals
- Delicious Audio — Kvasir: https://delicious-audio.com/new-pedal-fjord-fuzz-kvasir/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Fjord Fuzz is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The official catalog establishes a current core of fuzz products including Odin, Midgard, Thor, and Midnattsol, with additional fuzz identities documented through the builder's official videos and authoritative historical coverage. Embla is the clearest explicit Overdrive + Fuzz overlap. No standalone Distortion identity was established strongly enough to promote. Modulation, reverb, delay, and multi-effect functions were deliberately excluded.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 098

## Builder
**EarthQuaker Devices — Jamie Stillman / EarthQuaker Devices**

## Verification basis
EarthQuaker Devices' official site separates its pedal catalog into Distortion & Overdrive, Fuzz, and other effect families, and identifies the company as a hand-built pedal maker in Akron, Ohio. The current catalog and official Legacy pages were used as the main source for the dirt census. EarthQuaker's own product pages and articles were also used to verify older products that are no longer in production or no longer prominently listed in the main catalog.

The archive keeps product identity separate from mere operating range. A fuzz that can reach overdrive territory is not automatically counted as Overdrive, and a distortion pedal that contains an octave-fuzz stage is not automatically counted as Fuzz. A second category is retained where EarthQuaker itself explicitly describes the product as having that target dirt identity.

## Overdrive
- Blumes
- Gary
- Plumes
- Special Cranker
- Gray Channel — historical / discontinued
- Dunes — historical / discontinued
- Monarch — historical / discontinued
- Palisades — historical / discontinued
- Speaker Cranker — historical / discontinued
- Talons — historical / discontinued
- Westwood — historical / discontinued
- White Light — historical / discontinued

## Distortion
- Acapulco Gold
- Gary
- Sunn O))) HalfLife
- Sunn O))) Life Pedal / Life Pedal V2 — consolidated identity
- Zoar

## Fuzz
- Barrows
- Bellows — historical / discontinued
- Black Ash — historical / discontinued
- Chelsea
- Cloven Hoof — historical / discontinued
- Dirt Transmitter — historical / discontinued
- Dirt Transmitter Legacy Reissue — consolidated with Dirt Transmitter
- Erupter — historical / discontinued
- Fuzz Master General — historical / discontinued
- Fuzz Master General Legacy Reissue — consolidated with Fuzz Master General
- Gary
- Hizumitas
- Hoof
- Hoof Reaper — historical / discontinued
- Park Fuzz Sound — historical / discontinued
- Rancho de la Luna Dirt Transmitter — historical / discontinued
- Sound Shank — historical / discontinued
- Spires — historical / discontinued
- Terminal — historical / discontinued
- Tone Reaper — historical / discontinued
- Dream Crusher — historical / discontinued

## Product notes
- **Gary:** EarthQuaker explicitly names Gary as a pulse-width-modulated fuzz paired with a natural overdrive. It is therefore retained in both Fuzz and Overdrive. The overdrive side can move from clean boost through distortion, but the separate fuzz and overdrive identities are the manufacturer's own description.
- **Black Ash:** EarthQuaker explicitly describes Black Ash as a fuzz/overdrive. It is retained in both categories despite its overall fuzz-oriented identity.
- **Bellows:** the manufacturer calls it a Fuzz Driver and describes a range from amp-like grit through fuzzy saturation and distorted sounds. It remains Fuzz only because the named product identity is a fuzz/driver rather than a separately identified overdrive product.
- **Chelsea:** EarthQuaker labels Chelsea as a Low End Fuzz Driver and explicitly calls it the chosen bass fuzz for several LCD Soundsystem recordings. Its light-overdrive-to-distortion range is treated as behavior inside the fuzz identity, not a second category.
- **Hoof:** EarthQuaker describes the Hoof as a Muff-style fuzz that can produce warm, gritty overdrive at lower gain. It remains Fuzz only because the product identity is explicitly fuzz.
- **Hoof Reaper:** EarthQuaker describes the pedal as combining the Hoof and Tone Reaper fuzzes with an octave-up section. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Tone Reaper:** EarthQuaker's official Hoof Reaper page explicitly identifies the Tone Reaper as a vintage-style fuzz device. It is retained as a historical standalone fuzz identity.
- **Dream Crusher:** EarthQuaker's official Spires page explicitly refers to the discontinued Dream Crusher as a two-transistor fuzz. It is retained as a historical fuzz identity.
- **Spires:** EarthQuaker explicitly describes both channels as fuzz, one based on a Rosac Nu-Fuzz and the other on the discontinued Dream Crusher. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Fuzz Master General:** EarthQuaker's Legacy catalog identifies it as an Octave Fuzz Blaster. The Legacy Reissue is consolidated with the underlying Fuzz Master General identity.
- **Dirt Transmitter:** EarthQuaker identifies it as a Fuzz Driver. The Legacy Reissue is consolidated with the same underlying pedal identity.
- **Rancho de la Luna Dirt Transmitter:** the official Legacy catalog lists this as a Fuzz Driver and treats it as its own named collaboration/product identity, so it is retained separately from the standard Dirt Transmitter.
- **Cloven Hoof:** the official Legacy catalog identifies it as a Fuzz Grinder. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Erupter:** EarthQuaker's Legacy catalog identifies it as Ultimate Fuzz Tone. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Park Fuzz Sound:** the Legacy catalog identifies it as Vintage Germanium Fuzz Tone. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Sound Shank:** the Legacy catalog identifies it as Germanium Fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Terminal:** EarthQuaker explicitly calls Terminal a Destructive Fuzz Device and describes its fuzz control and fuzz range. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Acapulco Gold:** EarthQuaker classifies it as Power Amp Distortion. Its ability to become very aggressive does not create a separate Fuzz identity.
- **Sunn O))) Life Pedal:** EarthQuaker's current product page describes the product identity as a distortion with a blendable octave-up and booster. The internal octave-fuzz-inspired stage is not counted separately as Fuzz.
- **Sunn O))) HalfLife:** EarthQuaker explicitly identifies it as an Octave Distortion + Booster. It remains Distortion only.
- **Gray Channel:** EarthQuaker describes it as two channels of classic overdrive. Clipping settings can reach biting distortion, but the named product identity remains Overdrive.
- **Palisades:** EarthQuaker explicitly calls it an 808-style overdrive and a Mega Ultimate Overdrive. Its clipping options can become more distorted, but it remains Overdrive only.
- **Dunes:** EarthQuaker describes Dunes as a condensed take on the Palisades 808-style overdrive and calls it a transparent overdrive. It remains Overdrive only.
- **White Light:** EarthQuaker's Legacy Reissue page explicitly labels it Overdrive. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Monarch:** EarthQuaker's own published material identifies the discontinued Monarch as an overdrive. It is retained as a historical Overdrive identity.
- **Speaker Cranker:** the official Legacy catalog explicitly labels it Overdrive.
- **Talons:** the official Legacy catalog explicitly labels it High Gain Overdrive.
- **Westwood:** EarthQuaker's official material describes Westwood as a light-to-medium gain drive and the product identity as the Translucent Drive Manipulator. It remains Overdrive only.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Life Pedal octave fuzz stage:** not separately counted as Fuzz because EarthQuaker's product identity is explicitly a distortion with octave and boost functions.
- **Tentacle:** excluded because the standalone product identity is analog octave up, not one of the three target dirt categories.
- **Arrows, Bows, Tone Job, ZE​QD-Pre, and other boost/preamp/EQ products:** excluded because their primary identities are outside Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz even where they can push an amplifier harder.
- **Bit Commander, Data Corrupter, Pitch Bay, Rainbow Machine, The Wave Transformer, and other pitch/synth products:** excluded because their primary identities are octave, synth, pitch, or related effects rather than standalone dirt.
- **Modulation, delay, reverb, compressor, routing, and utility products:** excluded.
- **Version handling:** normal revisions and legacy reissues are consolidated when EarthQuaker presents them as the same underlying product identity. Life Pedal V2 and the original Life Pedal are treated as one product identity for the census.
- **Gain-range inflation:** products described as reaching overdrive or distortion territory remain in their primary EarthQuaker category unless the manufacturer explicitly gives them a second standalone target identity.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- EarthQuaker Devices About: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/about
- EarthQuaker Devices Devices: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/devices
- EarthQuaker Devices Distortion & Overdrive: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/overdrive
- EarthQuaker Devices Fuzz: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/fuzz
- EarthQuaker Devices Legacy: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/legacy
- Gary: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/gary
- Black Ash: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/black-ash
- Bellows: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/bellows
- Chelsea: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/chelsea
- Hoof Reaper: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/hoof-reaper
- Spires: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/spires
- Terminal: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/terminal
- Palisades: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/palisades
- Dunes: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/dunes
- White Light Legacy Reissue: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/white-light-reissue
- Gray Channel: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/gray-channel
- Sunn O))) Life Pedal: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/life-pedal
- Sunn O))) HalfLife: https://www.earthquakerdevices.com/half-life

## Status
**Verified research block.** EarthQuaker Devices is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The current and official Legacy catalogs establish a broad dirt history across Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz, with explicit two-category overlaps for Gary and Black Ash. The census deliberately keeps fuzz products from becoming extra overdrive/distortion entries simply because their gain controls reach those sounds, while the Life Pedal remains Distortion despite its octave-fuzz-inspired internal stage. Historical identities such as Monarch, Tone Reaper, Dream Crusher, and older EarthQuaker fuzz/drive products are retained where official EarthQuaker material directly establishes their product identity.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 099

## Builder
**Caroline Guitar Company — Philippe Herndon / Caroline Guitar Company**

## Verification basis
Caroline Guitar Company's official site describes the company as making pedals in Columbia, South Carolina. Its current product catalog, Legacy Products section, official product pages, manuals, and company articles were used to establish the dirt catalog. Older products are retained only where Caroline's own material directly identifies the product and its dirt function.

The archive keeps product identity separate from a pedal merely being capable of reaching another gain range. A named fuzz that can be used as an overdrive remains Fuzz unless Caroline explicitly presents it as an Overdrive product too. Clearly hybrid names such as “fuzzstortion” are retained in both relevant categories when the builder itself uses that identity.

## Overdrive
- Aaron Graves Overdrive
- The Blues
- Haymaker — historical / discontinued
- Icarus V2 / Icarus — historical / discontinued
- Wave Cannon / Cannonball — historical identity

## Distortion
- Wave Cannon Zero
- Wave Cannon MKII / Superdistorter — historical / discontinued
- CROM
- Wave Cannon / Cannonball — historical identity

## Fuzz
- CROM
- Hawaiian Pizza
- Shigeharu
- Olympia — historical / discontinued
- Wave Cannon / Cannonball — historical identity

## Product notes
- **Aaron Graves Overdrive:** Caroline explicitly calls this an Overdrive and describes it as a preamp/power-amp drive pedal. It is retained under Overdrive only.
- **The Blues:** Caroline describes this as a straightforward circuit that moves from transparent light drive to gnarly raw drive and identifies the product as predominantly drive. It is retained under Overdrive; the small amount of fuzz-like character mentioned in the description is not treated as a separate Fuzz identity.
- **Haymaker:** Caroline's own material explicitly calls Haymaker an overdrive and documents it as a discontinued pedal. It is retained as a historical Overdrive identity.
- **Icarus V2 / Icarus:** Caroline's official product page describes Icarus V2 as including rich overdrive alongside its buffer/boost functions. Because the product's standalone identity includes overdrive, it is retained under Overdrive; preamp and boost functions remain outside scope.
- **Wave Cannon / Cannonball:** Caroline's official comparison material identifies the original Wave Cannon/Cannonball as the company's 2010 starter pedal and describes its DOD OD-250/ProCo Rat-style frame. Caroline's own historical material also documents low-gain overdrive, true distortion, and a Havoc fuzz mode. It is therefore retained in all three target categories rather than forcing it into one box.
- **Wave Cannon MKII / Superdistorter:** Caroline explicitly describes the MKII as an unrepentant distortion pedal and calls it the Superdistorter. It remains Distortion only.
- **Wave Cannon Zero:** Caroline's current product page explicitly calls it a Distorter with Havoc and describes late-1970s/early-1980s big-amp distortion. It is retained under Distortion.
- **CROM:** Caroline calls CROM a Supa Tone Bender-style “fuzzstortion” and identifies its relationship to the earlier Olympia fuzz. The product is therefore retained in both Fuzz and Distortion.
- **Hawaiian Pizza:** Caroline's current product page explicitly identifies it as a fuzz. Older Caroline material calls it a versatile fuzz-drive, but the separate drive language is treated as behavior around the fuzz identity rather than a second Overdrive category.
- **Shigeharu:** Caroline explicitly labels Shigeharu “IC Fuzz + Octave” and describes its Muff-style fuzz character with a parallel octave-up circuit. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Olympia:** Caroline's own site documents the original Olympia as a fuzz pedal launched through Kickstarter. It is retained as a historical Fuzz identity.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Météore, Kilobyte, Parabola, and related multi-effect/preamp products:** excluded where the primary product identity is reverb, delay, or modulation even though Caroline describes internal overdrive/preamp stages.
- **Megabyte / Kilobyte delays:** excluded because their primary identities are delay products; their drive/preamp stages are not standalone dirt identities.
- **Arigato, Somersault, Parabola, and other modulation pedals:** excluded.
- **Special colorways and limited finish variants:** consolidated into the underlying pedal identity.
- **Caroline Shigeharu Limited:** treated as a limited variant of Shigeharu rather than a separate pedal identity.
- **Soviet Pizza:** treated as a limited Hawaiian Pizza variant rather than a separate pedal identity.
- **Alpha Gain:** not promoted as a separate core identity because Caroline presents it as a special repurposing/voicing of Icarus clipping stages rather than a necessary new regular product identity.
- **Gain-range inflation:** Hawaiian Pizza, Shigeharu, The Blues, and similar products are not given additional target categories solely because Caroline describes them as capable of overlapping neighboring gain textures.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Caroline Guitar Company home: https://carolineguitar.com/
- About: https://carolineguitar.com/about/
- Pedals catalog: https://carolineguitar.com/product-category/pedals/
- Legacy Products: https://carolineguitar.com/product-category/legacy-products/
- Aaron Graves Overdrive: https://carolineguitar.com/product/graves_drive/
- The Blues: https://carolineguitar.com/product/theblues/
- Hawaiian Pizza: https://carolineguitar.com/product/hawaiian-pizza/
- Shigeharu: https://carolineguitar.com/product/shigeharu/
- CROM: https://carolineguitar.com/product/crom/
- Wave Cannon Zero: https://carolineguitar.com/product/wavecannonzero/
- Wave Cannon MKII: https://carolineguitar.com/product/wave-cannon-mkii/
- Icarus V2: https://carolineguitar.com/product/icarusv2/
- Caroline Wave Cannon / Haymaker comparison: https://carolineguitar.com/so-what-is-the-difference-between/
- Haymaker documentation: https://carolineguitar.com/mailer_sept_12_23/
- Olympia history: https://carolineguitar.com/tag/caroline-olympia-fuzz/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Caroline Guitar Company is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The catalog establishes a mixed dirt history spanning Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Wave Cannon/Cannonball is the clearest three-category historical hybrid; CROM is explicitly a fuzzstortion and is retained in both Fuzz and Distortion. Hawaiian Pizza and Shigeharu remain Fuzz-only, while Aaron Graves, The Blues, Haymaker, and Icarus are Overdrive identities. Non-dirt modulation, delay, reverb, and preamp functions were deliberately kept outside the target catalog.

SOURCE FILE: research/builders/BLOCK-2026-09-17-100.md
============================================================
# Builder → Pedals Research Block 100

## Builder
**Electronic Audio Experiments (EAE) — John Snyder / EAE**

## Verification basis
Electronic Audio Experiments' official current pedal catalog and official Legacy Products pages were used as the main source for the census. EAE's own product pages and its “Which EAE drive should you buy?” guide explicitly describe many of the company's drive, distortion, and fuzz identities. Current products are separated from discontinued/hiatus products where the company itself does so.

EAE itself notes that “drive pedal” is often used as shorthand for pedals that distort a guitar signal and that boost, overdrive, distortion, and fuzz overlap heavily. The archive therefore does not infer extra categories from tone descriptions alone. A pedal is placed in a target category when EAE explicitly identifies that product with the category or gives it a distinct dirt function.

## Overdrive
- Halberd
- Citadel
- Model feT
- Limelight
- The Bard
- Dagger — historical / discontinued
- 0xEAE Boost — historical / discontinued collaboration
- Dude Incredible / Tube Voice channel — historical / discontinued
- Greathammer

## Distortion
- Longsword
- Surveyor
- Dude Incredible / Tube Voice channel — historical / discontinued

## Fuzz
- Glaive
- 0xEAE Fuzz — historical / discontinued collaboration
- Eldritch Blast — historical / discontinued collaboration
- Dude Incredible / Percolate channel — historical / discontinued

## Product notes
- **Halberd:** EAE explicitly identifies Halberd as a transistor-based overdrive and describes it as a medium-gain overdrive circuit. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Citadel:** EAE explicitly describes Citadel as a traditional overdrive as well as a preamp. The preamp function is outside the target categories, so it remains Overdrive only.
- **Model feT:** EAE explicitly describes Model feT as a smooth overdrive and an amplifier-style preamp. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Limelight:** EAE explicitly calls Limelight a dual-channel overdrive. Its separate boost is not counted as a dirt category; its ability to push the overdrive channel into heavy distortion does not create a second Distortion identity.
- **The Bard:** EAE explicitly calls The Bard a compact overdrive pedal. Higher Drive settings can distort harder, but the product identity remains Overdrive.
- **Dagger:** EAE's archived page describes Dagger as a Longsword-based drive pedal and explicitly identifies the Drive control as increasing overdrive. It remains Overdrive only.
- **0xEAE Boost:** EAE's Legacy catalog calls this a boost/overdrive with massive output. It is retained under Overdrive, while the boost function itself is outside the target scope.
- **Greathammer:** EAE explicitly calls Greathammer a bass-focused overdrive. Its very wide gain range and ability to become rowdy do not create a separate Distortion entry.
- **Longsword:** EAE's current and official technical material identify Longsword as a general-purpose distortion pedal. Its broad range includes lighter gain, but the manufacturer's own product identity is Distortion, so it is retained there rather than also being inferred as Overdrive.
- **Surveyor:** EAE explicitly describes Surveyor as a Tube Voice distortion generator and a compact reinterpretation of the IVP preamp. It remains Distortion only.
- **Dude Incredible / Tube Voice:** EAE describes the Tube Voice side as a transformer-loaded overdrive circuit with medium-gain and distorted textures. It is retained under both Overdrive and Distortion because the company explicitly describes it as an overdrive circuit that produces distinct distorted sounds as a core channel function.
- **Glaive:** EAE explicitly identifies Glaive as an octave fuzz and describes up to extreme gain, gating, and octave-up behavior. It remains Fuzz only.
- **0xEAE Fuzz:** EAE explicitly identifies this as a fuzz and calls it an octave fuzz companion. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Eldritch Blast:** EAE explicitly calls Eldritch Blast a fuzz built around a high-gain octave/intermodulation generator. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Dude Incredible / Percolate:** EAE explicitly describes this half of Dude Incredible as a Percolator-style fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Beholder:** excluded because EAE identifies it primarily as a reverb pedal with a fuzz circuit, making reverb the product's principal identity rather than a standalone dirt pedal.
- **Sending / Sending V2:** excluded because these are delay products with a colorful preamplifier, not standalone dirt identities.
- **Hypersleep, Mirror House, Scattering, Prismatic Wall, and other time/modulation/reverb products:** excluded.
- **µ Blaster and similar boost/utility products:** excluded when the primary identity is boost or signal shaping rather than Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.
- **Model feT / Citadel / Surveyor amplifier-emulation functions:** the amp/preamp aspect is outside the target categories; only the explicitly identified dirt function is counted.
- **0xEAE Bass Driver:** excluded because EAE presents it as an adaptation of a solid-state bass amplifier rather than as one of the three core dirt pedal identities. The successor Greathammer is retained because EAE explicitly calls it a bass-focused overdrive.
- **Glaive vs. 0xEAE Fuzz:** kept as separate named product identities because EAE presents Glaive as a new product and describes it as a reworking of the discontinued 0xEAE Fuzz for wider availability, rather than simply a version label.
- **Dude Incredible:** retained as a single product identity with its two explicit dirt channels rather than splitting the chassis into fake separate pedals. The channel-specific notes explain the Overdrive/Distortion and Fuzz functions.
- **B-stock, finish variants, normal revisions, and colorways:** consolidated with the underlying product identity.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- EAE current pedal catalog: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals
- EAE Legacy Products: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/legacy
- EAE “Which EAE drive should you buy?”: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/blog/2022/7/7/which-eae-drive-should-you-buy
- Longsword: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/longsword
- Halberd: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/halberd
- Citadel: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/citadel
- Model feT: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/model-fet
- Limelight: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/limelight
- Surveyor: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/surveyor
- Glaive: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/glaive
- Greathammer: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/greathammer
- The Bard: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/pedals/bard
- Dagger: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/legacy/dagger
- Dude Incredible: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/legacy/dude-incredible
- 0xEAE Fuzz: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/legacy/0xeae-fuzz
- Eldritch Blast: https://www.electronicaudioexperiments.com/legacy/eldritch-blast

## Status
**Verified research block.** Electronic Audio Experiments is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The current and legacy catalogs establish a substantial lineup across Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. The clearest category overlaps are within Dude Incredible, where EAE explicitly documents a transformer-loaded overdrive/distortion Tube Voice channel and a Percolator-style fuzz channel. Longsword and Surveyor are retained as Distortion identities, while Halberd, Citadel, Model feT, Limelight, Bard, Dagger, 0xEAE Boost, and Greathammer are retained as Overdrive identities. Glaive, 0xEAE Fuzz, Eldritch Blast, and the Percolate side of Dude Incredible are retained as Fuzz identities. Non-dirt multi-effects, delays, reverbs, boosts, and amp-oriented products without a distinct target dirt identity remain excluded.
