# Builder → Pedals Research Block 101

## Builder
**Black Arts Toneworks (BAT) — Mark Wentz / Black Arts Toneworks**

## Verification basis
Black Arts Toneworks' official site identifies the company as a pedal builder operating since 2010 and maintains a catalog of current and archived products. The official product pages and archive pages explicitly identify many of BAT's overdrive, distortion, and fuzz products. The catalog is especially heavy on fuzz and high-gain drive designs.

The archive follows BAT's own product identity instead of treating every possible gain sound as another category. Hybrid products are retained across categories when BAT itself describes separate dirt functions inside the same pedal.

## Overdrive
- Black Forest
- Quantum Mystic
- Witch Burner MkII
- Raw Heart OD
- Tres Diablos Ruidosos
- Coven
- BAT — historical / discontinued

## Distortion
- BAT — historical / discontinued
- Raw Heart OD
- Revelation — historical / discontinued
- Tres Diablos Ruidosos
- Priestess

## Fuzz
- Pharaoh
- Pharaoh Supreme
- Son of Pharaoh
- LSTR
- Black Sheep
- Priestess
- Crown of Horns — historical / discontinued
- FNORD
- Destroyer
- Sarcophagus
- Rabid Mammal
- Coven
- Tres Diablos Ruidosos

## Product notes
- **Black Forest:** BAT's product material identifies Black Forest as an overdrive rooted in the Colorsound Overdriver family. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Quantum Mystic:** BAT explicitly calls Quantum Mystic an Overdrive. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Witch Burner MkII:** BAT explicitly identifies Witch Burner MkII as an overdrive with very high gain. It remains Overdrive only even though the gain is aggressive.
- **Raw Heart OD:** BAT describes Raw Heart as a dual-circuit Overdrive/Distortion combining the Quantum Mystic and low-gain Witch Burner designs. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Tres Diablos Ruidosos:** BAT explicitly describes the three gain stages as moving from boost to edgy OD, classic distortion, and finally oversaturated fuzz. It is retained in all three target categories.
- **Coven:** BAT's own historical material identifies Coven as Pharaoh Fuzz plus Black Forest Overdrive in one enclosure. It is retained in both Fuzz and Overdrive.
- **BAT:** BAT describes this later pedal as a driver that can also function as a standalone distortion. Because BAT explicitly gives it a distortion identity while also calling it a driver, it is retained under Distortion; the archive does not turn the generic “driver” wording into a separate Overdrive entry without clearer product-level wording.
- **Revelation:** BAT explicitly describes Revelation as an amp-pushing device whose gain control dials in distortion. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Priestess:** BAT describes Priestess as a combination of multiple Muff-family gain stages producing dynamic and compressed fuzz. It is retained under Fuzz; its ability to produce aggressive saturation does not create a separate Distortion identity merely from sound range.
- **Pharaoh:** BAT explicitly describes Pharaoh as a fuzz pedal, with its roots and DNA in classic fuzz and a range from boost through light OD to saturated fuzz. It remains Fuzz only because those neighboring ranges are operating range within the named fuzz identity.
- **Pharaoh Supreme:** BAT explicitly describes Pharaoh Supreme as the “ultimate” fuzz-stortion built from the Pharaoh family. It is retained under Fuzz; the word “fuzz-stortion” is treated as a fuzz identity with distortion behavior, not automatically a second category without a separate named distortion function.
- **Son of Pharaoh:** BAT describes it as the same Pharaoh circuit family in a smaller enclosure and says it is still the Pharaoh. It is retained as a separate named pedal identity because BAT names and sold it as the Son of Pharaoh, but its category is Fuzz only.
- **LSTR:** BAT describes LSTR as the Pharaoh's fuzz-oriented counterpart with more fuzz and a different voicing. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Black Sheep:** BAT identifies Black Sheep as an all-transistor fuzz device. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Crown of Horns:** BAT describes the core as an OpAmp Muff with Pharaoh/LSTR tone-section traits. It remains Fuzz only; its bypassable tone stack can create a large volume boost, but that does not establish a separate overdrive identity.
- **FNORD:** BAT explicitly calls FNORD an octaver/fuzz and describes a blend of fuzz/distortion behaviors. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Destroyer:** BAT's official introduction describes the pedal as going from a controllable wall of fuzz to a destructive preamp texture. It is retained under Fuzz; the preamp role is outside scope.
- **Sarcophagus:** BAT explicitly describes Sarcophagus as a dual-fuzz combining complete Pharaoh and LSTR circuits. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Rabid Mammal:** BAT explicitly identifies the pedal as Boost/Fuzz. The boost side is outside the target categories, so only Fuzz is recorded.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Skyboost:** excluded because BAT presents it primarily as a boost/treble boost, even though its gain can become fuzzy.
- **Son of Pharaoh:** retained as a named product identity, but not duplicated under other categories simply because the Pharaoh family can reach lower-gain drive sounds.
- **Special color runs and custom-shop versions:** consolidated with their underlying pedal unless BAT presents a genuinely different named circuit/product identity.
- **Coven and Sarcophagus:** retained as single named pedals with multiple internal dirt circuits rather than pretending each circuit is a separate physical pedal.
- **Rabid Mammal:** boost side excluded from the three-category census; Fuzz retained because BAT explicitly identifies it as Boost/Fuzz.
- **Black Forest / Pharaoh family gain overlap:** neighboring fuzz or overdrive behavior is not promoted into additional categories without explicit builder-level product identity.
- **Custom Shop, one-off art pieces, B-stock, and ordinary finish variants:** excluded as separate identities or consolidated into the underlying pedal where appropriate.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Black Arts Toneworks home: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/
- Black Arts Toneworks About / FAQ: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/contact/
- Pedals archive: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/
- Pharaoh: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/pharaoh/
- Pharaoh Supreme: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/pharaoh-supreme/
- Witch Burner MkII: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/witch-burner/
- Raw Heart OD: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/raw-heart-od/
- Revelation: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/revelation/
- Priestess: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/priestess/
- Crown of Horns: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/crown-of-horns/
- FNORD: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/fnord/
- Rabid Mammal: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/rabid-mammal/
- Sarcophagus: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/sarcophagus/
- Tres Diablos Ruidosos: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/pedal/tres-diablos-ruidosos/
- Wave Cannon / Haymaker style comparison: https://www.blackartstoneworks.com/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Black Arts Toneworks is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The official catalog establishes a broad dirt history dominated by fuzz and heavy overdrive/distortion. The strongest multi-category cases are Raw Heart OD (Overdrive + Distortion), Coven (Overdrive + Fuzz), and Tres Diablos Ruidosos (Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz). Pharaoh, LSTR, Priestess, FNORD, Destroyer, Sarcophagus, Rabid Mammal, and related products are retained as Fuzz where BAT explicitly establishes that identity. Boost-only and other non-target functions remain outside the core census.

============================================================
# Builder → Pedals Research Block 102

## Builder
**Fairfield Circuitry — Fairfield Circuitry**

## Verification basis
Fairfield Circuitry's official site says the company has been making pedals since 2008 and that manufacturing is done in Hull, Québec. The current catalog and FAQ explicitly identify products such as Barbershop as overdrive, ~900 as fuzz, and 20% More as distortion. Fairfield's own FAQ and current product pages also document the relationship between the original Barbershop, the Millennium Edition, and Modèle B, plus the discontinued Four Eyes fuzz lineage behind ~900.

The archive uses Fairfield's own named product identity. A separate named pedal is retained when Fairfield sells/presents it as its own product, while ordinary revisions of the same product are consolidated. Gain-range descriptions are not automatically turned into extra dirt categories.

## Overdrive
- Barbershop — consolidated across V1 / Millennium Edition
- Modèle B — historical/current named product built from the Barbershop circuit

## Distortion
- 20% More

## Fuzz
- ~900
- The Unpleasant Surprise
- Four Eyes — historical / discontinued

## Product notes
- **Barbershop:** Fairfield explicitly calls this the Barbershop Overdrive. The FAQ says the V1 and Millennium Edition share the same circuit topology, so those revisions are consolidated into one Barbershop identity.
- **Modèle B:** Fairfield explicitly describes Modèle B as the same Barbershop circuit in a smaller enclosure and markets it as an “Always On Barbershop.” It is retained as a separately named product identity, while its category remains Overdrive.
- **20% More:** Fairfield's current catalog explicitly labels 20% More “Slew Limiting Distortion.” The product description also refers to synthetic distortion artifacts and JFET clipping. It is retained under Distortion only.
- **~900:** Fairfield explicitly labels ~900 a “Four Knob Fuzz” and a ferocious fuzz pedal built from two cascading JFET gain stages. The product page says its lineage traces back to the discontinued Four Eyes Fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **The Unpleasant Surprise:** Fairfield explicitly labels this an “Experimental Fuzz/Gate.” It is retained under Fuzz; the gate/experimental character is not treated as another dirt category.
- **Four Eyes:** Fairfield's current ~900 page explicitly identifies Four Eyes as the discontinued fuzz from which ~900 traces its lineage. It is retained as a historical Fuzz identity because Fairfield itself names it as a prior product.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Accountant:** excluded because Fairfield explicitly identifies it as a compressor. Makeup gain does not create a standalone Overdrive identity.
- **Meet Maude:** excluded because it is an analogue delay.
- **Randy's Revenge:** excluded because it is a ring modulator.
- **Shallow Water / Roger That:** excluded because their primary identities are modulation and FM modulation/demodulation.
- **Hors d'Oeuvre?:** excluded because Fairfield identifies it as an active feedback loop rather than one of the three core dirt categories.
- **Long Life:** excluded because Fairfield identifies it primarily as a parametric EQ/filter with gritty extras, not a standalone dirt pedal.
- **Board Member:** excluded because it is a CV-controlled voltage-sag utility; its ability to create unexpected fuzz sounds is not enough to establish it as a target dirt product.
- **Barbershop V1 vs. Millennium Edition:** consolidated because Fairfield states the circuit topology was not changed and describes the difference as a smaller enclosure plus control changes.
- **Gain-range inflation:** Barbershop/Modèle B are not expanded into Distortion merely because their drive can be increased, and ~900 is not expanded into Overdrive merely because its gain range is broad. Categories follow Fairfield's explicit product labels.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Fairfield Circuitry About: https://fairfieldcircuitry.com/pages/about
- Fairfield Circuitry current catalog: https://fairfieldcircuitry.com/collections/all
- Fairfield Circuitry FAQ: https://fairfieldcircuitry.com/pages/faq
- Barbershop: https://fairfieldcircuitry.com/products/the-barbershop
- Modèle B: https://fairfieldcircuitry.com/products/model-b
- 20% More: https://fairfieldcircuitry.com/products/20-more
- ~900: https://fairfieldcircuitry.com/products/900
- The Unpleasant Surprise: https://fairfieldcircuitry.com/products/the-unpleasant-surprise

## Status
**Verified research block.** Fairfield Circuitry is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. Its official material establishes Barbershop and Modèle B as Overdrive products, 20% More as Distortion, and ~900 plus The Unpleasant Surprise as Fuzz products. Fairfield also explicitly identifies the discontinued Four Eyes as the fuzz lineage behind ~900, so Four Eyes is retained historically. Non-dirt pedals and utility products remain excluded. The Barbershop V1/Millennium revisions are consolidated, while Modèle B remains separate because Fairfield markets it as a distinct named pedal even though it uses the same circuit.

SOURCE FILE: research/builders/BLOCK-2026-09-17-103.md
============================================================
# Builder → Pedals Research Block 103

## Builder
**Spaceman Effects — Spaceman Effects / Zak Martin**

## Verification basis
Spaceman Effects' official site maintains separate current and legacy equipment catalogs and directly labels individual products as overdrive or fuzz. Current and legacy product pages were used as the primary evidence. The archive keeps the builder's product identity separate from mere operating range: a fuzz that can reach overdrive or distortion is not automatically placed into those extra categories unless Spaceman presents that function as part of the product identity.

## Overdrive
- Apollo VIIb — current PDX Custom / Cadet versions consolidated
- Polaris — current/legacy versions consolidated
- Aphelion — historical / discontinued
- Saturn V — historical / discontinued

## Distortion
- None established as a standalone core distortion identity

## Fuzz
- Sputnik I
- Sputnik IIb — historical / discontinued
- Sputnik III — current/legacy versions consolidated
- Gemini IV
- Gemini III — historical / discontinued
- Spacerocket II
- Spacerocket — historical / discontinued
- Rumblefuzz II
- Rumblefuzz — historical / discontinued
- Charon
- Charon v2 Mod
- Titan II — historical / discontinued

## Product notes
- **Apollo VIIb:** Spaceman explicitly presents Apollo VIIb as a Preamp / Overdrive. The current handmade PDX Custom version and Cadet version use the same named product identity and are consolidated rather than counted as separate pedals.
- **Polaris:** Spaceman explicitly identifies Polaris as a Resonant Overdrive. The current Cadet and legacy/NOS versions are treated as one Polaris identity.
- **Aphelion:** the official page explicitly calls Aphelion a Harmonic Overdrive and describes it as an overdrive pedal. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Saturn V:** the official page describes Saturn V as a tone enhancer, harmonic boost, and low-gain overdrive. It is retained under Overdrive because Spaceman explicitly identifies the pedal as an overdrive as well as a boost. The boost function itself is outside scope.
- **Sputnik I:** the current product page explicitly labels Sputnik I Silicon Fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Sputnik IIb:** the current catalog identifies Sputnik IIb as a Deluxe Germanium Fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Sputnik III:** the official catalog identifies Sputnik III as Germanium Fuzz. The legacy and Cadet presentations are consolidated into the Sputnik III identity.
- **Gemini IV:** Spaceman explicitly calls Gemini IV a Dual Fuzz Generator with two parallel fuzz circuits. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Gemini III:** the official page identifies Gemini III as a Dual Fuzz Generator. It is retained historically even though Gemini IV is its later redesigned successor.
- **Spacerocket II:** Spaceman explicitly labels this an Octave Fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Spacerocket:** the official catalog identifies the earlier Spacerocket as an Intermodulation Fuzz. It is retained as a historical Fuzz identity.
- **Rumblefuzz II:** Spaceman's own page calls it a Rumblefuzz and describes the pedal as moving from overdrive into a wall of fuzz, with overdrive, distortion, and fuzz levels of gain available. The named product identity is Fuzz, so it remains Fuzz only. Rumblefuzz II is retained separately from the original Rumblefuzz because Spaceman presents it as a revisited and expanded product with substantial control and circuit changes.
- **Rumblefuzz:** the original pedal is explicitly identified as a warm, fat fuzz and a “fuzz-stortion.” It remains Fuzz only despite its light-overdrive range.
- **Charon:** Spaceman explicitly labels Charon a Germanium-Silicon Hybrid Fuzz. The wide range from crunchy overdrive through distortion to fuzz is treated as operating range inside the fuzz identity.
- **Charon v2 Mod:** Spaceman presents Charon v2 Mod as a new take on the original circuit and explicitly labels it Ge+Si Fuzz. It is retained as a separate named product from the original Charon.
- **Titan II:** Spaceman explicitly labels Titan II a Fuzz Machine. Its page describes fuzz, distortion, and overdrive across its gain range, but these are operating ranges within a product whose named identity is fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Atlas III / Atlas IV:** excluded because Spaceman presents Atlas primarily as a Preamp Booster / clean boost. Although it can push an amplifier into overdrive and distortion, those are effects on the amp rather than the product's named target dirt identity.
- **Saturn VI:** excluded because the product is presented primarily as a Harmonic Booster and only crosses into overdrive when the Drive control is used. It does not receive a separate Overdrive identity in this block.
- **Voyager II:** excluded because its primary identity is Dynamic Tremolo, even though it contains built-in overdrive.
- **Mercury and other harmonic boost products:** excluded as boost-first designs.
- **Charon / Charon v2 Mod:** retained as Fuzz rather than duplicating them into Overdrive or Distortion from their gain-range descriptions.
- **Rumblefuzz / Rumblefuzz II:** retained as Fuzz only despite explicit references to overdrive and distortion levels of gain, because Spaceman's named product identity is Fuzz.
- **Sputnik, Gemini, and Spacerocket revisions:** current and historical named generations are consolidated only where Spaceman presents them as the same named product or a normal version family. Gemini III and Gemini IV remain separate named identities because Gemini IV is presented as a redesigned and expanded successor, not simply a version-number revision.
- **No standalone distortion identity:** the reviewed official current and legacy catalogs did not establish a sufficiently direct Spaceman pedal whose primary product identity is Distortion.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Spaceman Effects home: https://spacemaneffects.com/
- Spaceman current collections: https://spacemaneffects.com/collections/equipment
- Spaceman legacy collections: https://spacemaneffects.com/collections/legacy
- Apollo VIIb: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/apollo-viib
- Apollo VIIb Cadet: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/apollo-viib-cadet
- Polaris: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/polaris
- Polaris legacy: https://spacemaneffects.com/collections/legacy/products/polaris-nos
- Aphelion: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/aphelion
- Saturn V: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/saturn-v/
- Sputnik I: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/sputnik-i
- Sputnik III: https://spacemaneffects.com/collections/equipment
- Sputnik IIb / collections: https://spacemaneffects.com/collections/equipment
- Gemini III: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/gemini-iii
- Gemini IV: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/gemini-iv
- Spacerocket II: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/spacerocket-ii
- Spacerocket: https://spacemaneffects.com/collections/spacelab-ii
- Rumblefuzz: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/rumblefuzz
- Rumblefuzz II: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/rumblefuzz-ii
- Charon: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/charon
- Charon v2 Mod: https://spacemaneffects.com/collections/spacemod-series
- Titan II: https://spacemaneffects.com/products/titan-ii/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Spaceman Effects is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. Its official current and legacy material establishes a compact overdrive catalog and a broader fuzz catalog. Apollo VIIb, Polaris, Aphelion, and Saturn V are retained as Overdrive identities because Spaceman explicitly identifies them that way. Sputnik, Gemini, Spacerocket, Rumblefuzz, Charon, and Titan products are retained as Fuzz identities. No standalone Distortion pedal was established strongly enough to promote. Boost-first and modulation products remain excluded, and gain-range descriptions are not used to manufacture extra categories.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 104

## Builder
**Old Blood Noise Endeavors (OBNE)**

## Verification basis
Old Blood Noise Endeavors' official current pedal catalog and individual product pages were used as the primary source for this block. The current catalog directly identifies Fault V2 as Overdrive + Distortion, Excess V2 as Distortion, and Haunt, Alpha Haunt, and Pardner as fuzz products. The archive follows OBNE's own product identity instead of turning every gain range or secondary circuit into another category.

## Overdrive
- Fault V2
- Beam Splitter V2

## Distortion
- Fault V2
- Beam Splitter V2
- Excess V2

## Fuzz
- Haunt Fuzz
- Alpha Haunt
- Pardner

## Product notes
- **Fault V2:** OBNE explicitly presents Fault as an overdrive pedal that ranges from boost to distortion, and the product title itself is “Fault V2 Overdrive + Distortion.” It is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Beam Splitter V2:** OBNE describes Beam Splitter as using three different overdrive voices, while the current home page presents Beam Splitter V2 as “Trereo Distortion” and the product controls describe the gain as shaping distortion in each drive section. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion as one named pedal, not split into separate internal drives.
- **Excess V2:** OBNE explicitly calls Excess a distortion paired with delay, chorus, or harmonized fifths. The distortion side is a core standalone dirt section, so it is retained under Distortion; the modulation/delay functions remain outside scope.
- **Haunt Fuzz:** OBNE explicitly identifies Haunt as a high-gain gated fuzz. Its smoother tones can approach overdrive, but that is treated as operating range inside the fuzz identity.
- **Alpha Haunt:** OBNE calls Alpha Haunt the expanded version of the Haunt silicon high-gain gated fuzz and explicitly identifies the core as fuzz. Its softer range and distortion control are not promoted into separate categories because the named product identity is Fuzz.
- **Pardner:** OBNE explicitly describes Pardner as a fuzz with a synthy texture and an additional overdriven effects loop called Lasso. The pedal is retained under Fuzz only because the overdrive is a secondary loop rather than a separate named dirt identity.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Screen Violence:** excluded because OBNE presents it primarily as a saturated modulated reverb with an overdrive side called Violence. The reverb/modulation system is the principal product identity rather than a standalone dirt pedal.
- **Dark Star:** excluded because OBNE identifies it as a reverb with an optional overdrive/crush function, not a standalone dirt product.
- **Black Bobbin Boost:** excluded because OBNE presents it as a dual clean boost. Its ability to become lightly overdriven does not establish an Overdrive identity.
- **MAW and other signal/utility products:** excluded where the primary product identity is microphone processing, routing, blending, or other non-target functionality.
- **Retired BL-series, reverb, delay, modulation, pitch, and utility products:** excluded because their primary identities are outside Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz.
- **Gain-range inflation:** Haunt, Alpha Haunt, Fault, and other products are not duplicated into extra categories solely because their gain controls reach neighboring sounds. Categories follow explicit OBNE product identity.
- **Colorways and ordinary revisions:** consolidated with the underlying product unless OBNE presents a materially distinct named dirt product. Pardner colorways are not separate identities.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- OBNE current pedals: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals
- OBNE all pedals: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/all-pedals
- Fault V2: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/p/fault-v2
- Beam Splitter V2: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/p/beam-splitter-v2
- Excess V2: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/p/excess-v2
- Haunt Fuzz: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/p/haunt-fuzz-w-clickless-switching
- Alpha Haunt: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/p/alpha-haunt-fuzz
- Pardner: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/p/pardner-fuzz
- Screen Violence: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/p/screen-violence
- Black Bobbin Boost: https://oldbloodnoise.com/pedals/p/blackbobbinboost

## Status
**Verified research block.** Old Blood Noise Endeavors is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. Its current official catalog establishes Fault V2 as Overdrive + Distortion, Beam Splitter V2 as a multi-voice Overdrive + Distortion product, Excess V2 as Distortion, and Haunt, Alpha Haunt, and Pardner as Fuzz. Secondary effects and non-dirt products were deliberately excluded, and the archive does not manufacture extra categories from gain-range descriptions alone.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 105

## Builder
**Fulltone — Mike Fuller / Fulltone USA**

## Verification basis
Fulltone's current official catalog separates Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz collections and currently lists the OCD family, Full-Drive family, PlimSoul, Hi-Mu, Mas Malo, Queen Bee, 69 Pedal mkII, 70 Pedal-BC, Soul-Bender v2, Ultimate Octave, and Octafuzz 2. Current Fulltone product pages were used as the primary source. Historical identities were added only where product-level evidence was sufficiently direct from Fulltone manuals or established specialist references.

The archive distinguishes product identity from mere gain range. A pedal that can move from overdrive into distortion is not automatically duplicated into both categories unless Fulltone itself presents it as both or gives the product a distinct distortion function. Fuzz products with distortion or overdrive range remain Fuzz-only unless the builder clearly establishes the second dirt identity.

## Overdrive
- OCD — current lineage, including OCDv2 / Limited Edition / OCD-Germanium family consolidated
- Full-Drive1
- Full-Drive2 — historical lineage, including MOSFET editions consolidated
- Full-Drive2 v2
- Full-Drive3
- PlimSoul mkII
- GT-500 — historical / discontinued
- Bass-Drive — historical / discontinued
- Full-Drive — original historical identity

## Distortion
- OCD
- OCD-Germanium
- Full-Drive2 v2
- PlimSoul mkII
- Hi-Mu
- Mas Malo
- GT-500 — historical / discontinued
- Distortion Pro — historical / discontinued

## Fuzz
- 69 Pedal mkII
- 70 Pedal-BC
- Queen Bee
- Mas Malo
- Soul-Bender v2
- Ultimate Octave
- Octafuzz 2 — historical / discontinued

## Product notes
- **OCD:** Fulltone's current site places the OCD family in both Overdrive and Distortion. The OCD product page describes its characteristic amp-like drive and distortion behavior; the archive retains the named OCD identity across its normal V1.x/V2-era lineage rather than creating a separate entry for every serial-number-era variation.
- **OCD-Germanium:** Fulltone sells this as part of the OCD family and also places it in the current Distortion collection. It is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion rather than treated as a separate unrelated family.
- **Full-Drive1:** Fulltone explicitly describes this as the Full-Drive overdrive sound without the boost channel. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Full-Drive2:** the historical Full-Drive 2 lineage is fundamentally an overdrive pedal with a boost/distortion channel. Fulltone documentation describes the Boost Mode as increasing distortion, while specialist product references classify the pedal primarily as Overdrive. The archive retains the family as Overdrive rather than creating a generic Distortion category from the boost channel alone.
- **Full-Drive2 v2:** Fulltone's current product page explicitly labels the DRIVE 2 section as adding distortion and the official catalog also places Full-Drive2 v2 in the Distortion collection. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion.
- **Full-Drive3:** Fulltone describes this as having independent OVERDRIVE and BOOST channels. It remains Overdrive; the boost is outside the target categories.
- **PlimSoul mkII:** Fulltone explicitly presents PlimSoul as a dual-stage clipping design combining soft-clipping overdrive and hard-clipping distortion and calls it an Overdrive/Distortion pedal. It is retained in both categories.
- **Hi-Mu:** Fulltone's current Distortion collection includes Hi-Mu and its product page says the Sustain control makes the sound more distorted. Because the official collection itself treats it as Distortion, it is retained there. The current page does not give it a separate explicit Overdrive identity, so it is not promoted into Overdrive from lower-gain behavior.
- **Mas Malo:** Fulltone explicitly describes this as Distortion/Fuzz, with massive fuzz and distortion and the ability to back down to more restrained tones. It is retained in both Distortion and Fuzz.
- **GT-500:** historical Fulltone documentation and period references identify the GT-500 as a combined overdrive and distortion pedal with separate circuits. It is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion as one named pedal.
- **Distortion Pro:** historical product documentation identifies the DP-1 as a dedicated Fulltone distortion pedal. It is retained under Distortion only.
- **Bass-Drive:** historical product references identify Bass-Drive as the bass-oriented member of the Full-Drive family and an overdrive pedal. It remains Overdrive only.
- **69 Pedal mkII:** Fulltone explicitly places this in the Fuzz collection and describes it as a FuzzFace-style germanium fuzz. Its bias control can produce overdrive/distortion sounds, but those are operating range within the fuzz identity.
- **70 Pedal-BC:** Fulltone explicitly identifies this as a 1970-style fuzz and describes much more fuzz than the 69 Pedal. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Queen Bee:** Fulltone explicitly describes Queen Bee as a fuzz using three germanium transistors. The page discusses added sustain and distortion but the named product identity is Fuzz, so it remains Fuzz only.
- **Soul-Bender v2:** Fulltone explicitly identifies Soul-Bender as a Tone Bender-based germanium fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Ultimate Octave:** Fulltone explicitly identifies the current Ultimate Octave as a transistor fuzz with a separate octave-up switch. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Octafuzz 2:** Fulltone explicitly identifies Octafuzz OF-2 as the Tycobrahe Octavia-type product and notes a Fuzz setting. It is retained historically as Fuzz even though the page mentions its unusual distortion character.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Ranger, 2B Booster, and other boost-first products:** excluded because their named identity is Boost rather than Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.
- **Deja'Vibe, tremolo, wah, switching, and power-supply products:** excluded.
- **Fuzz gain-range inflation:** 69 Pedal mkII, Queen Bee, Soul-Bender, Ultimate Octave, and Octafuzz 2 are not duplicated into Overdrive or Distortion merely because their controls can produce harder or lower-gain textures.
- **OCD serial/V1.x/V2 naming:** consolidated as one OCD family identity where Fulltone itself treats the changes as small revisions, while OCD-Germanium remains a distinct current named product.
- **Full-Drive family:** normal revisions and MOSFET editions are consolidated where they represent the same underlying named Full-Drive identity. Full-Drive2 v2 remains separate because Fulltone presents it as a distinct current redesign.
- **Historical products:** GT-500, Distortion Pro, Bass-Drive, and the original Full-Drive are retained only because direct product-level evidence exists in period manuals or established product references. Weakly supported one-offs are not added.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Fulltone USA current catalog: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/products/
- Fulltone Overdrive collection: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/collections/overdrive
- Fulltone Distortion collection: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/collections/distortion
- Fulltone Fuzz collection: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/collections/fuzz
- OCD V2: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/products/ocd-v2
- OCD-Germanium: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/products/ocd-germanium
- Full-Drive1: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/products/full-drive1
- Full-Drive2 v2: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/products/full-drive2-v2
- Full-Drive3: https://www.fulltoneusa.com/products/full-drive3

## Status
**Verified research block.** Fulltone is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The current official catalog establishes the company's present Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz families, while historical documentation supports GT-500, Distortion Pro, Bass-Drive, and the original Full-Drive identity. The clearest current multi-category products are OCD/OCD-Germanium, Full-Drive2 v2, PlimSoul mkII, and Mas Malo; GT-500 is retained historically as Overdrive + Distortion. Fuzz products remain Fuzz-only unless Fulltone explicitly presents a second target dirt identity.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 106

## Builder
**Dr. Scientist — Ryan Clarke / Tanya Clarke / Neil Graham**

## Verification basis
Dr. Scientist's official site identifies the company as a boutique pedal builder that began in 2005 and describes its pedals as hand-made in British Columbia, Canada. The current catalog directly identifies The Elements as an overdrive/distortion, Dungeon Crawler as a distortion, and Frazz Dazzler as a fuzz. Current official product pages were used as the primary source. Older multi-effects material was reviewed for restraint, but multi-effect products were not promoted into the core dirt census when dirt was only one component of a broader effect system.

The archive follows Dr. Scientist's named product identity rather than treating every gain setting as a separate category. A product is placed in more than one target category only when Dr. Scientist explicitly gives it those identities or distinct dirt functions.

## Overdrive
- The Elements

## Distortion
- The Elements
- Dungeon Crawler

## Fuzz
- Frazz Dazzler

## Product notes
- **The Elements:** Dr. Scientist explicitly calls The Elements a dual-channel overdrive/distortion with a wide gain range. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Distortion. Its three-band EQ, clipping options, and clean/equalizer role remain part of the same named pedal and are not counted separately.
- **Dungeon Crawler:** Dr. Scientist explicitly identifies Dungeon Crawler as a MOSFET-based distortion designed for extreme metal tones. It remains Distortion only even though it includes a boost stomp and broad gain control.
- **Frazz Dazzler:** Dr. Scientist explicitly identifies Frazz Dazzler as a dual-channel aggressive fuzz. The pedal's voltage-starve control can create glitchy and sub-octave textures, but these remain behavior inside the named fuzz identity rather than separate dirt categories.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **BitQuest:** excluded because Dr. Scientist presents it primarily as an eight-effect digital multi-effects pedal. It includes a built-in digital fuzz mode, but fuzz is only one selectable part of a much broader multi-effect platform.
- **BitQuest Eurorack Module:** excluded because it is the modular-synth version of the BitQuest multi-effects concept, not a standalone dirt pedal.
- **BoostBot:** excluded because Dr. Scientist presents it as a clean boost and display/utility pedal rather than Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.
- **Sunny Day Delay, Dusk, Phreak, Spectrum Analyzer, and other current effects:** excluded because their primary identities are delay, filter, ring modulation, analysis, or other non-target effects.
- **Frazz Dazzler gain range:** lower-gain operation and clipping changes do not create a separate Overdrive or Distortion identity because Dr. Scientist names the product as a fuzz.
- **The Elements gain range:** despite being capable of very clean and very high-gain sounds, both Overdrive and Distortion are explicitly part of the product identity, so both categories are retained.
- **Version/reissue handling:** normal revisions of the same named product are consolidated unless Dr. Scientist presents them as a distinct product identity.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Dr. Scientist home: https://www.drscientist.ca/
- Dr. Scientist About: https://www.drscientist.ca/about/
- The Elements: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/the-elements/
- Dungeon Crawler: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/dungeon-crawler/
- Frazz Dazzler: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/frazz-dazzler/
- BitQuest: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/bitquest/
- BitQuest Eurorack Module: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/bitquest-eurorack-module/
- BoostBot: https://www.drscientist.ca/pedals/boostbot/
- Current FAQs: https://www.drscientist.ca/faqs/

## Status
**Verified research block.** Dr. Scientist is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The current official catalog establishes The Elements as Overdrive + Distortion, Dungeon Crawler as Distortion, and Frazz Dazzler as Fuzz. BitQuest and other multi-effect/utility products remain excluded where dirt is only one component of a broader system. The archive does not manufacture extra dirt categories from gain range alone.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 107

## Builder
**IdiotBox Effects — Atarimatt**

## Verification basis
IdiotBox Effects' official About page identifies the builder as Atarimatt and says he has made effects and noiseboxes full-time under the IdiotBox name since 2011, designing the circuits and handling the build and graphics work himself. The current official catalog has a dedicated **FUZZ/DISTORTION/OVERDRIVE** category containing a broad range of dirt products. Product-level official pages were used to determine the specific dirt identity of each included pedal.

The archive follows the builder's named product identity and distinct dirt functions. Gain range alone does not create extra categories. A product is listed in multiple target categories only when the official product description gives it distinct overdrive, distortion, or fuzz functions.

## Overdrive
- Han-Taun Overdrive
- Power Drive
- Trench Cannon
- Necrofier Echo Drive
- repo man
- No Moon
- No Moon 2 — coming soon

## Distortion
- Blower Box Bass Distortion / Blower Box lineage
- Blower Box Deluxe
- Dungeon Master
- Heavy Weapon
- No Moon
- No Moon 2 — coming soon
- Death Master
- Trash Compactor
- Black Fortress Bass Distortion
- -D4 Distortion Reissue
- Landphil Bass Distortion
- Wild Kyle
- REDRUM V2
- Nihilizer — limited historical collaboration

## Fuzz
- Dungeon Master
- Ron Fucking Swanson Super Fuzz
- No Moon
- No Moon 2 — coming soon
- Tractor Beam
- Black Fortress Bass Distortion
- Monster Fuzz
- Blackout
- DeathRay
- Static Fuzz
- WHORES. Fuzz Freq V.2
- Blasteroid Fuzz
- Cool Buzz Bass Fuzz
- Lost Ark
- Hobo Santa Fuzz

## Product notes
- **Han-Taun Overdrive:** IdiotBox explicitly calls Han-Taun an overdrive and describes it as the builder's take on the Klon Centaur. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Power Drive:** IdiotBox explicitly describes Power Drive as a boost and overdrive derived from the Colorsound Power Boost. The builder also notes that lowering its voltage can produce fuzz, but the named product identity is Overdrive, so the archive does not promote it into Fuzz from operating range alone.
- **Trench Cannon:** IdiotBox describes this as the stand-alone version of the Screamer section from No Moon and gives it DRIVE, TONE, and LEVEL controls. The builder also presents it as a Tube Screamer replacement in the product testimonials. It is retained as Overdrive.
- **Necrofier Echo Drive:** IdiotBox explicitly calls this a Tube Screamer-style overdrive running into two echo circuits. The echo section is outside scope, while the overdrive section is retained.
- **repo man:** IdiotBox explicitly describes this as an echo running into an overdrive and another echo. It is retained as Overdrive; the echo circuits are not separately counted.
- **No Moon:** IdiotBox explicitly describes No Moon as a Big Muff, Tube Screamer, and RAT in one pedal, running in parallel. It therefore has distinct Fuzz, Overdrive, and Distortion functions and is retained in all three target categories.
- **No Moon 2:** IdiotBox explicitly says No Moon 2 contains the same separate Muff/Screamer/Rat-style circuits as the original and adds flexible routing. It therefore receives the same three target categories and is kept separate because it is a distinct named product.
- **Blower Box Bass Distortion / Blower Box lineage:** IdiotBox describes the Blower Box as a bass overdrive/distortion beast, with a dedicated distortion control and a current product identity centered on bass distortion. The archive retains it under Distortion rather than creating a separate Overdrive entry from broad gain wording.
- **Blower Box Deluxe:** IdiotBox explicitly describes the Deluxe as carrying the original Blower Box sound with a tighter, mid-focused voicing and controls named for the distortion section. It remains Distortion.
- **Dungeon Master:** IdiotBox explicitly describes Dungeon Master as part Muff, part SuperFuzz, and part HM-2, with a large gain/sustain range. Because the product description gives it both fuzz and HM-2-style distortion functions, it is retained in both Fuzz and Distortion.
- **Heavy Weapon:** IdiotBox describes Heavy Weapon as two of its heaviest pedals in one enclosure: the Blower Box and Dungeon Master, each providing separate high-gain dirt sections. It is retained under Distortion. The combined design does not create a new third dirt category.
- **Death Master:** The official product page describes a filter feeding a DRIVE circuit aimed at the Celtic Frost "To Mega Therion" sound, and its product testimonials identify it as a distortion pedal that can reach lower-gain overdrive behavior. The archive keeps it under Distortion and does not promote it to Overdrive based only on the lower-gain description.
- **Trash Compactor:** IdiotBox explicitly identifies this as the stand-alone RAT section of No Moon and gives it a dedicated DISTORTION control. It is retained under Distortion.
- **Black Fortress Bass Distortion:** IdiotBox explicitly describes a main HM-2-style distortion section plus a parallel analog octave fuzz section. It therefore receives both Distortion and Fuzz.
- **-D4 Distortion Reissue:** IdiotBox explicitly calls the -D4 a straight-up distortion pedal. Its description notes that lower gain can act as overdrive, but this is treated as gain range inside the named Distortion identity, so it remains Distortion only.
- **Landphil Bass Distortion:** IdiotBox identifies this as Landphil's signature bass distortion and describes it as a modified, more tweakable Blower Box Bass Distortion. It remains Distortion.
- **Wild Kyle:** IdiotBox explicitly calls this the signature distortion for Kyle Shutt of The Sword and describes its high-gain three-band EQ. It remains Distortion only.
- **REDRUM V2:** IdiotBox explicitly describes the original Redrum as a RAT-style distortion into an HM-2-style tone stack and the V2 as a revised version with expanded controls. It remains Distortion.
- **Nihilizer:** IdiotBox identifies the NIHILIZATION side as a unique distortion with volume, tone, and an internal distortion control. The other side is a noise synth. The distortion side is sufficient for inclusion under Distortion; the product is marked as a limited historical collaboration because only 60 were made.
- **Ron Fucking Swanson Super Fuzz:** IdiotBox explicitly describes this as its take on the classic Super Fuzz circuit. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Tractor Beam:** IdiotBox explicitly describes Tractor Beam as the stand-alone Muff circuit from No Moon, with SUSTAIN, TONE, and VOLUME controls. It is retained under Fuzz.
- **Monster Fuzz:** IdiotBox explicitly calls this a souped-up Super Fuzz octave-style fuzz. The internal voltage control changes behavior, but the product remains Fuzz only.
- **Blackout:** IdiotBox explicitly describes Blackout as two Muff circuits mixed in parallel, each with its own fuzz, tone, and output controls. It remains Fuzz only.
- **DeathRay:** IdiotBox explicitly calls DeathRay a square-wave fuzz and describes it as the revamped version of its earlier Death Ray Frequency Mangler. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Static Fuzz:** IdiotBox explicitly identifies Static Fuzz as a fuzz that can move from crunchy overdriven sounds to heavily compressed textures. Those are behaviors within the fuzz identity, so it remains Fuzz only.
- **WHORES. Fuzz Freq V.2:** IdiotBox explicitly describes this as a Russian germanium transistor fuzz combined with a wah-style filter. The limited metallic-black color version is the same product identity and is not counted separately.
- **Blasteroid Fuzz:** IdiotBox explicitly calls Blasteroid a vintage fuzz based on the Earth Sounds Research Graphic Fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Cool Buzz Bass Fuzz:** IdiotBox explicitly describes Cool Buzz as a bass fuzz with fuzz, low, high, volume, and mid controls. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Lost Ark:** IdiotBox explicitly calls Lost Ark a glitchy octave fuzz using a PLL-based design. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Hobo Santa Fuzz:** IdiotBox explicitly calls this a Muff-style fuzz using old Russian germanium transistors. It remains Fuzz only.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Aquabox:** excluded because IdiotBox presents it as a Blue Box / Green Ringer octave-effects combination rather than a dedicated Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz product.
- **Dead Space Invader:** excluded because it is a noise/LFO effect for making non-musical sounds between songs, not a dirt pedal.
- **Orange Cycle:** excluded because it is an 8-step square-wave sequencer/noisebox, not a dirt product.
- **Mad Doctor Deluxe:** excluded because it is a tremolo/stutter effect.
- **Energizer:** excluded because IdiotBox explicitly identifies it as a clean boost.
- **Dragon Sound Tone Booster:** excluded because it is a boost product.
- **TK-421 Modification:** excluded because it is a tone/level maximizer derived from the Landphil control stage, not a named dirt product.
- **End Of Line:** excluded because it is a kill-switch/signal-control utility.
- **Bad Dreams, Bat Licker, Crypto Keeper, and other current products without clear target dirt identity:** excluded rather than guessed into the census.
- **LIMITED Mystery Effect #1 Reissue and other mystery effects:** excluded because the product identity is intentionally unspecified.
- **WHORES. Fuzz Freq Gate metallic-black version:** consolidated into WHORES. Fuzz Freq V.2 because the official page presents it as a limited colorway/run of the same Fuzz Freq product rather than a separate circuit identity.
- **Power Drive fuzz behavior:** not promoted into Fuzz because IdiotBox names it a boost/overdrive and the fuzz result comes from voltage-starving that same circuit.
- **-D4 lower-gain behavior:** not promoted into Overdrive because IdiotBox names it a distortion and describes overdrive behavior as part of its gain range.
- **Static Fuzz overdriven tone:** not promoted into Overdrive because IdiotBox names it a fuzz and describes the overdriven sound as one part of its range.
- **Version and color handling:** ordinary enclosure changes and colorway runs are consolidated unless the builder presents a distinct named product or materially distinct circuit/function.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- IdiotBox Effects About: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/about
- IdiotBox Effects current products: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/products
- IdiotBox Effects Fuzz/Distortion/Overdrive category: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/category/fuzz-distortion-overdrive
- No Moon: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/thatsnomoon
- No Moon 2: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/no-moon-2
- Han-Taun Overdrive: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/han-taun-overdrive

- Trench Cannon: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/trench-cannon
- Necrofier Echo Drive: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/necrofier-echo-drive
- repo man: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/repo-man
- Blower Box Bass Distortion: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/blower-box-bass-distortion
- Blower Box Deluxe: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/blower-box-deluxe
- Dungeon Master: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/dungeon-master
- Heavy Weapon: https://www.idiotboxeffects.com/product/heavy-weapon

## Status
**Verified research block.** IdiotBox Effects is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The official catalog shows a substantial dirt lineup, including dedicated overdrive, distortion, and fuzz products plus several explicitly multi-function designs. The strongest hybrid case is **No Moon / No Moon 2**, which explicitly combine Muff, Screamer, and RAT-style circuits in one pedal. Dungeon Master combines Muff/SuperFuzz and HM-2-style distortion functions, while Black Fortress combines HM-2-style distortion with a parallel octave fuzz. Power Drive and other fuzz-capable gain stages are not inflated into extra categories when their named identity remains Overdrive or Distortion.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 108

## Builder
**Stone Deaf FX — Luke Hilton / Stone Deaf team**

## Verification basis
Stone Deaf FX is a UK pedal builder founded by Luke Hilton. Reputable industry coverage identifies Hilton as the founder and describes Stone Deaf's work as a range of drive, distortion, and fuzz pedals. The current Stone Deaf site confirms the present dirt lineup and product identities, while older specialist coverage is used only for historical/discontinued products whose names and functions are otherwise directly documented.

The archive follows Stone Deaf's named product identity and distinct dirt functions. Gain range alone does not create extra categories. A pedal is listed in more than one target category when Stone Deaf or strong product-level documentation explicitly gives it multiple target dirt functions.

## Overdrive
- PDF-2
- PDF-1X Ghost
- Kliptonite — historical / discontinued
- Trashy Blonde — historical / discontinued

## Distortion
- PDF-2
- PDF-1X Ghost
- Warp Drive — current/legacy lineage
- Trashy Blonde — historical / discontinued
- PDF-1 — historical / discontinued

## Fuzz
- PDF-2
- Kliptonite — historical / discontinued
- Fig Fumb — current/legacy lineage
- Rise & Shine — current/legacy lineage

## Product notes
- **PDF-2:** Stone Deaf's current product page explicitly describes PDF-2 as offering overdrive, distortion, and fuzz in one pedal. It is therefore retained in all three target categories.
- **PDF-1X Ghost:** Stone Deaf explicitly identifies this edition as a Parametric Overdrive / Distortion and describes it as a tone-shaping pedal with enough gain to move into heavier tones. It is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion. The internal gain trim and cleaner boost setting do not create additional categories.
- **PDF-1:** the original Parametric Distortion Filter is a historical/discontinued product. Historical product descriptions identify a switchable clean/dirty circuit with distortion and overdrive/fuzz behavior. The archive conservatively retains PDF-1 under Distortion because that is its named product identity; lower-gain and fuzz behavior are treated as operating range rather than automatically creating extra categories.
- **Kliptonite:** specialist product coverage describes Kliptonite as one-part overdrive and one-part fuzz, with the two gain structures blendable using the Mirror Mix control. It is therefore retained in both Overdrive and Fuzz.
- **Trashy Blonde:** historical Stone Deaf coverage identifies the pedal as Marshall-style overdrive with a dedicated distortion mode. It is retained in both Overdrive and Distortion as one named product.
- **Warp Drive:** Stone Deaf currently identifies Warp Drive as a high-gain distortion with an integrated noise gate. The limited black edition is treated as part of the same product lineage rather than as a separate pedal entry.
- **Fig Fumb:** Stone Deaf explicitly identifies Fig Fumb as a Muff-style fuzz with a parametric filter and noise gate. The Fig Fumb LTD/Black Edition is treated as the same named product lineage rather than creating a duplicate entry.
- **Rise & Shine:** Stone Deaf explicitly identifies Rise & Shine as Fuzz + Octave Up Fuzz + Octave Tremolo. Only the fuzz function belongs in the core dirt census; the octave and tremolo portions are outside scope.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Q Boost Ltd / Q Boost:** excluded because it is a boost-first product rather than Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.
- **Noise Reaper:** excluded because it is a noise-gate product.
- **EP-1 Expression:** excluded because it is an expression/utility pedal used to control other Stone Deaf effects.
- **Tremotron:** excluded because it is a tremolo product.
- **Syncopy:** excluded because it is a delay product.
- **Rise & Shine octave tremolo:** the tremolo and octave functions are not separately counted as dirt categories; only its explicitly named fuzz identity is retained.
- **Fig Fumb gain range:** although the product can move across heavy and distorted textures, Stone Deaf names it as a Muff-style fuzz, so it remains Fuzz only.
- **PDF-1 gain behavior:** its original documentation mentions overdrive/fuzz behavior, but the named product identity is the Parametric Distortion Filter and the archive keeps it under Distortion rather than inflating every operating mode into a separate category.
- **PDF-1X Ghost:** the clean boost functionality is excluded because Boost is outside the three target categories. Overdrive and Distortion remain because Stone Deaf explicitly names both.
- **Variant/finish handling:** ordinary color editions and direct reissues are consolidated unless Stone Deaf presents them as a materially different named product. Fig Fumb LTD and Warp Drive LTD are therefore kept within their main product lineages.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Stone Deaf FX home/current catalog: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/
- Stone Deaf all pedals: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/collections/all
- Stone Deaf distortion collection: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/collections/distortion-pedals
- PDF-2: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/pdf-2
- PDF-1X Ghost: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/pdf-1x-ghost
- Warp Drive: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/warp-drive
- Warp Drive LTD: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/warp-drive-black-edition
- Fig Fumb: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/fig-fumb
- Fig Fumb LTD: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/fig-fumb-black-edition
- Rise & Shine: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/rise-shine
- EP-1 Expression: https://www.stonedeaffx.com/products/ep-1-expression
- Founder/history reference: Reverb interview with Luke Hilton, https://reverb.com/de/news/stone-deaf-fx-and-the-next-wave-of-british-tone
- Founder/company history: MusicRadar workshop profile, https://www.musicradar.com/news/the-workshop-stone-deaf-fx
- Historical Kliptonite review: Premier Guitar, https://www.premierguitar.com/gear/stone-deaf-kliptonite-review
- Historical Trashy Blonde review: Premier Guitar, https://www.premierguitar.com/gear/reviews/stone-deaf-effects-trashy-blonde-paracentric-distortion-filter-review
- Historical PDF-1 references: Effects Database, https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/stonedeaf/pdf1
- Historical PDF-1 review: MusicRadar, https://www.musicradar.com/reviews/guitars/stone-deaf-pdf-1-512687

## Status
**Verified research block.** Stone Deaf FX is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The current official catalog clearly establishes PDF-2 as a multi-category Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz product, PDF-1X Ghost as Overdrive + Distortion, Warp Drive as Distortion, Fig Fumb as Fuzz, and Rise & Shine as Fuzz. Historical product documentation adds Kliptonite as Overdrive + Fuzz, Trashy Blonde as Overdrive + Distortion, and the original PDF-1 as a historical Distortion product. The archive deliberately avoids turning boost, filter, octave, tremolo, or mere gain-range behavior into additional dirt categories.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 109

## Builder
**ThorpyFX — Adrian Thorpe / ThorpyFX team**

## Verification basis
ThorpyFX's official About page identifies Adrian Thorpe as the founder and says the company launched in 2014, with its first public pedal, Gunshot, released in April 2015. The same page says ThorpyFX hand-builds its pedals in-house in Britain. The current official catalog and individual product pages were used as the primary evidence for current dirt products. Official ThorpyFX blog posts, manuals, and the builder's own product/video pages were used for older products where those identities remain directly documented.

The archive follows the builder's named product identity rather than treating every gain setting as a new category. A product is placed in multiple target categories only where ThorpyFX explicitly gives it multiple dirt identities or distinct dirt sections.

## Overdrive
- Bunker
- Electric Lightning
- The Dane — MKI / MKII lineage consolidated
- Kudu
- Peacekeeper
- Gunshot — current MKIIB / older versions consolidated
- Warthog
- Cogmeister

## Distortion
- Warthog
- Cogmeister

## Fuzz
- Fallout Cloud
- Field Marshal — MKII / original lineage consolidated
- WOPR
- Shaftesbury Duo Fuzz — standard / Heritage versions consolidated
- Six Zero
- Hanami
- Boneyard — historical / limited
- Veteran — historical / limited
- Old Timer
- Warthog
- Cogmeister

## Product notes
- **Gunshot:** ThorpyFX explicitly identifies Gunshot as an overdrive, describing its cascading gain engine and the original goal of making an overdrive that responds like a real valve amplifier. The current MKIIB keeps the same core sonic identity, so the lineage is consolidated rather than split into version duplicates.
- **Peacekeeper:** ThorpyFX explicitly calls Peacekeeper a low-gain overdrive. Its broad range from near-clean edge-of-breakup through low/mid gain remains part of the overdrive identity.
- **Kudu:** ThorpyFX explicitly calls Kudu an overdrive and describes it as a mid-gain drive and gain-stacking pedal. It remains Overdrive only.
- **The Dane:** ThorpyFX explicitly describes both the MKI and MKII as a dual mid-gain overdrive and boost pedal. The boost side is outside the archive's target categories, so The Dane remains Overdrive only.
- **Electric Lightning:** ThorpyFX describes this as a valve overdrive and boost. The boost side is outside scope; the named drive identity is Overdrive.
- **Bunker:** ThorpyFX presents Bunker as a Plexi Overdrive. It remains Overdrive only because no separate named distortion or fuzz identity is established in the product description.
- **Warthog:** ThorpyFX names Warthog a distortion and separately explains that its gain controls can move from clean boost through overdrive into distortion and, at extreme settings, fuzz. An official ThorpyFX guidance article explicitly says it can provide boost, overdrive, distortion, or fuzz. Because the builder itself assigns all three target dirt identities to the pedal, Warthog is retained in Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz.
- **Cogmeister:** ThorpyFX explicitly describes Cogmeister as a single pedal containing booster, overdrive, distortion, and fuzz sections in series. It therefore receives all three target dirt categories while the boost function itself remains outside the archive.
- **Fallout Cloud:** ThorpyFX explicitly identifies Fallout Cloud as a fuzz and describes it as a Triangle-era-inspired four-transistor fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Field Marshal:** ThorpyFX explicitly identifies Field Marshal as a fuzz, specifically a modern version of the Lovetone Big Cheese. Its tone-stack bypass and boostable balance control remain functions of the fuzz circuit, not extra dirt categories.
- **WOPR:** ThorpyFX explicitly identifies WOPR as a combination built from the Fallout Cloud concept plus a silicon Rangemaster-inspired circuit. The current product title calls it Fuzz and Boost, so it remains Fuzz only.
- **Shaftesbury Duo Fuzz:** ThorpyFX presents the pedal as the Shaftesbury Duo Fuzz, a recreation of a vintage Duo Fuzz with two fuzz voicings. The standard and Heritage editions are consolidated because they are the same named product with different enclosure formats/features.
- **Six Zero:** ThorpyFX describes Six Zero as a vintage-style fuzz combined with a tremolo. The fuzz is the target dirt identity; the tremolo is outside scope.
- **Hanami:** ThorpyFX explicitly identifies Hanami as a germanium fuzz and describes it as the successor to the earlier Boneyard concept. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Boneyard:** ThorpyFX and reputable coverage identify Boneyard as a germanium fuzz covering multiple Tone Bender-era voices. It remains Fuzz only; different transistor-mode and bias settings are behaviors inside the named fuzz product.
- **Veteran:** ThorpyFX's official 2016 product video describes Veteran as a combined Germanium Fuzz and Rangemaster-style treble booster, with the fuzz side first in the signal path. The archive retains Veteran historically as Fuzz only because the other section is a boost rather than Overdrive or Distortion.
- **Old Timer:** ThorpyFX explicitly describes Old Timer as a three-in-one pedal containing an Octavia-type octave fuzz, a Fuzz Face-style section called Tacit Blue, and a Uni-Vibe. It therefore receives Fuzz only; the Uni-Vibe section is outside scope.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Scarlet Tunic:** excluded because ThorpyFX presents it primarily as an amp emulator rather than a named Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz pedal.
- **The J:** excluded because ThorpyFX explicitly presents it as an analog amp emulator based on the Lazy J20. Its ability to distort is part of the amp-emulation behavior, not a separately named dirt identity.
- **Heavy Water:** excluded because it is a dual boost product. ThorpyFX discusses overdrive-like results when stacking its two boost sides, but the named identity remains Boost.
- **Team Medic:** excluded because ThorpyFX presents it as an EQ/boost/buffer utility rather than a target dirt product.
- **Chain Home:** excluded because it is a tremolo plus boost product.
- **Deep Oggin, Camoflange, Pulse Doppler, ER-2:** excluded as modulation effects.
- **For Teddy:** excluded because the current product listing does not establish a sufficiently clear target dirt identity for the archive.
- **Fuzz Remains DIY kit:** excluded because the archive is cataloging finished builder products, not DIY kits.
- **WOPR boost section:** not promoted to Overdrive because ThorpyFX identifies that side as a Rangemaster-inspired boost while the target dirt identity of the complete pedal is Fuzz.
- **Six Zero tremolo section:** not counted separately because modulation is outside scope.
- **Boneyard transistor modes:** not counted as separate pedals or categories because they are alternate operating modes inside one named fuzz product.
- **Normal revisions and enclosure versions:** consolidated where ThorpyFX presents them as the same named product lineage. This includes Gunshot versions, The Dane MKI/MKII, Field Marshal versions, Shaftesbury standard/Heritage formats, and similar normal revisions.
- **2000 AD collaboration editions:** not added as separate product records where ThorpyFX explicitly describes them as special editions of Gunshot, Warthog, and Fallout Cloud. The underlying ThorpyFX pedal identities are already captured above.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- ThorpyFX About Us: https://thorpyfx.com/de/pages/about-us
- ThorpyFX current pedals: https://thorpyfx.com/collections/thorpyfx-pedals
- ThorpyFX Overdrive collection: https://thorpyfx.com/collections/all/overdrive
- ThorpyFX Fuzz collection: https://thorpyfx.com/collections/all/fuzz
- ThorpyFX manuals archive: https://thorpyfx.com/en-us/pages/manuals
- Gunshot: https://thorpyfx.com/en-us/collections/modulation-pedals-copy/products/the-gunshot-overdrive
- Peacekeeper: https://thorpyfx.com/en-us/collections/thorpyfx-pedals/products/the-peacekeeper-low-gain-overdrive-copy
- Kudu: https://thorpyfx.com/products/the-kudu
- The Dane MKI: https://thorpyfx.com/products/the-dane-overdrive-and-booster-peter-danish-pete-honores-signature-pedal
- The Dane MKII: https://thorpyfx.com/en-us/collections/modulation-pedals-copy/products/the-dane-mkii
- Electric Lightning: https://thorpyfx.com/en-us/products/the-electric-lightning-valve-overdrive-and-booster-chris-bucks-signature-pedal
- Warthog: https://thorpyfx.com/products/the-warthog-distortion
- Warthog guidance: https://thorpyfx.com/blogs/effects-pedal-guidance/why-the-warthog
- Cogmeister: https://thorpyfx.com/products/cogmeister
- Fallout Cloud: https://thorpyfx.com/en-us/products/the-fallout-cloud-fuzz
- Field Marshal MKII: https://thorpyfx.com/en-us/products/the-field-marshal-fuzz-mkii
- WOPR: https://thorpyfx.com/products/wopr
- Shaftesbury Duo Fuzz: https://thorpyfx.com/products/shaftesbury-duo-fuzz-heritage-edition-copy
- Six Zero: https://thorpyfx.com/products/the-six-zero-vintage-fuzz-trem
- Hanami: https://thorpyfx.com/products/hanami-germanium-fuzz
- Old Timer: https://thorpyfx.com/en-us/products/the-old-timer
- ThorpyFX × 2000 AD: https://thorpyfx.com/blogs/news/thorpyfx-x-2000-ad-two-british-icons-collide
- ThorpyFX Veteran announcement: https://thorpyfx.com/blogs/news/a-busy-summer-has-yielded-an-awesome-collaboration
- ThorpyFX official Veteran video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1j7ySW7WhKE
- Historical Boneyard reference: https://www.guitarworld.com/news/thorpyfx-boneyard

## Status
**Verified research block.** ThorpyFX is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The current official catalog establishes a strong overdrive and fuzz presence, with Warthog and Cogmeister explicitly spanning Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Veteran and Boneyard are retained as historical fuzz products, while boost-first, amp-emulator, and modulation products are excluded. The 2000 AD special editions are consolidated with their underlying ThorpyFX pedal identities rather than counted as variant duplicates.

# Builder → Pedals Research Block 110

## Builder
**Mr. Black — Jack Deville / Jack Deville LTD.**

## Verification basis
Mr. Black's official About page says the product line was founded in early 2012 as a special division of Jack Deville LTD., and that Jack Deville designs the circuits and the pedals are handmade in Portland, Oregon. The current Mr. Black catalog still lists dedicated overdrive, distortion, and fuzz products, while Mr. Black's historical product pages and established pedal references document additional discontinued dirt products.

The archive follows named product identity rather than assuming that every high-gain setting creates a second category. Boost-first products and modulation/reverb products remain outside the census.

## Overdrive
- Deuce Coupe — current/reintroduced lineage
- Bass OD Mk. II
- BB-74x
- Vintage Overdrive
- Black LTD. Bass OD — historical / limited
- Black LTD. Overdriver — historical / limited
- Black LTD. Overdriver Mk. II — historical / limited
- Classic 88 — historical / limited
- Gold Drive "One Hundred" — historical / limited
- Jalapeño — historical
- JDS-50 — historical / discontinued
- OD-503 Mini Amplifier Overdrive — historical / mini series
- Orro
- Snake Drive — historical
- Sour Cream & Onion — historical
- Super-Extra Five Thousand — historical / limited, clean boost + amplifier overdrive

## Distortion
- ThunderClaw
- White Widow — historical / discontinued
- Black LTD. Distortion — historical / limited

## Fuzz
- SludgeMaster
- Black LTD. Darling Fuzz — historical / limited
- Darling Fuzz / Black Fuzz lineage consolidated
- Doomstick Fuzz — historical
- Doomstick II Fuzz — historical / later revision
- Barbeque Fuzz — historical

## Product notes
- **Deuce Coupe:** Mr. Black's current page describes the revived Deuce Coupe as a naturally responsive overdrive with a separate Ultra Boost function. The boost remains outside scope, while the named drive identity is retained as Overdrive.
- **Bass OD Mk. II:** Mr. Black explicitly describes this as a bass overdrive/preamp with touch-sensitive grind. It remains Overdrive only.
- **BB-74x:** historical references identify BB-74x as an overdrive built around the response of an 18-watt EL84 combo. It remains Overdrive.
- **Vintage Overdrive:** Mr. Black explicitly identifies this as an analog overdrive and boost. The boost function is outside scope, so it remains Overdrive.
- **Black LTD. Overdriver / Overdriver Mk. II:** Mr. Black's own historical pages explicitly call both products overdrive. They remain historical Overdrive entries, with the Mk. II kept separate as a distinct named limited product.
- **Classic 88:** established historical listings identify Classic 88 as a re-engineered tube-style overdrive. It is retained historically under Overdrive.
- **Gold Drive "One Hundred":** established historical listings identify this as an EL34-style drive designed around hot British amplifier roar. It is retained as Overdrive; it is not promoted into Distortion solely because it can reach aggressive gain.
- **Jalapeño:** historical catalog references identify this as an Overdrive. It remains Overdrive.
- **JDS-50:** established product references identify JDS-50 as an Overdrive. It remains Overdrive.
- **OD-503 Mini Amplifier Overdrive:** the product name itself and historical catalog references identify it as an amplifier-style Overdrive. It remains Overdrive.
- **Orro:** established references identify Orro as a mid-gain Overdrive. It remains Overdrive only.
- **Sour Cream & Onion:** historical catalog references identify this product as an Overdrive. It remains Overdrive.
- **Super-Extra Five Thousand:** historical catalog references identify it as a clean boost plus amplifier overdrive. It is included for the Overdrive side while the boost function remains outside scope.
- **ThunderClaw:** Mr. Black's current page explicitly calls ThunderClaw a high-gain distortion pedal. It remains Distortion only despite its ability to clean up with the guitar volume.
- **White Widow:** established references identify White Widow as a dedicated Distortion. It remains Distortion only.
- **Black LTD. Distortion:** Mr. Black's historical product page explicitly identifies this as a high-gain distortion, with a limited run of 50 pieces. It remains Distortion.
- **SludgeMaster:** Mr. Black explicitly identifies SludgeMaster as a high-gain fuzz built around the late-1970s IC distortion/sustainer sound. It remains Fuzz only.
- **Black LTD. Darling Fuzz:** Mr. Black's historical page identifies this limited product as a fuzz. It remains Fuzz.
- **Darling Fuzz / Black Fuzz:** historical references identify Black Fuzz as the discontinued version/lineage of Darling Fuzz. The archive consolidates those names rather than counting a color/limited naming change as a second fuzz product.
- **Doomstick Fuzz / Doomstick II Fuzz:** specialist coverage identifies Doomstick as a one-knob, aggressively gated fuzz and later Doomstick II as its follow-up/revoiced version. These remain Fuzz entries and are treated as one Doomstick lineage unless Mr. Black presents a materially distinct named product.
- **Barbeque Fuzz:** historical product coverage identifies Barbeque as a Mr. Black Fuzz. It remains Fuzz only.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Boost Tiger, Black LTD. Treble Booster, Boost Drive One-Hundred, Black LTD. Power Drive:** excluded because their named identity is primarily Boost rather than Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz.
- **BloodMoon, SuperMoon, Eterna, Heaven's Gate, Dark Echo, TunnelWorm, GilaMondo, Pana-Trem, Vintage Ensemble, Mod.One, and other modulation/reverb/delay products:** excluded because they are outside the three target dirt categories.
- **High-Five:** excluded because it is an instant power-chord generator/effect rather than a dedicated dirt pedal.
- **Mini Octaves and other octave/modulation-only products:** excluded.
- **Black LTD. Darling Fuzz color/limited variants:** consolidated with the underlying Darling Fuzz identity.
- **Doomstick naming:** Doomstick and Doomstick II are treated as one product lineage for now because the later version is a revision of the same named fuzz concept; no separate circuit-family record is created from the revision alone.
- **High-gain behavior:** ThunderClaw, Vintage Overdrive, JDS-50, Orro, and other gain pedals are not automatically duplicated into adjacent categories merely because they can produce more saturated tones.
- **Historical limited products:** retained where product-level references clearly identify the dirt function; weakly documented one-offs and mystery products are excluded.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Mr. Black About: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/pages/about-us
- Mr. Black current products: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/collections/all
- Mr. Black Overdrive & Distortion collection: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/collections/pedals/overdrive-distortion
- Deuce Coupe: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/collections/pedals/products/deuce-coupe
- Bass OD Mk. II: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/collections/pedals/products/bass-od-mk-ii
- SludgeMaster: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/collections/pedals/products/sludgemaster
- ThunderClaw: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/collections/pedals/products/thunderclaw
- Vintage Overdrive: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/collections/pedals/products/vintage-overdrive
- Black LTD. Darling Fuzz: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/products/black-ltd-darling-fuzz
- Black LTD. Distortion: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/products/black-ltd-distortion
- Black LTD. Overdriver: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/products/black-ltd-overdriver
- Black LTD. Overdriver Mk. II: https://www.mrblackpedals.com/products/black-ltd-overdriver-mk-ii
- Historical Mr. Black product index: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/mrblack
- Doomstick Fuzz coverage: https://www.musicradar.com/news/hit-your-tone-with-mr-blacks-doomstick-for-none-more-heavy-fuzz
- Doomstick coverage: https://www.gearnews.com/mr-black-doomstick-one-knob-fuzz-pedal/
- BB-74x reference: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/mrblack/bb74x
- JDS-50 reference: https://dirtboxlayouts.blogspot.com/2025/03/mr-black-jds-50-overdrive.html
- Orro reference: https://dirtboxlayouts.blogspot.com/2025/03/mr-black-orro-overdrive.html
- 2017 historical catalog coverage: https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/gpx-blog/2017-new-guitar-pedals-of-the-year

## Status
**Verified research block.** Mr. Black is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The current catalog establishes Deuce Coupe and Bass OD Mk. II as current Overdrive products, SludgeMaster as current Fuzz, and ThunderClaw as current Distortion. Historical Mr. Black pages and established product references add BB-74x, JDS-50, Orro, Black LTD. Overdriver products, Black LTD. Distortion, White Widow, Classic 88, Gold Drive "One Hundred", Darling Fuzz, Doomstick, and Barbeque Fuzz. The archive remains conservative around boost-first products, modulation/reverb products, and gain-range overlap.
