# Builder → Pedals Research Block 111

## Builder
**Chase Bliss — Joel Korte / Chase Bliss team**

## Verification basis
Chase Bliss's official About page identifies Joel Korte as the founder and says the company began in 2013. The current official pedal catalog lists Brothers AM as a current product, while the legacy/current catalog pages and official manuals document older dirt-focused products including Brothers, Bliss Factory, Condor, and Preamp MKII. Official Chase Bliss product pages and manuals are the primary sources used here.

The archive follows named product identity and explicit dirt functions. Boost-only behavior, incidental clipping, and gain range are not enough by themselves to create an Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz entry.

## Overdrive
- Brothers AM — current
- Brothers — historical / discontinued
- Condor HiFi — historical / discontinued
- Preamp MKII — historical / discontinued

## Distortion
- Brothers AM — current

## Fuzz
- Brothers — historical / discontinued
- Bliss Factory — historical / discontinued
- Preamp MKII — historical / discontinued

## Product notes
- **Brothers AM:** Chase Bliss explicitly describes the two gain channels as selectable between boost, overdrive, or distortion. It therefore receives both Overdrive and Distortion. The added treble-booster circuit is outside the archive target categories.
- **Brothers:** the official Brothers manual describes two independent analog channels containing six circuits across boost, drive, and fuzz, with each channel able to select its own effect type. It is therefore retained historically as Overdrive/Drive + Fuzz. The builder does not document a separate Distortion identity for Brothers, so it is not promoted into Distortion.
- **Condor HiFi:** Chase Bliss explicitly describes Condor HiFi as part EQ, part filter, and part overdrive, with a dedicated drive section ranging from sparkly boost to full crunch. It remains Overdrive only. Its filter and internal modulation are outside scope.
- **Preamp MKII:** Chase Bliss describes Preamp MKII as a do-it-all pedal for drive, filtering, and fuzz, with a preamp section providing responsive breakup and a separate fuzz section with Open and Gated modes. It is retained as Overdrive + Fuzz. The official page does not establish a separate Distortion identity, so it is not listed there.
- **Bliss Factory:** the official manual identifies Bliss Factory as a two-germanium-transistor fuzz derived from the Fuzz Factory concept. The DRIVE control increases distortion within the fuzz circuit, but the named identity is Fuzz, so it remains Fuzz only.

## Explicit exclusions / restraint
- **Clean:** excluded because Chase Bliss presents it primarily as a creative compressor/limiter, even though it can be pushed toward overdrive and fuzz-like behavior. Those are operating modes of a compressor rather than the primary dirt identity.
- **Warped Vinyl, Wombtone, Gravitas, Thermae, Tonal Recall, MOOD, blooper, Habit, Dark World, CXM 1978, Generation Loss, Lossy, Onward, Big Time, and other non-target products:** excluded because their primary identities are vibrato, phaser, modulation, delay, reverb, looping, sampling, or other effects.
- **Preamp MKII distortion:** not promoted into Distortion merely from broad breakup/gain language. Chase Bliss explicitly separates the pedal's preamp/drive section from its fuzz section but does not name it as a dedicated distortion product.
- **Bliss Factory distortion wording:** not promoted into Distortion because the manual uses distortion language to describe how the fuzz DRIVE control behaves inside the fuzz circuit.
- **Brothers boost modes:** excluded from the archive's target categories because Boost is outside scope.
- **Brothers AM treble booster:** excluded as Boost rather than Overdrive/Distortion/Fuzz.
- **Condor filter/modulation sections:** excluded because they are EQ/filter/modulation functions rather than additional dirt categories.
- **Normal revisions and current/legacy handling:** current Brothers AM is kept as its own named product; older Brothers, Bliss Factory, Condor HiFi, and Preamp MKII are retained historically because Chase Bliss still documents them in its official manuals/catalog.

## Primary / authoritative sources
- Chase Bliss About: https://www.chasebliss.com/about
- Chase Bliss pedals catalog: https://www.chasebliss.com/pedals
- Chase Bliss manuals: https://www.chasebliss.com/manuals
- Brothers AM: https://www.chasebliss.com/brothers-am
- Brothers AM quick start guide: https://www.chasebliss.com/brothers-am
- Brothers official manual: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/622176a9b8d15d57ffbf5700/t/622cbdf307ee315ce99dae65/1647099386297/Brothers_Manual_Pedal_Chase%2BBliss.pdf
- Bliss Factory official manual: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/622176a9b8d15d57ffbf5700/t/622cbd62f0d13e1b216bece3/1647099237661/Bliss%2BFactory_Manual_Chase%2BBliss.pdf
- Condor HiFi: https://www.chasebliss.com/condor-hifi
- Preamp MKII: https://www.chasebliss.com/preamp-mkii
- Chase Bliss discontinued-products notice: https://www.chasebliss.com/legacy-blog

## Status
**Verified research block.** Chase Bliss is a previously uncovered builder in the fresh census. The strongest target dirt cases are Brothers AM (Overdrive + Distortion), Brothers (Overdrive/Drive + Fuzz), Preamp MKII (Overdrive/Drive + Fuzz), Condor HiFi (Overdrive), and Bliss Factory (Fuzz). The archive remains conservative around compressor clipping, internal fuzz distortion language, boost stages, and the company's much larger modulation/delay/reverb catalog.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 112

## Builder
**Hudson Electronics UK**

Builder identity is tied directly to the Hudson Electronics product pages. Current Hudson pages identify the pedals as individually hand-built and repeatedly name **Michael Hudson** as the builder. The surviving catalog contains both current pedals and an official archived section.

## Scope result
Hudson has a strong, unusually broad dirt footprint across the three target categories. Current products are concentrated around the Broadcast family and Sidecar, while the archived catalog preserves a substantial group of fuzz and overdrive designs.

Normal Broadcast family editions and color/retail variants are consolidated rather than counted as separate pedals. Limited collaborations are retained only when they are materially distinct named products.

## Overdrive
- **Broadcast** — current
- **Sidecar** — current
- **Broadcast-AP** — current/legacy signature model
- **Motorcycle** — limited collaboration with Joe's Pedals
- **Overdrive 999** — historical / discontinued

## Distortion
- **Broadcast** — current
- **Sidecar** — current

## Fuzz
- **Broadcast** — current, because Hudson explicitly documents the high-gain Broadcast family reaching fuzz
- **Sidecar** — current, because Hudson explicitly says it can reach fuzz at extreme settings
- **Fuzz (MKIV Tone Bender)** — historical / archived
- **White Heat** — historical / archived
- **Full Range Bomb** — historical / archived, boost + fuzz
- **Soul Bomb** — historical / archived, fuzz + boost
- **Pretty Flamingo** — historical / archived
- **Blackbird** — historical / archived
- **Buzzard** — historical / archived
- **Bluebird** — historical / archived
- **Overdrive Eight Fifty** — historical; retained as Fuzz based on surviving specialist catalog classification and its BMP/Russian Muff lineage
- **Standard Machine** — historical
- **Shapes Of Things** — historical
- **Buzzoid** — historical
- **Stroll On** — historical / archived
- **Stroll On Jnr** — historical
- **Soul Face** — historical / archived
- **Mr Soul** — historical / archived

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Broadcast:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Hudson's official Broadcast description covers transparent overdrive at lower gain and heavier distorted sounds at high gain; the Broadcast family documentation explicitly reaches fuzz.
- **Sidecar:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Hudson describes it as an 808-derived overdrive that can cover classic overdrive and distortion and, at extreme settings, fuzz.
- **Broadcast-AP:** Overdrive only. Hudson explicitly says it covers clean boost through medium-gain overdrive and can become overloaded/broken/fuzzy at maximum gain, but does not present a separate distortion identity.
- **Motorcycle:** Overdrive only. Hudson describes it as a modified Sidecar overdrive and a revisit of the historical Overdrive 999.
- **Pretty Flamingo:** Fuzz only. Hudson calls it a 1967-style fuzz; its access to lighter overdrive/distortion tones is treated as behavior inside the fuzz design, not a second target identity.
- **Buzzard:** Fuzz only. Hudson describes it as a fuzz design with a dark boost side and notes dense, chewy distortion as a sound, but does not present a separate distortion product identity.
- **Full Range Bomb:** Fuzz only. Hudson describes a footswitchable light fuzz circuit alongside parallel boosts; boost remains outside the archive's target categories.
- **Soul Bomb:** Fuzz only. Hudson explicitly describes the Soul side as a germanium fuzz cascaded into a boost; boost remains outside scope.

## Historical/variant restraint
- The official Hudson product catalog currently shows the current pedal group as Broadcast, Broadcast dual-foot-switch, Sidecar, Broadcast AP-II, Broadcast-AP, and limited Broadcast editions. These are not treated as a dozen separate Broadcast products. The underlying Broadcast identity is consolidated.
- The official archived category lists 15 historical products. Only the dirt-relevant entries are pulled into the target census; **Gospel Bomb** is excluded as boost-first. Other non-target or ambiguous items are not promoted merely because they can become dirty.
- Broadcast AP-II remains outside the categorized dirt list for now. Hudson presents it as a dual preamplifier with large gain ranges that can reach heavily distorted sounds, but the surviving official description does not establish a separate named Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz identity strongly enough for conservative classification.
- The surviving official Overdrive Eight Fifty page is not reliably available in the current crawl. It is included only because the product is confirmed in Hudson's archived product catalog and an established effects database classifies it as fuzz and identifies its old-Ibanez-OD850/BMP lineage. This is a lower-confidence historical classification than the directly documented Hudson pages.
- No component, schematic, cloning, or internal-build information is being recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Hudson Electronics UK — Pedals catalog: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product-category/pedals/
2. Hudson Electronics UK — Archived catalog: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product-category/archived/
3. Hudson Electronics UK — Archived catalog page 2: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product-category/archived/page/2/
4. Hudson Electronics UK — Broadcast: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/broadcast/
5. Hudson Electronics UK — Broadcast versions: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/broadcast-versions/
6. Hudson Electronics UK — Broadcast dual foot switch: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/broadcast-dual-foot-switch/
7. Hudson Electronics UK — Broadcast-AP: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/broadcast-ap/
8. Hudson Electronics UK — Broadcast AP-II: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/broadcast-ap-ii/
9. Hudson Electronics UK — Sidecar: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/sidecar/
10. Hudson Electronics UK — Motorcycle: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/motorcycle/
11. Hudson Electronics UK — Overdrive 999: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/overdrive-999/
12. Hudson Electronics UK — Fuzz (MKIV): https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/fuzz/

14. Hudson Electronics UK — Full Range Bomb: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/full-range-bomb/

16. Hudson Electronics UK — Pretty Flamingo: https://hudsonelectronicsuk.com/product/pretty-flamingo/

26. Premier Guitar — Hudson 2014 product launch (Overdrive 999 / Blackbird / Buzzard): https://www.premierguitar.com/hudson-electronics-unveils-the-overdrive-999-blackbird-and-buzzard
27. Premier Guitar — Hudson 2015 product launch (Broadcast / Fuzz / Pretty Flamingo): https://www.premierguitar.com/hudson-electronics-unveils-the-broadcast-fuzz-and-pretty-flamingo

## Research note
This block deliberately favors Hudson's own catalog language. The builder has enough surviving official evidence to establish a substantial three-category footprint without forcing every high-gain behavior into an extra category.

## Next
**Block 113**: continue the fresh Builder → Pedals census with another previously uncovered multi-pedal builder or builder group.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 113

## Builder
**BlackOutEffectors**

The surviving BlackOutEffectors site identifies the company as a boutique guitar-effects manufacturer founded in 2008, with the company beginning in Vancouver and moving to Asheville in 2009. The historical catalog is strongly associated with founder/designer Kyle Tompkins. The current site is best treated as a surviving reference/catalog rather than evidence of a continuously operating production line.

## Scope result
BlackOutEffectors is a previously uncovered builder with a concentrated but important dirt catalog. Its strongest identity is fuzz, with a smaller overdrive footprint. The builder's own catalog explicitly labels Mantra as Overdrive and describes Fix'd Fuzz products as fuzz devices that also contain boost/overdrive stages. The surviving catalog does **not** establish a distinct BlackOutEffectors Distortion product identity strongly enough for this conservative census, so no standalone Distortion entry is created.

Normal revisions and closely related limited editions are consolidated where they represent the same underlying named pedal lineage.

## Overdrive
- **Mantra Overdrive** — historical / established catalog product
- **Fix'd Fuzz / Fix'd Fuzz V3** — fuzz-based hybrid with an explicit light-overdrive range
- **Fix'd Fuzz Deluxe** — historical / deluxe hybrid; builder explicitly describes boost + overdrive alongside fuzz

## Distortion
- **None established under the archive's conservative rule.**

BlackOutEffectors products can produce aggressive, heavily clipped tones, and outside databases sometimes place pedals such as Musket or Blunderbuss in distortion categories. That is not enough here to create a separate Distortion identity when BlackOutEffectors itself presents those products as fuzz designs.

## Fuzz
- **Fix'd Fuzz / Fix'd Fuzz V3** — historical/current catalog lineage
- **Fix'd Fuzz Deluxe** — historical / deluxe hybrid
- **Musket Fuzz / Musket Fuzz V2** — historical/current catalog lineage
- **FUBÄR Fuzz** — historical / limited
- **Blunderbuss Fuzz** — historical / special-run hybrid fuzz
- **Twosome Dual Fuzz** — historical; dual fuzz combining the Musket and Fix'd Fuzz Deluxe concepts
- **Crystal Dagger** — historical; octave-fuzz section combined with ring modulation / phasing functions
- **Fuzz Bear** — historical / limited

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Fix'd Fuzz / V3:** Fuzz + Overdrive. BlackOutEffectors calls the pedal a handmade fuzz box with boost and overdrive capability, and the surviving description says the stages can be individually engaged or bypassed.
- **Fix'd Fuzz Deluxe:** Fuzz + Overdrive. BlackOutEffectors describes it as a hydra of handmade fuzz boxes, boosts, and overdrives.
- **Mantra:** Overdrive only. BlackOutEffectors explicitly titles it Mantra Overdrive and describes a range from refined low-gain overdrive through aggressive drive.
- **Musket:** Fuzz only. BlackOutEffectors explicitly calls it a highly tweakable, fuzzed-up version of the vintage Muff family. The archive does not promote the pedal to Distortion merely because external databases sometimes use a broader distortion category.
- **Blunderbuss:** Fuzz only. BlackOutEffectors calls it a Fuzz, even though some secondary catalogs place it under distortion as well. The builder's own identity wins under the archive rule.
- **Crystal Dagger:** Fuzz only. The product combines octave fuzz with ring modulation/phasing functions, but the target dirt identity remains fuzz.

## Historical/variant restraint
- **Fix'd Fuzz** and **Fix'd Fuzz V3** are treated as one product lineage. BlackOutEffectors' surviving catalog presents V3 as the current reference form, while specialist catalog records preserve the original named pedal.
- **Musket Fuzz** and **Musket Fuzz V2** are consolidated into one Musket lineage rather than counted as separate products.
- **Twosome Dual Fuzz** and its special/limited editions are treated as one underlying Twosome lineage rather than a separate entry for every graphics or dealer run. The important functional identity is the dual-fuzz combination of the Musket and Fix'd Fuzz Deluxe concepts.
- **Crystal Dagger** remains a Fuzz entry despite also being an octave/ring-mod/phaser hybrid. Those added modulation functions do not become separate dirt categories.
- **Gravity Boost** and other boost-first products are excluded. Sibling, Whetstone, and Cadavernous are excluded because they are modulation/reverb products rather than target dirt pedals.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. BlackOutEffectors — Effects catalog: https://blackouteffectors.com/effects/
2. BlackOutEffectors — home/about reference: https://blackouteffectors.com/
3. Effects Database — Blackout Effectors model index: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout
4. Effects Database — Fix'd Fuzz: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout/fixdfuzz
5. Effects Database — Mantra Overdrive: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout/mantra
6. Effects Database — Dual Gain Mantra: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/blackout/mantra/dualgain
7. Reverb — Blackout Effectors brand archive: https://reverb.com/brand/blackout-effectors
8. Reverb — Blunderbuss Hybrid Fuzz: https://reverb.com/p/blackout-effectors-blunderbuss
9. Reverb — Twosome Dual Fuzz: https://reverb.com/p/blackout-effectors-twosome
10. Analogue Haven — Mantra Overdrive / manual: https://www.analoguehaven.com/blackout-effectors/mantra-overdrive/
11. Blackout Effectors Mantra manual mirror: https://www.analoguehaven.com/blackout-effectors/mantra-overdrive/manual.pdf
12. Stinkfoot — Blackout Effectors model/reference list: https://stinkfoot.se/archives/5903
13. Guitar Pedal X — Blackout Effectors directory entry: https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/guitar-pedal-directory---favourite-pedals-by-preferred-pedal-makers

## Research note
BlackOutEffectors is useful precisely because the catalog is compact and very fuzz-heavy. The archive preserves the builder's own naming and functional language rather than forcing every aggressive clipping sound into a separate Distortion category. That keeps the census useful later when individual pedal records are built.

**Block 114**: continue the fresh Builder → Pedals census with another previously uncovered multi-pedal builder or builder group.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 114

## Builder
**Mythos Pedals**

The official Mythos Pedals About page identifies **Zach Broyles** as the person who started Mythos in 2010 and describes the company as a small team operating outside Nashville, Tennessee. The current official catalog separates products into Overdrive, Fuzz, Boost, Octave, Modulation, Utility, and Limited & Exclusive collections, with a separate Retired Models collection.

## Scope result
Mythos has a substantial dirt catalog spanning all three target categories. Its official catalog is especially useful because it explicitly labels most products by category, while individual product pages also make several hybrid cases clear.

This block consolidates normal colorways, blemished listings, and routine Mjolnir editions rather than treating them as separate pedal identities. Distinct named collaborations or materially different product identities are retained when they function as recognizable named products.

## Overdrive
- **Erlking Overdrive** — current
- **Mjolnir Overdrive** — current
- **Envy Pro Overdrive** — current
- **Chupacabra CMOS Overdrive** — current
- **Herculean Deluxe Overdrive** — current
- **Olympus Overdrive** — current
- **Extra Hot Dual Overdrive** — current / dealer-exclusive
- **Extra Hot "Extra Spicy" Dual Overdrive** — current / limited-exclusive
- **Herculean / Herculean V2** — historical lineage
- **210 Double Drive Deluxe** — historical / retired
- **SusMaryOsep** — historical / retired collaboration

## Distortion
- **The Victor Distortion** — limited / historical-current release
- **Golden Fleece Fuzz** — current, because Mythos explicitly describes it as blending fuzz, distortion, and overdrive

## Fuzz
- **Golden Fleece Fuzz** — current
- **Big Puft Fuzz Device** — current lineage
- **Hephaestus Fuzz** — current
- **Argo Octave Fuzz** — current
- **Argo Boost Deluxe** — current, because the pedal contains the full Argo octave-fuzz circuit alongside its standalone boost function
- **1347 Silicon Fuzz** — limited / current
- **AU-79 Fuzz** — limited / exclusive
- **High Road Fuzz Mini** — limited / exclusive
- **Positron Fuzz / Positron Collider lineage** — current + historical predecessor consolidated
- **210 Double Drive Deluxe** — historical / retired, because Mythos explicitly describes its gain range as reaching from boost and overdrive through gnarly fuzz tones

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Golden Fleece Fuzz:** Fuzz + Distortion + Overdrive. Mythos directly calls it a dirt box that blends all three target categories, so all three are retained.
- **210 Double Drive Deluxe:** Overdrive + Fuzz. Mythos describes two drive circuits covering slight boost, sweet overdrive crunch, and gnarly fuzz tones. Boost itself remains outside the archive's target categories.
- **AU-79 Fuzz:** Fuzz + Overdrive. Mythos's FAQ says the AU-79 is lower gain and smoother, functioning as an "overdrive meets fuzz" design and intended to stack with other drives.
- **Argo Boost Deluxe:** Fuzz only within the target taxonomy. Mythos explicitly describes the unit as containing the Argo octave-fuzz circuit plus a standalone boost mode. Boost is not a target category, so only Fuzz is recorded.
- **SusMaryOsep:** Overdrive only within the target taxonomy. It contains a Silicon Treble Booster, Herculean-based Overdrive, and an echo section. The booster and echo remain outside scope.
- **Herculean Deluxe:** Overdrive only. Mythos presents both internal sides as overdrive circuits, including a D-style amp-like overdrive and a Blues Breaker-based overdrive.
- **Extra Hot Dual Overdrive:** Overdrive only. Mythos explicitly presents both sides as overdrive circuits, even though one side can approach fuzz-like dirt at extreme settings.
- **Chupacabra CMOS Overdrive:** Overdrive only. Mythos describes pseudo-fuzz-like tones at the top of the gain range, but the builder's named identity remains Overdrive.
- **Big Puft:** Fuzz only. Mythos describes mellow distortion through annihilating fuzz, but names and categorizes the product as a fuzz rather than a separate distortion design.
- **Positron:** Fuzz only. Mythos describes the LOUD setting as producing fuller distortion, but the product remains explicitly a Ram's Head/Supa Tonebender-derived fuzz identity.

## Historical/variant restraint
- **Mjolnir** variants such as 1N34A Gold, 1N34A Silver, GE Mjolnir, Joey Landreth Mjolnir, and Wildwood Mjolnir are consolidated into the **Mjolnir Overdrive** lineage rather than counted as separate pedals merely because the enclosure, component set, or collaboration changes.
- **Golden Fleece** versions, including standard, High Road, and related retailer/limited forms, are not inflated into separate products unless Mythos gives them a distinct named identity. The **AU-79** is retained separately because Mythos presents it as a distinct named exclusive product with a different low-gain overdrive-meets-fuzz character.
- **Big Puft** colorways, including Inverse and retailer-exclusive finishes, are consolidated into one Big Puft lineage.
- **Positron Collider** is treated as the historical predecessor to the current **Positron Fuzz**, not as a second unrelated pedal family.
- **D-Style Herculean Overdrive - Blem** is treated as a sales/finish listing for the Herculean lineage rather than a separate product.
- **Extra Hot Dual Overdrive - Blem** is treated as a sales/finish listing, not a new pedal.
- Boost-first products such as **Luxury Drive Boost**, **Cestus Treble Booster**, and the booster portion of SusMaryOsep or Argo Boost Deluxe are not separately categorized because Boost is outside the archive's target taxonomy.
- **Argonaut Octave Up** is excluded because its primary identity is octave-up rather than a dirt pedal. Other modulation, delay, utility, and parts/accessory products are likewise excluded.
- No component, schematic, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Mythos Pedals — About Us: https://mythospedals.com/pages/about-us
2. Mythos Pedals — Products: https://mythospedals.com/collections/all
3. Mythos Pedals — Overdrive collection: https://mythospedals.com/collections/overdrive
4. Mythos Pedals — Fuzz collection: https://mythospedals.com/collections/fuzz
5. Mythos Pedals — Limited & Exclusive Models: https://mythospedals.com/collections/limited-exclusive-models
6. Mythos Pedals — Retired Models: https://mythospedals.com/collections/retired-models
7. Mythos Pedals — Erlking Overdrive: https://mythospedals.com/products/erlking-overdrive
8. Mythos Pedals — Mjolnir Overdrive: https://mythospedals.com/products/mjolnir
9. Mythos Pedals — Envy Pro Overdrive: https://mythospedals.com/products/envy-pro-overdrive
10. Mythos Pedals — Chupacabra CMOS Overdrive: https://mythospedals.com/products/chupacabra-cmos-overdrive
11. Mythos Pedals — Herculean Deluxe Overdrive: https://mythospedals.com/collections/all/products/herculean-deluxe
12. Mythos Pedals — Olympus Overdrive: https://mythospedals.com/products/olympus-overdrive
13. Mythos Pedals — Extra Hot Dual Overdrive: https://mythospedals.com/products/extra-hot-overdrive
14. Mythos Pedals — Golden Fleece Fuzz: https://mythospedals.com/products/golden-fleece-fuzz
15. Mythos Pedals — Big Puft Fuzz Device: https://mythospedals.com/collections/fuzz/products/big-puft-fuzz
16. Mythos Pedals — Hephaestus Fuzz: https://mythospedals.com/products/hephaestus-mini-fuzz
17. Mythos Pedals — Argo Boost Deluxe: https://mythospedals.com/products/argo-boost-deluxe
18. Mythos Pedals — 1347 Silicon Fuzz: https://mythospedals.com/products/1347-silicon-fuzz
19. Mythos Pedals — Positron Fuzz: https://mythospedals.com/products/positron-fuzz
20. Mythos Pedals — 210 Double Drive Deluxe: https://mythospedals.com/products/210-double-drive-deluxe
21. Mythos Pedals — SusMaryOsep: https://mythospedals.com/collections/retired-models/products/susmaryosep
22. Mythos Pedals — FAQ: https://mythospedals.com/pages/faq
23. Mythos Pedals — The Victor Distortion: https://mythospedals.com/products/the-victor-distortion

## Research note
Mythos is a particularly clean fit for the archive because the builder's own storefront openly separates Overdrive and Fuzz collections and now includes a named Distortion release. The most important hybrid is Golden Fleece, where Mythos itself explicitly names all three target dirt identities. The archive does not turn every high-gain or fuzzy setting into another category when the builder continues to name the product as an Overdrive or Fuzz.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 115

## Builder
**OKKO FX**

OKKO FX's official site identifies the company as a German pedal builder based in Leipzig and states that it has been making pedals since 2003, with current pedals handbuilt by musicians and technicians there.

## Scope result
OKKO FX has a strong current and historical dirt footprint. The present catalog includes multiple overdrive and distortion products plus the Holy Grit hybrid and a current Green Russian Muff-derived fuzz release. Historical references establish the Black Beast as a distinct fuzz/distortion product and preserve the original TwinSonic.

Normal anniversary editions and routine visual variants are consolidated into their underlying named pedal lineages where the product identity is unchanged.

## Overdrive
- **DIABLO DUAL** — current
- **DIABLO GH Signature** — current
- **TWINSONIC MKII** — current
- **The Dynamist** — current
- **TwinSonic** — historical lineage
- **DIABLO** — historical/current lineage consolidated with the Diablo family rather than counting ordinary revisions as separate pedals
- **Holy Grit** — current hybrid Overdrive + Fuzz

## Distortion
- **DIABLO DUAL** — current
- **MOTÖRBASS** — current
- **Black Beast** — historical, explicitly classified as Fuzz + Distortion by authoritative specialist catalog evidence

## Fuzz
- **HOLY GRIT** — current hybrid Overdrive + Fuzz
- **BFGRMWB / Big Fucking Green Russian Muff With Benefits** — current
- **Black Beast** — historical hybrid Fuzz + Distortion

## Explicit category overlaps
- **DIABLO DUAL:** Overdrive + Distortion. OKKO's official page explicitly calls it a 2-channel overdrive/distortion.
- **HOLY GRIT:** Overdrive + Fuzz. OKKO's official page explicitly labels it "Overdrive Fuzz Preamp" and describes the gain control moving from gritty overdrive into low-gain fuzz.
- **Black Beast:** Fuzz + Distortion. The surviving specialist catalog describes it as a large-sounding fuzz/distortion pedal, with its gain structure moving from harmonic distortion into nasty fuzz.
- **DIABLO GH Signature:** Overdrive only within scope. OKKO presents it as overdrive plus an independent clean boost; boost remains outside the target taxonomy.
- **TWINSONIC MKII:** Overdrive only. OKKO describes two stackable overdrive circuits, even though the combined range can approach vintage distortion or fuzz territory. Those sounds are not promoted into separate target categories.
- **The Dynamist:** Overdrive only. It is the current visual/identity successor to the Diablo Dual concept and is presented as a dual FET overdrive. High-gain behavior does not create a separate Distortion entry.
- **BFGRMWB:** Fuzz only. The official current catalog names it as a Green Russian Muff-derived fuzz product; the "with benefits" phrasing does not create a second dirt identity.

## Historical/variant restraint
- **TwinSonic / TwinSonic MKII** are treated as one product lineage with a current MKII form and a historical original rather than as unrelated products.
- **DIABLO / DIABLO DUAL / DIABLO GH** remain distinct named products where the design identity materially differs, but ordinary anniversary/color/enclosure variants are not counted separately.
- **DIABLO 20th Anniversary** is a limited edition of the Diablo and is consolidated with the Diablo lineage rather than counted as a separate pedal.
- **Dirty Diablo** is treated as a variation of the Diablo family and is not promoted to a separate builder/product lineage unless later evidence establishes a materially independent design identity.
- **The Dynamist** is retained as its own current named product because OKKO currently sells it under that name, while its relationship to the Diablo Dual is documented as an evolution/reframing of the same core concept.
- Boost-first products such as the clean-boost section of Diablo GH remain outside the target taxonomy. Compressor, buffer, and other non-target products are excluded.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. OKKO FX — official home/catalog: https://okko-fx.com/
2. OKKO FX — DIABLO DUAL: https://okko-fx.com/DIABLO-DUAL-p261206223
3. OKKO FX — HOLY GRIT: https://okko-fx.com/HOLY-GRIT-p469583005
4. OKKO FX — DIABLO 20 limited edition: https://okko-fx.com/DIABLO-20-ltd-big-box-p591989832
5. Effects Database — TwinSonic: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/okko/twinsonic
6. Effects Database — Black Beast: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/okko/blackbeast
7. Guitar Pedal X — Holy Grit coverage: https://www.guitarpedalx.com/news/gpx-blog/2022-august-pedal-chain-update---episode-viii---summer-flange-fling
8. Delicious Audio — TwinSonic V2: https://delicious-audio.com/okko-fx-twinsonic-v2/
9. MusicRadar — Diablo GH Signature: https://www.musicradar.com/news/okko-unleashes-diablo-gh-gregor-hilden-signature-overdrive-pedal
10. One Thousand Pedals — TwinSonic MKII manufacturer-spec reference: https://onethousandpedals.com/pedal/okko-fx-twinsonic-mkii
11. Little Box Effects — The Dynamist: https://www.littleboxeffects.com/product/okko-the-dynamist-dual-fet-overdrive

## Research note
OKKO is a useful addition because its catalog demonstrates exactly why the archive's conservative classification matters. DIABLO DUAL is explicitly Overdrive + Distortion, Holy Grit is explicitly Overdrive + Fuzz, and Black Beast is an historically documented Fuzz + Distortion hybrid. By contrast, TwinSonic MKII and The Dynamist can reach more saturated territory but remain Overdrive because that is how the builder presents their core identities.

## Next
**Block 116**: continue the fresh Builder → Pedals census with another previously uncovered multi-pedal builder or builder group.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 116

## Builder
**Catalinbread Effects**

Catalinbread's official About page says the company began in 2003 on Nic Harris's kitchen table, moved to Portland, Oregon, in 2006, and currently manufactures more than 30 unique pedals by hand. The current catalog is much larger as a storefront because it also contains variants, accessories, B-stock, and non-dirt effects.

## Scope result
Catalinbread is a major multi-category dirt builder with a broad current and historical catalog. The official site explicitly separates a Foundation Overdrive Series from a Boost/Fuzz/Distortion Series, and individual product pages provide unusually clear functional language for several hybrid designs.

This block consolidates ordinary colorways, B-stock listings, anniversary graphics, and routine reissues where they represent the same underlying pedal lineage. Distinct named collaborations or materially different products are retained.

## Overdrive
- **Dirty Little Secret** — current
- **Dirty Little Secret Deluxe** — current
- **SFT** — current
- **RAH** — current
- **Sabbra Cadabra** — current
- **Formula No. 55 / Formula 55** — current/legacy lineage
- **Formula 5F6** — current
- **Formula 51** — current/reissue lineage
- **Galileo** — current
- **Galileo 4K** — current
- **Little Secret** — current
- **Knight School Overdrive** — current built pedal
- **SideArm Overdrive** — current
- **Silver Kiss Overdrive (2007 Reissue)** — current/reissue
- **Maxon 40th Anniversary Catalinbread Modified Overdrive (OD808-40C)** — current/exclusive collaboration
- **Misnomer (Cabinet Series)** — current/limited Cabinet Series, based on the Systech Overdrive

## Distortion
- **SFT** — current
- **Katzenkönig** — current
- **Carbide Distortion** — current
- **Crooner Preamp** — current

## Fuzz
- **SFT** — current
- **Katzenkönig** — current
- **Crooner Preamp** — current
- **Fuzzrite** — current
- **Fuzzrite Germanium** — current
- **Fuzzrite Mini** — current
- **Giygas 2K Fuzz** — current/reissue
- **Manx Loaghtan Fuzz** — current/reissue
- **Merkin Fuzz** — current/reissue
- **Karma Suture GE** — current/legacy catalog lineage
- **Karma Suture SI** — current/legacy catalog lineage
- **Octapussy** — historical/catalog lineage
- **Antichthon** — historical/retired with limited reissues
- **Perseus / Perseus DIO lineage** — current/historical octave-fuzz lineage

## Explicit category overlaps
- **SFT:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Catalinbread's current SFT description explicitly says the pedal moves from clean to slightly overdriven tones, into high-gain distortion, and can produce huge fuzz sounds. The company also describes the Stoner mode as an overdrive-meets-fuzz approach.
- **Katzenkönig:** Distortion + Fuzz. Catalinbread explicitly says it combines a Tone Bender MkII fuzz with a RAT-style distortion, producing a pedal that sings like fuzz while staying tight like distortion.
- **Crooner Preamp:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Catalinbread explicitly states that its gain range covers clean boost, overdrive, distortion, and full-throated fuzz. Clean boost remains outside scope.
- **Dirty Little Secret Deluxe:** Overdrive only. It includes a separate built-in boost, but the primary dirt section is the Marshall-style amp/preamp circuit. Boost is outside the target taxonomy.
- **Silver Kiss:** Overdrive only. Catalinbread explicitly describes medium-gain OD plus clean boost and EQ; the boost function is not a second dirt category.
- **Formula 51:** Overdrive only. The page describes everything from gritty cleans to heavy distortion, but the product is a Foundation Overdrive and no separate named distortion identity is established.
- **Manx Loaghtan:** Fuzz only. Catalinbread identifies it as a Big Muff-derived fuzz, even though the design can reach some low-gain OD sounds.
- **Giygas 2K:** Fuzz only. The builder explicitly calls it a fuzz; the lower end of its Fuzz control can act as gritty boost, but that does not create a second target dirt category.
- **Antichthon:** Fuzz only. Catalinbread describes it as an other-worldly fuzz tremolo. Tremolo remains outside scope.
- **Perseus DIO:** Fuzz lineage is retained only at the product-family level where Catalinbread describes the device as fuzz mixed with analog octave down. Its octave-down function is not a separate target dirt category.

## Historical/variant restraint
- **Dirty Little Secret** generations and ordinary edition/color variants are consolidated into the core Dirty Little Secret lineage unless Catalinbread gives them a materially distinct named product, such as **Dirty Little Secret Deluxe**.
- **Formula No. 55 / Formula 55** and **Formula 51 / Formula No. 5** reissue forms are treated as underlying pedal lineages rather than multiplied by artwork or storefront naming.
- **Galileo** and **Galileo 4K** are retained as distinct named generations because Catalinbread presents the 4K as a new chapter in the Galileo lineage with a materially expanded EQ section.
- **Fuzzrite**, **Fuzzrite Germanium**, and **Fuzzrite Mini** are retained separately because they are distinct named products with different transistor/packaging identities rather than mere cosmetic variants.
- **Karma Suture GE/SI** remain separate named versions because Catalinbread's own manuals distinguish the two builds; both remain within Fuzz rather than being promoted to Distortion merely because the circuit is aggressive.
- **SFT Blackout** and **SFT Fireworks** are graphics/collection editions of the SFT and are consolidated into SFT.
- **Katzenkönig Fireworks** is likewise consolidated into the base Katzenkönig.
- **Blood Donor** is not separately counted in this block because Catalinbread's own 2020 release notice describes it as a revamped and fine-tuned Katzenkönig rather than establishing a cleanly separate target dirt identity.
- **Catalinbread Bitters** is excluded even though it has a digital distortion section because its primary identity is a multi-effects processor with distortion, phaser, and digital sound-mangling functions.
- **Naga Viper, Epoch Boost, VariOboost, and similar boost-first products** are excluded because Boost is outside the archive's target taxonomy.
- **Perseus DIO** is not split into a separate distortion entry merely because its dry path can produce boosted distortion. Its primary product identity is the octave-down/fuzz design.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Catalinbread — About: https://catalinbread.com/pages/about

15. Catalinbread — Giygas 2K Fuzz: https://catalinbread.com/products/giygas-2k-fuzz

20. Catalinbread — Galileo 4K: https://catalinbread.com/products/galileo-4k

22. Catalinbread — Misnomer: https://catalinbread.com/products/misnomer-cabinet-series

24. Catalinbread — Blood Donor release notice: https://catalinbread.com/blogs/news/catalinbread-releases-blood-donor-for-charity

## Research note
Catalinbread is a particularly valuable builder for the archive because its current site openly separates Foundation Overdrive products from a broader dirt series and maintains both current and retired references. The strongest hybrid is SFT, where the builder itself documents the full progression from overdrive through distortion and into fuzz. Katzenkönig is a clear Fuzz + Distortion hybrid, and Crooner is an unusually explicit all-three dirt product.

The block intentionally does not turn every mention of saturation, clipping, or amplifier breakup into another category. Products remain in their builder-stated identities unless Catalinbread explicitly establishes a distinct target dirt function.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 117

## Builder
**JHS Pedals**

JHS Pedals is a previously uncovered builder with a broad dirt catalog spanning Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. The current official catalog separates Overdrive, Fuzz/Distortion, Preamp, and other effect families, while the official history preserves older product lineages and the Legends of Fuzz series.

## Scope result
JHS has one of the broadest dirt catalogs encountered in this fresh census. The archive records current products plus clearly documented historical/legacy lineages, while consolidating routine revisions, ordinary graphics, hand-painted editions, and other finish-level variants.

JHS's own product pages are used as the primary classification source. A pedal is not promoted into another dirt category merely because its gain control can reach a sound associated with that category. Where JHS explicitly names multiple target dirt identities, all of those identities are retained.

## Overdrive
- **AT Mini** — current
- **Notadümblo V2** — current
- **Morning Glory Clean** — current
- **424 Gain Stage** — current
- **Kilt 10** — current
- **Notaklön** — current
- **Morning Glory V4** — current
- **Bonsai** — current
- **Kilt V2** — current/legacy
- **AT+** — current
- **Angry Charlie V3** — current
- **The Violet** — current
- **3 Series Screamer** — current
- **Charlie Brown V4** — current
- **Moonshine V2** — current
- **PG-14** — current
- **3 Series Overdrive** — current
- **SuperBolt** — current/legacy lineage
- **Twin Twelve** — current/legacy lineage
- **Double Barrel** — current/legacy lineage
- **Charlie Brown** — historical lineage
- **Moonshine** — historical lineage
- **Morning Glory** — historical/current lineage
- **State Line** — historical limited
- **Steak and Eggs Overdrive/Compressor** — historical collaboration; Overdrive retained, compressor excluded
- **424 Gain Stage** — current multi-category preamp/drive product

## Distortion
- **Hard Drive** — current
- **3 Series Distortion** — current
- **The Violet** — current
- **424 Gain Stage** — current
- **Kilt V2 / Kilt 10** — current/legacy lineage
- **PackRat** — current
- **Colour Box V2 / Colour Box 10** — current lineage
- **Crayon** — current
- **All American Distortion** — historical / discontinued lineage
- **Cheese Ball** — current/historical lineage

## Fuzz
- **Kilt V2 / Kilt 10** — current/legacy lineage
- **Muffuletta** — current
- **3 Series Fuzz** — current
- **Colour Box V2 / Colour Box 10** — current lineage
- **Crayon** — current
- **Cheese Ball** — current/historical lineage
- **Coyote** — current, octave-fuzz product
- **EHX by JHS Big Muff 2** — current collaboration
- **Bender** — historical/current Legends of Fuzz lineage
- **Pollinator** — historical/current fuzz lineage
- **Smiley** — historical/current Legends of Fuzz lineage
- **Crimson** — historical/current Legends of Fuzz lineage
- **Firefly** — historical/current fuzz lineage
- **4 Wheeler** — historical/current fuzz lineage
- **Mini Foot Fuzz** — historical/current fuzz lineage
- **Bun Runner** — historical/current fuzz lineage
- **73 Rams Head** — historical/current fuzz lineage
- **Supreme** — historical/current fuzz lineage
- **Coyote** — current
- **Muffuletta** — current

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Kilt V2 / Kilt 10:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. JHS explicitly describes the Kilt as a dirt box covering overdrive, distortion, and fuzz, and the Kilt 10 page gives distinct settings that move between those three target identities.
- **424 Gain Stage:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. JHS's current page titles it Preamp / Distortion / Overdrive and explicitly describes smashed-out fuzz in its gain range.
- **PackRat:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. JHS describes the RAT family as spanning overdrive through distortion and all the way to fuzz, and the PackRat is built around multiple RAT evolutions.
- **Colour Box V2 / Colour Box 10:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. JHS explicitly titles the product Preamp / EQ / Overdrive / Distortion / Fuzz / DI Box.
- **Crayon:** Distortion + Fuzz. JHS explicitly titles the current product Preamp / Distortion / Fuzz and describes compressed overdrive and fuzz as the central sounds. The archive does not separately add Overdrive because JHS does not use it as a distinct category on the product page.
- **Cheese Ball:** Distortion + Fuzz. JHS explicitly titles it Distortion / Fuzz and describes both gated fuzz and distortion sounds.
- **3 Series Distortion:** Distortion only. JHS notes that it can reach heavy overdrive and fuzz, but the product's named identity is Distortion, so those operating ranges are not promoted to separate categories.
- **3 Series Fuzz:** Fuzz only. JHS explicitly calls it a fuzz while mentioning distortion and pushed-amp overdrive as possible sounds. Those are treated as behavior inside the fuzz design.
- **All American Distortion:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz historically. JHS explicitly describes the RAT-style pedal as spanning clean/grit, heavy overdrive, and compressed sustaining fuzz while naming it All American Distortion.
- **Steak and Eggs:** Overdrive within target scope. The product is an Overdrive/Compressor collaboration; the compressor half is outside the archive's dirt taxonomy.

## Historical/variant restraint
- **Morning Glory** revisions are consolidated into the Morning Glory lineage. JHS's history documents the family and rare higher-gain experiments without treating every small revision or paint variant as a separate pedal.
- **Kilt V1, Kilt V2, and Kilt 10** are consolidated as one Kilt lineage for archive identity purposes, while the current Kilt 10 and Kilt V2 are both retained as named products where the official catalog still distinguishes them.
- **PackRat White** is consolidated into the PackRat lineage rather than counted as a separate circuit identity.
- **Colour Box V2 and Colour Box 10** are consolidated as one Colour Box lineage unless later research establishes a materially different named circuit identity.
- **Muffuletta** modes are not split into multiple pedals. The product is one JHS pedal containing several analog Muff-family voices.
- **Bonsai** is retained as one product despite its multiple Tube Screamer-style modes.
- **Legends of Fuzz** products such as Bender, Smiley, Crimson, Firefly, and 4 Wheeler are retained as named fuzz products because JHS presents the series as a collection of distinct historic fuzz circuits.
- **Coyote** is retained as Fuzz only. Although it includes octave behavior, its primary identity is an octave fuzz, not a separate octave-up effect entry.
- **Double Dragon** is excluded from the dirt census because JHS presents it primarily as an analog octave device. Its octave-up section can produce distortion, but distortion is not promoted to a separate target identity.
- **DIY kits, buffers, utilities, modulation, delay, reverb, and other non-target products** remain outside scope.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. JHS Pedals — Collections: https://jhspedals.info/collections
2. JHS Pedals — All Products: https://jhspedals.info/collections/all-products
3. JHS Pedals — Overdrive collection: https://jhspedals.info/collections/overdrive-distortions
4. JHS Pedals — Fuzz / Distortion collection: https://jhspedals.info/collections/fuzz-distortion
5. JHS Pedals — History: https://jhspedals.info/pages/history
6. JHS Pedals — Kilt V2: https://jhspedals.info/products/kilt-v2
7. JHS Pedals — Kilt 10: https://jhspedals.info/collections/overdrive-distortions/products/kilt-10
8. JHS Pedals — 424 Gain Stage: https://jhspedals.info/products/424-gain-stage
9. JHS Pedals — PackRat: https://jhspedals.info/collections/all-products/products/packrat
10. JHS Pedals — Colour Box V2: https://jhspedals.info/products/colour-box-v2
11. JHS Pedals — Crayon: https://jhspedals.info/products/crayon
12. JHS Pedals — Hard Drive: https://jhspedals.info/products/hard-drive
13. JHS Pedals — 3 Series Overdrive: https://jhspedals.info/products/3-series-overdrive
14. JHS Pedals — 3 Series Distortion: https://jhspedals.info/products/3-series-distortion
15. JHS Pedals — 3 Series Fuzz: https://jhspedals.info/products/3-series-fuzz
16. JHS Pedals — Muffuletta: https://jhspedals.info/products/muffuletta
17. JHS Pedals — Cheese Ball: https://jhspedals.info/collections/all-products/products/cheese-ball
18. JHS Pedals — Coyote: https://jhspedals.info/collections/all-products/products/coyote
19. JHS Pedals — EHX by JHS Big Muff 2: https://jhspedals.info/products/big-muff-2
20. JHS Pedals — Bender: https://jhspedals.info/products/bender
21. JHS Pedals — Pollinator: https://jhspedals.info/products/pollinator
22. JHS Pedals — All American Distortion: https://jhspedals.info/products/all-american-distortion
23. JHS Pedals — Double Barrel V4: https://jhspedals.info/products/double-barrel-v4

## Research note
JHS is particularly valuable to the archive because it documents both original designs and historically oriented reproductions. The cleanest multi-category cases are Kilt, 424 Gain Stage, PackRat, Colour Box, and All American. The archive keeps those overlaps explicit while refusing to turn ordinary high-gain behavior into duplicate dirt categories.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 118

## Builder
**Analog Man**

Analog Man / Mike Piera is a previously uncovered builder with a focused but historically significant dirt catalog. The builder's own site documents handmade Sun Face, Astro Tone, Peppermint Fuzz, Sun Lion, King Of Tone, and Prince Of Tone products, with the King Of Tone and Prince Of Tone explicitly providing both overdrive and distortion modes.

## Scope result
Analog Man has a smaller dirt lineup than some recent blocks, but it clearly spans all three target categories. This block keeps the census conservative: named dirt products and explicitly documented modes are retained, while modifications to other companies' pedals, boost-only products, and routine transistor/finish options are not counted as separate Analog Man pedal identities.

Analog Man's own product pages and manuals are the primary classification sources. A product is not promoted into another category merely because it can become heavier or fuzzier at extreme settings. The archive uses the builder's explicit product identity or explicit named operating mode.

## Overdrive
- **King Of Tone** — current
- **Prince Of Tone** — current

## Distortion
- **King Of Tone** — current; explicit Distortion mode
- **Prince Of Tone** — current; explicit Distortion mode

## Fuzz
- **Sun Face** — current
- **Astro Tone** — current; Fuzz product
- **Peppermint Fuzz** — current/legacy; Fuzz product
- **Sun Lion** — current; Fuzz/Booster combination

## Explicit category overlaps
- **King Of Tone:** Overdrive + Distortion. Analog Man's current page and V4 manual explicitly provide separate OD and DISTORTION modes for each side of the pedal.
- **Prince Of Tone:** Overdrive + Distortion. Analog Man's manual explicitly lists Normal Overdrive and Distortion modes, with Boost as a third mode outside the target taxonomy.

## Historical / variant restraint
- **King Of Tone Ver. 2 / Ver. 3 / Ver. 4** are treated as one King Of Tone lineage rather than separate dirt identities. Analog Man documents the revisions as versions of the same pedal and retains the same core OD/DIST concept.
- **Prince Of Tone** remains one product despite its internal mode choices. Boost is outside the target taxonomy; OD and Distortion are retained because Analog Man explicitly names both.
- **Sun Face** transistor options such as germanium and silicon are not split into separate pedals. They are documented options within the Sun Face product family.
- **Peppermint Fuzz / Dark Peppermint Fuzz** are consolidated as one Peppermint Fuzz identity; the builder describes Dark as a related version using a different transistor source rather than a separate dirt design.
- **Sun Lion** is recorded as Fuzz because Analog Man explicitly describes it as the combination of Sun Face fuzz and Beano Boost. The Beano Boost side is outside the target taxonomy.
- **Astro Tone** remains Fuzz only. Analog Man describes it as a fuzz and says it sits somewhere between fuzz and distortion; that broader operating range is not enough to create a separate Distortion entry.
- **Sun Bender / early three-transistor Tonebender work** is not added as a separate current product because Analog Man's own Sun Lion history says those versions were stopped rather than presenting a clean, independently maintained named product listing.
- **Modified Boss, DOD, MXR, Ibanez, and other third-party pedals** are not counted as Analog Man-built product identities. Analog Man clearly performs modifications, but the archive's builder census is centered on pedals the builder makes or made as its own named products.
- **Beano Boost, Bad Bob, and other boost-first products** remain outside the dirt taxonomy.
- **Comprossor, Juicer, Chorus, delay, buffers, switchboxes, and other non-target products** remain outside scope.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Analog Man — Who Are We / history and product overview: https://www.analogman.com/whoarewe.htm
2. Analog Man — King Of Tone: https://www.analogman.com/kingtone.htm
3. Analog Man — King Of Tone V4 manual: https://www.analogman.com/manuals/KingofToneManualV4-2020.pdf
4. Analog Man — Prince Of Tone manual: https://www.analogman.com/manuals/Prince%20of%20Tone%20Manual0820.pdf
5. Analog Man — Sun Face / Fuzz Face page: https://www.analogman.com/fuzzface.htm
6. Analog Man — Sun Face manual: https://www.analogman.com/manuals/PDFs%20%28may%20be%20older%29/sunface.pdf
7. Analog Man — Astro Tone Fuzz: https://www.analogman.com/astrotone.htm
8. Analog Man — Peppermint Fuzz: https://www.analogman.com/pepper.htm
9. Analog Man — Sun Lion: https://www.analogman.com/sunlion.htm
10. Analog Man — Sun Lion manual: https://www.analogman.com/manuals/SunLionManual.pdf
11. Analog Man — King Of Tone waiting-list/status page: https://www.analogman.com/kotdelay.htm

## Research note
Analog Man is a useful multi-category builder for the archive because the dirt lineup crosses classic fuzz families and the King Of Tone / Prince Of Tone drive family. The cleanest overlap evidence comes directly from Analog Man's own manuals: OD and DISTORTION are named modes, while Sun Face, Astro Tone, Peppermint Fuzz, and Sun Lion are explicitly presented as fuzz products.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 119

## Builder
**Benson Amps**

Benson Amps is a previously uncovered builder with a pedal catalog that spans Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Benson's own site identifies Chris Benson as the builder and says its amps and pedals are completely handmade in Portland, Oregon. The current pedal catalog lists Portable Distortion 424 MKII, Germanium Boost, Preamp, Germanium Preamp, Delay, Störkn B0kš, Deep Sea Diver Fuzz-Echo, and Florist. citeturn226367search0turn806913search1

## Scope result
The archive records Benson's named dirt products and explicit dirt identities. The classification stays conservative: a pedal is not promoted into another target category just because it can get dirtier at extreme settings. Where Benson explicitly describes a product as overdrive/fuzz or distortion/fuzz, those target identities are retained.

## Overdrive
- **Preamp Pedal** — current; explicitly described as clean boost/overdrive/fuzz
- **Germanium Preamp Pedal** — current; explicitly described as overdrive/boost/fuzz
- **Germanium Fuzz** — current/legacy; authoritative Benson/Mixwave guide documents clean boost, overdrive, and fuzz

## Distortion
- **Portable Distortion 424 MKII** — current; Benson explicitly presents it as a distortion pedal

## Fuzz
- **Preamp Pedal** — current; explicitly described as clean boost/overdrive/fuzz
- **Germanium Preamp Pedal** — current; explicitly described as overdrive/boost/fuzz
- **Germanium Fuzz** — current/legacy
- **Stonk Box** — current/legacy; MK1-style fuzz product
- **Störkn B0kš** — current; Benson explicitly describes it as a heavily modified MK1 fuzz and says it produces crushing fuzz tones
- **Deep Sea Diver Fuzz-Echo** — current; explicitly described as a fuzz with echo

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Preamp Pedal:** Overdrive + Fuzz. Benson explicitly calls it a clean boost/overdrive/fuzz pedal. citeturn644100search2
- **Germanium Preamp Pedal:** Overdrive + Fuzz. Benson explicitly describes the result as overdrive/boost/fuzz. citeturn806913search4
- **Germanium Fuzz:** Overdrive + Fuzz. The authoritative Mixwave Benson Germanium Fuzz guide describes it as a clean boost, overdrive, or full fuzz design. The builder's warranty page also identifies the Germanium Fuzz as a Benson pedal. citeturn160195search2turn806913search5

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Germanium Fuzz** is retained as a named Benson fuzz identity even though it is absent from the current retail pedal grid. It is documented by Benson's own warranty material and by the official Mixwave representation updated in 2026. citeturn806913search1turn806913search5turn160195search2
- **Stonk Box** is retained as a named fuzz product. Benson's current warranty page still names the Stonk Box, while contemporary launch coverage identifies it as a Benson fuzz pedal. citeturn160195search1turn160195search3
- **Störkn B0kš** is counted separately from Stonk Box because Benson presents it as a named collaboration and an augmented version of Stonk Box with a distinct product identity, not merely a finish or ordinary revision. citeturn160195search0
- **Deep Sea Diver Fuzz-Echo** remains Fuzz only. Benson explicitly describes the dirt side as fuzz and the other half as echo; echo is outside the archive taxonomy. citeturn806913search3
- **Portable Distortion 424 MKII** remains Distortion only. Benson describes it as a portable distortion/preamp product, with clean boosting and other lower-gain behavior treated as operating range rather than separate target identities. citeturn644100search8
- **Germanium Boost** is outside scope as a boost-first product even though Benson notes that it can push an amplifier into distortion. citeturn806913search2
- **Florist, Delay, Surf's Up, amps, plugins, and other non-target products** remain outside the Builder → Pedals dirt census. citeturn806913search1turn226367search0
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Benson Amps — About / homepage: https://www.bensonamps.com/
2. Benson Amps — Guitar Pedals catalog: https://www.bensonamps.com/guitarpedals
3. Benson Amps — Preamp Pedal: https://www.bensonamps.com/guitarpedals/preamp
4. Benson Amps — Germanium Preamp Pedal: https://www.bensonamps.com/guitarpedals/germaniumpreamp
5. Benson Amps — Portable Distortion 424 MKII: https://www.bensonamps.com/guitarpedals/424-mkii-preamp
6. Benson Amps — Störkn B0kš: https://www.bensonamps.com/guitarpedals/strkn-b0k
7. Benson Amps — Deep Sea Diver Fuzz-Echo: https://www.bensonamps.com/guitarpedals/deep-sea-diver-fuzz-echo
8. Benson Amps — Pedal Warranty: https://www.bensonamps.com/pedalwarranty
9. Mixwave — Benson Germanium Fuzz User Guide, updated May 5, 2026: https://support.mixwave.com/help/benson-germanium-fuzz-user-guide
10. Reverb — Benson Stonk Box release coverage: https://reverb.com/news/video-benson-amps-releases-the-stonk-box

## Research note
Benson is useful to the archive because its current catalog is compact but unusually clear about its dirt language. The Preamp family explicitly crosses overdrive and fuzz, while the 424 MKII is the cleanly named distortion product. Germanium Fuzz and the Stonk family add distinct fuzz identities without turning every gain range into another category.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 120

## Builder
**Greer Amps**

Greer Amps is a previously uncovered builder with a large dirt-focused catalog. Greer's own site presents its pedal line around drives, distortions, fuzzes, and boosts, and the current pedal catalog includes a mix of overdrive, distortion, and fuzz products. Greer's own description of the company says its focus is distortion broadly understood to include overdrive and fuzz, so this block uses the archive's narrower target taxonomy and only assigns a product to multiple target categories when the product identity or authoritative documentation supports it.

## Scope result
Greer has one of the more useful historical/current catalogs for this census because its documented products range from low-gain overdrive through high-gain distortion and dedicated fuzz designs. Current products are recorded alongside clearly documented historical products, while routine graphics, ordinary revisions, and boost-first devices are not multiplied into separate dirt identities.

## Overdrive
- **Mori Drive** — current; explicitly described as a mid-focused overdrive. 
- **Lightspeed Organic Overdrive** — current; explicitly described as overdrive.
- **Southland Harmonic Overdrive** — current; explicitly described as a higher-gain overdrive.
- **Mizuki Blue** — current; explicitly described as a dual-JFET overdrive.
- **Tomahawk Deluxe Drive** — current; explicitly described as a medium-gain overdrive.
- **Black Mountain Crunch Drive** — current; Greer presents it as a drive product with substantial overdrive/crunch range.
- **Royal Velvet** — current; explicitly described as British drive/preamp.
- **SOMA 63 Vintage Preamp** — current; Greer explicitly says it can be used as an overdrive pedal.
- **Supa Cobra Overdrive** — current; explicitly described as overdrive.
- **Peacemaker** — current/small-batch; explicitly described as a drive-meets-distortion product.
- **390 Vintage OD/Preamp** — historical/limited; explicitly described by Greer as an overdrive/preamp.
- **Brown Bomber** — historical; authoritative cataloging describes it as overdrive.
- **Burning Goat** — historical/current lineage; explicitly described as medium overdrive.
- **Ghetto Stomp** — historical; documented as natural amp-breakup overdrive with higher-gain distortion/fuzz range.
- **No.9 Drive and Distortion** — historical; product title explicitly includes drive and distortion.
- **Relic Drive** — historical; explicitly described as overdrive.
- **Tone Smuggler** — historical; explicitly described as transparent overdrive.
- **Little Samson** — historical collaboration with Elliott Guitars; Greer describes chunky drive at lower gain before its higher-gain distortion range.
- **Fuzzy Wooly Bear** — historical; authoritative cataloging describes it as a fuzz/overdrive pedal.

## Distortion
- **Gorilla Warfare MKII** — current; Greer explicitly presents it as its RAT-style distortion pedal.
- **Peacemaker** — current/small-batch; explicit drive-meets-distortion identity.
- **Ghetto Stomp** — historical; authoritative documentation places it in the fuzz/distortion range and identifies rhythm-distortion use.
- **No.9 Drive and Distortion** — historical; explicit product identity includes distortion.
- **Sucker Punch Distortion** — historical/discontinued; explicitly presented as a high-gain distortion pedal.
- **Little Samson** — historical collaboration with Elliott Guitars; explicitly described as Greer's higher-gain distortion pedal.
- **Green Giant** — historical; explicitly described as a distortion unit.
- **Hammer** — historical/discontinued; explicitly cataloged as distortion.
- **Sweetback Driver** — historical; authoritative cataloging places it in distortion while also identifying its boost/preamp behavior.
- **Batch O' Ass** — historical; documented as distortion that borders on fuzz.

## Fuzz
- **Super Hornet** — current; explicitly described by Greer as a fuzz with octave-fuzz operation.
- **Black Fuzz** — historical/legacy; explicitly described as a fuzz product.
- **Razor Burn** — historical; explicitly described as a hybrid Fuzz Face variant.
- **SOUL THRUST FUZZ UNIT** — historical/custom shop; explicitly documented as a fuzz.
- **Fuzzy Wooly Bear** — historical; explicitly described as a fuzz/overdrive pedal.
- **Batch O' Ass** — historical; documented as distortion/fuzz with the product description explicitly saying it blurs the line between distortion and fuzz.
- **Ghetto Stomp** — historical; authoritative documentation explicitly says it covers the range of fuzz and distortion.
- **Tarpit** — historical; cataloged as an integrated-circuit fuzz machine.

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Peacemaker:** Overdrive + Distortion. Greer's current product description directly calls it a drive-meets-distortion design.
- **Ghetto Stomp:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Greer's historical product documentation describes natural amp-breakup overdrive, while authoritative cataloging explicitly places it in the fuzz/distortion range.
- **No.9 Drive and Distortion:** Overdrive + Distortion. The product's own name explicitly joins drive and distortion, and Greer's catalog places it in both drive and distortions/fuzzes collections.
- **Little Samson:** Overdrive + Distortion. Greer's documentation describes chunky drive at lower gain and identifies it as a higher-gain distortion pedal.
- **Fuzzy Wooly Bear:** Overdrive + Fuzz. Authoritative cataloging explicitly calls it a fuzz/overdrive pedal.
- **Batch O' Ass:** Distortion + Fuzz. Contemporary product documentation describes it as distortion bordering on fuzz.

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Greer's current catalog and older sitemap** are both used. Current products are not assumed to have existed in exactly their present form historically, and historical products are labeled as such where the evidence is clear.
- **Ghetto Stomp** is kept as one lineage rather than splitting the older hand-painted and later powder-coated versions; Greer's own 2014 release notice describes those as versions of the same pedal.
- **Super Hornet** remains one fuzz identity; its octave function is not promoted into a separate target category.
- **Fuzzy Wooly Bear** is one product identity despite germanium/silicon ordering options.
- **Sucker Punch, Hammer, Green Giant, Little Samson, and older Greer products** are retained as historical products where authoritative documentation identifies them as distinct named pedals.
- **Moonshot Germanium Pre, Sonic Boom, Sure Shot Clipping Boost, and Special Request** remain outside the dirt census as boost/preamp-first products even when they can push an amplifier into more saturation.
- **Third-party collaborations** are retained only where Greer is explicitly the pedal builder and the product has a distinct named identity, as with Little Samson / Elliott Guitars.
- **Routine finish changes, custom colors, and ordinary enclosure revisions** are not counted as separate product identities.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Greer Amps — Pedals: https://www.greeramps.com/collections/pedals
2. Greer Amps — About / Nick Greer: https://www.greeramps.com/pages/about-us
3. Greer Amps — Site Map / current and historical pedal collections: https://www.greeramps.com/pages/sitemap
4. Greer Amps — Mori Drive: https://www.greeramps.com/products/mori-drive
5. Greer Amps — Lightspeed Organic Overdrive: https://www.greeramps.com/products/lightspeed
6. Greer Amps — Southland Harmonic Overdrive: https://www.greeramps.com/products/southland-harmonic-overdrive
7. Greer Amps — Mizuki Blue: https://www.greeramps.com/products/mizuki-blue
8. Greer Amps — Tomahawk Deluxe Drive: https://www.greeramps.com/products/tomahawk-deluxe-drive
9. Greer Amps — Black Mountain Crunch Drive: https://www.greeramps.com/products/black-mountain-crunch-drive
10. Greer Amps — Royal Velvet: https://www.greeramps.com/products/royal-velvet
11. Greer Amps — SOMA 63 Vintage Preamp: https://www.greeramps.com/products/soma-63-vintage-preamp
12. Greer Amps — Supa Cobra Overdrive: https://www.greeramps.com/products/supa-cobra-overdrive
13. Greer Amps — Peacemaker: https://www.greeramps.com/collections/pedals/products/peacemaker
14. Greer Amps — Gorilla Warfare MKII: https://www.greeramps.com/products/gorilla-warfare-mkii

## Research note
Greer is especially useful to the archive because the builder's catalog makes the transition from overdrive to distortion to fuzz unusually visible. The cleanest hybrid cases are Ghetto Stomp, Peacemaker, Little Samson, Fuzzy Wooly Bear, Batch O' Ass, and No.9 Drive and Distortion. The archive still avoids turning every high-gain or boost-like operating range into another category.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 121

## Builder
**Suhr**

Suhr is a previously uncovered builder with a clearly divided pedal catalog covering Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Suhr's current pedal pages organize its dirt products into those categories and explicitly identify Eclipse as a dual-channel overdrive/distortion, Shiba Drive as overdrive, Riot as distortion, and Rufus/Thornicus as fuzz. citeturn571468search0turn571468search1turn571468search3turn571468search9

## Scope result
The archive records Suhr's named dirt products from the current and legacy catalog where the builder itself clearly identifies the product as Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz. The classification remains conservative: a distortion pedal that can push an amplifier into overdrive is not automatically added to Overdrive, and a fuzz pedal that reaches distorted tones is not automatically added to Distortion.

## Overdrive
- **Shiba Drive** — current; explicitly described as overdrive. citeturn571468search3
- **Shiba Drive Reloaded** — current/legacy lineage; explicitly described as an overdrive product. citeturn571468search3
- **Kiko Loureiro Signature Shiba Drive Reloaded** — current; signature Shiba Drive overdrive. citeturn571468search3
- **Eclipse** — current; dual-channel overdrive/distortion. citeturn571468search1turn571468search9

## Distortion
- **Riot** — current; explicitly described as a high-gain distortion pedal. citeturn571468search12
- **Riot Mini** — current; Riot-family distortion product. citeturn571468search0
- **Riot Reloaded** — legacy/discontinued; Suhr's catalog identifies the Reloaded version as the higher-gain development of the original Riot distortion circuit. citeturn571468search1turn571468search10
- **Eclipse** — current; dual-channel overdrive/distortion. citeturn571468search1turn571468search9

## Fuzz
- **Rufus Fuzz** — current/legacy lineage; explicitly listed by Suhr as a fuzz pedal. citeturn571468search0
- **Rufus Reloaded Fuzz** — legacy; Suhr's catalog lists Rufus Reloaded as the later version of the Rufus fuzz. citeturn571468search0turn571468search10
- **Thornicus Fuzz** — current; explicitly listed by Suhr as a fuzz pedal. citeturn571468search0

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Eclipse:** Overdrive + Distortion. Suhr explicitly describes the pedal as a dual-channel overdrive/distortion and says it covers low-gain overdrive through tighter high-gain tones. citeturn571468search1turn571468search9

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Riot / Riot Reloaded** are retained as the Riot lineage, with the original Riot and later Reloaded version distinguished only where Suhr's catalog clearly names the product. The archive does not multiply minor hardware revisions. citeturn571468search1turn571468search12
- **Rufus / Rufus Reloaded** are treated as one fuzz lineage with the named Reloaded version retained as a historical product rather than counting enclosure or finish variants separately. citeturn571468search0turn571468search10
- **Shiba Drive / Shiba Drive Reloaded / Kiko Loureiro Signature Shiba Drive Reloaded** are retained as named products because Suhr's catalog explicitly distinguishes those product names, but ordinary revision details are not expanded beyond the named lineage. citeturn571468search3
- **Riot** remains Distortion only even though Suhr says its output can push an amplifier into natural overdrive; that is treated as operating behavior rather than a separate target identity. citeturn571468search12turn571468search24
- **Rufus** and **Thornicus** remain Fuzz only. Suhr's category placement is sufficient for the archive, without promoting heavier operating ranges to Distortion. citeturn571468search0
- **Brighton Treble Boost, Dual Boost, Koko Boost, compression, modulation, delay, buffers, and tone tools** remain outside the dirt census. citeturn571468search0turn571468search14
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Suhr — Pedals: https://www.suhr.com/electronics/pedals/
2. Suhr — Electronics / Pedal categories: https://www.suhr.com/electronics/
3. Suhr — Distortion pedals: https://www.suhr.com/electronics/pedals/distortion/
4. Suhr — Overdrive pedals: https://www.suhr.com/electronics/pedals/overdrive/
5. Suhr — Pedal product category / legacy listings: https://www.suhr.com/product-category/pedals/
6. Suhr — Eclipse: https://www.suhr.com/product/eclipse/
7. Suhr — Riot: https://www.suhr.com/product/riot/
8. Suhr — Riot User Guide: https://www.suhr.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/09/Riot-pedal-FINAL-1.pdf

## Research note
Suhr is useful to the archive because its current catalog makes the dirt taxonomy unusually explicit. Eclipse is the key overlap, while Shiba Drive, Riot, and the Rufus/Thornicus lineages give the archive clean examples of dedicated Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz identities without forcing cross-category inflation.
