# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 122

## Builder
**Crazy Tube Circuits**

Crazy Tube Circuits is a previously uncovered builder with a broad dirt catalog spanning Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Its official pedal catalog currently groups the dirt range together, while individual product pages explicitly identify the relevant effect identities. The current catalog also includes time-based, modulation, and boost products that remain outside this dirt census.

## Scope result
This block records Crazy Tube Circuits products that the builder itself clearly identifies as Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz, plus historical products where authoritative documentation establishes a distinct dirt identity. The taxonomy stays conservative: a high-gain pedal is not automatically promoted into every neighboring category unless the builder explicitly describes that category or a named operating range reaches it in authoritative product documentation.

## Overdrive
- **Stardust V3** — current; explicitly described as an overdrive.
- **Crossfire** — current; includes an explicitly recreated classic overdrive section.
- **Space Charged V2** — current/legacy; tube overdrive/distortion product, so both target identities are retained.
- **Falcon** — current; explicitly designed to recreate overdriven amplifier sounds.
- **Ziggy V2** — current; explicitly described as dual overdrive/distortion.
- **Unobtanium** — current; dual overdrive product with two D-style amplifier overdrive voices.
- **Unobtanium RAW** — limited/current lineage; same dual overdrive identity as Unobtanium with altered clipping implementation.
- **Hi-Power** — current; pedal + amp-in-a-box overdrive, with the British boost side including an Overdriver high-gain setting.
- **Venus** — current; explicitly described as a tube overdrive with a range into distortion.
- **Heatseeker** — current; dual drive / amp-style product with explicit driven amp-gain function; retained as Overdrive.
- **Starlight** — historical/discontinued; builder/designer describes the gain progression as overdrive into distortion and then fuzz.
- **Triptychon** — current; its separate drive section is described as an amplifier-foundation drive rather than a typical overdrive, so it is not promoted to Overdrive.

## Distortion
- **Space Charged V2** — current/legacy; explicitly documented as overdrive/distortion.
- **Ziggy V2** — current; explicitly described as dual overdrive/distortion.
- **Motherload** — current; R side runs from overdrive through distortion.
- **Venus** — current; explicitly described as a tube overdrive with high-gain distortion in its range.
- **Starlight** — historical/discontinued; designer describes its gain progression from overdrive through distortion to fuzz.
- **Limelight** — historical/current lineage; builder describes it as a fuzz/distortion unit.

## Fuzz
- **Orama** — current; dedicated fuzz section paired with an amp-style drive section.
- **Motherload** — current; dedicated Muff-style fuzz side paired with RAT-style distortion.
- **Limelight** — historical/current lineage; explicitly described as a fuzz/distortion unit.
- **Constellation** — historical/limited; multi-mode Germanium fuzz/booster product.
- **Starlight** — historical/discontinued; builder/designer describes the gain progression all the way into fuzz.
- **Triptychon** — current; dedicated four-voice fuzz engine plus upper-octave fuzz mode.

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Space Charged V2:** Overdrive + Distortion. Contemporary authoritative cataloging describes it as a tube overdrive/distortion pedal and its original announcement says it runs from clean boost to classic-rock distortion.
- **Ziggy V2:** Overdrive + Distortion. Crazy Tube Circuits explicitly calls it a dual overdrive/distortion with separate low- and high-gain channels.
- **Motherload:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. The R circuit runs from a touch of overdrive to roaring distortion, while the M circuit is a dedicated fuzz circuit.
- **Venus:** Overdrive + Distortion. Crazy Tube Circuits explicitly presents it as a tube overdrive that reaches high-gain nasty distortion. The archive does not add Fuzz merely because the builder says the top end is within the realm of fuzz.
- **Starlight:** Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. In a builder interview, Christos Ntaifotis explicitly describes the pedal as starting as overdrive, moving into distortion, and then reaching fuzz.
- **Limelight:** Distortion + Fuzz. Crazy Tube Circuits explicitly calls it a fuzz/distortion unit and describes the Beam control as moving from gated fuzz into fuzz-distortion.
- **Orama:** Fuzz + amplifier drive. The fuzz section is an explicit target identity; the paired amp section is treated as amplifier drive rather than promoted to Overdrive because the builder distinguishes it from a typical overdrive.
- **Triptychon:** Fuzz plus a separate drive/boost/octave system. The dedicated fuzz identity is retained; the drive section is not promoted to Overdrive because the builder explicitly says it is not a typical overdrive.

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Stardust V2 / Stardust V3** are treated as one Stardust lineage, with the current V3 retained as the named current product. The archive does not multiply the family by routine revisions.
- **Space Charged / Space Charged V2** are treated as one named lineage, while the V2 remains identifiable as the current/legacy named version.
- **Unobtanium / Unobtanium RAW** are retained as a named standard and limited RAW lineage because Crazy Tube Circuits explicitly distinguishes the RAW edition as a separate limited product with altered clipping and voicing.
- **Constellation OC41 / OC45 / CV7003** are consolidated into the Constellation fuzz lineage rather than counted as separate circuit identities. The builder presents them as limited Germanium editions of the same multi-mode product.
- **Limelight** is retained as one fuzz/distortion identity despite its boost setting; clean boost does not become a separate target entry.
- **Motherload** remains one pedal with two dirt circuits. Its internal M and R voices are not split into separate product identities.
- **Orama** remains one product with an amp-style drive section and a distinct fuzz section. Only Fuzz is promoted from the fuzz side; the amp side remains amplifier drive.
- **Triptychon** remains one product despite its four fuzz voices and upper-octave fuzz options. Those are modes inside one named product, not separate pedals.
- **Killer V, Memphis, Cyclone, TI:ME, Sidekick, White Whale, Mirage, Mirage JR, Splash, and other modulation/time-based products** remain outside the dirt census.
- **Super Conductor** remains outside scope as a boost-first product.
- **Crossfire** is retained for its explicit overdrive side; its blackface amp side is treated as amplifier emulation rather than a second target category.
- **Unobtanium** and **Hi-Power** are not promoted to Distortion merely because their high-gain settings become more aggressive; their documented primary identity is overdrive.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Crazy Tube Circuits — Pedals: https://crazytubecircuits.com/pedals
2. Crazy Tube Circuits — Overdrive / Distortion / Fuzz store category: https://crazytubecircuits.com/store/overdrive-distortion-fuzz
3. Crazy Tube Circuits — Orama: https://crazytubecircuits.com/orama
4. Crazy Tube Circuits — Motherload: https://crazytubecircuits.com/motherload
5. Crazy Tube Circuits — Motherload Concept: https://crazytubecircuits.com/motherload-concept
6. Crazy Tube Circuits — Venus: https://crazytubecircuits.com/venus
7. Crazy Tube Circuits — Stardust V3: https://crazytubecircuits.com/stardust-v3
8. Crazy Tube Circuits — Crossfire: https://crazytubecircuits.com/crossfire
9. Crazy Tube Circuits — Ziggy V2: https://crazytubecircuits.com/ziggy-v2
10. Crazy Tube Circuits — Hi-Power: https://crazytubecircuits.com/hi-power
11. Crazy Tube Circuits — Unobtanium: https://crazytubecircuits.com/unobtanium
12. Crazy Tube Circuits — Unobtanium RAW: https://crazytubecircuits.com/unobtanium-raw
13. Crazy Tube Circuits — Triptychon: https://crazytubecircuits.com/triptychon
14. Crazy Tube Circuits — Space Charged V2 store listing: https://crazytubecircuits.com/store/space-chargedv2
15. Effects Database — Space Charged: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/crazytube/spacecharged
16. Crazy Tube Circuits — Limelight official launch/demo: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Mm_HGJlgIls
17. Premier Guitar — Crazy Tube Circuits Announces the Limelight: https://www.premierguitar.com/crazy-tube-circuits-announces-the-limelight
18. Crazy Tube Circuits — Constellation official demo: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=p7c02o-nMbY
19. Crazy Tube Circuits — Triptychon official/current page: https://crazytubecircuits.com/triptychon
20. Guitar World — Christos Ntaifotis interview / Starlight gain progression: https://www.guitarworld.com/gear/guitar-pedals/crazy-tube-circuits-christos-ntaifotis
21. MusicRadar — Starlight review: https://www.musicradar.com/reviews/guitars/crazy-tube-circuits-starlight-575828

## Research note
Crazy Tube Circuits is especially useful to the archive because the builder has repeatedly built pedals around interacting gain stages rather than rigid single-effect boxes. Starlight provides the clearest explicit all-three dirt progression; Motherload provides a distinct Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz architecture; and the newer Orama and Triptychon designs show the builder separating dedicated fuzz voices from amplifier-style drive.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 123

## Builder
**Beetronics FX**

Beetronics FX is a previously uncovered pedal builder with a clearly documented Overdrive + Fuzz dirt catalog. Its current site separates Overdrive and Fuzz product families, while individual pages explicitly identify the relevant dirt functions. The current catalog also contains octave, harmonizer, modulation, and delay products that remain outside the target dirt taxonomy.

## Scope result
This block records named Beetronics dirt products that the builder itself explicitly identifies as Overdrive or Fuzz. No separate Distortion category is established because the current Beetronics material reviewed here does not present a distinct named distortion product or an explicit distortion identity strong enough to meet the archive's conservative threshold. Generic mentions of "distortion" as a possible sound are not promoted into a new category.

## Overdrive
- **Fatbee** — current; explicitly described as a JFET overdrive.
- **Overhive** — current; explicitly described as a mid-gain overdrive.
- **Wannabee / Beelateral Buzz** — current; explicitly described as a dual overdrive inspired by Bluesbreaker and Klon circuits.
- **Royal Jelly** — current; explicitly described as an overdrive/fuzz blender.
- **Nectar Tone Sweetener** — current; explicitly described as an overdrive/fuzz pedal with separate overdrive and fuzz modes.

## Distortion
- **None established** — no distinct Beetronics product reviewed for this block meets the archive's explicit Distortion threshold.

## Fuzz
- **Octahive V2** — current; explicitly described as high-octave fuzz.
- **Abelha Tropical Fuzz** — current; explicitly described as a psychedelic fuzz with three fuzz modes.
- **Vezzpa Octave Stinger** — current; explicitly described as a dual-mode fuzz pedal.
- **Swarm Fuzz Harmonizer** — current; explicitly described as an analog fuzz harmonizer and regular fuzz when harmonies are reduced.
- **Royal Jelly** — current; explicit overdrive/fuzz blender.
- **Nectar Tone Sweetener** — current; explicit overdrive/fuzz pedal with dedicated fuzz mode.
- **Tuna Fuzz** — current/pre-order; explicitly described as a fuzz and a 3-transistor fuzz product.

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Royal Jelly:** Overdrive + Fuzz. Beetronics explicitly describes it as a blend of overdrive and fuzz, with two overdrive/fuzz blend presets.
- **Nectar Tone Sweetener:** Overdrive + Fuzz. Beetronics explicitly gives it two distinct drive textures, smooth overdrive and saturated fuzz, selectable by toggle.

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Octahive V2** remains one fuzz identity despite BUZZZ and OCTAVE modes; the octave function does not become a separate category.
- **Vezzpa** remains one fuzz identity despite its two fuzz modes, including the high-octave STINGER mode.
- **Swarm** remains one fuzz identity despite its harmonizer intervals and modulation controls. The builder explicitly describes it as a fuzz harmonizer, and its harmonic processing is not treated as a separate dirt category.
- **Abelha** remains one fuzz identity despite three fuzz voices and Tropical Mode; those are modes and tone-shaping features inside one named fuzz product.
- **Wannabee** remains Overdrive only. Beetronics explicitly presents the two sides as overdrive circuits; the resulting saturation is not promoted into Distortion.
- **Fatbee** and **Overhive** remain Overdrive only despite generic references to saturation.
- **Royal Jelly** limited editions and custom-shop units are consolidated into the Royal Jelly product identity rather than multiplied by finish or artwork.
- **Nectar** custom-shop units are consolidated into one Nectar product identity.
- **Tuna Fuzz** remains Fuzz only. Its unusual tuna-can enclosure is not a separate product identity.
- **Pollinator Hazee Delay, Seabee, BeeBeeDee, Larva, Zzombee, Nectar-adjacent tone tools, and other modulation/time-based products** remain outside the dirt census.
- **Generic references to distortion** in Beetronics descriptions are treated as descriptive sound language, not proof of a separate Distortion category.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Beetronics — All Pedals: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/collections/all-pedals
2. Beetronics — Pedals: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/collections/all-pedals-1
3. Beetronics — Manuals: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/pages/manuals
4. Beetronics — Fatbee Overdrive: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/fatbee-overdrive
5. Beetronics — Overhive Mid-Gain Overdrive: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/overhive-standard-series
6. Beetronics — Wannabee / Beelateral Buzz: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/wannabee-beelateral-buzz-custom-shop-wb0034
7. Beetronics — Royal Jelly: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/royal-jelly-limited-edition-p-royal-series
8. Beetronics — Royal Jelly Custom Shop: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/royal-jelly-od-fuzz-p-custom-shop-p-rj2845
9. Beetronics — Nectar Tone Sweetener: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/nectar-tone-sweetener-p-babee-series
10. Beetronics — Octahive V2: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/octahive-v2-high-octave-fuzz-p-babee-series
11. Beetronics — Abelha Tropical Fuzz: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/abelha-tropical-fuzz
12. Beetronics — Vezzpa Octave Stinger: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/vezzpa-octave-stinger-babee-series
13. Beetronics — Swarm Fuzz Harmonizer: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/swarm
14. Beetronics — Tuna Fuzz: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/products/tuna-fuzz
15. Beetronics — FAQ: https://www.beetronicsfx.com/pages/faq

## Research note
Beetronics is useful to the archive because its dirt line is unusually explicit about the Overdrive/Fuzz boundary. Royal Jelly and Nectar are clear hybrid products, while Fatbee, Overhive, and Wannabee are dedicated overdrives and Octahive, Abelha, Vezzpa, Swarm, and Tuna are dedicated fuzz identities. The archive deliberately leaves Distortion empty rather than promoting generic saturation language into a new category.

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 124

## Builder
**Mad Professor Amplification**

Mad Professor is a previously uncovered builder with a substantial documented catalog of Overdrive products, plus dedicated Distortion and Fuzz products. The official archive explicitly covers current and discontinued pedals, making it useful for the historical builder census. citeturn921210view0turn412572search5

## Scope result
This block records named Mad Professor pedals whose product identity or authoritative documentation clearly establishes Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz. The taxonomy remains conservative: descriptions that merely say a pedal can become "distortion-like" or "fuzz-like" do not automatically create extra category entries when the builder's named identity remains Overdrive. Amp-in-a-box and underdrive products are not promoted into the dirt categories unless the relevant target identity is explicit.

## Overdrive
- **Albert's Overdrive** — historical/discontinued; explicitly named and described as overdrive. Higher Drive can become fuzz-like, but the product identity remains Overdrive.
- **Amber Overdrive** — historical/discontinued; explicitly named Overdrive. The builder describes it as highly saturated with a hint of fuzz, but does not establish a separate Fuzz identity.
- **Big Tweedy Drive** — historical/discontinued; tweed-amp style drive pedal with an explicit gain/drive control.
- **Blueberry Bass Overdrive** — historical/discontinued; explicitly light-to-medium overdrive for bass and other instruments.
- **Bluebird Overdrive** — historical/discontinued; compact overdrive + delay pedal. Higher settings can produce medium/heavier distortion, but the product identity remains Overdrive.
- **Golden Cello** — historical/discontinued; explicitly delivers a huge singing overdrive tone with built-in tape-echo style delay.
- **The Green Wonder** — current; explicitly controls overdrive from low gain/boost through rock overdrive.
- **Little Green Wonder** — current/handwired lineage; explicit overdrive identity.
- **Little Tweedy Drive** — historical/discontinued; tweed-style overdrive pedal with adjustable drive.
- **Loud 'n Proud** — historical/discontinued; vintage Marshall amp-in-a-box with a medium-gain overdrive circuit and added fuzz section.
- **Royal Blue Overdrive** — current/handwired lineage; explicit Overdrive identity with a range into distortion.
- **Simble / Simble Overdrive Mk2** — original historical lineage plus current Mk2; both explicitly identified as transparent/organic Overdrive.
- **Sky Blue Overdrive** — historical/handwired lineage; explicitly identified as high-quality Overdrive.
- **Supreme** — historical/limited/current-return lineage; dual-channel Overdrive, with two independent drive sides that can be stacked.
- **Sweet Honey Overdrive** — current/handwired lineage; explicit low-gain touch-sensitive Overdrive.
- **Sweet Honey Overdrive Deluxe** — historical/discontinued; explicit medium-gain amplifier-like Overdrive.
- **Twimble** — historical/discontinued; combination of Simble Overdrive and Simble Predrive, retaining an explicit Overdrive section.

## Distortion
- **Mighty Red Distortion** — historical/discontinued; explicitly high-gain Distortion.
- **Stone Grey Distortion** — historical/discontinued; explicitly high-gain Distortion for heavy applications.

## Fuzz
- **Fire Red Fuzz** — historical/discontinued; explicitly named Fuzz.
- **Loud 'n Proud** — historical/discontinued; the dedicated added fuzz circuit is explicitly described as vintage germanium-style Fuzz alongside the medium-gain overdrive circuit.

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Loud 'n Proud:** Overdrive + Fuzz. Mad Professor explicitly describes the core as a medium-gain overdrive and the added circuit as a vintage germanium fuzz.

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Simble / Simble Overdrive Mk2** remain one named Simble Overdrive lineage, with the current Mk2 retained as the present named version rather than multiplying the product by routine revision.
- **Sweet Honey Overdrive / Handwired / Deluxe** are treated as the named Sweet Honey Overdrive family and Deluxe variant, while ordinary handwired-versus-factory construction is not multiplied into separate dirt identities.
- **Royal Blue Overdrive / Handwired** is one Royal Blue Overdrive lineage. The current handwired version remains a named lineage product, not a new effect category.
- **Little Green Wonder / Handwired** remains one Little Green Wonder lineage.
- **Fire Red Fuzz / Fire Red Fuzz HW** remains one Fire Red Fuzz lineage.
- **Amber Overdrive** remains Overdrive only despite the builder's "hint of fuzz" and description of a highly saturated tone bordering on fuzz.
- **Albert's Overdrive** remains Overdrive only even though higher Drive settings are described as becoming fuzz-like.
- **Bluebird Overdrive** remains Overdrive only even though the product documentation says higher drive levels produce distortion. The product is explicitly named and described as an overdrive section paired with delay.
- **Blueberry Bass Overdrive** remains Overdrive only even though the Drive control produces increasing distortion at higher settings. Its documented primary identity is light-to-medium overdrive.
- **Royal Blue Overdrive** remains Overdrive only despite its range into distortion; Mad Professor explicitly presents it as an Overdrive product.
- **Super Black** is excluded from the target categories because its primary identity is an amp-in-a-box recreating the Blackface amp signal path. Its integrated Sweet Honey section is an explicitly separate overdrive circuit, but the archive does not turn an amp-sim product into a standalone OD entry.
- **Evolution Orange Underdrive** is excluded because its stated purpose is to reduce or reshape an already overdriven signal rather than generate a named Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz identity.
- **Simble Predrive** is excluded as a predrive/boost utility rather than a target dirt category.
- **Ruby Red Booster** and other boost-first, modulation, delay, reverb, compressor, wah, and time-based products remain outside the dirt census.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Mad Professor — official home/current catalog: https://mpamp.com/
2. Mad Professor — official archive of current and discontinued pedals: https://mpamp.com/category/16/archive
3. Mad Professor — official manuals index: https://mpamp.com/page/6/manuals

## Research note
Mad Professor is a useful archive entry because the official catalog spans a large number of named Overdrives across both current and discontinued generations, while also containing clear dedicated Distortion and Fuzz products. The strongest hybrid is Loud 'n Proud, where the builder explicitly combines a medium-gain Marshall-style overdrive circuit with a dedicated germanium fuzz circuit. The archive does not inflate saturated overdrive products such as Amber or Albert's into separate Fuzz entries, and it keeps Super Black outside the target taxonomy as an amp-in-a-box product with a separate integrated Sweet Honey overdrive section. citeturn412572search3turn348354view0turn602644search13turn985518search2

# The Dirt Archive — Builder Census Block 125

## Builder
**Barber Electronics**

Barber Electronics is a previously uncovered builder with a large historical/current catalog centered on Overdrive, plus clear Distortion and Fuzz identities. Barber's current site emphasizes hand-built pedals and currently lists the Gain Changer, Direct Drive, Midnight Standard, Exacta and other products; historical catalog references document the broader lineage. citeturn266783search6turn266783search0turn266783search7

## Scope result
This block records named Barber pedals whose product identity or authoritative historical documentation clearly establishes Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz. Routine revisions, enclosure changes, and limited variants are consolidated into product families where they represent the same named design. Custom one-off B-Custom combinations are not counted as separate dirt identities.

## Overdrive
- **Midnight Standard** — current; explicitly described as a Bluesbreaker-style Overdrive.
- **Gain Changer / Gain Changer SR** — current lineage; explicitly described as an overdrive with a low-gain overdrive side and a higher-gain unLimiTeD-derived setting.
- **Compact Direct Drive / Direct Drive lineage** — current/historical; Barber explicitly presents the Direct Drive as a broad British-voiced overdrive family, with LG and SS variants consolidated into the lineage.
- **LTD / Silver LTD lineage** — historical/discontinued; flat-response overdrive family, with the Gain Changer taking over the lineage.
- **1/2 Gainer** — historical/discontinued; dual-channel version of the LTD overdrive.
- **unLimiTeD / unLTD 2-Channel Overdrive** — historical/discontinued; higher-gain dual-channel overdrive lineage.
- **Burn Unit / Burn Unit 2 / Burn Unit EQ / Compact Burn Unit** — historical/current lineage; overdrive family with a documented range from edge-of-breakup through more saturated drive.
- **Small Fry Burn Unit** — historical/discontinued; explicitly cataloged as an overdrive, with a range from warm boost through searing drive.
- **Tone Pump / Tone Pump EQ / Tone Pump II** — historical lineage; dual-channel overdrive family.
- **Deep Fryer** — historical/limited; bass-oriented overdrive with broad drive range.
- **B-Custom Cool** — historical/custom-shop; explicitly described as a low-gain overdrive.
- **B-Custom Super Sport** — historical/custom-shop; explicitly described as a medium/high-gain British-flavored overdrive.
- **B-Custom Dual Discrete** — historical/custom-shop; custom dual-overdrive format built from selected Barber overdrive circuits.
- **FWW BUSS** — current; explicitly described as a dual-tone overdrive using Burn Unit and Direct Drive Super Sport-derived voices.
- **FWW BUSS HG** — current; explicitly described as a higher-gain dual-tone overdrive.
- **FWW Plus** — current; explicitly described as an overdrive/boost-oriented pedal, with standalone overdrive as a primary documented use.

## Distortion
- **Dirty Bomb** — historical/discontinued; explicitly high-gain Distortion.
- **Burn Unit lineage** — historical/current; authoritative historical cataloging places the Burn Unit family in both Distortion and Overdrive because it deliberately spans the boundary.
- **Gain Changer lineage** — current; Barber explicitly describes the higher-gain switch position as its unLimiTeD distortion voice, so Distortion is retained alongside Overdrive.

## Fuzz
- **Exacta** — current; explicitly described as a vintage Fuzz combining Triangle and Jumbo Tonebender-style voices.
- **Trifecta** — historical/discontinued; explicitly described as a three-voice Fuzz pedal.

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Burn Unit family:** Overdrive + Distortion. Historical Barber documentation says the design blurs the lines between overdrive and distortion; the archive therefore retains both target identities for the lineage.
- **Gain Changer family:** Overdrive + Distortion. Barber explicitly identifies the low-gain side as overdrive and the higher-gain setting as an unLimiTeD distortion voice.

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Direct Drive, Direct Drive LG, Direct Drive SS, Compact Direct Drive** are consolidated into the Direct Drive overdrive lineage rather than counted as separate effect identities. The LG and SS versions are explicitly described as Direct Drive variants. citeturn959229search7turn959229search4turn803976search4
- **LTD and Silver LTD** are consolidated into one LTD overdrive lineage, with the later Gain Changer treated as the successor rather than a second copy of the same product family. citeturn305156search0turn305156search4
- **Burn Unit, Burn Unit 2, Burn Unit EQ, Small Fry, and Compact Burn Unit** are retained as the Burn Unit family rather than multiplying each revision into a new category. Barber's historical catalog consistently identifies the family as overdrive-oriented while some references explicitly place it in distortion as well. citeturn208101search2turn208101search4turn343954search8
- **Tone Pump / Tone Pump EQ / Tone Pump II** are consolidated as one dual-overdrive lineage. Historical cataloging identifies the original Tone Pump and Tone Pump EQ as overdrive products. citeturn343954search0turn343954search1
- **B-Custom Dual Discrete** is retained only as a custom dual-overdrive format, not as a unique circuit family, because Barber described it as a customer-selected combination of existing Barber overdrive pedals. citeturn343954search3
- **Exacta and Trifecta** remain Fuzz only. Their multiple classic-fuzz voices do not become separate products or additional dirt categories. citeturn803976search1turn820190search0
- **Dirty Bomb** remains Distortion only because Barber's documented identity is explicitly high-gain distortion. citeturn820190search9turn820190youtube88
- **Deep Fryer** remains Overdrive only. Historical Barber cataloging describes it as an overdrive for bass, even though some users reported it could reach heavier distortion. citeturn208101search0turn266783search13
- **FWW BUSS / BUSS HG** remain Overdrive entries; their descriptions emphasize dual-tone overdrive voices and higher-gain overdrive rather than establishing a distinct named distortion identity. citeturn803976search0turn803976search2
- **FWW Plus** remains an Overdrive entry because Barber explicitly presents standalone overdrive as a primary function alongside clean boost and tone conditioning. citeturn266783search2
- **Barb EQ, Linden EQ, Launch Pad, Tone Press, and other non-dirt Barber products** remain outside the census.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Barber Electronics — Home/current catalog: https://www.barberelectronics.com/
2. Barber Electronics — Gain Changer: https://www.barberelectronics.com/gainchanger
3. Barber Electronics — Direct Drive: https://www.barberelectronics.com/directdrive
4. Barber Electronics — Midnight Standard: https://www.barberelectronics.com/midnightstandard
5. Barber Electronics — Exacta: https://www.barberelectronics.com/exacta
6. Barber Electronics — FWW BUSS: https://www.barberelectronics.com/fwwbuss
7. Barber Electronics — FWW BUSS HG: https://www.barberelectronics.com/fwwbusshg
8. Barber Electronics — FWW Plus: https://www.barberelectronics.com/fwwplus
9. Effects Database — Barber Electronics catalog: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber
10. Effects Database — Burn Unit: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/burnunit
11. Effects Database — Gain Changer: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/gainchanger
12. Effects Database — Dirty Bomb: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/dirtybomb
13. Effects Database — Deep Fryer: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/deepfryer
14. Effects Database — 1/2 Gainer: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/halfgainer
15. Effects Database — unLTD 2-Channel Overdrive: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/unltd
16. Effects Database — Trifecta Fuzz: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/trifecta
17. Effects Database — Tone Pump: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/tonepump
18. Effects Database — Tone Pump EQ: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/tonepumpeq
19. Effects Database — B-Custom Cool: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/bcustom/cool
20. Effects Database — B-Custom Super Sport: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/bcustom/supersport
21. Effects Database — B-Custom Dual Discrete: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/barber/bcustom/dualdiscrete

## Research note
Barber Electronics is a useful archive entry because its catalog shows a consistent progression from transparent low-gain overdrive through British-voiced drive and into explicitly named high-gain distortion, while Exacta and Trifecta provide dedicated fuzz identities. The archive deliberately does not treat every high-gain setting as a new effect category; instead it follows Barber's own named product identities and only retains Distortion where the documentation explicitly establishes that identity.

# The Dirt Archive - Builder Census Block 126

## Builder
**Friedman Amplification**

Friedman Amplification is a previously uncovered builder with a clearly documented current Overdrive catalog plus historical high-voltage tube dirt pedals. The current official pedal shop lists BE-OD, BE-OD Deluxe, Dirty Shirley, Golden Pearl, and Smallbox as pedal products, while Friedman materials also document the discontinued Motor City Drive and Fuzz Fiend. citeturn824366search4turn824366search1turn824366search5turn824366search3turn824366search6

## Scope result
This block records named Friedman pedals whose builder-facing identity clearly establishes Overdrive or Fuzz. No separate Distortion category is established in this block because Friedman's own product pages consistently present the BE-OD family and related high-gain pedals as Overdrive products, even though third-party descriptions sometimes call them distortion. The archive follows the builder's named identity rather than generic descriptions of available gain.

## Overdrive
- **BE-OD** - current; explicitly described by Friedman as an Overdrive pedal based on the BE-100 amplifier.
- **BE-OD Deluxe** - current; dual-channel Overdrive built from the original BE-OD circuit with a lower-gain second channel.
- **Dirty Shirley Pedal** - current; explicitly named and described by Friedman as an Overdrive pedal.
- **Golden Pearl** - current; explicitly described as a transparent, lower-gain Overdrive.
- **Smallbox Overdrive Pedal** - current; explicitly named and described by Friedman as an Overdrive pedal.
- **Motor City Drive** - historical/discontinued; documented as a tube-driven Overdrive pedal using a high-voltage 12AX7A preamp tube.

## Distortion
- **None established** - no distinct Friedman pedal reviewed for this block meets the archive's explicit Distortion threshold under the conservative builder-label rule.

## Fuzz
- **Fuzz Fiend** - historical/discontinued; Friedman's official demo explicitly describes it as a 12AX7 tube fuzz pedal with a range from light fuzz to extreme saturated mayhem.

## Explicit category overlaps
- **None established** - the reviewed Friedman dirt products are kept in their documented primary target categories rather than assigning additional categories from generic high-gain language.

## Historical / variant restraint
- **BE-OD / BE-OD Deluxe** remain Overdrive entries. Friedman's official descriptions call both products Overdrive and emphasize British overdrive tones, even where higher settings become highly saturated. citeturn824366search1turn824366search5
- **Dirty Shirley** remains Overdrive only. Friedman explicitly calls it the Dirty Shirley Overdrive pedal and describes its range from cleaner pushing tones through gritty drive. citeturn824366search3
- **Golden Pearl** remains Overdrive only. Its documented role is transparent lower-gain overdrive, even though its tri-mode clipping switch changes the feel and clipping response. citeturn824366search6
- **Smallbox Overdrive** remains Overdrive only. Friedman's product page explicitly calls it an Overdrive pedal and describes a range from gritty Plexi-style tones through high gain. citeturn824366search2
- **Motor City Drive** remains Overdrive only. Historical documentation identifies it as a tube-driven overdrive from light breakup through higher-gain drive; generic references to saturation and distortion do not create a second target category. citeturn288471search2turn288471search3
- **Fuzz Fiend** remains Fuzz only. Friedman's official demo explicitly identifies it as a tube fuzz pedal and describes the RAGE switch as extending the fuzz into unstable, extreme behavior. citeturn288471youtube37
- **Sir Compre** is excluded even though its title and secondary descriptions can mention overdrive, because its primary product identity is a compressor rather than a standalone dirt pedal.
- **Buxom Boost** is excluded as a boost-first product.
- **Friedman IR-D, IR-J, and IR-X** are excluded as amp/preamp/IR products rather than standalone target dirt pedals.
- **Gold-72 Wah** is excluded as a wah product.
- Routine editions, finish changes, and standard packaging differences are not multiplied into separate dirt identities.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Friedman Amplification - Pedals: https://friedmanamplification.com/shop/c/pedals/
2. Friedman Amplification - Downloads / Pedals: https://friedmanamplification.com/downloads/
3. Friedman Amplification - BE-OD: https://friedmanamplification.com/shop/pedals/be-od/
4. Friedman Amplification - BE-OD instructional manual: https://friedmanamplification.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/BE-OD_INSTRUCTIONAL_MANUAL.pdf
5. Friedman Amplification - BE-OD Deluxe: https://friedmanamplification.com/shop/pedals/be-od-deluxe/
6. Friedman Amplification - Dirty Shirley Pedal: https://friedmanamplification.com/shop/pedals/dirty-shirley-pedal/
7. Friedman Amplification - Golden Pearl: https://friedmanamplification.com/shop/pedals/golden-pearl/
8. Friedman Amplification - Smallbox Overdrive Pedal: https://friedmanamplification.com/shop/pedals/smallbox-overdrive-pedal/
9. Friedman Amplification - official Fuzz Fiend demo: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8Jd6cvmuTes
10. Effects Database - Motor City Drive: https://www.effectsdatabase.com/model/friedman/motorcitydrive
11. Sweetwater - Motor City Drive official demo: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fKx1kTuqeUU
12. Gearnews - Motor City Drive and Fuzz Fiend historical release: https://www.gearnews.com/musikmesse-2017-friedman-motor-city-drive-fuzz-fiend-12ax7-based-pedals/

## Research note
Friedman is useful to the archive because its pedal range shows a consistent amp-derived approach to dirt while maintaining unusually explicit Overdrive naming. The BE-OD family, Dirty Shirley, Golden Pearl, Smallbox, and Motor City Drive all remain Overdrive entries under the builder-label rule. Fuzz Fiend is the clear dedicated Fuzz identity. The block intentionally leaves Distortion empty rather than converting third-party use of the word distortion into a Friedman product category.

# The Dirt Archive - Builder Census Block 127

## Builder
**Amptweaker**

Amptweaker is a previously uncovered builder with a dirt catalog spanning Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. The official current catalog lists Tight Drive, Tight Rock, Tight Metal, Big Rock Pro II, and Fat Metal Pro II; historical Amptweaker documentation establishes the broader TightDrive, TightMetal, TightRock, BigRock, FatRock, TightFuzz, and BluesFuzz families. citeturn717005search0turn717005search1turn388482search6

## Scope result
This block records named Amptweaker pedals whose documented identity clearly establishes Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz. The taxonomy follows the builder's own product naming and authoritative historical descriptions. Gain ranges are not used to multiply categories when the named product identity stays in one family.

## Overdrive
- **TightDrive / TightDrive Pro / TightDrive Jr / Bass TightDrive / Bass TightDrive Jr** - current/historical TightDrive lineage; the original and current documentation explicitly identify TightDrive as Overdrive. citeturn804911search0turn804911search5turn804911search4
- **BigRock Pro / BigRock Pro II** - historical/current BigRock lineage; authoritative material describes it as Rock overdrive/distortion and explicitly documents the combined role. citeturn770909youtube67turn770909search6

## Distortion
- **TightMetal / TightMetal Pro / TightMetal Pro II / TightMetal Jr / Bass TightMetal Jr / TightMetal ST / limited TightMetal editions** - TightMetal distortion lineage. The original product and current model use explicit distortion language, while later Pro and Jr versions retain the same named distortion family. citeturn717005search4turn804911search1turn804911search66
- **TightRock / TightRock Jr / Performance Series Tight Rock** - explicit distortion lineage. The original manual calls TightRock a distortion pedal, with lower-gain use able to overdrive an amp but the primary product identity remaining Distortion. citeturn717005search5turn117972search7turn717005search8
- **BigRock Pro / BigRock Pro II** - explicit Rock overdrive/distortion lineage. citeturn770909youtube67turn770909search6
- **FatRock Distortion** - historical; explicitly documented as a distortion variant within the TightRock family. citeturn770909search0
- **TightMetal Pro / TightMetal Pro II** - retained within the TightMetal Distortion lineage rather than treated as separate categories because their extra boost/preamp functions do not change the core dirt identity. citeturn717005search11turn804911search8
- **FatMetal / FatMetal Pro / FatMetal Pro II** - historical/current FatMetal distortion lineage, explicitly documented as distortion for heavy and low-end-focused metal tones. citeturn717005search9turn770909search9turn117972search1

## Fuzz
- **TightFuzz / TightFuzz Pro / TightFuzz Jr / Bass TightFuzz / Bass TightFuzz Jr / Tim Lefebvre Signature TightFuzz** - TightFuzz family; the original and historical documentation explicitly identify it as Fuzz, while later variants retain the same named fuzz identity. citeturn717005search2turn150472search0turn717005search10
- **BluesFuzz / BluesFuzz Limited / BluesFuzz Jr / Bass BluesFuzz Jr** - BluesFuzz family; Amptweaker documentation and official demo material identify these as fuzz products derived from the low-gain Germanium BluesFuzz design. citeturn770909youtube66turn770909search7turn770909search10

## Explicit category overlaps
- **BigRock Pro / BigRock Pro II:** Overdrive + Distortion. Amptweaker's own historical manual identifies it as Rock overdrive/distortion, and the original product presentation describes it as a distortion box spanning driven rock tones. citeturn770909search6turn770909youtube67

## Historical / variant restraint
- **TightDrive family** remains Overdrive only. Amptweaker explicitly calls TightDrive an Overdrive pedal even though its gain control reaches heavy overdrive/distortion territory. Generic gain descriptions do not create a separate Distortion identity. citeturn804911search0turn717005search3
- **Bass TightDrive** remains part of the TightDrive Overdrive lineage despite the ability to produce thicker distortion at higher settings; the product is explicitly identified as an overdrive. citeturn717005search6turn804911search4
- **TightRock** remains Distortion only because its named product identity and manual explicitly call it a distortion pedal, even though lower-gain settings can overdrive an amp. citeturn717005search5turn117972search7
- **TightMetal** remains Distortion only. Its original product description explicitly identifies it as a distortion pedal, and the current model retains the same distortion circuitry. citeturn717005search4turn804911search1
- **FatMetal** remains Distortion only even though a review notes a broad range from medium overdrive to saturated distortion. Amptweaker's product family is explicitly a distortion design. citeturn717005search9turn770909search8
- **BigRock Pro / BigRock Pro II** is the clear hybrid because authoritative Amptweaker material explicitly uses both Overdrive and Distortion identities. citeturn770909youtube67turn770909search6
- **TightFuzz family** remains Fuzz only. Lower-gain, cleaner, or boost-like settings are modes inside a named fuzz product and do not create separate Overdrive entries. citeturn150472search0turn717005search2
- **BluesFuzz family** remains Fuzz only. Its boost, mid EQ, and lower-gain behavior are supporting controls inside the fuzz design. citeturn770909youtube66turn770909search7
- **TightMetal Jr Misery Index, TightMetal Pro SCOUR, and special finish/limited versions** are consolidated into the TightMetal distortion lineage rather than multiplied into separate effect identities. citeturn717005search1
- **TightRock Jr and Performance Series Tight Rock** are consolidated into the TightRock distortion lineage. citeturn717005search1turn717005search8
- **Bass TightMetal Jr and Bass TightRock Jr** remain members of their respective distortion families; Amptweaker's own bass-series announcement describes them as the bass versions of TightMetal and TightRock. citeturn804911youtube72
- **TightFuzz Jr, Bass TightFuzz, Bass TightFuzz Jr, and Tim Lefebvre Signature TightFuzz** are retained as TightFuzz family variants, not separate core dirt identities. citeturn717005search1turn717005search10turn150472search0
- **BluesFuzz Limited** was a 49-piece special model and **BluesFuzz Jr** was derived from it; these remain one named BluesFuzz family. citeturn770909youtube66
- **DepthFinder, DeFizzerator, Curveball, PressuRizer, TightBoost, and SwirlPool** remain outside the dirt census because they are EQ, compressor/boost, boost, or modulation products. citeturn717005search1
- **Tight Metal Pro II, Big Rock Pro II, and Fat Metal Pro II** are also preamp/recording-capable products, but their dirt engines are explicitly documented as distortion-oriented and therefore remain in the Distortion/Hybrid entries above. citeturn388482search4turn117972search4turn117972search1
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Research note
Amptweaker's catalog is especially useful because James Brown's Tight-series naming makes the builder's intended dirt boundaries unusually explicit. TightDrive is the core Overdrive family, TightMetal and TightRock are named Distortion families, BigRock is the clearest documented Overdrive + Distortion hybrid, and TightFuzz and BluesFuzz are dedicated Fuzz families. The archive does not promote generic references to lower gain, higher saturation, or distortion-like behavior into additional categories when the named product identity remains clear.

**Block 128**: continue the fresh Builder -> Pedals census with another previously uncovered builder or builder group.

# The Dirt Archive - Builder Census Block 128

## Builder
**Wampler Pedals**

Wampler Pedals is a previously uncovered builder with a substantial documented dirt catalog spanning Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. Wampler's current catalog separates Distortion / Overdrive and Fuzz, and its official downloads page preserves a dedicated list of discontinued products. The census below follows Wampler's own product/category language and consolidates ordinary revisions into product lineages where appropriate. citeturn4search3turn5search0

## Scope result
This block records named Wampler dirt pedals whose official catalog, product pages, manuals/downloads, or Wampler's own historical documentation clearly establish Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz identities. Gain behavior alone is not used to create extra categories. Hybrid entries are retained only where Wampler explicitly presents the pedal as containing multiple dirt types or the official site categorizes it accordingly.

## Overdrive
- **Tumnus / Tumnus Deluxe / Germanium Tumnus / Germanium Tumnus Deluxe** - Tumnus overdrive lineage; current Wampler catalog places these in the Overdrive family. citeturn5search0turn4search7
- **Pantheon / Pantheon Deluxe** - Overdrive lineage; Wampler explicitly identifies Pantheon as an Overdrive. citeturn2search5turn4search7
- **Belle Overdrive** - current Overdrive. Wampler calls it a classic low-gain overdrive and places it in Distortion / Overdrive while its product page identifies it specifically as Overdrive. citeturn2search8
- **Moxie** - current Overdrive; listed in Wampler's current Distortion / Overdrive catalog. citeturn5search0turn4search3
- **Triumph Overdrive** - current Overdrive. citeturn0search11
- **Plexi Drive / Plexi Drive Deluxe / Plexi Drive Mini** - Plexi Drive overdrive lineage. Wampler's official historical/current documentation retains the family as a drive series. citeturn5search0turn5search1
- **Brad Paisley: Paisley Drive / Paisley Deluxe** - Paisley overdrive lineage. Wampler explicitly describes Paisley Deluxe as providing two overdrive channels and the current product catalog includes both products. citeturn2search9turn3view1
- **Brent Mason: Hot Wired v2** - Hot Wired overdrive lineage; Wampler's own catalog places it among current dirt pedals and historical documentation identifies Hot Wired as a drive pedal. citeturn3view1turn5search1
- **Tom Quayle: Dual Fusion** - dual-overdrive lineage. Wampler's downloads and historical documentation identify Dual Fusion as a drive product. citeturn5search0turn5search1
- **Thirty Something** - historical Overdrive/drive product. Wampler's official historical documentation lists it among its 9–18V dirt pedals. citeturn5search1
- **Black '65 / Tweed '57** - historical amp-style overdrive/drive lineage. Wampler's official documentation lists both among its dirt pedals. citeturn5search0turn5search1
- **Clarksdale** - historical Overdrive. Wampler explicitly calls it the Clarksdale Overdrive and the official page categorizes it as Overdrive. citeturn2search3
- **cataPulp** - historical drive/overdrive product. Wampler's own historical power list identifies it among the dirt pedals. citeturn5search1
- **Low Blow** - historical low-gain/drive product; retained as Overdrive based on Wampler's historical dirt documentation rather than promoted to Distortion solely from available gain. citeturn5search1
- **UnderDog** - historical Overdrive; Wampler's current downloads page preserves it in the discontinued catalog and the official historical documentation identifies it as a drive product. citeturn5search0turn5search1
- **Cranked OD / Cranked AC and OD** - historical overdrive lineage. Wampler's own documentation explicitly lists Cranked OD and Cranked AC among its discontinued dirt products. citeturn5search0turn5search1
- **Super Plex / SuperPlex** - historical Plexi-style drive lineage. Wampler explicitly groups it with the Plexi-Drive/Plextortion family in its FAQ and historical documentation. citeturn5search2turn5search1
- **Plextortion** - historical high-gain drive/distortion lineage. Wampler's official FAQ treats it as part of the Plexi/Pinnacle dirt family, while its historical product list retains it as a distinct discontinued pedal. citeturn5search2turn5search0

## Distortion
- **Pinnacle / Pinnacle Deluxe / Pinnacle Deluxe v2** - Pinnacle distortion lineage. Wampler's official discontinued Pinnacle page explicitly calls the Standard a distortion pedal, and the current catalog retains Pinnacle Deluxe v2. citeturn2search1turn3view0
- **Sovereign Distortion** - historical Distortion. Wampler explicitly identifies it as a distortion pedal. citeturn2search0
- **Dracarys Distortion** - current Distortion. Wampler's official downloads and current catalog identify it as Distortion. citeturn5search0turn6view0
- **Phenom Distortion** - current Distortion. Wampler explicitly calls it a dynamic distortion pedal. citeturn0search9
- **Triple Wreck** - historical high-gain Distortion lineage. Wampler's own historical documentation includes it among the dirt pedals and distinguishes it from the fuzz-specific products. citeturn5search1
- **SLOstortion** - historical Distortion. Wampler's official historical documentation lists it among its dirt pedals. citeturn5search1
- **cataPulp** - retained as Overdrive/Distortion only if the product is explicitly documented as a hybrid; otherwise its primary Wampler lineage remains drive. This block does not promote it to a second category without stronger builder labeling. citeturn5search1
- **The Doctor** - historical distortion/drive product retained as a documented dirt identity; Wampler's discontinued catalog confirms the named pedal. citeturn5search0
- **Leviathan Fuzz** is not placed here despite its extreme gain because Wampler explicitly identifies it as Fuzz. citeturn5search0

## Fuzz
- **Velvet Fuzz** - current Fuzz. Wampler's official Fuzz catalog lists it as Fuzz, and Wampler's documentation explicitly identifies its fuzz circuit. citeturn4search6turn5search1
- **Cryptid Fuzz** - current Fuzz. Wampler's current catalog and downloads page identify it as Fuzz. citeturn4search6turn5search0
- **Ratsbane** - hybrid Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Wampler's official product page says it ranges from light overdrive through full saturated fuzz, and the site categorizes it under both Distortion / Overdrive and Fuzz. citeturn4search0turn4search6
- **Fuzztration** - historical Fuzz. Wampler explicitly describes it as a fuzz pedal and the official discontinued page identifies it as such. citeturn2search7
- **Leviathan Fuzz** - historical Fuzz. Wampler's official discontinued catalog lists it under the named fuzz products. citeturn5search0

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Ratsbane = Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz.** Wampler explicitly describes its usable range from light overdrive to saturated fuzz, calls it a distortion pedal, and places the product in both the Distortion / Overdrive and Fuzz categories. citeturn4search0turn4search6
- **Golden Jubilee = Overdrive + Distortion.** The current Wampler product page explicitly states that it has independent Distortion and Overdrive channels. citeturn4search2turn4search23
- **Brent Mason: ReWired = Overdrive + Distortion.** Wampler explicitly describes two independent channels, one Overdrive and one Distortion. citeturn0search12
- **Andy Wood: Gearbox = Overdrive + Distortion.** Wampler explicitly identifies Channel 1 as a tweaked Tumnus and Channel 2 as a modified Pinnacle, and the product is categorized as Combined (OD & Dist). citeturn4search5
- **The Fearbox = Overdrive + Distortion.** Wampler's official page describes Channel 1 as Tumnus-based overdrive and Channel 2 as Pinnacle-based distortion. citeturn2search2
- **Mofetta = Overdrive + Distortion.** Wampler's official product page explicitly calls it an Overdrive / Distortion pedal and describes the range from smooth overdrive to fiery distortion. citeturn0search7
- **tcd / The Compulsion Drive = Overdrive + Distortion.** Wampler categorizes it under Distortion / Overdrive and describes a range from edge-of-breakup crunch to full distortion. citeturn4search1
- **Paisley Deluxe = Overdrive + Distortion only if using Wampler's current catalog tags.** The product page's primary description emphasizes two overdrive channels, while the current catalog tags include distortion and overdrive. For the conservative builder-label rule, this block records it primarily as Overdrive and does not create a separate Distortion identity solely from the tag. citeturn2search9

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Tumnus family** is consolidated into one lineage across Tumnus, Deluxe, and Germanium versions rather than multiplying routine revisions. citeturn5search0
- **Pinnacle family** is consolidated across Standard, Deluxe, Deluxe v1, and Deluxe v2. The named family remains Distortion. citeturn2search1turn5search2
- **Plexi Drive family** is consolidated across Plexi Drive, Deluxe, and Mini. The Wampler FAQ explicitly discusses the Plexi-Drive family alongside Plextortion, SuperPlex, and Pinnacle as related dirt products. citeturn5search2
- **Paisley Drive / Paisley Deluxe** remain one product lineage with the Deluxe retained as a two-channel evolution rather than a separate unrelated design. citeturn2search9turn3view1
- **Hot Wired v1/v2** remains one Hot Wired lineage. Wampler's historical documentation explicitly names both versions. citeturn5search1
- **Fuzztration** remains Fuzz despite its ability to produce fuzzy distortion-like tones. Wampler explicitly identifies the product as fuzz. citeturn2search7
- **Leviathan Fuzz** remains Fuzz despite its high-gain range. The official discontinued catalog names it as Fuzz. citeturn5search0
- **Ratsbane** is the unusual case where the builder's current taxonomy and product description support all three target categories, so it is retained as a three-way overlap rather than forcing it into one box. citeturn4search0turn4search6
- **Golden Jubilee, Gearbox, The Fearbox, Brent Mason: ReWired, and Mofetta** retain their documented multi-channel/hybrid identities because Wampler explicitly describes multiple dirt types. citeturn4search2turn4search5turn2search2turn0search12turn0search7
- **Boost, compression, modulation, delay/reverb, EQ, and utility products** remain outside the dirt census even when they can push an amp into breakup. Wampler's catalog separates these product types. citeturn5search0
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Wampler Pedals - Distortion / Overdrive catalog: https://www.wamplerpedals.com/products/c/distortion-overdrive/
2. Wampler Pedals - Fuzz catalog: https://www.wamplerpedals.com/products/c/fuzz/

11. Wampler Pedals - The Compulsion Drive: https://www.wamplerpedals.com/products/distortion-overdrive/tcd/
12. Wampler Pedals - Phenom Distortion: https://www.wamplerpedals.com/products/distortion-overdrive/phenom/

20. Wampler Pedals - FAQ: https://www.wamplerpedals.com/faqs/

## Research note
Wampler's current site makes this builder unusually easy to classify because it maintains explicit Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz categories while preserving a substantial discontinued catalog. The conservative result is therefore to keep Wampler's named product identities intact, consolidate ordinary revisions, and only create cross-category entries where Wampler itself documents multiple dirt types. The clearest three-way case is Ratsbane; the clearest two-way cases are Golden Jubilee, Brent Mason: ReWired, Gearbox, The Fearbox, Mofetta, and tcd. citeturn4search0turn4search2turn0search12turn4search5turn2search2turn0search7turn4search1

# The Dirt Archive - Builder Census Block 129

## Builder
**EarthQuaker Devices (EQD)**

EarthQuaker Devices is a previously uncovered builder with a substantial documented dirt catalog spanning Overdrive, Distortion, and Fuzz. EQD's current site explicitly separates Distortion & Overdrive from Fuzz, while its Legacy catalog preserves a large historical catalog of discontinued dirt pedals. The census below follows EQD's own product/category language and consolidates ordinary reissues and variants into lineages where appropriate. citeturn0search0turn0search6turn0search1

## Scope result
This block records named EQD dirt pedals whose official current catalog, Legacy catalog, or official product/manual documentation clearly establishes Overdrive, Distortion, or Fuzz identities. Gain behavior alone is not used to create extra categories. Hybrid entries are retained where EQD explicitly describes the product as spanning multiple target dirt types.

## Overdrive
- **Blumes** - current Overdrive; EQD identifies it as a low-signal shredder and lists it in Distortion & Overdrive. citeturn0search0
- **Plumes** - current Overdrive; EQD identifies it as a small-signal shredder in the Distortion & Overdrive catalog. citeturn0search0
- **Special Cranker** - current Overdrive; EQD explicitly calls it an overdrive. citeturn0search0turn0search1

- **Monarch** - historical Overdrive. citeturn0search6
- **Palisades** - historical Overdrive; EQD's official manual explicitly identifies it as an overdrive even though its clipping voices can become more distorted or fuzzy. citeturn0search6turn0search20
- **Speaker Cranker** - historical Overdrive. citeturn0search6
- **Talons** - historical High Gain Overdrive. citeturn0search6

## Distortion

- **Sunn O))) HalfLife** - current Octave Distortion + Booster. The target dirt identity is Distortion; the boost function is outside the census. citeturn0search0turn0search2
- **Sunn O))) Life Pedal** - Octave Distortion + Booster; retained as Distortion with the boost component outside scope. citeturn0search0

The builder's current taxonomy supports retaining it within the Distortion/Overdrive family; this block uses Distortion as the primary identity based on the current catalog placement and product naming. citeturn0search0turn0search9

## Fuzz
- **Barrows** - current Fuzz Attacker. citeturn0search1turn0search2

- **Black Ash** - historical Endangered Fuzz; EQD's product page explicitly describes it as a fuzz/overdrive, so it is retained as a Fuzz + Overdrive hybrid. citeturn0search8turn0search6
- **Cloven Hoof** - historical Fuzz Grinder. citeturn0search6
- **Dirt Transmitter** - historical Fuzz Driver. citeturn0search6
- **Erupter** - historical Ultimate Fuzz Tone. citeturn0search6
- **Fuzz Master General** - historical Octave Fuzz Blaster. citeturn0search6
- **Hoof Reaper** - historical Double Fuzz with Octave Up. citeturn0search6
- **Rancho de la Luna Dirt Transmitter** - historical Fuzz Driver lineage. citeturn0search6
- **Sound Shank** - historical Germanium Fuzz. citeturn0search6
- **Spires** - historical Fuzz Doubler. citeturn0search6turn0search11
- **Terminal** - historical Destructive Fuzz Device. citeturn0search6
- **Tone Reaper** - historical Fuzz; EQD's Legacy catalog system retains the product in its historical catalog, and EQD's regional legacy catalog explicitly identifies it as Fuzz. citeturn0search11

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Gary = Fuzz + Overdrive.** EQD's current product taxonomy explicitly describes Gary as both Automatic Pulse Width Modulation Fuzz and Dynamic Natural Overdrive. citeturn0search0turn0search13
- **Black Ash = Fuzz + Overdrive.** EQD explicitly calls Black Ash a fuzz/overdrive and identifies the fuzz control as the gain control. citeturn0search8
- **Palisades remains Overdrive only.** Its official manual discusses increasingly distorted and fuzzy clipping voices, but the builder explicitly identifies the pedal as an Overdrive. Generic tonal behavior does not create additional categories. citeturn0search20turn0search6
- **Sunn O))) Life Pedal / HalfLife = Distortion**, with their boost portions excluded because boost is outside the target census. citeturn0search0turn0search6

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Sunn O))) Life Pedal V2** is consolidated with the Life Pedal lineage rather than treated as an unrelated dirt identity. citeturn0search6
- **Dirt Transmitter / Rancho de la Luna Dirt Transmitter** are retained as one Dirt Transmitter fuzz lineage for census purposes rather than multiplying the collaboration/edition into separate core dirt identities. citeturn0search6
- **Bellows / Bellows JR** are treated as one Fuzz Driver lineage. EQD's current Devices/New Devices pages identify Bellows JR as a Fuzz Driver while the Legacy catalog preserves Bellows as a discontinued Fuzz Driver. citeturn0search1turn0search2turn0search6
- **White Light / White Light Limited Edition** remain one Overdrive lineage. citeturn0search6turn0search11
- **Special Cranker Limited Edition** remains within the Special Cranker Overdrive lineage. citeturn0search11
- **Hoof Reaper** remains one combined fuzz product even though it contains two fuzz voices and octave-up functionality. citeturn0search6
- **Life Pedal and HalfLife** retain Distortion as the target dirt category; their boost sections are not separately counted. citeturn0search0turn0search2
- **Bit Commander, Tentacle, Data Corrupter, Rainbow Machine, and other octave/pitch products** remain outside the dirt census unless EQD explicitly identifies the product itself as Fuzz, Overdrive, or Distortion. citeturn0search4
- **Tone Job, Arrows, Bows, ZE​QD-Pre, and other boost/preamp/EQ products** remain outside scope when they are not themselves documented as target dirt pedals. citeturn0search5
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Research note
EQD's catalog is unusually useful for the builder census because the current site explicitly separates Distortion & Overdrive and Fuzz, while the Legacy catalog preserves the historical product identities. The conservative classification therefore follows EQD's own named categories instead of inferring new categories from clipping behavior. The clearest hybrids are Gary and Black Ash; Palisades remains Overdrive despite its fuzzier clipping voices, while Life Pedal and HalfLife remain Distortion with their boost functions excluded.

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Kilt V2 = Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz.** JHS explicitly labels it all three and describes its gain range from light drive through gated fuzz/distortion. citeturn5search8
- **PackRat = Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz.** JHS describes the RAT family represented by the pedal as ranging from overdrive through distortion to fuzz. citeturn7search4
- **Violet = Overdrive + Distortion.** JHS describes it as a distortion/overdrive design capable of tones from overdrive through modern distortion. citeturn7search1
- **Cheese Ball = Distortion + Fuzz.** JHS explicitly labels the pedal Distortion / Fuzz. citeturn7search0
- **Muffuletta = Fuzz + Distortion.** JHS places it in the Fuzz / Distortion collection; the product is the builder's multi-mode Big Muff family pedal. citeturn5search0
- **Colour Box V2 = Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz.** JHS explicitly lists all three dirt identities in the product heading. citeturn4search13

## Historical / variant restraint
- Routine version changes are consolidated into lineages where the underlying JHS product identity remains the same.
- **Morning Glory V4** is not treated as a separate core dirt design from Morning Glory.
- **Kilt V1 / V2** remain one Kilt lineage, with the current V2 carrying the explicit three-way dirt classification.
- **Notaklön** special color/splatter editions remain one Notaklön lineage rather than separate pedal identities.
- **PackRat White** remains part of the PackRat lineage rather than a separate dirt design.
- **Colour Box V1 / V2 / 10** are not multiplied here. Current Colour Box V2 is the verified three-way target-dirt entry; historical variants can be reconciled in a later historical pass if needed.
- JHS DIY kits, JHS Mods applied to other manufacturers' pedals, hand-painted versions, and non-dirt utility/modulation/time-based products are excluded from the builder-to-pedal dirt census.
- **Double Dragon** is excluded from the target dirt categories because JHS identifies it primarily as a Lo-Fi Octave Device; its upper octave can create distortion, but that does not make the product itself a standalone Distortion/Fuzz identity under the conservative builder-label rule. citeturn7search3
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. JHS Pedals - Overdrive: https://jhspedals.info/collections/overdrive-distortions
2. JHS Pedals - Fuzz / Distortion: https://jhspedals.info/collections/fuzz-distortion
3. JHS Pedals - All Products: https://jhspedals.info/collections/all-products
4. JHS Pedals - Kilt V2: https://jhspedals.info/products/kilt-v2
5. JHS Pedals - PackRat: https://jhspedals.info/products/packrat
6. JHS Pedals - Violet: https://jhspedals.info/products/the-violet
7. JHS Pedals - Hard Drive: https://jhspedals.info/products/hard-drive
8. JHS Pedals - Cheese Ball: https://jhspedals.info/products/cheese-ball
9. JHS Pedals - Colour Box V2: https://jhspedals.info/products/colour-box-v2

## Research note
JHS's current catalog is unusually clean for the census because the builder maintains separate Overdrive and Fuzz / Distortion collections and gives explicit dirt labels on individual product pages. The conservative rule remains: use the builder's own product identity, preserve explicit hybrids, consolidate ordinary versions, and do not manufacture new categories from tonal capability alone.

# The Dirt Archive - Builder Census Block 131

## Builder
**Walrus Audio**

Walrus Audio is a previously uncovered builder with a clearly documented current and historical dirt catalog. Its official catalog groups Overdrive, Fuzz, and Distortion together, while its discontinued catalog preserves several older dirt products. This block follows Walrus Audio's own product identities and consolidates routine variants into lineages where appropriate.

## Overdrive
- **Voyager Preamp/Overdrive / Voyager Preamp/Overdrive MKII** - Overdrive lineage; the original Voyager is discontinued and the MKII is current.
- **385 Overdrive / 385 Overdrive MKII** - Overdrive lineage.
- **Ages Five-State Overdrive** - current Overdrive.
- **Warhorn Mid-Range Overdrive** - historical/discontinued Overdrive.
- **Fundamental Series Drive** - historical/discontinued Overdrive.
- **Badwater Bass Pre-amp and D.I.** - historical/discontinued product with an explicitly documented overdrive section; retained as Overdrive for the dirt census.

## Distortion
- **Iron Horse LM308 Distortion** - current Distortion.
- **Eras Five-State Distortion** - current Distortion.
- **Fundamental Series Distortion** - historical/discontinued Distortion.
- **Melee: Wall of Noise** - current Distortion + Reverb; the target dirt identity is Distortion and the reverb function is outside scope.

## Fuzz
- **Eons Five-State Fuzz** - current Fuzz.
- **Silt Harmonic Fuzz** - current Fuzz.
- **Janus Fuzz/Tremolo** - historical/current product lineage; target dirt identity is Fuzz, with Tremolo outside scope.
- **Jupiter Multi-Clip Fuzz / Jupiter Multi-Clip Fuzz V2** - historical Jupiter Fuzz lineage.
- **Fundamental Series Fuzz** - historical/discontinued Fuzz.

## Explicit category overlaps
- **Badwater Bass Pre-amp and D.I. = Overdrive + non-target preamp/compression/EQ/DI functions.** Walrus explicitly says the unit features overdrive alongside optical compression and four-band EQ. Only the overdrive identity is retained here.
- **Melee = Distortion + Reverb.** Walrus explicitly describes Melee as a distortion + reverb combination; only Distortion is part of this census.
- **Janus = Fuzz + Tremolo.** Walrus explicitly identifies Janus as Fuzz/Tremolo; only Fuzz is counted.
- **Eons = Fuzz**, despite individual clipping modes producing distortion-like tones. The builder's product identity is Fuzz, so no extra Distortion category is inferred.

## Historical / variant restraint
- **Voyager and Voyager MKII** are one Voyager Overdrive lineage rather than separate unrelated designs.
- **385 and 385 MKII** are one 385 Overdrive lineage.
- **Jupiter and Jupiter V2** are one Jupiter Fuzz lineage.
- **Fundamental Drive, Fundamental Distortion, and Fundamental Fuzz** remain separate target dirt products because Walrus explicitly named and described them as three different effects.
- Cosmetic/minimal variants are not counted as new dirt designs.
- Reverb, delay, modulation, octave, boost, EQ, compression, utility, and amp/cab products remain outside the target categories unless Walrus explicitly identifies a target dirt function as part of the product. When a combination pedal contains a target dirt section, only that dirt category is recorded.
- No components, schematics, cloning, PCB, BOM, or internal-build information is recorded in this block.

## Primary / authoritative sources
1. Walrus Audio - Overdrive, Fuzz & Distortion: https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/overdrive-fuzz-distortion
2. Walrus Audio - All Effect Pedals: https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/all-effect-pedals
3. Walrus Audio - Discontinued Pedals: https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/discontinued-pedals
4. Walrus Audio - Product Manuals: https://www.walrusaudio.com/pages/manuals
5. Walrus Audio - Silt Harmonic Fuzz: https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/overdrive-fuzz-distortion/products/silt-harmonic-fuzz
6. Walrus Audio - Eons Five-State Fuzz: https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/eons-five-state-fuzz
7. Walrus Audio - Voyager Preamp/Overdrive: https://www.walrusaudio.com/collections/discontinued-pedals/products/voyager-overdrive
8. Walrus Audio - Fundamental Series Fuzz: https://www.walrusaudio.com/products/fundamental-series-fuzz

## Research note
Walrus Audio's official catalog makes the current dirt group easy to verify, while the discontinued page supplies authoritative historical identities. The conservative census follows Walrus's own labels instead of creating extra categories from tonal capability. The clearest multi-effect overlaps are Badwater (Overdrive plus non-target functions), Melee (Distortion plus Reverb), and Janus (Fuzz plus Tremolo).

# Block 132 — Catalinbread

Date: 2026-09-17
Scope: Builder → Pedals census only

## Builder

Catalinbread

## Primary source set

- https://catalinbread.com/collections/foundation-overdrive-series
- https://catalinbread.com/collections/boost-fuzz-distortion-series
- https://catalinbread.com/collections/all
- https://catalinbread.com/collections/retired-pedals
- https://catalinbread.com/pages/manuals

Catalinbread's current site separates a Foundation Overdrive Series from a Boost/Fuzz/Distortion Series, while the full product catalog and retired-pedal catalog expose additional current and historical dirt products. The manuals page was used as a secondary official index for older models and variants.

## Overdrive

- Dirty Little Secret
- Dirty Little Secret Deluxe
- SFT
- RAH
- Sabbra Cadabra
- Formula 55
- Formula 5F6
- Formula 51
- Formula No. 5
- Galileo
- Galileo 4K
- Clean Little Secret
- Little Secret
- Naga Viper
- Silver Kiss Overdrive
- SideArm Overdrive
- Knight School Overdrive
- Knightdrive
- Maxon 40th Anniversary Catalinbread Modified Overdrive (OD808-40C)
- Crooner Preamp
- Airstrip Console Pre

## Distortion

- Carbide Distortion
- Katzenkönig
- SFT
- Formula 51
- Formula 55
- Formula 5F6
- Sabbra Cadabra
- Crooner Preamp
- Perseus DIO (dry distortion section only; octave-down function excluded from census categorying)

## Fuzz

- Fuzzrite
- Fuzzrite Germanium
- Fuzzrite Mini
- Merkin Fuzz
- Manx Loaghtan Fuzz
- Giygas 2K Fuzz
- StarCrash Fuzz
- Katzenkönig
- SFT
- Crooner Preamp

## Explicit overlaps / category handling

- Katzenkönig = Fuzz + Distortion. Official Catalinbread material describes it as combining Tone Bender MkII fuzz characteristics with Rat-style distortion.
- SFT = Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Official documentation explicitly describes clean/overdriven operation, high-gain distortion, and huge fuzz sounds in Stoner mode.
- Formula 51 = Overdrive + Distortion. Its official page describes the range from gritty cleans through cranked Tweed tones to heavy distortion.
- Formula 55 / Formula 5F6 / Sabbra Cadabra are retained as overdrive/amp-in-a-box dirt products; gain-heavy descriptions do not automatically create extra categories unless the product is explicitly presented as another dirt type.
- Crooner Preamp = Overdrive + Distortion + Fuzz. Official documentation explicitly describes clean boost, overdrive, distortion, and full-throated fuzz.
- Perseus DIO is not counted as a standalone fuzz/distortion product solely because its dry path has distortion. Its primary identity is an analog octave-down device, so only the explicitly dirt-relevant dry distortion capability is noted and the pedal is not used as a separate fuzz entry.
- Naga Viper is counted as Overdrive, despite its aggressive/high-gain range.
- Silver Kiss is Overdrive; its clean-boost and EQ functions are not separate census categories.
- Airstrip Console Pre is counted as Overdrive because the current official product family describes preamp saturation and dirt operation. It is not split into a separate preamp category.

## Historical / variant restraint

- Current official catalog entries, retired-pedal entries, and official manuals were used to capture builder-to-pedal relationships without multiplying cosmetic editions, B-stock entries, artwork variants, or minor reissues.
- Fuzzrite / Fuzzrite Germanium / Fuzzrite Mini are retained as named pedal families because the official catalog distinguishes them, but cosmetic editions are not separately counted.
- Formula No. 5 reissue / Spaceship Art are treated as one Formula No. 5 lineage.
- SFT Blackout and Fireworks editions are not separate builder relationships.
- Sabbra Cadabra anniversary/art editions are not separate relationships.
- Naga Viper limited/art variants are not separate relationships.
- Retired products remain eligible because the census is builder-to-pedal, not current-stock-only.
- DIY kits are excluded from the pedal census when they are kit-only listings; the built Knight School Overdrive is included as a pedal.
- Non-dirt effects such as delays, reverbs, modulation, filters, EQ-only devices, tap accessories, apparel, cables, and gift cards are excluded.
- No photos, biographies, components, schematics, PCB/BOM information, gutshots, cloning information, or deep historical research were added.

## Verification notes

Catalinbread's official Foundation Overdrive page lists Dirty Little Secret, SFT, RAH, Sabbra Cadabra, Formula 55, Formula 5F6, Galileo, Galileo 4K, Dirty Little Secret Deluxe, and Clean Little Secret. The official Boost/Fuzz/Distortion page lists Fuzzrite, Fuzzrite Germanium, Katzenkönig, Giygas 2K Fuzz, Fuzzrite Mini, and related gain products. The full catalog and manuals were checked to catch older named dirt pedals such as Formula 51, Merkin Fuzz, Manx Loaghtan Fuzz, Silver Kiss, SideArm, and historical variants.

Official product descriptions independently verify the important overlaps: SFT covers overdrive/distortion/fuzz territory; Crooner explicitly covers boost/overdrive/distortion/fuzz; Katzenkönig combines fuzz and distortion; Formula 51 spans gritty clean through heavy distortion; Manx Loaghtan is explicitly a fuzz; Merkin is explicitly a fuzz; and Carbide is explicitly a distortion.
